#CH 161
#TITLE: Two Awkward Encounters
Madam Qian went on, "Your father is the head of this household. Since he agreed, your mother and I have no reason to object. No matter how many concubines you take, remember this: once Zhiyu marries into our family, she will be your primary wife, and you must hold her in your heart. Go on now, take Zhiyu for a stroll."
Qiao Wenxian had meant to use taking a concubine to vent his bitterness over the marriage, yet the gesture fell flat. Left with no choice but to obey, he gave a courteous nod. "After you, Lady Miao."
Miao Zhiyu rose to her feet. This was her first time spending time alone with Young Master Qiao, and she treasured the chance enough to set aside her displeasure over the concubine for the moment. She stepped beside him and said, "After you, Young Master Qiao."
Saying no more, Qiao Wenxian descended from the pavilion and headed toward the gardens. Miao Zhiyu hurried after him, striving to keep pace shoulder to shoulder. Watching their son lead his bride-to-be off for a walk, Madam Qian and Concubine Mi beamed with delight, and Madam Qin looked just as pleased.
Madam Qian turned her gaze back to the playing field just as Chen Wenxin swung her club and knocked a ball straight into a hole. "Young Madam Zhu has a fine hand at chuiwan," she remarked. "She may not match what I could do in my youth, yet among women of her generation, skill like hers is rare."
"Young Master Chen plays well too," Madam Qin added. "It looks like the brother and sister will take the first match."
Chatting and laughing, the three women went on watching the game.
Trailing behind his master and Lady Miao, Nan Shuang could not stop thinking about Qiao Wenxian taking Xiaguo as a concubine. He needed to forget Xiaguo fast, or how could he face serving his master in the days ahead? The quickest way to forget one person, he decided, was to put someone new in his heart.
Who should take that place? His eyes fell on the maid walking beside him. He had paid her little mind before, yet looking closer today, she was not bad-looking at all. Leaning in, he murmured, "Hey, what is your name?"
The maid took no offense. Her mistress was about to marry Young Master Qiao, so getting on good terms with his attendants was only sensible. "My name is Youfan," she whispered back.
"I am Nan Shuang. Let us be friends from now on, alright?" he asked with care.
"Sure," Youfan agreed without hesitation.
That single crisp word lifted a weight off Nan Shuang's chest, and a satisfied smile spread across his face.
Meanwhile, Shen Jianchen left the field and entered the gardens. After searching a short while, he spotted the two he was looking for walking toward him. Quickening his stride to meet them, he flicked a glance at Qianli Xun before addressing Mengrong. "You made Brother search quite a while."
Happy that he had come looking for her on his own, Qiao Mengrong smiled. "What did Brother need me for?"
"The chuiwan matches have started, and I want to join. It takes a team of two, and I do not care to partner with anyone else. Will you pair up with me?"
The request put Qiao Mengrong in a bind. "I would love to team up with Brother, truly, yet I do not know how to play chuiwan, and I do not like it either."
"No matter. I can teach you," Shen Jianchen said in an encouraging tone. "Chuiwan is simple enough. You will pick it up in two swings."
Qiao Mengrong stayed hesitant, afraid clumsy playing would embarrass him.
Qianli Xun saw straight through Shen Jianchen's little scheme. "Since Mengrong feels uncomfortable, Your Highness would do better to skip the match and sit in the pavilion watching the others play with her."
Disappointment washed over Shen Jianchen. "Youqi Jushi makes a fair point. Do not force yourself, Mengrong. Brother will sit the match out."
Unwilling to let him down, Qiao Mengrong hit on an idea. "Brother, why not team up with Shuzhen instead?"
Hearing the exact suggestion he had angled for, Shen Jianchen put on a reluctant front. "Very well. Seeing that Youqi Jushi is your closest confidante, I suppose I can make do."
Qianli Xun understood what drove him, yet watching him play on Mengrong's innocence and kindness to steer her like that set her temper flaring. She shot Shen Jianchen a glare, then turned a warm smile on Mengrong. "I am not in the mood for chuiwan today, Mengrong. I just want to stay and chat with you."
Guilt pricked at Shen Jianchen. He had no business using Mengrong for his own private feelings. "Forget it. I will not play after all. Go on chatting with Youqi Jushi."
He turned to head back to the pavilion, only for Qiao Mengrong to catch his sleeve. "Do not go, Brother. Watch me handle this." She turned to plead with Shuzhen. "Shuzhen, please say yes? Brother offering to partner with you does you great honor. He is a prince, so you ought to show him some face."
Qianli Xun hesitated, then froze as Qiao Wenxian and Miao Zhiyu came into view, strolling side by side as they admired the garden scenery. She had thought seeing them together would leave her untouched, yet a sharp ache pierced her chest. Letting go of old feelings was never as simple as saying the words.
As the pair drew near, she raised her voice on purpose. "Alright! I agree to team up with Prince Yi!"
Shen Jianchen blinked in surprise. Much as he had wanted her agreement, her sudden turnaround struck him as odd. One look over his shoulder showed him Wenyuan and Lady Miao walking up, and he understood at once why she had said yes. His spirits sank lower still.
Qiao Wenxian saw Shuzhen and knew she had seen him too. Remembering her icy manner earlier and how she had handed Xiaguo over to be his concubine, anger flared inside him. A branch of blooming peach blossoms hung right beside the path, so he reached up and plucked a flower. "Zhiyu, this blossom is lovely. Let me place it in your hair."
Startled and thrilled to hear him use her given name and offer to pin a flower in her hair, Miao Zhiyu glanced past him, spotted Youqi Jushi a short distance away, and grasped the truth at once. She might as well play along. They were betrothed, after all, with no reason to shy from onlookers. "Young Master Qiao... no, Wenyuan, the flower you picked is lovely indeed. I adore it."
Qiao Wenxian tucked the peach blossom beside Miao Zhiyu's chignon. Against the soft petals, her delicate face looked more radiant than ever.
Seeing the man she had once loved place a flower in another woman's hair left Qianli Xun fighting a wave of awkwardness.
Worried for her friend, Qiao Mengrong tugged on her brother's sleeve and called out in a low voice, "Brother."
Shen Jianchen had seen the whole display. Beneath his own pang of gloom, a spark of satisfaction flickered. Wenyuan's clumsy attempt to provoke jealousy would backfire and only push Qianli Xun to give up on him for good. Reading Mengrong's silent plea from her tug on his robe, he stepped forward to greet them. "Wenyuan, I looked everywhere for you earlier, and here you are keeping a fair lady company."
Miao Zhiyu dropped into a swift curtsy. "Your Highness Prince Yi, Zhiyu pays her respects."
"No need for ceremony, Lady Miao," Shen Jianchen said. "In ten days you will marry into the Qiao family. Once you are one of them, there is no call to stand on formality with me." For a brief moment, his own betrothal slipped his mind. If Qianli Xun and Wenyuan had loved each other so much and still ended up torn apart, it could only mean she was meant for him. A smile crept onto his face.
Prince Yi's warm words lifted Miao Zhiyu's spirits higher still. She had first fallen for Young Master Qiao over his literary talent, yet now she saw how much family standing mattered too. Once she became a Qiao bride, aside from Princess Jiahe, who in all of Lin'an could rival her?
Qiao Wenxian had no heart to banter with Prince Yi. He flicked a glance at Shuzhen, who strolled up arm in arm with Princess Jiahe without a flicker of distress on her face.
Once they drew close, Miao Zhiyu curtsied to the princess as well. "Had I known Youqi Jushi and Your Highness were strolling in the gardens, I would have joined you. I do not care for chuiwan in the slightest."
"Nor do I," Qiao Mengrong replied. "Still, with Young Master Qiao by your side, I would hardly dare steal you away."
Unwilling to stand in silence, Qianli Xun forced out a remark so Wenyuan would think she cared nothing at all. "I happen to enjoy chuiwan. I just agreed to team up with Prince Yi. I have not had a proper workout in ages, so this is a good chance to stretch my limbs."
Shen Jianchen bit back a laugh. Her cover for her awkwardness was clumsy at best. Who on earth would chime in when you bragged about loving chuiwan to two women who hated it?
Sure enough, nobody picked up the thread.
Unwilling to leave the woman he loved hanging in silence, Shen Jianchen stepped in. "Since Youqi Jushi has agreed to partner with me, shall we head to the field now?"
Qianli Xun had been itching to leave anyway. Wenyuan stood mute, staring holes into her, and lingering longer might upset Miao Zhiyu. "When Prince Yi commands, Shuzhen dares not refuse."
Shen Jianchen glanced at Qiao Wenxian. "Wenyuan, care to come watch?"
Qiao Wenxian tore his gaze from Shuzhen's face. "If Your Highness is taking the field, I shall of course go cheer you on."
The five of them retraced their steps together.
Hardly had they stepped out of the gardens onto the chuiwan grounds when Lin Jue approached with his bride, seeking out Prince Yi. Having arrived late, Lin Jue had gone to Prince Yi's pavilion first, only to find the personal guard Bai Feng standing alone inside. Word from bystanders sent him out toward the gardens, and now he stumbled right into the prince himself. Stepping forward, he bowed low. "Your Highness, this humble official arrived late and failed to pay respects in time. Please forgive the oversight."
"This is a chuiwan field, not Lin'an Prefecture's yamen," Shen Jianchen said. "Rank does not count here. Relax, Division Commander Lin; no need to be so stiff."
"Your Highness speaks true. I shall obey your instructions."
Shen Jianchen glanced at the woman beside Lin Jue. She was no peerless beauty, yet she was more than a match for Lin Jue. "Is this your new wife?"
Before Lin Jue could answer, the bride dipped into another deep curtsy. "On our wedding day, my husband told me Your Highness graced us with your presence. I had hoped to pay my respects then, yet Your Highness departed early, so only today can I fulfill the rite."
Shen Jianchen recalled that day: bored to tears after watching the couple bow to heaven and earth, he had slipped out on the excuse of visiting the latrine. "Mengrong had an urgent matter that day, so I had to leave ahead of time. I hope you will excuse it. Besides, we will have plenty of chances to meet."
"Your Highness is most gracious," the bride replied. "My husband brought me here today for the sole purpose of paying respects together. Only after greeting Your Highness is our wedding truly complete."
Qianli Xun already knew Lin Jue had remarried, and she sighed inwardly. No matter the era, divorced men hated being lonely and rushed straight into another match. Plain as day, this bride felt quite content with Lin Jue, chiming in to assert her place as lady of the house.
Seeing her brother had no wish to drag out small talk with Lin Jue's bride, Qiao Mengrong stepped in. "I hear you are not from Lin'an. Where is your maiden home?"
Guessing this must be Princess Jiahe, whom her husband had made a point of mentioning, the bride curtsied again. "Indeed, I am not from Lin'an. My family hails from Weizhou."
"That is quite a distance. Marrying so far from home cannot be easy. How should we address you?"
"My maiden surname is Bai, and my given name is Meiruo."
"We are all around the same age, so from now on we will call you Meiruo."
"To be called so by Your Highness is Meiruo's great fortune."
Pleased to see his new bride winning the princess's favor, Lin Jue threw a smug glance at Zhu Shuzhen, thinking, You turned up your nose at me, yet plenty of others know my worth. Look at us now: I have a fresh bride, while you are tangled in a pile of messes. Heh!
Since Mengrong had said "we," Qianli Xun could not stay silent. She stepped forward. "If Her Highness calls you Meiruo, I shall call you Meiruo too. We are all friends now. Whenever you feel like strolling through Lin'an's markets, just invite us along."
Delighted to gain another acquaintance, Bai Meiruo asked, "May I ask how to address you, miss?"
"She is the famed poetess of Jiangnan," Qiao Mengrong introduced her, "known far and wide as Youqi Jushi."
Lin Jue had already told Bai Meiruo about his divorced former wife, though she had never expected to run into her here. Seeing no hint of awkwardness on the other woman's face, Bai Meiruo relaxed at once. "Back in Weizhou, a dear friend of mine loved Youqi Jushi's ci lyrics and spoke of them nonstop, so your name stayed fresh in my mind. Meeting you today is the blessing of three lifetimes."
Miao Zhiyu stepped up as well. "My surname is Miao, given name Zhiyu. Call me whatever you like."
"Pleased to meet you, Lady Miao," Bai Meiruo said, pouring on the flattery. "What struck me most upon arriving in Lin'an was how fresh and lovely the young women here are. Yet meeting you two and Her Highness today shows me everyone I saw before was plain as mud."
"I am not from Lin'an, though," Qiao Mengrong clarified.
"Even so, Your Highness was born with peerless beauty, and nourished by Lin'an's water and air, you look lovelier still."
Shen Jianchen had heard enough syrupy chatter. Just then, the referee's call rang out across the field: "Young Master Chen takes the Middle Tally first! Victory!"
Everyone looked toward the green, where Chen Haoyu hoisted his club and jumped up and down in glee.
Qianli Xun scoffed inside: How childish!
Chen Wenxin stepped up to the prize table, lifted a jadeite bangle from the tray, and held it high for all to see with a triumphant grin. Seeing her sister-in-law happy warmed Qianli Xun's heart too.
Chen Haoyu's strutting rubbed Shen Jianchen the wrong way. One moment ago he had let himself believe Qianli Xun would end up his; one look at Chen Haoyu snapped him back to reality, the betrothal blocking his path like a wall.
"Youqi Jushi, come play chuiwan with me at once. Let us take on Young Master Chen for a round."
"Give me a moment to get ready at my pavilion," Qianli Xun agreed on the spot, more than eager to escape this circle of awkward company.
#CH 162
#TITLE: A Mild Match of Chuiwan
Once Prince Yi spoke, the group broke off their chatter and scattered. Lin Jue led Bai Meiruo back to their seats in the pavilion, while Qiao Wenxian and Miao Zhiyu returned to their elders. Since her brother was taking the field, Qiao Mengrong followed him to see to his change of clothes.
Qianli Xun returned to the Zhu family's pavilion and found no sign of Matron Wei, guessing she had gone off to mingle and build ties with the other matrons.
Spotting her mistress, Kuqiao hurried up. "Miss, you are back at last."
"I only went for a short stroll," Qianli Xun teased. "You make it sound like I vanished for hours."
"Young Madam went off to play chuiwan, Matron Wei went off chatting, and Xiaguo wandered off somewhere just a moment ago. That left only me and Hongdou, and she never says a word. I was bored to death."
Qianli Xun glanced at Hongdou. The girl really was quiet, probably still adjusting after being promoted to personal maid. "Kuqiao, if you are bored, go hang out with Bai Feng. Hongdou can attend me here."
"Can I really?"
"Why not? Go on."
"Thank you, Miss!" Kuqiao skipped out of the pavilion to find Bai Feng.
Hongdou stepped closer. "Miss, what do you need me to do?"
"I am going to play chuiwan. Tie my sleeve-tying cord and fetch me a club."
Obedient as ever, Hongdou took out the cord she had prepared in advance, tied back her mistress's sleeves, handed over the club with both hands, and fell back into silence at her side.
"Do not be so stiff around me from now on," Qianli Xun told her. "Talk more. I like chatty maids."
"Yes, Miss."
"If you get bored, feel free to wander around."
"I do not care to wander. I will stay right here and watch Miss play chuiwan."
Qianli Xun did not push her. Club in hand, she stepped down from the pavilion and walked onto the field, where Shen Jianchen already stood waiting in fresh sporting attire.
Stepping across the thin mat of dry grass poked through with tender green shoots, Qianli Xun felt reluctant to crush the sprouts and tried stepping around them. Then she caught herself: dozens of people had trampled this turf all morning, so what difference would her caution make? Once the match started, she would have no mind to spare for new grass anyway. She planted her boots down and strode straight ahead.
Trampled grass either fought with all its might to keep growing or surrendered, withered to dust, and vanished without a trace. Just like the bare patch of dirt under Shen Jianchen's feet, where the turf had given up under fate's relentless pounding. Would she too have to bow to fate and let it toss her around in the palm of its hand?
"What are you thinking about?"
Shen Jianchen's greeting snapped her out of it. "Nothing. When do we start?"
"In a moment."
Spotting her sister-in-law and Chen Haoyu walking up, Qianli Xun nodded toward them. "Here come our opponents."
"Teaming up with me to play against your own family—are you sure that is alright?" Shen Jianchen teased.
"My sister-in-law is family; Chen Haoyu is not," Qianli Xun snapped. "Cut the nonsense in front of me, or I will go tattle on you to Mengrong."
Hearing she did not count Chen Haoyu as family brightened Shen Jianchen's mood. "I have done nothing wrong."
"Baited Mengrong into begging me to be your partner—that counts as wrong."
Before he could reply, Chen Wenxin and Chen Haoyu arrived and bowed to Prince Yi.
"I heard Your Highness wants to play a match against Haoyu and me, so I brought him right over to pay respects," Chen Wenxin said. "If we give offense during the game, please forgive us."
"There is no high or low on the chuiwan field," Shen Jianchen said. "Do not hold back on my account, Sister-in-Law Wenxin."
Mid-bow, Chen Haoyu frowned to himself. Prince Yi teams up with Zhu Shuzhen and calls my sister Sister-in-Law? Does he fancy Zhu Shuzhen? No way. Even I would not touch that tigress, so why would a prince?
The address bewildered Chen Wenxin as well. "I will certainly play my best, yet for Your Highness to call a commoner like me Sister-in-Law seems rather..."
Qianli Xun shot Shen Jianchen a sharp glare, fuming inside: Transmigrated into a prince and you still talk without a filter!
Seeing Chen Haoyu had triggered Shen Jianchen's urge to stake his claim, making an intimate title slip out. Under Qianli Xun's glare, he hurried to patch it over. "Mengrong and Youqi Jushi are closest friends. Being Youqi Jushi's sister-in-law makes you Mengrong's sister-in-law too, so calling you Sister-in-Law does no harm."
Chen Haoyu let out a breath. So that was all it was—he did not like Zhu Shuzhen after all. Then again, why was he even nervous? If Prince Yi really took a shine to Zhu Shuzhen, wouldn't that take a massive headache right off his hands?
A Qiao family maid called out, "Next match: Young Master Chen versus Prince Yi! The prize is our madam's favorite gold ruyi scepter!"
Spectators near and far craned their necks toward the wooden box in the maid's hands, where a gleaming gold ruyi scepter rested.
"Would Madam Qiao ever put up a prize that lavish if Prince Yi were not playing?" someone muttered.
"You can say that again," another voice agreed.
The referee shouted, "Let the match begin! Prince Yi's side, red flags! Young Master Chen's side, yellow flags!"
Ending their chat, the four players walked club-in-hand to the first starting pad, where the referee had already placed the ball.
Qianli Xun squinted down the turf. Some holes in the grass bore yellow flags and some bore red. If she guessed right, their team had to sink balls into the yellow-flagged holes to score. Eyeballing the distance, she judged this opening round an easy shot.
The yellow team went first. Chen Haoyu stepped up to the starting pad, set his stance, swung, and sent the ball rolling straight into a red-flagged hole. Chen Wenxin went second and sank hers with equal ease. Third came Shen Jianchen; a shot like this held zero pressure for him, and a light tap sent his ball hopping straight into a yellow-flagged hole. Last was Qianli Xun. Having watched closely, she found it little different from modern golf. One clean stroke, and her ball dropped straight in.
"Your skill has come on in leaps and bounds, Shuzhen," Chen Wenxin praised.
Each player took two more shots from the same pad, leaving both teams dead even with one tally apiece.
The four followed the referee toward the next starting pad. Prince Yi strode out front alongside the referee while Zhu Shuzhen walked with Chen Wenxin. Seeing an opening, Chen Haoyu sidled up. "Zhu Shuzhen, you know martial arts and Prince Yi knows martial arts. If the two of you team up and beat us, that is an unfair victory."
Qianli Xun stopped dead and glared at him. "Do yourself a favor and crack open a book sometime. You do not even know what 'unfair victory' means, throwing phrases around at random!"
"Then what does it mean?"
"Cannot be bothered to teach you." Remembering their engagement, she grabbed Chen Haoyu and pulled him aside. Chen Wenxin smiled and kept walking without minding them.
Chen Haoyu shook off her hand. "What are you dragging me over here for?"
"Tell me straight," Qianli Xun whispered. "Is your grandmother still planning to stall?"
"Of course she is."
"Good. The wedding date is right around the corner. Everything from here on rests on you, so stay sharp."
"Relax. Grandmother dotes on me most and listens to everything I say."
Having reached the second starting pad, where the red team had the first stroke, Shen Jianchen turned to invite Qianli Xun up—only to spot her huddled in private conversation with Chen Haoyu. Jealousy flared in his chest. "Youqi Jushi! Hurry over! It is your turn to strike!"
Hearing his call, Qianli Xun ditched Chen Haoyu and strode over to the second starting pad.
Watching from the side, Chen Wenxin studied Prince Yi's expression. Something felt off, though she dared not follow the thought further. She shook her head. What wild nonsense am I imagining? It makes no sense at all!
The turf stretching between the second pad and its target hole rose and dipped over uneven mounds, making a clean sink tricky. None of that fazed Qianli Xun. She raised her club and swung; the ball sailed over the bumps and dropped dead center into the opposing hole.
Chuiwan lacked the roaring thrill of polo, so even with Prince Yi on the field, plenty of guests kept chatting and strolling as they pleased. Only die-hard chuiwan fans like Madam Qian, climbers eager to curry Prince Yi's favor like Lin Jue, or devoted onlookers like Mengrong kept their eyes fixed on the match. Qiao Wenxian watched because he cared about Shuzhen, and Miao Zhiyu watched only wherever Wenyuan's eyes went.
When Qianli Xun sank her ball, Madam Qian clapped in admiration. "I never guessed Miss Zhu had such talent at chuiwan!"
Concubine Mi sniffed. "What good is skill at chuiwan? For a woman, a good reputation is what counts."
"Concubine Mi speaks the truth," Madam Qin chimed in.
Both women saw Madam Qian's face darken and caught their slip at once. "Do not take it to heart, Sister," Concubine Mi scrambled to explain. "I meant Miss Zhu."
Madam Qin hurried to mend her words too. "A woman with both chuiwan skill and a sterling reputation is best of all—just like you, Madam."
Only then did Madam Qian smile again. "Watching them play has my hands itching. I might step out for a round myself later."
Seeing the Qiao women look down on Youqi Jushi eased the tightness in Miao Zhiyu's chest a little.
After making the rounds of several other pavilions, Matron Wei at last strolled over to the Qiaos' pavilion. She had saved them for last on purpose; after how the Qiaos had treated her young mistress, showing up late made both her stance and her displeasure clear.
"Madam Qian is a master at chuiwan," Matron Wei called out. "You really ought to give the younger generation a demonstration."
Everyone turned to see Matron Wei approaching. Madam Qian laughed. "I was just wondering why Matron Wei was visiting every pavilion except ours! Look how timely you are. Bring a seat!"
The pavilion was crowded, and every stool was already taken. "Madam, there are no stools left," a maid reported.
Miao Zhiyu stood up at once. "Let Matron Wei have mine."
"Oh, I could never do that!" Matron Wei demurred. "Lady Miao is a precious noble daughter. Please sit back down. I can stand while I chat with Madam."
"Do not stand on ceremony, Matron. I have been sitting a long while and would like to go for a walk anyway."
In truth, Miao Zhiyu could not bear sitting there another minute watching Qiao Wenxian stare nonstop at the playing field. She wanted fresh air in the gardens by herself. Dropping a curtsy to the elders, she left without waiting for leave.
A maid moved the stool beside Madam Qian, and Matron Wei accepted it. One glance at Young Master Qiao showed he paid zero heed to Lady Miao, his eyes locked onto the field—and right onto her own young mistress. So Young Master Qiao had not let go after all.
Matron Wei kept her focus on the elders and struck up conversation. "May I ask when the wedding between your two families has been set?"
"Our in-laws are eager," Madam Qin jumped in, "so they moved it up another week, to the third day of the third month."
"That is right around the corner," Matron Wei said. "Wangyue Pavilion will be sure to send a generous gift." She said it solely to signal that her mistress had moved on from Young Master Qiao.
Aside from Qiao Wenxian, every person in the pavilion smiled knowingly.
Madam Qian looked out at Chen Haoyu swinging on the field. "A second marriage tie between the Zhu and Chen families—everyone is green with envy. The more I look today, the better matched Miss Zhu and Young Master Chen seem. When have the two families set their wedding?"
Chen Haoyu's name was mud across Lin'an; calling her young mistress a fine match for him was a barbed dig at her reputation. Irritation flared in Matron Wei's chest, yet her tone stayed polished. "Envied indeed. I hear Young Master Chen is slated to inherit the Chen family business, so his elders insisted on a bride of peerless virtue, beauty, and learning. My young mistress excels with both brush and fist—the sort of treasure the Chens could search for with a lantern and still not find. That is why they are in such a rush to welcome her home. Their date is in the third month too, just after Young Master Qiao's."
The sting in her words did not go unnoticed.
Realizing she had spoken out of turn, Madam Qian smoothed things over. "What a spring of double blessings! The Qiao family will send a grand gift when the time comes."
Concubine Mi jumped right in. "Hearing the children are thriving delights us all. Matron, you must drop by to chat with Sister and me more often, and when you return to Zhu Manor, tell Madam Xiang she is always welcome at Sister's gatherings."
With nothing more to gain by pressing them, Matron Wei nodded. "Of course. I will make a special trip back to Zhu Manor to pass your message along to my madam."
Sitting beside them, Qiao Wenxian caught every undercurrent of the polite fencing. Fed up, he stood and walked off without a word.
"Wenyuan, where are you going?" Concubine Mi called out.
He ignored her.
"That boy!" Concubine Mi grumbled.
Unwilling to air her son's faults in front of Matron Wei and Madam Qin, Madam Qian covered for him at once. "Where else could he be going? Off to find Zhiyu, of course. Leave him be and let us keep talking."
The excuse thrilled Madam Qin. "Wenyuan cannot bear being parted from my Zhiyu even for a moment. So fond before the wedding, they will be closer still once married."
Madam Qian and Concubine Mi chuckled along in delight.
Matron Wei shook her head inside. What a trio of master self-deceivers.
#CH 163
#TITLE: A Dull Match, Yet Someone Watches
After her laugh faded, Madam Qian turned back to the field and marveled again. "Miss Zhu's chuiwan skill is remarkable, neck and neck with Prince Yi. Looking at how this is going, the Chen siblings are bound to lose."
"Too soon to call it, Sister," Concubine Mi disagreed. "I have kept my eyes on them the whole time. Both sides have three tallies each; there is no telling who will win yet."
A seasoned player herself, Madam Qian had already read the match. "You seldom play, Sister, so you miss the fine points. They tied the first three tallies, yet in both technique and power, the Chen siblings fall short of Prince Yi and Miss Zhu. The Chens took the Middle Tally earlier only because their rivals played poorly. Against real opponents today, they will fall apart by the Grand Tally."
Hearing her young mistress praised warmed Matron Wei's heart. Watching Shuzhen knock yet another ball cleanly into the opposing hole, she beamed. "I never saw my mistress play chuiwan before. I wonder when she picked up such skill."
Madam Qin searched her memory. "Now that you mention it, Matron, I believe Miss Zhu played once before, and her form was nothing special then. She has improved by leaps and bounds."
Madam Qian recalled what Chen Wenxin had remarked upon arriving. "Have you forgotten, Matron Wei? Young Madam Zhu said earlier that she taught her. Truly, the student has surpassed the teacher."
Matron Wei chuckled. "Look at my failing memory."
Out on the field, the match moved at an easy pace, the four players chatting as they went. Paired together, Qianli Xun and Shen Jianchen naturally talked the most. Shen Jianchen thought partnering up for chuiwan had been a stroke of genius: he could walk openly beside her and talk without restraint. Winning or losing mattered little; he only hoped the match would stretch on as long as possible.
From her pavilion, Qiao Mengrong kept her eyes fixed on her brother. Watching his strong, nimble swings, that old sense of strangeness she had not felt in months crept back into her chest.
When they were children, a year after her brother had brought her into Prince Yi's Manor and his health had briefly improved, they had wandered outside the gates and come upon a group of boys playing chuiwan on packed dirt. Her brother had joined in for the first time, yet the ball refused to obey him and never went near a hole. He had quit on the spot and sworn never to touch chuiwan again—and he had kept his word.
When he had sought her out in the gardens earlier to play, she had grown so used to the brother who had come back from the brink of death that she had given it little thought. Now, sitting still and watching close, sorrow tugged at her. He truly was not the brother she used to know.
Reading her mistress's mood, Xilan offered gentle comfort. "His Highness excels at everything now. You ought to be glad, and hold the prince he is today in your heart."
"Xilan, tell me—do I love the brother from before, or the brother now?"
"Before or now, they are both His Highness himself."
"You are right. Why am I letting my mind wander again? I have not had thoughts like that in ages." Worried Bai Feng might have overheard, Qiao Mengrong scanned the pavilion, saw no sign of him, and asked in relief, "Where is Bai Feng?"
"Kuqiao called him away."
Half joking and half earnest, Qiao Mengrong sighed. "Bai Feng is a good lad. I had half a mind to match the two of you up, yet Kuqiao beat me to him."
"I never want to marry," Xilan replied. "I mean to stay by Your Highness's side for life."
Touched beyond words, Qiao Mengrong answered with warm silence and looked back at the field. Her brother stood beside Shuzhen, chatting as they watched Chen Haoyu line up a shot. They looked so cheerful. It was the first time she had ever seen her brother talk with another woman in such bright spirits. Were it anyone other than Shuzhen, she would have sworn he fancied the girl.
Why had her brother never looked that carefree when talking to her?
An answer soon came to her: the spring sun was bright, the field was lively, and dozens of eyes rested on him. Staying stiff would ruin the mood and make him seem aloof. And why did he keep drifting straight to Shuzhen's side, leaving her no chance to chat with her sister-in-law? Because they were teammates, of course—they had to discuss strategy.
Satisfied with her own reasoning, Qiao Mengrong went back to watching him play. Truth be told, chuiwan bored her stiff; if her brother were not on the field, she would not even bother looking.
After leaving the pavilion, Miao Zhiyu strolled alone through the gardens. Recalling how Qiao Wenxian had placed the peach blossom in her hair, she reached up to touch the petals nestled neatly by her chignon. A soft smile curved her lips as she imagined him trailing after her.
She glanced over her shoulder, only to see Youfan behind her and no trace of Qiao Wenxian. Disappointment dulled her mood as she walked on at a slow pace. Her marriage was settled, and to the man she had longed for—Heaven had been kind to her. Yet the thought of that concubine soured her joy all over again.
Just then, a voice called from behind: "Lady Miao!"
Miao Zhiyu turned and saw Xiaguo, Youqi Jushi's maid. Really, what nerve Youqi Jushi had—unable to marry Young Master Qiao herself, she was planting her own maid beside him. What scheme could she be cooking up?
"What do you want?" Miao Zhiyu asked in a cool tone.
Stepping close, Xiaguo dropped into a proper maid's curtsy, as though greeting her mistress. "Lady Miao must already know that on the day you wed Young Master Qiao, I am to be carried into Qiao Manor too. Since we will live under the same roof, I hope Lady Miao will find room to tolerate me."
Youfan flared up on her mistress's behalf. "Hey! What kind of talk is that?"
"I mean no disrespect," Xiaguo replied. "I only wished to learn Lady Miao's true thoughts, and to hope we can live in harmony once we are in the Qiao household."
Miao Zhiyu looked Xiaguo up and down. The girl had decent looks, and a figure no worse than Youqi Jushi's. "To speak plain, this came out of nowhere and I have not made full peace with it yet. What baffles me is why Youqi Jushi would give you to Wenyuan."
"Do not misunderstand, Lady Miao. My mistress had nothing to do with it. It was... it was me. I fell in love with Young Master Qiao and begged him to take me as a concubine. At first he refused, yet I pleaded over and over until he took pity on me and agreed. Today he asked my mistress in person, and she nodded yes with Master Qiao standing right there."
Xiaguo's earnest manner sounded genuine enough, and Miao Zhiyu's tone softened. "When did you fall for Wenyuan?"
"The very first time my mistress met Young Master Qiao."
"The first time?" Miao Zhiyu remembered well that when she and Youqi Jushi had first met Wenyuan, only Kuqiao had been at Shuzhen's side—and Shuzhen's heart had still belonged to Young Master Xu back then. "I do not recall Youqi Jushi having a maid like you at that time."
Catching her own slip, Xiaguo hurried to correct herself. "Not that first time! I meant the first time my mistress saw him after he came back from Bianliang. That was when I fell for him. I meant to wait until my mistress married him and then beg her to let him take me in, yet then everything went sideways..."
"Since Youqi Jushi did not plant you beside Wenyuan on purpose, I need not fuss over it," Miao Zhiyu said. "Besides, what good would fussing do when Master Qiao has already agreed? Once we are at Qiao Manor, know your place, and I will allow you yours."
"Thank you, Lady Miao!"
"Run along now. Do not bother me again."
Xiaguo curtsied and skipped back the way she had come in high spirits. The Qiao chuiwan tournament had paid off beyond her wildest dreams: her future was settled in a single afternoon, leaving only the wedding sedan to arrive.
Watching Xiaguo's retreating back, Youfan scowled. "Miss, anyone can tell Xiaguo is a scheming little minx. You should show a primary wife's authority right now and put her in her place, or she will walk all over you once you are in Qiao Manor."
Miao Zhiyu scoffed. "I am a lady from an official household. Why stoop to sparring with a country maid? What sort of family are the Qiaos? Do you think they will ever take a servant girl seriously?"
Saying no more, Youfan resumed walking beside her mistress.
Meanwhile, after leaving the pavilion, Qiao Wenxian had hidden in a quiet corner watching Shuzhen on the field, replaying sweet memories of their old outings together. Then his mind drifted to the day she broke things off—her face and her words colder than winter snow. Looking back at the green, he saw her chatting and laughing with Prince Yi, nothing like himself, still drowning in heartbreak.
"Heartless woman!" Qiao Wenxian sighed.
Staying close beside his master, Nan Shuang could guess nine-tenths of his thoughts. "Stop dwelling on Youqi Jushi, Young Master. You are about to take a bride—and a... a concubine too. What a grand occasion."
Catching the sour edge in his tone, Qiao Wenxian knew his impulsive promise to take Xiaguo had stung Nan Shuang. "Do you hold it against me, Nan Shuang?"
"Of course I do! Young Master knew very well that I liked Xiaguo."
"Ah, you and I are two pitiful souls, both in love with women we cannot have."
"I am not like you, Young Master. At least Youqi Jushi loved you back once. Xiaguo never spared me a single glance."
"With things as they are, you and Xiaguo are out of the question. Later, I will find you someone far better."
Nan Shuang perked up at once. "Young Master, may I fancy Youfan?"
"Youfan?" Having paid zero notice to Miao Zhiyu's maid, Qiao Wenxian drew a total blank.
"Lady Miao's maid. She was very pleasant to me today and even agreed to be my friend."
Seeing Nan Shuang bounce back with such cheer filled Qiao Wenxian with envy. If only he could let go of a love half as fast.
Just then, Cao Ban and An Mingxuan strolled past and spotted Qiao Wenxian tucked away in the shadows with Nan Shuang. Puzzled, they walked over. "Brother Qiao, why are you hiding back here?" Cao Ban asked.
Qiao Wenxian turned around. "When did you two get here?"
"Ages ago," An Mingxuan said. "You were either with Lady Miao or sitting with your elders, so we dared not interrupt. If you are free now, come join us. Brother Dongfang is here too."
"Where is he hiding out? I did not see hide nor hair of him at the field." Preoccupied with Shuzhen as he was, Qiao Wenxian had not forgotten his friends; Prince Yi had been easy to spot, yet the three scholars had vanished without a trace.
"Your mind was all on Lady Miao, so how could you notice us?" Cao Ban teased. "Chuiwan is a bore. We found a quiet stretch outside the field for a picnic and kite-flying. Care to come along?"
Before his master could answer, Nan Shuang jumped in excitement. "Yes, yes! I love flying kites! Say yes, Young Master!"
Qiao Wenxian glanced back toward the field. Shuzhen had just sunk another shot, beaming in triumph without a clue that he stood watching from the sidelines. Better to clear his head elsewhere. "Chuiwan is dull enough. I will come chat with you."
Beyond the chuiwan grounds stretched a broad meadow bathed in warm spring sunlight. Young people with no taste for chuiwan or social climbing had gathered here to enjoy the spring breeze and send kites soaring high into the clouds.
Marvelling that the Chen estate boasted such a fine meadow, Qiao Wenxian spotted Dongfang Chenze sitting with several young gentlemen on a rough cloth spread over the grass, a low wooden table loaded with food and drink set before them. His eldest sister, Qiao Wenhui, sat among them too—natural enough, since she and Dongfang Chenze were already betrothed.
#CH 164
#TITLE: Spotting Kites in the Sky
Qiao Wenxian stepped across the newly sprouted grass toward the group. Sharp-eyed Wenhui spotted her brother first and rose to meet him. "You came too, Brother. Why did you not bring Sister Miao?"
Unwilling to speak Miao Zhiyu's name, Qiao Wenxian brushed past the question and chided her instead. "You sneak off to a pleasant spot like this and do not even call your brother?"
"I tried calling you! The moment you stepped onto the chuiwan grounds, you wandered around with Nan Shuang as if your soul had left your body. I shouted several times and you never even heard me." Mentioning Nan Shuang, she peered behind him. "Where is Nan Shuang anyway?"
An Mingxuan pointed a short distance off. "Look over there. The second he arrived, he ran off to hijack someone else's kite."
Sure enough, Nan Shuang was taking a kite spool from Huayu and launching into play, looking nothing like a lad who had just lost his sweetheart.
Wenhui laughed and scolded, "Brother, stop spoiling Nan Shuang, or he will forget he is supposed to be waiting on you."
Qiao Wenxian turned the scolding right back on her. "A young lady sitting out here among a pack of young gentlemen is hardly proper. Head back to the pavilion to keep Mother company, or go play with Wenxiu."
"Fine, I am going." Wenhui cast a parting smile back at Dongfang Chenze on the grass, bade her brother farewell, and strolled toward the pavilions.
The three young men walked over to Dongfang Chenze, and the other gentlemen around the table gave up their spots to go fly kites. Settling onto the cloth, the quartet soaked up the spring sun.
Dongfang Chenze poured Qiao Wenxian a cup of sweet rice wine. "You made it at last, Brother Qiao. I thought you were so busy getting ready to be a groom that you tossed the rest of us out of your mind."
Qiao Wenxian knocked the cup back in one gulp. "Do not mention being a groom to me. You all know where my heart lies."
"It is time to step forward, Brother Qiao," Dongfang Chenze urged. "Let the past stay in the past."
"Easier said than done. I just cannot let go. I envy you and Wenhui—lovers united at last. Why can I not marry the woman I love?" He poured himself another cup and drank.
Cao Ban chimed in to comfort him. "I just saw Youqi Jushi teamed up with Prince Yi for chuiwan. She looked cheerful enough—she has probably moved on. As a man, Brother Qiao, you ought to take it in stride."
Qiao Wenxian fell mute. The brighter Shuzhen smiled, the deeper his heart ached. He poured another cup and downed it hard.
An Mingxuan shot Cao Ban a sharp look, and Cao Ban realized he had put his foot in his mouth. "Listen to my big mouth, always saying the wrong thing. I will drink a penalty cup." He poured one out and raised it in apology.
Qiao Wenxian paid no attention, lost in gloom.
Dongfang Chenze cast about for a fresh subject. "Poetry gatherings have been popping up all over Lin'an lately. On top of the usual annual salons, I hear at least ten new ones have sprouted up, each drawing over fifty guests. When will a poetry gathering hosted by Dongfang Chenze make a name for itself across Lin'an? That would make me content."
"You always swore quality mattered more than headcount," An Mingxuan pointed out. "Why envy big crowds all of a sudden?"
"Turns out my old view was dead wrong. I figured having Brother Qiao and Youqi Jushi headline my salon would put it on the map, yet how many people in Lin'an even know I host one? Nobody is clawing over one another for my invitations. So yes, numbers are the key."
Dongfang Chenze felt pleased with his change of topic; even if Brother Qiao remained quiet, maybe listening would pull his thoughts away from heartbreak.
Catching his friend's intent, Cao Ban kept the ball rolling. "Here is an idea, Brother Dongfang. Since last Mid-Autumn Festival, you have collected a fair stack of Youqi Jushi's poems and lyrics. Bring those out and send invitations far and wide. Half the city will flock to your door on reputation alone."
"Brilliant! Consider it done." Dongfang Chenze turned to Qiao Wenxian. "Brother Qiao, you have to come and back me up when the time comes."
Qiao Wenxian drank yet another cup of sweet rice wine. "Count me in. If it features her verses, of course I will go—if only to honor the days when we called each other kindred spirits."
The three friends exchanged helpless glances and carried on with idle chatter.
Back on the chuiwan field, both teams had fought through the Middle Tally. When Chen Wenxin sank her ball into the opposing hole, the match stayed deadlocked, pushing them into the Grand Tally.
The referee set the ball on the pad and gestured toward a distant hole perched on high ground with no flag beside it. "One stroke each. This decides the match."
Seeing the steep rise, Chen Wenxin nearly gave up. "Maybe I should concede right now. Getting this far is already plenty. I will never clear a slope that high."
"Do not talk defeat before we swing, Sis," Chen Haoyu protested. "We will not know who wins until we play."
Buoyed by her brother, Chen Wenxin decided to give it a try. Qianli Xun caught Shen Jianchen's questioning gaze and gave a crisp nod to show she had no trouble at all.
More onlookers gathered at the pavilion rails now that the final round had arrived, eager to see who would claim the prize.
Prince Yi stepped up first. Setting his stance and gauging the distance, he struck with measured force. The ball traced a clean arc through the air and dropped neatly into the elevated hole.
Qiao Mengrong's heart had climbed into her throat; the instant it landed, she blurted out, "I knew Brother could do it!"
"Truly His Majesty's son!" Madam Qian applauded. "What a gorgeous shot!"
Having left his bride behind, Lin Jue had wandered over to the pavilion of Lin'an's eminent men—Qiao Shouren and Zhu Jingyuan among them—where wine cups clinked back and forth. Every man turned to watch the field and erupted in praise at Prince Yi's stroke.
Chen Wenxin stepped up next. Knowing her odds were slim, she gave it her best swing anyway, yet either her force fell short or the slope proved too steep; the ball kissed the rim of the hole and rolled back down.
"Young Madam Zhu is formidable among women players," Qiao Shouren remarked. "Reaching this stage is already impressive."
"Master Qiao is kind," Zhu Jingyuan replied. "Still, Wenxin's skill falls far short of Prince Yi's."
Qianli Xun came third. If Shen Jianchen could make the shot, she certainly could—yet sinking it plain and simple felt too dull. Why not put on a little show? Channeling her martial internal strength down the club shaft into the ball, she swung.
The ball shot skyward and began spinning in tight circles midair, holding every spectator spellbound.
Madam Qian gaped. "I have never seen a stroke like that! Matron Wei, Miss Zhu is extraordinary—do you think it will drop in?"
"If my mistress hit it that way," Matron Wei said with pride, "in it goes."
Out on the field, Shen Jianchen smiled to himself. You always have to outdo everyone else—even turning chuiwan into a stunt show.
Chen Haoyu snorted. "Show off like that, Zhu Shuzhen, and it will drop straight short—worse than my sister's shot."
"Nobody asked for your commentary!" Qianli Xun shot back.
"Hold your tongue, Haoyu!" Chen Wenxin intervened before they could start snapping at each other.
Even as Chen Wenxin spoke, Qianli Xun flicked a pebble she had palmed beforehand. The stone struck the spinning ball dead on in midair; knocked out of its spin by the impact, the ball shot forward as if guided by design and dropped straight into the hole.
A chorus of cheers rolled across the pavilions: "Bravo!"
"The match is decided," the referee announced. "No need to continue."
Chen Haoyu balked. "Decided or not, I still want my final swing so everyone can admire my form."
Since the grounds belonged to the Chen family, the referee gave Young Master Chen his face. "Go right ahead, Young Master."
Stepping to the pad, Chen Haoyu swung hard. The ball soared high—only to plummet halfway short of the target.
Shen Jianchen scoffed inwardly. Hah! Skill like that, and he thinks he can marry the woman I love? If she had not turned me down, you would be out of an engagement by now!
Never one to miss a chance to needle Chen Haoyu, Qianli Xun jeered, "Your shot was miles worse than my sister-in-law's, Chen Haoyu."
Chen Haoyu took it in stride. "Both Prince Yi and you are monsters out here. Losing to the two of you is no shame at all."
Glancing up by chance, Qianli Xun spotted kites bobbing in the distant sky and brightened. "Look! People are flying kites!"
Shen Jianchen stepped beside her. "Shall we go fly kites in a bit?"
Before Qianli Xun could answer, Chen Wenxin stepped up and curtsied. "Your Highness, the match is over and the prize awaits. I must take Shuzhen back now, so I will leave the prize to you."
Realizing he had pushed too close and caught Young Madam Zhu's suspicion, Shen Jianchen gave a polite nod. "As you wish."
Chen Wenxin took Shuzhen's hand and called, "Haoyu!" Handing his club to the referee, Chen Haoyu bowed to Prince Yi and trailed after his sister.
Watching the three walk off together—looking every inch a real family—sent a cold pang through Shen Jianchen. Unwilling to let Qianli Xun slip away so soon, he moved to wrap up the prize and think of a plan.
Not that he had to fetch it himself. Having wrapped up his chat with Kuqiao and returned just in time to see his master win, Bai Feng dashed onto the field, claimed the gold ruyi scepter, and hoisted it gleaming into the air.
"Prince Yi's team takes the Grand Tally! Victory!" the referee proclaimed.
Already changed into playing clothes with a club in hand, Madam Qian approached Prince Yi. "Your Highness, would you and Miss Zhu care to pair up again for a match against me?"
Having lost all interest in chuiwan and thinking only of how to get Qianli Xun out to the kite meadow, Shen Jianchen turned her down straight away. "I would not dare refuse Grandmother's gracious invitation, yet I am a bit winded, and Miss Zhu mentioned wanting a rest too. Better I return to the pavilion and admire Grandmother's form from there."
"As Your Highness pleases," Madam Qian said, turning to look for another opponent.
Walking back toward the pavilion, Chen Wenxin could not shake her unease. Throughout the match, Prince Yi had acted odd around Shuzhen, grabbing every chance to edge close and dropping remarks ill-fitting for a prince. Worried he might harbor untoward designs on Shuzhen, she scolded her younger brother along the way. "Haoyu, your wedding to Shuzhen is right around the corner, and you do not even think to take her for a stroll?"
"No thanks!" Qianli Xun jumped in at once. "I have zero desire to walk anywhere with Chen Haoyu."
"See?" Chen Haoyu grinned. "Not my fault—she is the one turning me down. I am off to look around elsewhere. Enjoy yourselves."
Chen Wenxin sighed in defeat. Push as she might, neither of the two cared a whit.
Back in the pavilion, Matron Wei had already returned and heaped praise on her young mistress. "Miss played like nobody before or since!"
"Since Matron is family, I will accept the compliment without false modesty."
Laughter rippled through the pavilion.
Just as Qianli Xun moved to sit, she spotted Mengrong approaching with Xilan. "Mengrong is coming for me again. I will go meet her." Stepping down from the pavilion, she greeted her friend. "Coming to drag me out for another walk, Mengrong?"
"Coming to invite you, yes, though not for a walk. I want to fly kites—will you come with me?"
Overhearing every word, Chen Wenxin stepped out of the pavilion. "Princess Jiahe is inviting our Shuzhen to fly kites—is Prince Yi going too?"
"Brother accompanies me wherever I go, so of course he is coming."
The answer poked at Chen Wenxin's nerves again, yet she talked herself down. Prince Yi and Princess Jiahe were betrothed; if the princess saw nothing amiss, why was she fretting over shadows? Perhaps Prince Yi treated Shuzhen with special favor simply because she was the princess's closest friend.
"Since Her Highness invited you in person, Shuzhen, go have fun for a while."
"Thanks, Sister-in-Law!" Qianli Xun linked arms with Mengrong. "Let us go. Where is the kite meadow?"
"Brother says it is on the big meadow right behind the chuiwan grounds."
The two headed off in bright spirits. All three of Shuzhen's maids moved to follow, only for Matron Wei to step in front of them. "Hongdou alone will do. You two stay here."
Hongdou hurried after her mistress at once.
"Why stop us, Matron?" Kuqiao asked in puzzlement.
"Use your eyes," Matron Wei replied. "Her Highness brings only one maid, and Prince Yi's guard is still standing in his pavilion. If our young mistress brings three attendants, would that not look like she is trying to outshine royalty?"
Noticing Bai Feng had stayed put, Kuqiao let the matter drop.
Xiaguo, however, frowned. "Miss always brought both Kuqiao and me along before, and Her Highness never minded—they stayed close as ever. Matron is being way too fussy."
Matron Wei fixed Xiaguo with a sharp glare. "Kuqiao did not complain, so what are you mouthing off about when you are about to become another family's concubine? Bringing two maids was passable before, yet marching around with three is flashy and begs for gossip."
Unconvinced, Xiaguo pushed back. "If you feel that way, Matron, why did you not stop Miss from taking Hongdou on? Or tell her to leave Hongdou home today instead of blocking the two of us now?"
"Sharp little tongue you have there," Matron Wei retorted. "Miss need not worry about you getting bullied at Qiao Manor. As for my reasons, that is none of your concern. Bottom line: when Miss goes to fly kites, the two of you stay put."
Left with nothing to say, Xiaguo sat back down. Her own future was locked in anyway; none of the rest mattered to her.
#CH 165
#TITLE: Flying Kites on the Meadow
Trusting Matron Wei's judgment without question, Chen Wenxin backed her up to soothe the two maids. "Listen to Matron Wei—she is never wrong. You wait on your mistress all day long, so take the chance to catch your breath." As she spoke, she looked out over the field, where Madam Qian had formed a fresh team and stood squared off against Lin Jue and his bride.
Neither maid said another word, and Matron Wei held her peace as well. Irritated as she was with Xiaguo, she admitted the girl had a point. Her mistress had a soft heart and loved taking in maids, not realizing a swollen retinue brought trouble of its own. Matron Wei had been away when Xiaguo was taken in; when Hongdou joined, her mistress had been reeling from heartbreak and saddled with an unwanted betrothal, so Matron Wei had indulged her. More to the point, Xiaguo's heart was no longer with her mistress and Kuqiao would marry sooner or later. Someone loyal and dependable had to be trained up to take their place, and hardworking, honest Hongdou was the clear choice.
Back on the chuiwan grounds, the surrounding walls and pavilions had blocked the view, so Qianli Xun had never guessed a kite meadow lay beyond. "How did you know we could fly kites back here, Mengrong?"
"Brother told me. I had no plans to come, yet he saw how bored I was with chuiwan and offered to bring me out."
"Did Prince Yi pack kites along for a chuiwan match?"
"No, Brother said the Chen family set up kites on the back meadow."
Half skeptical, Qianli Xun walked on beside Mengrong as the path opened wide. Soon a meadow even larger than the chuiwan field unfolded before them, stretching green as far as the eye could see.
"What a splendid spot for a spring outing!" Qianli Xun exclaimed.
"Even lovelier than West Lake?"
"West Lake is gorgeous, of course, yet I go there so often the novelty has worn off. This meadow is brand new to me. Do not tell me the Chens own this too?"
Shen Jianchen walked up just in time to catch her question. "Of course it belongs to the Chen family."
With Mengrong right beside them, Qianli Xun dipped into a curtsy. "Your Highness Prince Yi."
"Thank you for teaming up with me, Youqi Jushi," Shen Jianchen said. "I cannot hog the prize all to myself, so I had Bai Feng hand it over to Kuqiao."
"Does that not mean I am hogging Your Highness's prize instead?"
"No matter. As a prince, I have all the treasures I could ask for. Do not stand on ceremony over one gold ruyi scepter."
Qianli Xun opened her mouth to say she did not care for it either, yet Qiao Mengrong cut in. "Take it, Shuzhen. It is a token of goodwill from Brother—and from me too."
"If it is a token from Mengrong, then I will accept without fuss," Qianli Xun agreed.
Shen Jianchen pointed toward an old man sitting on the grass not far away, framing bamboo kites. "Come, Mengrong, let me buy you a kite."
"Yes, and buy one for Shuzhen too, Brother," Qiao Mengrong said, pulling her friend along.
"Alright, one for each of you," Shen Jianchen agreed, already scheming how to steal a turn flying kites with Qianli Xun.
Once the two girls chose their kites, Shen Jianchen asked, "How much for these?"
"No silver needed," the old kite-maker answered. "Pick whichever ones you fancy."
Concern furrowed Qianli Xun's brow. "You put all this labor into making such fine kites—if you take no money, how do you make a living?"
Just then Chen Haoyu sauntered up with Qinggua trailing behind and burst out laughing at the question. "This meadow belongs to the Chens, and the kite-maker works for the Chens. We pay him regular wages—miles better than sleeping rough on the streets hawking kites."
Setting his work down, the old man stood and greeted him. "Good day, Young Master Chen!"
"Carry on with your work," Chen Haoyu told him, then turned to bow to the royal pair. "Your Highness Prince Yi, Princess Jiahe—if either of Your Highnesses wants pointers on flying kites, just say the word. The servants here know how, and I can fetch a couple for you."
Already annoyed by Chen Haoyu's appearance, Shen Jianchen turned colder still at the man's intrusion. "Unnecessary. Do you think this prince flies a kite worse than a servant? Dismissed. Do not disturb us."
Sensing Prince Yi's lingering hostility, Chen Haoyu bowed low to beat a hasty retreat. "I leave Your Highnesses to your pleasure."
Disgusted by his bootlicking, Qianli Xun caught Chen Haoyu's eye and shot him a fierce glare. Chen Haoyu answered with a disdainful sneer.
Qiao Mengrong caught the exchange. Once Chen Haoyu walked off, she asked with concern, "Shuzhen, the two of you do not seem to get along at all. What will you do after you marry him?"
"Not just 'seem'—we flat-out cannot stand each other. Relax, I am not marrying him."
"Is that even possible? Parents decide marriages. I fear..."
Seizing the opening, Shen Jianchen let his true intent slip through. "With the betrothal in place, you will have no choice but to wed—unless you find someone who outranks both the Zhu and Chen families and agree to marry him instead. Only then could the engagement be broken."
Qianli Xun knew full well what he meant. She had refused him before, and she would refuse him again. What rattled her was his recklessness—dropping hints that transparent right in front of Mengrong! Holding her kite made curtsying awkward, yet she still sank into a solemn bow. "Shuzhen thanks Your Highness for your concern, yet I must settle my own affairs. Leaning on someone else only trades one marriage for another. Shuzhen has already decided never to marry anyone in this life."
Qiao Mengrong mulled over her brother's words. Who in all of Lin'an could outrank both the Zhu and Chen families? Only Brother himself. What had he meant by that? Was he offering to step in because Shuzhen was her friend? Unease gripped her. "Never mind Shuzhen refusing—even I think Brother's idea is improper. Right now, nobody in Lin'an outranks both families except Brother, yet Brother is betrothed to me..."
Terrified Mengrong might see straight through him, Qianli Xun jumped in. "Do not get the wrong idea, Mengrong! Prince Yi never meant that. He only spoke out of concern because I am your closest friend."
Shen Jianchen burned to tell Qiao Mengrong outright whom he loved, yet seeing Qianli Xun cornered held him back. He forced out a stiff explanation. "Do not overthink it, either of you. I spoke off the cuff—just warning Youqi Jushi how impossible breaking off a betrothal is."
His words eased the knot in Qiao Mengrong's chest. "Impossible indeed. Brother could help with your divorce from Lin Jue, yet even he is powerless against a betrothal."
"You and Prince Yi have already helped me more than I can repay, and I am grateful from the bottom of my heart," Qianli Xun said. "Your Highness need not go out of your way for me over the Qiao family anymore. I... I stopped holding a grudge against the Qiaos long ago."
Shen Jianchen guessed she brought up the Qiao family just to hand Mengrong a clean explanation. Sure enough, Mengrong relaxed further—so Brother had been trying to make amends because the Qiaos broke their word to the Zhus and tore two lovers apart.
Reluctant to the bone, Shen Jianchen backed up her cover story. "I did wish to help because of the Qiao family. Since Youqi Jushi has made up her mind, I will meddle no further. Come, Mengrong, Brother will take you kite-flying."
Rejected once again—and told "never to marry anyone" with such finality—stung Shen Jianchen raw. Yet he still could not give up the woman he had chased so long, or rather, that vivid soul inside her. How pathetic he felt. Whether CEO of a major corporation or son of the Song Emperor, standing before the woman he loved stripped away every shred of pride. Maybe rank meant nothing in the face of love.
Qiao Mengrong took her brother's hand. "Shuzhen, I am hopeless with kites, so Brother has to teach me. Do you know how?"
"I have flown them before," Qianli Xun nodded. "I know the basics."
The three strolled toward the center of the meadow, passing people sunbathing on the grass, couples strolling in the spring breeze, and crowds launching kites. Spotting Nan Shuang and Huayu running with a kite, Qianli Xun scanned the grounds and saw Qiao Wenxian drinking with the three scholars on the grass; none of them seemed to have noticed her yet.
She thought about putting her kite down and slipping away when Mengrong called out, "Hurry up, Shuzhen! My kite is taking off!"
With Shen Jianchen guiding her hands, Mengrong's kite lifted into the air. Glowing with joy, she glanced back and forth between the soaring kite and her brother. Her radiant happiness roused a pang of envy in Qianli Xun—and deepened her resolve never to hurt her. Silently, she wished Mengrong happiness like this forever. Shen Jianchen looked attentive helping her, and the two made a handsome pair. If only he could let go of his pre-transmigration feelings, care for Mengrong wholeheartedly, and step into the late Zhao Pu's shoes as her true brother. Having come so close to raising Mengrong's suspicions just now, Qianli Xun resolved to avoid meeting Shen Jianchen from this day on.
Unable to leave and left flying a kite on her own, she decided to face it head-on—she could get a kite aloft by herself. Setting it on the grass, she unspooled a stretch of line, preparing to run backward and let the wind catch it.
"Miss, let me help," Hongdou spoke up beside her.
To someone bracing to stand alone, that quiet offer brought real warmth. "Hold the kite up and run that way."
Following her lead, Hongdou lifted the kite and sprinted across the grass while Qianli Xun fed out the line bit by bit. In seconds, the kite leaped from Hongdou's hands and climbed high into the sky.
"Miss! It is flying! Wonderful!" Hongdou clapped and cheered.
"Come over here, Hongdou! Fly it with me!"
"Yes!" Hongdou dashed back to her side, and mistress and maid laughed together as the kite danced above them.
Hearing her laughter, Shen Jianchen glanced over and saw her kite soaring high. An odd hollowness settled in his chest. He had meant to give her the cold shoulder, make her feel the sting of being left out and regret turning him away. Instead, she looked happier than ever without him—which only soured his own mood.
"Mengrong, I am tired of flying kites. Wenyuan is over there; I will go chat with him."
Without waiting for an answer, Shen Jianchen let go of the spool and strode toward Qiao Wenxian and his friends.
Something about her brother felt different today, though Qiao Mengrong could not pin down what. Distracted by worry, she slackened her grip on the string, and the kite sank back toward the grass.
"Brother! The kite is falling!"
Shen Jianchen heard her call and pretended not to, walking right on. An urge to drop the mask swelled inside him—even to find the right moment to tell Mengrong the whole truth.
Ignored by her brother for the very first time, Qiao Mengrong froze, at a loss.
Seeing her mistress stricken, Xilan hurried to smooth it over. "Do not fret, Your Highness. His Highness must not have heard you. Wait until he finishes talking with the young gentlemen and ask him then."
"Perhaps so," Qiao Mengrong murmured, half the strength drained from her voice.
Having watched it all unfold, Qianli Xun handed her line off. "Keep playing, Hongdou. I will go check on Mengrong." Walking over, she teased gently, "What is this? Prince Yi steps away and you cannot keep a kite up? Let me help."
"I do not feel like flying kites anymore, Shuzhen. Walk with me across the meadow."
"Alright. Let us head that way." Qianli Xun pointed away from where the gentlemen sat, and once Mengrong agreed, they strolled off together. Xilan gathered the fallen kite, returned it to the kite-maker, and trotted to catch up.
Glancing back once, Qiao Mengrong saw her brother seated comfortably with the young scholars, deep in conversation. Turning forward, she let out a heavy sigh.
"Why the sigh?"
"I called out to Brother just now and he ignored me. I do not know why."
Guilt stabbed Qianli Xun—she blamed herself for getting too close to Shen Jianchen during the chuiwan match. "Here I thought it was something serious! He must not have heard you. Even if he did, it is nothing to fuss over. Prince Yi is a man, with a man's own thoughts. He cannot orbit a woman every single second, no matter how fond of her he is."
"Maybe... you are right. I should be more careful from now on and stop clinging to Brother. Keep it up too long and he is bound to grow sick of me. After all, the brother now is not the same as before."
Qianli Xun had no words to answer. The real Prince Yi was dead, leaving Mengrong in a heartbreaking spot—yet forcing Shen Jianchen to love someone he did not love was its own cruelty. Why did life force everyone into impossible corners, even after transmigrating into a prince?
"Why so quiet, Shuzhen?"
"Oh, I was just thinking you should stay just as you have always been. No need to force yourself to change, or Prince Yi will find it strange."
"Alright. When Brother comes looking for me later, I will ask him face-to-face why he ignored me."
"That is the spirit. Ask straight out and clear up any misunderstanding."
A whiff of sizzling roast meat drifted over on the breeze. Qiao Mengrong looked around. "Where is that smell coming from? It smells delicious."
Sniffing the air, Qianli Xun recalled catching Chen Haoyu roasting meat by West Lake. "If I am not mistaken, Chen Haoyu has dragged his buddies out here for barbecue. Let us steer clear of him."
#CH 166
#TITLE: The Skewer-Roasting Rake Harasses Hongdou
Just as Qianli Xun and Qiao Mengrong turned to head the other way, Qinggua came bounding up with a platter of sizzling meat skewers. "Wait up, ladies!"
Forced to halt, they watched Qinggua bow to the princess first. "Princess Jiahe, Miss Zhu—my young master and his friends are roasting skewers over there. Seeing you walk by, he sent me over with a few for you to taste."
Both girls peered in the direction he gestured, yet saw nobody at all. "Where?" Qianli Xun asked. "There is not a soul in sight."
"The ground dips down over there," Qinggua grinned. "You only spot them once you walk right up to the rim."
"Aha." Having been active for hours, Qianli Xun felt her stomach rumble. She took the skewers and passed half to Mengrong. "Chen Haoyu is treating—waste not, want not."
"When you finish, please set the bamboo skewers back on the platter so I can carry them back," Qinggua reminded them—then stayed planted right there, staring straight at the two girls.
Under his unblinking gaze, neither felt comfortable taking a bite.
"Why are your eyes glued to these skewers?" Qianli Xun asked on purpose. "Do you grudge Her Highness and me a taste?"
"Never! I will turn right around—eat in peace, ladies."
Once Qinggua spun around, the two dug in, praising how well-seasoned the roast meat was.
Seizing the opening, Qinggua boasted over his shoulder, "My young master is a wizard at roasting skewers! Once Miss Zhu marries into the Chen family, you are in for the good life."
Taking a bite and chewing, Qianli Xun brushed it right off. "I have seen firsthand what sort of man Chen Haoyu is. Save your breath—no amount of sweet talk on his behalf will move me."
"My master acts wild only because nobody has ever kept him in line," Qinggua argued. "I hear him say Miss is fierce and he seems scared stiff of you. Once you marry in and rein him in, he will drop all his bad habits for sure."
"Even you admit he is hopeless. Why on earth would I marry a hopeless rake? And since when is it my duty to raise the Chen family's son for them?"
Qinggua had only been making conversation; hearing her sharp retort, he shut his mouth—especially since his master called Miss Zhu a tigress behind her back anyway. Funny pair, Qinggua mused: neither one was much of a prize, yet both looked down their noses at the other.
Finishing her meat, Qianli Xun collected Mengrong's empty sticks and held them out. "Turn around and take your skewers. Chen Haoyu did one decent thing today—pass on our thanks from Her Highness and me."
"Will do! Enjoy your walk; I must report back to my master."
Qianli Xun and Mengrong continued their stroll across the wide meadow, a soft spring breeze brushing their cheeks, each lost in her own thoughts.
Still gnawed by her brother's strange behavior, Qiao Mengrong glanced back toward the young gentlemen; her brother sat right where he was, showing no sign of coming over. "Shuzhen, I do not know why, yet I keep feeling there is another person in Brother's heart."
A woman's intuition was sharp, and with Shen Jianchen letting his feelings leak out every other turn, it would have been a miracle if Mengrong had not noticed.
Qianli Xun scrambled to cover for him. "No way—do not go inventing worries."
"I am not inventing anything. I suspected it the moment Brother came back from death's door. I went to the market with him, and the instant he saw a fortune-teller's stall, he stopped to ask for a divination about finding someone. I guessed right then he was looking for a woman."
So Shen Jianchen had started searching for her the very instant he transmigrated. A warm, tangled flutter stirred in Qianli Xun's chest, yet one look at Mengrong's downcast face snuffed it out. "You are overthinking this."
"Later I learned he was searching for Qianli Xun, that famed huaben writer, and I breathed easier. Yet I still cannot fathom why—after casting lots in Bianliang and posting Bai Feng outside West Third Alley in Lin'an, knowing the writer was right here in the city—Brother gave up searching out of the blue."
Even though Qianli Xun already knew how hard Shen Jianchen had hunted for her, hearing the story told through Mengrong's uneasy questions moved her so much she forgot to reply.
"Shuzhen? What is wrong?"
Mengrong's voice jolted her awake. "Oh, nothing. I just think you should stop racking your brain over puzzles with no answers. You and Prince Yi have an Imperial Marriage Decree; he is bound to marry you."
"Even so, I keep worrying Brother does not want to marry me at all."
This topic was walking on thin ice, and pressing further would only pile more guilt on her head. "Look at this glorious spring day, Mengrong! Let us toss every gloomy thought aside and enjoy ourselves. Tomorrow will turn out bright!"
A smile broke across Qiao Mengrong's face. "You are wonderful, Shuzhen. You always bring such bright, uplifting cheer."
The compliment sat heavy on Qianli Xun's conscience. If Mengrong ever learned who occupied Prince Yi's heart, she would never call her wonderful again. "Mengrong, I will never do anything to betray you."
"Why say that out of the blue?" Mengrong asked, puzzled.
"Blame yourself for saying something so touching," Qianli Xun deflected.
Smiling with pure, open trust, Qiao Mengrong took her hand, and the two soaked in the spring warmth side by side.
Most of the meadow lay flat, save for one sunken hollow that served Chen Haoyu's purposes well. He had servants haul in a brazier, kindle the charcoal, and thread the skewers ahead of time; once the chuiwan match wrapped up, he met two cronies there to roast meat.
Empty platter in one hand and bamboo sticks in the other, Qinggua hopped down into the hollow. "Young Master, Miss Zhu and Her Highness ate every single skewer! They raved about the flavor and sent their thanks."
Sprawled on a felt rug munching a skewer, Chen Haoyu scoffed. "I only shared on Princess Jiahe's account. Otherwise Zhu Shuzhen would never get a taste of my handiwork."
Setting the platter down, Qinggua grinned and fawned. "I talked you up to Miss Zhu too, told her nobody roasts meat better than—"
"Scram! Does perfection like me need your bragging?" Chen Haoyu sneered.
Qinggua stayed put, knowing "scram" was just his master's verbal tic; all he needed to do was zip his lip.
A young rake seated opposite Chen Haoyu chuckled. "Brother Chen always swore he would stay footloose and fancy-free, never marrying unless he met true love—and look at you now, betrothed all the same!"
"What can a betrothal do to me? Even if I end up marrying Zhu Shuzhen, I will live just as I please. Nobody changes this young master!" Chen Haoyu filled his cup and raised it high. "Drink up! 'When one has one's way in life, one must enjoy it to the full. The golden wine cup must not face the moon unfilled.'" [TN: Translated by Chan Sin-wai]
Cup raised, the friend mocked him before taking a sip. "Whoa! Brother Chen reciting poetry? Cramming in advance so you can keep up with your poetess bride?"
Draining his cup, Chen Haoyu shot back, "Can you speak like a normal person? Every three-year-old knows those two lines—dare tell me you do not?"
"My bad! Penalty cup." The friend downed his wine too.
The other crony tending the grill flipped the skewers, shook spice over them, and leered. "Miss Zhu is a real looker, though. Sets my heart fluttering every time I see her—shame I only dare look and not touch. Brother Chen is a lucky dog! After the wedding night, you have to tell us all about how she—"
"Get lost!" Chen Haoyu barked. "Nothing decent ever comes out of a dog's mouth!"
"Look at Brother Chen sticking up for his bride! Now that is a rare sight."
"Stick up for her, my foot! If she caught wind of what you just said, you could kiss your worthless hide goodbye!"
The griller glanced up toward the rim of the hollow. "They are long gone—cannot hear a thing. Who knew Young Master Chen was scared stiff of Zhu Shuzhen? Come your wedding, you will earn the grand title of henpecked husband! Hahaha!"
"Laugh all you want—I am not marrying her in the first place!"
"Scared of earning that henpecked title?"
"Drop dead! When I marry, it will be a fresh young maiden. She has been married once already—she is nowhere near my league. If her father were not the Prefect, my parents would never have agreed."
Flipping the meat, the griller sighed in mock envy. "I would not turn her down. Why did the Prefect not come knocking at my door?"
Ignoring him, Chen Haoyu grabbed another skewer and bit in.
Chewing through his own skewer, the first crony leaned close. "You need not worry anyway. Word has it Lin Jue married Zhu Shuzhen yet never even laid a finger on her—why else did he haunt Chunxiang Brothel and drag Chun Jinqiao straight into Lin Manor? Heh!"
"Which proves all the more she is poison," Chen Haoyu shot back. "She is a tigress! Even Lin Jue could not tame her, so what chance do I have?"
"Her father is the Prefect of Lin'an. Breaking off that match will not be easy."
"I have it all mapped out. If I truly cannot break it off, I will marry her home and we will keep out of each other's hair—each living our own life."
"Look at Brother Chen, planning every move ahead."
"If a man takes no thought for the distant future, sorrow waits close at hand. Got to look ahead."
"Miracles never cease! Since when did the champion of living for the moment start preaching foresight?"
"No choice—this betrothal threatens my whole creed of living for the moment, so I had to spare it a thought."
"Heh, fair enough."
Feeling nature call, the grilling crony stood up. "Take over the skewers, Qinggua. I need to relieve myself."
"Right away!" Qinggua stepped in and tended the meat.
Climbing out of the hollow, the young rake scanned the meadow. Kite-flyers dotted the grass, hardly a private spot. Spotting a low earthen mound nearby, he strolled behind it and took care of business.
Relieved and cheerful, he adjusted his robe and headed back. Glancing toward the kite-flyers, his gaze snagged on a young girl—rosy cheeks, bright grin, utterly charming. Judging from her dress, she was some household's maid. His heart leaped. Just a maid! If he got his hands on her, who would fuss? Her master might even gift her to him.
Bold as brass, he sauntered over.
It was Hongdou. While her mistress walked with the princess, she had flown the kite alone, growing handier and happier by the minute, wrapped up in the fun without noticing the lecher creeping up.
"Hey there!" he called out as he closed in. "Which household do you serve?"
One look at his greasy leer disgusted Hongdou, and she sidestepped away.
He pressed closer. "What fun is flying a kite? Come join Big Brother for roast meat, then come back to my estate. Keep Big Brother happy and you will live in clover."
Hongdou sidestepped again.
He trailed right after her. "Big Brother fell for you at first sight! Come here and give me a kiss!" Puckering his lips, he lunged for her cheek.
Pushed past her limit, Hongdou forgot the kite. Why waste the fist techniques Miss had drilled into her? She swung a punch straight into his face, then planted a kick that sent him sprawling backward onto the grass. Only then did the forgotten kite flutter down onto the turf.
"Ow! Owww!" Clutching half a face already swelling purple, the rake scrambled up and cursed. "You little wretch! Having this young master take a shine to you is your blessing, and you dare hit me?! Speak up! Whose maid are you? I will have your master flay you alive!"
The meadow was vast, so few noticed the scuffle; engrossed in talking with Mengrong, Qianli Xun had no idea what had happened to her maid.
Down in the nearby hollow, however, Chen Haoyu heard the commotion and scrambled up the slope. One look at the maid rang a bell—Zhu Shuzhen's new girl! He rushed over and waved her off. "Go on, shoo! Back to your kite! Even her maid packs a wallop—like mistress, like servant!"
Retrieving her kite, Hongdou glared daggers at the rake. "Try getting fresh with me again and I will crack your skull open!"
"You know that brat, Brother Chen?"
Watching Hongdou's departing back, Chen Haoyu shook his head. "Out of everyone to grope, you pick Zhu Shuzhen's maid. Got what was coming to you, huh?"
"No way—I am going to settle accounts with Zhu Shuzhen!"
