"Settle accounts, my foot! Go find her and you will only catch a second beating!"
"Ah! Poor Brother Chen, stuck marrying her. Now I see why you are desperate to break it off."
"Glad you get it. A woman ought to be gentle, and Zhu Shuzhen does not have a gentle bone in her body. If that maid had been the gentle sort, you would have had your way instead of eating dirt."
"So I just swallow this loss in silence?"
"Swallow the loss, or swallow another fist. Take your pick."
The rake pretended to weigh his options. "Forget it. I will swallow the loss."
"Smart move. Come on, back to our skewers."
Descending back into the hollow, they spent the next stretch explaining the bruised cheek, enduring fresh rounds of mockery, and sighing all over again that no man in his right mind should marry Miss Zhu.
#CH 167
#TITLE: A Hasty Exit from the Chuiwan Grounds
Qianli Xun and Qiao Mengrong were strolling along in cheerful conversation when Qiao Wenxian stepped into their path. The scent of wine clung to him, and his eyes locked straight onto Shuzhen.
Qiao Mengrong tugged Shuzhen's sleeve. "It is Young Master Qiao. You two talk—I will wait over there."
Reading the raw longing on Wenyuan's face frightened Qianli Xun; she feared softening if she stood alone with him. She caught Mengrong's wrist at once. "Stay right here with me as a witness, or folks might think Young Master Qiao and I are still tangled up."
Qiao Mengrong stayed put. From his gaze alone, she felt the depth of his love for Shuzhen and ached for the pair torn apart.
"Shuzhen," Qiao Wenxian began, "I will ask just one question. Had those rumors never spread and had our families sealed the betrothal, would you have married me willingly?"
After a beat of hesitation, Qianli Xun nodded. "Yes. Because back then, that was what I wanted."
"Let me ask one more..."
"You said just one question."
"I need to know... were your feelings for me back then real?"
"They were real," she answered with candor.
A pained smile touched Qiao Wenxian's lips. "I knew you loved me then. And... now?"
Three questions already. Unwilling to let him press further, Qianli Xun steeled her voice. "Now... I have let go of Wenyuan. I ask Young Master Qiao to let go of Shuzhen as well."
His brief smile vanished. "Must you address me so stiffly from now on? Casting aside even friendship? We once looked upon each other as kindred spirits."
"Shuzhen was naive before. I will have no more kindred spirits. Please stop thinking of Shuzhen as one."
"You... can be this ruthless? Leaving me not even... a last shred of hope?"
The tremor in his voice struck straight at Qianli Xun's chest. "Young Master Qiao jests. There was never any hope between us, let alone a last shred."
Struggling to steady his breathing, Qiao Wenxian forced his tone even. "It is all my fault... Can you promise me one thing? Even if we cannot be together, we remain friends. Even if you no longer call me a kindred spirit, call me Wenyuan, and let me still call you Shuzhen."
Unable to bear wounding him further, Qianli Xun nodded. "I promise. In return, promise me you will never seek me out alone again. Live your life in peace. If you live well, I will be glad."
"Alright. I will listen to you. No more talking alone, and I will live a steady life. Whatever makes you happy, I will do." Turning on his heel, Qiao Wenxian walked back toward the young gentlemen.
Mengrong noticed her brother glancing their way, likely worried about Young Master Qiao. "Ah, I truly feel for you and Young Master Qiao. I hope he recovers soon."
"Nothing to mourn," Qianli Xun replied. "I have made peace with it. Everyone has their own road to walk. Mine and Wenyuan's failed to intertwine, so we walk our separate paths. Once he grows used to his own road, he will let go."
Her words turned Qiao Mengrong's thoughts inward. "I wonder if Brother's road and mine will intertwine."
"Of course they will. Your betrothal binds your road tight to Prince Yi's."
"A betrothal is one thing, yet I fear Brother's heart is not with me. Just like Young Master Qiao—marrying Miao Zhiyu while his heart stays with you. I actually feel sorry for Lady Miao."
Mengrong spoke without intent, yet every word hit Qianli Xun like a stone. If Mengrong ever discovered Prince Yi held her own best friend in his heart, their friendship would shatter on the spot. Qianli Xun regretted teaming up with Shen Jianchen for chuiwan and tagging along to fly kites. Women were perceptive; Mengrong had surely sensed something off in Prince Yi's manner today. Better to leave now before Shen Jianchen pulled another stunt.
"Mengrong, I am not feeling well. I want to head home."
"You were fine a moment ago—what came over you?"
"I worry Wenyuan might come over again and hurt Lady Miao if she sees. I want to get back to Wangyue Pavilion soon."
"I was only rambling just now—do not take it to heart."
"It has nothing to do with you."
"Alright then. Once you have a free moment, come visit me at Youlin Retreat."
"I fear... I will not have time to visit Youlin Retreat for a while."
"Why?"
"The wedding date is closing in. I have to find a way to get our two families to cancel the betrothal. Once it is settled, I will... I will visit you at Youlin Retreat."
Pity crossed Qiao Mengrong's face. "You will have to rely on yourself this time, Shuzhen. Even Brother cannot help."
Qianli Xun caught the faint edge beneath her words: Mengrong did not want her brother involved in this. Just as well—neither did she. Leaving the remark untouched, she said, "I should be off. Go find Prince Yi before he blames me for hogging you all afternoon."
Parting from Mengrong with a tangled heart, Qianli Xun headed straight back toward the chuiwan field. Being busy with the betrothal was only an excuse; the real truth was that she meant to stop visiting Youlin Retreat and decline every invitation from Prince Yi from here on.
Spotting her mistress parting ways with the princess, Hongdou wound up the kite, jogged back to return it to the kite-maker, and sprinted to catch up.
After bidding her sister-in-law farewell at the pavilion, Qianli Xun boarded her carriage with Matron Wei and her maids for the ride back to Wangyue Pavilion.
Inside the carriage, Kuqiao drew the gold ruyi scepter from her sleeve. "Miss, Bai Feng said Prince Yi sent today's prize for you, as thanks for partnering with him in chuiwan."
"Put it away. When the Qiaos come to fetch Xiaguo, pack it in her dowry."
Hearing her name, Xiaguo asked with caution, "Miss, you do not blame me, do you?"
"Why would I blame you?"
"Young Master Qiao agreed to take me as his concubine..."
Far from blaming Xiaguo, Qianli Xun felt a twinge of envy. "You are overthinking it. Even as a concubine, you cannot arrive at Qiao Manor empty-handed. I will have Matron Wei prepare a proper dowry for you. It cannot match Miao Zhiyu's, of course—you are entering as a concubine, and my purse cannot compare with the Miaos'—yet it will be plenty to keep you comfortable there."
Tears spilled down Xiaguo's cheeks. "Miss, I will never forget your immense kindness as long as I live! What I cannot repay in this life, I will serve you as ox and horse in the next!"
"Cut out the 'next life' and 'ox and horse' nonsense. You owe me nothing. No matter how each of you three ended up with me, I treat you all like family. Every one of you will get a dowry when you wed—Kuqiao gets the biggest share, since she has stood by me longest."
All three maids blinked back tears, moved to the core.
Shen Jianchen watched Qianli Xun leave with clear eyes. With the woman he loved gone, he had no reason to linger. Rising to bid the scholars farewell, he walked over to meet Mengrong.
Seeing her brother walk straight toward her, Qiao Mengrong brightened, thinking his old warmth had returned. All her doubts vanished as she rose to meet him. "Brother, Shuzhen left, and I want to head home too."
"Why did Youqi Jushi hurry off instead of keeping you company?"
"Young Master Qiao scared her off! He did not care a bit that I was standing right there—said such bold things it made my ears burn."
Relief washed over Shen Jianchen. As long as he was not the reason she fled.
Then Qiao Mengrong recalled the kite incident. "Brother, why did you ignore me when I called you earlier?"
Part of him burned to spill the truth, yet this was neither the time nor the place. "Oh, I was lost in thought and never heard you. Brother's fault—it will not happen again."
The tension drained from Qiao Mengrong's face. "I thought Brother had someone else in his heart and no longer cared to talk to me."
Her words hit straight on the mark. Skipping a direct denial, Shen Jianchen steered elsewhere. "I wonder if that rascal Bai Feng handed the gold ruyi scepter to Kuqiao for Youqi Jushi. I would hate for folks to think me a stingy prince."
Noticing he had not hurried to deny it, Qiao Mengrong stiffened again. "Why does Brother care so much about where that scepter ended up?"
"You overthink everything—must be tired. Let us hurry back to Youlin Retreat." Taking her hand, Shen Jianchen led her toward the chuiwan grounds.
His gentle scolding and the warmth of his hand felt like reassurance enough, easing her heart a touch. Still, watching everyone else's wedding dates approach while hers dragged all the way to Mid-Autumn Festival felt far too long. What if they could marry this spring too?
She recalled back in Bianliang how Imperial Attendant Liang often visited Prince Yi's Manor on His Majesty's behalf, and she had always received him—slipping him silver on the side until they had grown quite chummy. Why not write to Imperial Attendant Liang and ask him to persuade His Majesty to move the wedding date forward, before delays bred fresh trouble?
Her mind made up, Qiao Mengrong followed her brother past the chuiwan grounds, through the gardens, and into their carriage back to Youlin Retreat.
Qiao Wenxian had watched Qianli Xun leave too, guessing her hasty farewell to the princess was on his account. If you want nothing to do with me, he told himself, I will grant you your wish.
Once Prince Yi left as well, Qiao Wenxian poured cup after cup for himself, downing six straight until a heavy buzz clouded his eyes. "Starting today, I am letting go of these feelings..."
"That is the spirit!" Dongfang Chenze applauded.
"...and keeping Shuzhen locked in my heart forever. Nobody will ever take her place."
The three friends stared at one another.
"If you are letting go of the feelings," An Mingxuan asked, "why keep the woman locked in your heart?"
"Is there a difference between the person and the feelings?" Cao Ban scratched his head.
Half-drunk, Qiao Wenxian sneered at them. "Neither of you has ever been in love—you know nothing."
Both turned to Dongfang Chenze. "Do you get it, Brother Dongfang?"
Shaking his head, Dongfang Chenze recited a couplet: "'Though master painters fill this boundless world, / Not one can paint a single broken heart.'" [TN: Translator's rendering]
"Why quote those two lines?" Cao Ban asked, baffled. "How does that answer our question?"
An Mingxuan explained, "Brother Dongfang means that even though he has a woman he loves, like those 'master painters,' he cannot capture Brother Qiao's sorrow."
"Oh, put like that, it makes sense." Cao Ban turned back to Qiao Wenxian. "We may not fathom the difference between the person and the feelings, Brother Qiao, yet so long as you figure out what to hold onto and what to release, the three of us can rest easy."
Drinking on with hollow eyes, Qiao Wenxian murmured, "She wants me to let go, so I let go. She wants me to stop bothering her, so I stop. She wants me to marry Miao Zhiyu, so I marry her. She wants me to take a concubine, so I take one. She wants me to live a good life, so I will live one..."
The three friends traded another helpless look. Letting go? He was just finding a brand-new way to torture himself.
Once Prince Yi departed the chuiwan grounds, the other guests drifted off in turn. Nobody needed to call an end to the Qiaos' tournament; it wound down on its own.
After nightfall and supper, Qiao Mengrong had Xilan lay out brush and ink and composed her letter—pleading with Liang Shicheng to talk His Majesty into advancing her wedding to Prince Yi. Which month should she ask for? Brush poised, she thought it over. Young Master Qiao's wedding fell in the third month, and Shuzhen's with Young Master Chen was in the third month too. Clearly the third month was auspicious; she would request the third month as well.
Stamping the page with Princess Jiahe's seal, she folded it into an envelope, tied the cord, and handed it to Xilan. "Take this to the relay station tonight so it rides out for Bianliang at first light."
Having glimpsed a few lines as her mistress wrote, Xilan knew the letter begged Imperial Attendant Liang to move the wedding up. Never mind the princess—Xilan herself could not wait another day. His Highness was far too fine a catch, and Lin'an's maidens far too charming. Only once the wedding was sealed could either of them breathe easy.
#CH 168
#TITLE: Seeing Xiaguo Off and Storming the Chen Manor
A few days later, the Qiao family sent out wedding invitations across Lin'an for their son's marriage. An invitation went to Zhu Manor as well, yet none came to Wangyue Pavilion—both because the Qiaos were taking a maid from there as concubine and out of tact regarding Miss Zhu's past bond with their son. Qianli Xun felt the same and appreciated their restraint.
Xiaguo's dowry had been packed five days ahead. On the wedding morning, dressed in the concubine's bridal robes sent by the Qiaos and draped under a red veil, she sat waiting in the maids' quarters.
Sitting beside her, Kuqiao kept her company. "You got your wish at last, yet I still cannot warm up to you. Feels like you stole the man Miss loved."
Xiaguo held no grudge. "Wrong! The way I see it, I am marrying Young Master Qiao in Miss's stead..."
"If Miss married him, she would be primary wife. You are going in as a concubine—how does that count as taking her place?"
"Fair point. I just want to guard Young Master Qiao for Miss's sake, so Lady Miao does not steal his heart."
Approaching the door with Hongdou to see Xiaguo off, Qianli Xun overheard the exchange. After a beat of hesitation, she stepped inside and spoke with quiet gravity. "Remember this, Xiaguo: I have no need for you to guard anyone for me. Marrying into the Qiao family is your own choice and has nothing to do with me."
Lifting her veil, Xiaguo dropped to her knees before her mistress. "Miss, I can no longer serve you, and knowing how deep Young Master Qiao's feelings for you run, I could not help thinking that way."
Qianli Xun reached down and pulled her up. "Do not kneel to me anymore, and stop fretting over me. Go to Qiao Manor and build a good life for yourself."
Xiaguo nodded. "I will remember Miss's words and live well."
Qianli Xun called Hongdou forward, and the maid opened a wooden box in her hands. From inside, Qianli Xun drew out a sheet of paper covered in ink and red seals. "Here is your deed of sale. Today I tear it up right before your eyes. From this moment on, you belong to no one as a maid."
Watching the torn scraps flutter from her mistress's hands to the floor, Xiaguo wept with emotion. "I will remember Miss's kindness forever."
Lowering the red veil back over Xiaguo's head, Qianli Xun said, "Stop keeping the word 'kindness' on the tip of your tongue."
Matron Wei's voice called from outside. "Miss, the Qiao bridal sedan has arrived and waits at the gate."
Without answering aloud, Qianli Xun instructed Kuqiao, "Walk Xiaguo out with Hongdou."
Supporting the veiled bride on either side, the two maids guided her with care so she would not trip.
Matron Wei waited in the courtyard. "Your dowry is already outside, ready to follow the sedan into Qiao Manor," she told Xiaguo. "Worried you would have no one of your own there, Miss had me pick a maid from Wangyue Pavilion to accompany you."
"Thank you, Miss! Thank you, Matron!" Xiaguo said from beneath her veil.
"Aimo!" Matron Wei called.
"Here, Matron."
"From now on, you follow Xiaguo. Serve her well at Qiao Manor."
"Yes, Matron." Aimo stepped behind Xiaguo and trailed them to the gate.
Outside Wangyue Pavilion, Kuqiao and Hongdou helped Xiaguo into the waiting sedan while Aimo took her place beside the poles. As the bearers lifted the chair, Xiaguo parted the curtain, peeked under her veil, and called out, "Now that I am gone, you two take proper care of Miss!"
"Save your breath," Kuqiao retorted. "Even when you were here, I did all the work anyway!"
Kuqiao spoke truth; Xiaguo's heart had never been in serving her mistress. Yet leaving today, remembering how generously Miss had treated her, a genuine pang of parting tugged at her. Once the curtain dropped and she settled her veil back into place, though, regret gave way to pure bliss. Not only had she met a mistress this kind, she was marrying the man she loved—even as a concubine, she counted herself luckier than most women alive.
The sedan moved off, and the two maids watched the bearers and dowry porters vanish down the street before latching Wangyue Pavilion's gate and heading toward their mistress's chambers.
Back in her own room, Qianli Xun sat at her desk staring into space. Today was Wenyuan's wedding, and a strange hollowness settled over her—too empty to invent plots, too empty to pick up a brush, drifting back through sweet memories of their time together. Those bright days had not ended so long ago, yet they already felt half a lifetime away.
Entering the room, the two maids sensed her mood at once; Kuqiao tugged Hongdou's sleeve and slipped back out without a sound.
Qianli Xun never even noticed them come and go, sinking deeper into memory. The more she remembered, the sharper the ache grew. Picturing Wenyuan sharing a wedding chamber with someone else tonight sliced at her chest like a blade; an absurd urge surged up to storm Qiao Manor, grab Wenyuan's hand before the whole crowd, and run to the ends of the earth.
She clamped down on the fantasy and gave herself a mental slap. You played the cool, rational woman in front of Wenyuan and everyone else—what are you getting dramatic over now? Wake up! Your own wedding date is looming; figure out how to kill this engagement to Chen Haoyu!
One thought of the betrothal got her out of her chair, wiping Wenyuan's wedding from her mind. Chen Haoyu had only said his grandmother would stall, without explaining how. She could not sit around waiting for a miracle. Staging scenes before her father had failed twice; why not take the show straight to Chen Manor? Let the Chen elders see Miss Zhu's ferocious side firsthand—maybe they would recoil in horror and demand the engagement be broken themselves.
Her mind set, she strode out of her room and shouted toward the maids' quarters. "Hongdou! Come out with me!"
Both Kuqiao and Hongdou emerged, and Kuqiao pouted. "Ever since Hongdou arrived, Miss plays favorites and never takes me out anymore."
Qianli Xun laughed. "I am going out to raise hell and do not want you getting hurt. Stay home and wait for good news."
Fearing her mistress might do something rash over Young Master Qiao's wedding, Kuqiao gasped. "Miss, please do not go make a scene at Qiao Manor—"
"What are you imagining? What is Qiao Manor to me? I am heading to the Chens'."
"The Chens' is no better! Your reputation just recovered—make another scene and it will sink worse than before!"
"Stop fussing. What is reputation worth next to happiness?"
Walking up just then, Matron Wei caught the exchange and knew at once what her mistress planned. "Do not stop her, Kuqiao—you could not stop her if you tried. Let her go fight one round for her own life. Who knows, she might get her wish."
Qianli Xun wrapped her arms around Matron Wei. "Matron is the best!"
Smoothing a stray lock behind her mistress's ear, Matron Wei smiled. "You truly are not the girl you used to be—bold enough to fight for what you want. Matron is proud of you. Do whatever you judge right, only remember not to injure anyone. And if today fails, think hard on what I urged you before."
Stepping back, Qianli Xun racked her brain, drawing a blank. "Could Matron remind me what you urged?"
"What you will do if you truly must marry into the Chen family," Matron Wei said in earnest.
Only then did it come back to her; she had never sat down to face that question head-on. "I promise, Matron. If today fails, I will think it through."
"Go on, and come back early."
Matron Wei watched her mistress stride off with Hongdou.
"Matron," Kuqiao fretted, "aren't you scared Master will find out you indulged Miss and punish you?"
"Of course I am scared, yet I hate seeing Miss unhappy even more. Enough worrying—go about your chores and wait for good news."
"What if it is bad news?"
"There is no such thing as bad news."
Kuqiao blinked. "What do you mean, Matron? Do you really think Miss will scare the Chens into breaking the betrothal?"
Matron Wei gave a cryptic chuckle. "You are young yet and do not grasp that nothing in life is wholly bad. Enough chatter—I have errands to run."
Blocking her path, Kuqiao pouted. "Matron always loves talking in riddles! I am not letting you leave until you explain!"
Matron Wei chuckled. "Seeing how loyal you have been to Miss since childhood, I will humor you. Tell me: do you think it is better for Miss to marry into the Chen family, or not to marry?"
"Not to marry, of course!" Kuqiao answered in a heartbeat. "That is what Miss has wanted all along."
"To my eyes, marrying is fine, and not marrying is fine too."
"What does that mean?"
"Marrying brings its own rewards, and staying unwed brings its own. Look for the bright side in everything."
"I do not buy that!" Kuqiao protested. "By that logic, why did you back Miss's divorce from Lin Jue?"
"That was different." Declining to explain further, Matron Wei patted her arm. "Mule over it yourself. I have work to do."
After Matron Wei walked away, Kuqiao puzzled over it half the morning without cracking it, and gave up with a convenient excuse: Matron Wei must be getting old and rambling—if it were really true, why was Miss knocking herself out trying to escape?
Outside Chen Manor, the towering front gates stood shut beneath an imposing plaque reading "Chen Manor," radiating an air of wealth and authority that made visitors shrink back.
Hongdou leaned close. "Miss, maybe we should turn back?"
"Why back out after coming all this way? You know kung fu—what is there to fear? Since we are here, leaving without a proper ruckus would be a crime."
"Alright! If Miss is not scared, neither am I. I will charge right in with you."
"Remember—do not hurt anyone too bad."
"Got it. I will knock."
Hongdou marched up and pounded three times: *Bang! Bang! Bang!*
A gatekeeper cracked the door and poked his head out. "Who are you? What business do you have banging on Chen Manor's gate?"
Hongdou looked to her mistress; Qianli Xun gave a crisp nod.
With one kick, Hongdou blew the door wide open, sending the gatekeeper staggering backward, and snatched him by the collar. "The Chen family's future young madam has come to scout the territory and see if her future in-laws are easy to get along with! If you know what is good for you, call out every master in the house to form a welcoming line for Miss Zhu!"
Seeing trouble at the door, the gatekeeper was no pushover himself and swung a punch at Hongdou. Hongdou sidestepped with ease and punched him flying several paces back. Within seconds, a swarm of servants rushed up, several clutching heavy staves.
Wiping blood from his swollen lip, the downed gatekeeper bellowed, "Jump her together!"
The servants surged at Hongdou, who fought them off with brisk strikes.
Stepping unhurried across the threshold, Qianli Xun called out to the brawling servants, "With your half-baked kung fu, you cannot even lay a hand on my maid! Do yourselves a favor and fetch my future father-in-law and mother-in-law—I want a proper chat with them!"
Outmatched and already nursing bruises, the servants backed off, and one sprinted inside to alert the masters.
Hongdou barked at the gatekeeper who had opened the door, "You! Fetch a chair for my mistress at once!"
Having tasted her fist, the servant dared not argue—and besides, Miss Zhu was about to become his young madam anyway. He scrambled into the gatehouse, hauled out a chair, and set it beside her. "Please sit, Miss. Master and Madam will be right out."
Qianli Xun lowered herself into the chair, well pleased with the opening act. Once Chen Haoyu's parents laid eyes on a daughter-in-law like this, they were bound to regret ever sealing the betrothal.
The Qiaos had sent the Chen family a wedding invitation too, of course. Considering the Qiaos had jilted the Zhus while his own son was engaged to Miss Zhu, Chen Jianglin had found it awkward to attend in person and sent his second brother, Chen Jiangshu, and his wife to represent the Chens instead.
Back in the inner hall, Chen Jianglin was playing chess with Yao Qingyan and talking over plans for their son's wedding when a servant burst past the maids into the room. "Master! Madam! Disaster! The future young madam—Miss Zhu—just fought her way through the gates demanding to see her future father-in-law and mother-in-law!"
Yao Qingyan set down her chess piece in shock. "What? Fought her way in?"
"Yes, fought right in! Look at my face, Madam—her maid beat me black and blue!"
Sure enough, his face was blotched with fresh bruises.
"What do we do, Master?" Yao Qingyan asked.
"Go back and tell Miss Zhu to wait a moment," Chen Jianglin instructed the servant. "Madam and I will be right there."
"Yes, Master." The servant hurried out.
Yao Qingyan frowned in concern. "Miss Zhu really is a handful. No wonder Haoyu refused to marry her."
"Formidable indeed," Chen Jianglin nodded. "Still, Prefect Zhu warned me ahead of time: those two staged scenes in front of him twice without getting anywhere. Seeing her father will not budge, Miss Zhu must have brought the act here to force a broken engagement."
"So do we break it off or not?"
"Of course not! Why would grown adults walk straight into a pair of brats' trap?"
"Master is right. I will follow your lead."
"Come along, Madam. Let us go meet our future daughter-in-law and see firsthand just how fierce she is."
Setting his piece aside, Chen Jianglin stepped down from the couch with his wife and strode toward the main gate with their maids trailing behind.
#CH 169
#TITLE: Wrecking Chen Manor to Force a Broken Engagement
Seated in her chair, Qianli Xun waited for Chen Haoyu's parents to appear. From her sister-in-law, she had heard they were decent, deeply devoted people whose sole failure in life was raising a useless son. How could she shake them up enough to swear off marrying her into their family?
Scanning the courtyard, she spotted a square dressed stone beside a flowerbed, a bit bigger than an inkstone. "Hongdou, fetch me that stone."
Hongdou dashed over, scooped the block up with both hands—not too heavy—balanced it on one palm, wiped the dust off with her handkerchief, and presented it to her mistress.
Qianli Xun tossed the stone once in her palm. Decent heft—plenty to put a scare into Chen Haoyu's parents.
Just then Qinggua came hurrying out at word of the ruckus. "Miss Zhu, what is going on? My young master is not home today. If you are mad at him, wait until he gets back to vent!"
Qianli Xun put on a stern scowl. "Who said I came for your young master? If I want to thrash him, I can do it anywhere—why bother coming to Chen Manor? I came today to meet my future in-laws so they can get a good look at the bride they are getting, lest they get a shock later!"
Speechless, Qinggua shrank to the side. This future young madam swaggering around like a street boss felt worlds apart from the girl he had met at the chuiwan grounds—was she even the same person?
Tossing the stone up and down, Qianli Xun crossed one leg over the other on purpose as movement caught the corner of her eye. A man and woman approached surrounded by maids, roughly the same age as Zhu Shuzhen's parents—Chen Haoyu's father and mother. Proper etiquette called for rising to bow, yet she had come to pick a fight; letting them see an insolent brat might speed the breakup along. As they closed in, she put on a sneer and eyed the elders sideways.
Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan sized up their future daughter-in-law from head to toe. Having met Miss Zhu a handful of times before, they remembered an educated, gentle, graceful young lady. Strip away the stone in her hand, the sidelong glare, and the cocky crossed leg—have her stand and greet them as Aunt and Uncle—and she would still look every inch a noble daughter. Had Chen Jianglin not briefed her beforehand, Yao Qingyan would have stepped right into her mother-in-law role and dressed the girl down.
Seeing the pair study her in silence, Qianli Xun hiked her crossed leg even higher. "You two are Chen Haoyu's parents? My future father-in-law and mother-in-law?"
Without answering, Chen Jianglin asked, "You are Miss Zhu?"
"Same name walking, same surname sitting—Zhu Shuzhen at your service!" Qianli Xun barked in a mock wandering-heroine swagger.
Husband and wife traded a glance. That brash tone fit zero part of a sheltered daughter.
"What brings you here today?" Yao Qingyan asked.
Dropping her crossed leg—the posture cramped her anyway—Qianli Xun kept hefting the stone. "Since I am about to marry Chen Haoyu, I came to show you my true colors ahead of time so neither of you cries regret later. If you do not like what you see, it is not too late to call off the wedding right now."
At the words "call off the wedding," Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan exchanged a knowing smile.
"Well, your future mother-in-law and I have seen plenty today," Chen Jianglin said breezily. "You may head home now."
That unbothered brush-off threw Qianli Xun off stride. Leave before getting what she came for? Not a chance! "Neither of you said whether you can stomach me like this!"
"Whether we accept you is between the two sets of parents," Yao Qingyan smiled. "Go home and wait for word."
Was today's whole performance about to flop too? She was not letting the curtain drop yet. Eyeing the block in her palm—she had not been hefting it for nothing—she rose to her feet. "Hold off on chasing me out! Since I came all this way, let me show you what I can really do."
Cradling the stone on her left palm, she cocked her right fist and drove it straight down. The square stone cracked clean in two and clattered to the flagstones.
Setting out to inspect the Chen family shops, Chen Haodong had reached the gate just in time to catch the spectacle and ducked out of sight to watch. "Fierce as a beast," he marveled under his breath. "No wonder Haoyu wants no part of her. Yet Brother Yan fell for her? Baffling." He stayed tucked in the shadows, eager to see how the showdown played out.
Yao Qingyan had squeezed her eyes shut the instant the fist came down; opening them to see two jagged halves of stone on the ground left her stunned. How did a slip of a girl pack strength like that?
Qianli Xun dusted off her hands. "You saw it yourselves. My fists hit hard, and they specialize in people I despise. I will level with you: in all of Lin'an, the man I despise most is Chen Haoyu. Marry me into this house and your son will not see a single peaceful day. Save the hesitation and give me an answer right now: do you want me marrying in to beat Chen Haoyu black and blue every day, or will you go talk to my father about calling off this betrothal?"
Chen Jianglin paused a beat before answering. "This match was sealed by the elders of both houses; it is not something to cancel on a whim. Haoyu has always been an unruly rake, beyond a father's control. I am counting on you to marry in and whip him into shape for me."
Qianli Xun almost had to applaud the pair's slick deflection—turning a thousand pounds of force aside with four ounces of effort and leaving her swinging at air. Plan A dead? Time for Plan B! "If you care so little for your own son, I will not waste my breath warning you! Bring brush and paper right now! Put it down in black and white that if Zhu Shuzhen beats Chen Haoyu to death, she owes no life in return, sign it and press your thumbprints, and I will walk straight out!"
Before Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan could weigh how to respond, a sharp voice rang out: "Write no such thing!"
Every head turned toward Old Madam Xia, marching up in a fury on her walking stick, supported by maids as she hurried forward with trembling steps.
A flicker of unease pricked Qianli Xun at riling up an elderly woman, yet she had gone too far to retreat—and maybe the grandmother's arrival would seal the breakup at last.
"Mother, why did you come out?" Yao Qingyan rushed up to meet her.
"Why did I come out? You have the nerve to ask?" Old Madam Xia snapped as she walked. "Something this huge happens and neither of you sends word! Good thing I overheard the servants gossiping, or I would never have known someone stormed Chen Manor!"
Servants scrambled to set down a chair; a maid took her cane and eased her onto the seat.
Settling in, Old Madam Xia glared daggers. "So you are Miss Zhu? Jiangnan's famed poetess? The one they call Youqi Jushi?"
"Respects to you, Elder Madam. That would be me," Qianli Xun answered.
"Hmph! What business brought you raising a storm at Chen Manor?"
Chen Jianglin stepped forward to explain, only for his mother to cut him off. "Not a word from you. I want to hear it from her."
"I already explained once, yet I do not mind telling Elder Madam again," Qianli Xun said, well pleased to see the old woman bristle at her brash swagger. "First, so the Chens can see my true face before the wedding and not cry foul afterward. Second, to tell you straight: if you want to cancel this match, there is still time."
"At least you are blunt and hide nothing—better than your father," Old Madam Xia scoffed. "Using his rank as Prefect of Lin'an to dump a daughter nobody else wants onto our doorstep, never once breathing a word that the celebrated Youqi Jushi is a raging shrew! No wonder Haoyu fought tooth and nail against marrying you. Right here in front of everyone, I give you my answer: the Chen family is calling off this match! Go back and notify Prefect Zhu yourself. Whatever score your father held over Haoyu to force this betrothal, the Chen family will settle it in coin—tell him to name his price!"
Hearing Old Madam Xia spell out the cancellation in plain, ringing words sent Qianli Xun's spirits soaring—even as one line snagged her ear. Her father had held something over Chen Haoyu to force the betrothal, something the Chens would pay silver to settle? What on earth had happened?
No time to unravel riddles right now; ending the betrothal came first. "Elder Madam talks real sense—leagues ahead of your son and daughter-in-law! Shuzhen thanks you, and I will go tell my father right away." She spun to leave.
"Hold on!" Chen Jianglin stepped in. "Do not rush off, Miss Zhu. Calling off a betrothal takes deliberation."
Qianli Xun halted. "What now? Going back on it already?"
Chen Jianglin turned to his mother. "Marriage is a lifelong matter, not to be tossed aside lightly. I beg Mother to reconsider."
"Reconsider what?!" Old Madam Xia snapped. "All you two care about is billing and cooing with each other, never sparing a thought for Haoyu's happiness! Besides, Haoyu has heavy responsibilities ahead of him—how could he take a wife with a tarnished name?"
Overhearing from the shadows, Chen Haodong's heart skipped a beat. Did Grandmother intend to hand the Chen family business to Haoyu, and was that why she wanted this match broken?
"Mother, Haoyu is nowhere near fit for heavy responsibilities," Chen Jianglin argued. "Even if you wish it, I will never agree. All I ask is that he marry, have children, and live out a peaceful life."
His uncle's words loosened the knot in Chen Haodong's chest a notch.
"Even without heavy responsibilities, he is not marrying Miss Zhu!" Old Madam Xia stood firm.
Qianli Xun piled right on. "Exactly—do not marry me! My name is mud, I cannot stand Chen Haoyu, and once I move in I will probably thrash him every other day. Elder Madam dotes on her grandson so much—you surely cannot bear watching me pound him, right?"
Alarm deepened on Old Madam Xia's face. "Listen to the words coming out of her mouth! How could we ever welcome someone like that into Chen Manor?"
Thrilled—with the matriarch firmly on her side, the wedding was nine-tenths dead—Qianli Xun grinned. "Listen to Elder Madam, Uncle Chen, and go settle the breakup with my father. I will wait for the good news at Wangyue Pavilion."
Cocking her fist, she brought it smashing down on the chair she had sat in, splintering it to pieces, then swaggered out the gate with Hongdou.
Every onlooker froze, eyes bugging wide and mouths hanging open at the sheer power in her punch.
"Look at that!" Old Madam Xia cried. "Not a shred of breeding! The Qiaos refused to take her, so they try offloading the menace onto us? Over my dead body!"
Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan exchanged helpless sighs—who knew Miss Zhu would torch every scrap of a lady's dignity just to wriggle out of a match?
Catching his wife's glance, Chen Jianglin stepped to his mother's side. "Come inside, Mother. Let us talk this over properly."
"What is there to talk over? Cancel the match and be done with it!"
A maid handed over her cane, and the old woman struggled to her feet. Yao Qingyan hurried to take her arm. "Mother, we mean discussing how to cancel it cleanly. We cannot ruin goodwill between the two households, so we need a thorough plan."
"Very well. I will sit in your rooms a while."
Supported by both son and daughter-in-law, Old Madam Xia headed toward their courtyard.
Back in the shadows, Chen Haodong frowned. Eldest Uncle had no mind to give Haoyu the business, yet Grandmother clearly did—and among the elders, her vote carried enormous weight. With his parents away representing the Chens at the Qiao wedding, there was nobody to consult yet; he would head to the shops first.
Once the courtyard cleared, he strolled past the gate, where servants were sweeping up split stone and splintered wood. "A real she-demon," Chen Haodong sneered under his breath. "Haoyu is in for misery if he marries her. Hmph!"
Ignoring the servants' polite greetings, he whistled his way out the gate.
#CH 170
#TITLE: Calling Off the Match, Then Stalling Again
Once Old Madam Xia was settled in a chair inside their room, Yao Qingyan dismissed the maids, shut the door, and glanced at Chen Jianglin.
Reading her cue, Chen Jianglin sat down and spoke straight to his mother. "Mother, you walked straight into a trap today."
Old Madam Xia's brow knotted in displeasure. "What trap?"
Taking the seat beside her with a placating smile, Yao Qingyan explained, "Blame Jianglin for not briefing you in time, Mother. Prefect Zhu paid a special visit to our silk shop last week to warn him that those two brats are staging tantrums on purpose to force both families into breaking the betrothal. They staged a brawl outside Zhu Manor once, and before New Year's they wrecked the prefecture officials' banquet at Juxiang Tower—even startling Prince Yi. Luckily Prefect Zhu did not take the bait."
"Prefect Zhu warned me that after failing with him, they were sure to bring the circus to our gate, and told us to ignore it," Chen Jianglin added.
Even understanding the ploy, Old Madam Xia stayed grim. "Hmph! Put-on or not, I watched with my own two eyes as she smashed a solid chair to splinters with one bare fist! Once Haoyu marries her, one tiny misstep and she might snap his neck!"
Chen Jianglin had no answer for that. He ran an empire of trade with effortless precision, yet domestic tangles lived up to the saying: not even an upright judge could untangle family affairs—which explained how his son had turned out the way he had.
Seeing her husband tongue-tied, Yao Qingyan stepped in. "Mother, Wenxin sings Miss Zhu's praises to me all the time, and she would never lie to her own family. Since Miss Zhu came specifically to make a scene, all that rough swagger must have been an act..."
Old Madam Xia thumped her cane against the floor. "Are you Haoyu's mother or hers, sticking up for an outsider? Act all she wants—can you fake the force to split stone and wood? Come the wedding night, my grandson will not even make it into the bridal chamber! You two can kiss any hopes of grandchildren goodbye!"
Now Yao Qingyan fell silent; one more word and she would be branded the worst mother in Lin'an.
Chen Jianglin tried leaning on the Prefect's authority. "If Mother insists on breaking off the match, I will obey. Only—Chen family trade grew this vast thanks in no small part to Lin'an Prefecture's goodwill. Offend Prefect Zhu over a jilted daughter, and I fear..."
"Hmph! Stop waving the Prefect to scare me! Even without Wenxin linking the two houses, with the Chen family's standing in Lin'an's merchant circles, the yamen would not dare touch us. Besides, Prefect Zhu is a fair man who will not mix private grudges with official duty." Having already mulled over her stalling plan, Old Madam Xia had worked through every angle and batted her son's argument right back.
Noticing her husband's yielding tone had softened the old woman's mood, Yao Qingyan followed suit. "Hearing Mother put it that way sets my mind at rest. We will obey Mother and cancel the Zhu match—though I do worry about one thing..."
Pleased by her daughter-in-law's deference, Old Madam Xia eased her tone. "Worry about what?"
Putting on a troubled frown, Yao Qingyan baited the hook. "I worry Prefect Zhu will dig up the rumor incident and refuse to let it go. He agreed to settle it privately only because he wanted a marriage alliance with our family. If we break off the betrothal now, what if he blows the matter open and drags both houses into court?"
Out of his mother's sight, Chen Jianglin shot his wife a silent thumbs-up. Yao Qingyan hid a triumphant smirk as Old Madam Xia knitted her brows in deep thought.
After a pause, Old Madam Xia said, "I already told Miss Zhu to tell Prefect Zhu we will settle it with money."
"Brilliant idea, Mother," Chen Jianglin praised smoothly. "Any trouble silver can fix is no trouble at all."
Yet doubt crept over Old Madam Xia's face. "Wenxin told me her father-in-law prizes his reputation above everything. What if he refuses silver and demands face instead? Haoyu spread rumors slandering his daughter's honor, and now we jilt her—Prefect Zhu will lose face all over the city. Silver may not smooth this over, and it really could end up in court."
Yao Qingyan put on a brave, unbothered front. "Let it go to court, then! Even if the yamen throws Haoyu in jail, we can spread silver around upstairs and down so he lives decently inside. Besides, it is only slander—they will hold him a few days at most and turn him loose."
"Bah! Bah! Bah! Watch your unlucky tongue!" Old Madam Xia scolded, then paused in alarm. "Does the yamen actually convict people for spreading rumors?"
Mixing half-truth with tall tale, Chen Jianglin laid it on thick. "Three years back, I heard two households went to court over one spreading rumors about the other. Once the yamen confirmed it, they sentenced the culprit to fifty strokes of the heavy bamboo! Word was he was first slated for five days in prison, yet because his son was heading to Bianliang for the imperial exams and a jail record would ruin him, the family bribed their way into trading prison for fifty blows. Fifty heavy strokes nearly beat the poor man to death..."
"Stop! Say no more!" Thoroughly spooked, Old Madam Xia waved him off. "Jianglin, go to Zhu Manor at once! Tell Prefect Zhu the Chen family will not cancel the betrothal—yet after Miss Zhu's tantrum today, the wedding must be postponed. As for when it takes place, that depends on when Miss Zhu mends her temper!"
Through nimble teamwork, husband and wife had saved the betrothal—only to land right back on Old Madam Xia's stalling tactic. The sole difference was that what had been the grandmother's private scheme was now forced out into the open. How long would the stall drag on? If Miss Zhu never mended her temper, would Haoyu stay single forever? Lacking a better move for now, they could only take it one step at a time.
Out on a bender, Chen Haoyu had planned to crash at one of his outside residences for a few days—until gossip that Qiao Wenxian was marrying today reminded him his own wedding loomed right around the corner, killing his mood to stay out. He had quizzed his grandmother several times about how she meant to stall; Old Madam Xia had whispered she would fake an illness so the whole Chen household dropped wedding preparations, giving them an airtight excuse to push back the date. Not a permanent fix, yet with nothing better on hand, he hurried home to coordinate the day she would fall "sick."
Watching the street from Chen Manor's gate, Qinggua hurried up the instant his master's carriage pulled in and offered a hand. "Young Master, you are back! Something huge happened at home!"
Ignoring the hand, Chen Haoyu leaped down, his face lighting up at the thought his grandmother had launched the plan already. "Did Grandmother fall ill?"
"No, Old Madam is healthy as an ox—"
Chen Haoyu aimed a kick at Qinggua. "Spit it out straight! What happened?"
Dodging sideways, Qinggua hurried on. "Do not get mad, Young Master, I am telling you! Miss Zhu came and raised hell at the gate!"
"Hah! Fun like that and she did not invite me? How rude!" Chen Haoyu chuckled, striding inside.
Trotting alongside, Qinggua painted the scene. "You should have seen it, Young Master! Miss Zhu's maid laid out several gatekeepers in three blows! And Miss Zhu was scarier still—split a thick stone block with one punch and smashed a chair to splinters! Scared Old Madam into yelling for a broken engagement on the spot!"
Chen Haoyu halted in his tracks, overjoyed. "She really agreed to cancel the wedding?!"
"Would I lie? Miss Zhu would never have left otherwise!"
"Did my parents agree too?"
"Master and Madam looked reluctant, yet they always listen to Old Madam, so they will come around."
"Well I will be damned—Zhu Shuzhen went behind my back and pulled off a miracle! Maybe I should treat her to dinner tonight as thanks!"
Beaming, Chen Haoyu made a beeline for Old Madam Xia's courtyard.
Qinggua chattered nonstop at his heels. "Folks always rave about Miss Zhu being a poetess writing all those mushy lyrics, and she looks dainty enough, so I figured she was soft as silk—never guessed she packs more punch than I do!"
Chen Haoyu flaunted his learning. "Listen to your uncultured mouth—'mushy'? That is called the delicate and restrained style, got it?"
"Heh, Young Master always knows more than me," Qinggua grinned. "Now I finally see why you refused to marry Miss Zhu and called her a she-demon. Marry her, and you could kiss brothels, gambling dens, and chasing pretty young maidens goodbye..."
"Scram! Can you say something pleasant for once?"
"Sure! Qinggua wishes Young Master a smooth broken engagement and a gentle, obedient young madam in her place!"
Much nicer to the ears. Chen Haoyu strutted on, grinning ear to ear.
Old Madam Xia's spacious courtyard bloomed with spring flowers, centered around a tall apricot tree shedding pale petals on the breeze. With the indoor rooms still holding winter's chill, she had returned from her son's quarters and settled onto a rattan chair outside under a blanket, eyes closed to soak up the sun while wondering how long she could drag out her grandson's match. Plainly Miss Zhu wanted no part of the Chens and would never soften her ways—could they really stall forever?
"Grandmother!"
Her eyes fluttered open, and a radiant smile broke over her face at the sight of her grandson. "Why are you just getting back? Come sit right here by Grandmother."
A maid set a stool beside her chair; Chen Haoyu perched on it and planted a kiss on her cheek.
Old Madam Xia beamed with delight. "My grandson is the sweetest, always knowing how to cheer Grandmother up."
"Grandmother is the person I love most in this whole world," Chen Haoyu cooed.
"What about your parents?"
"I love them whenever they are not lecturing me."
"And if I lecture you?"
"When would Grandmother ever lecture me?"
"Little rascal—Grandmother could never bear to."
After sufficient buttering up, Chen Haoyu pivoted to business. "Grandmother, Qinggua told me my unbridled bride-to-be stormed the house and you agreed to cancel the match. Is that true?"
"Of course! How could I let you marry a terror like that and suffer?"
"Grandmother really is the best!"
"Still, after talking it over with your parents, we cannot cancel the match after all..."
Alarm shot through Chen Haoyu. "Why not?!"
A shadow of reproach touched Old Madam Xia's smile. "Ask yourself—this mess is all your own doing."
The rumors! Too late for regrets now. "Grandmother, you saw firsthand how terrifying Zhu Shuzhen is, and you said yourself you do not want me marrying her! Think of a way to break it off!"
"You think I do not want to? Our only move is still my original plan: stall!"
"Stall again? Isn't there anything better?"
Old Madam Xia explained with patience. "Stalling now is different from before. Earlier it was only between the two of us, with no excuse to give the Zhus. After Miss Zhu's spectacle today, we have every right to go straight to Prefect Zhu and lay down terms."
"What terms?"
"Tell him we will welcome Miss Zhu through our doors the day she mends her temper."
"Zhu Shuzhen hates my guts—she will never mend her temper! That pushes the wedding off till kingdom come! I was lukewarm on this plan before, yet thinking about it now, it is genius!"
A crease returned to Old Madam Xia's brow. "Only it leaves you in a bind—how will you bring home a wife you actually like?"
"I am a man, Grandmother—no need to fret over me! Worst case, I take concubines first and still give you plenty of great-grandsons. The day the Zhus run out of patience and demand a breakup themselves, I promote a concubine to primary wife, and everybody wins!"
Old Madam Xia lit up at once. "Trust my clever grandson to clear the clouds in two sentences!"
Grandmother and grandson laughed louder and brighter, filling the whole courtyard with glee.
Standing nearby, Qinggua followed the drift: no cancellation, just endless stalling. What baffled him was why even a rampage like Miss Zhu's could not break the match. Old Madam blamed Young Master—what on earth had he done to trap himself so tight? Qinggua had once wondered if his master had taken liberties with Miss Zhu, yet today proved Young Master could not even get within arm's reach of her. Unable to crack the puzzle, he simply smiled along with the pair.
#CH 171
#TITLE: Hearts Worn Thin for Their Children
Leaving Chen Manor, Qianli Xun first thought of marching straight to Zhu Manor to tell her father the Chens had agreed to call off the engagement, then stopped herself lest they blow up into another shouting match. Better to return to Wangyue Pavilion, put every word Old Madam Xia had spoken down on paper, and send Kuqiao to deliver the letter. That spared a head-on clash while letting her weigh every phrase so her father could not brush off the Chens' refusal.
Back at her desk, she found writing to her father harder than drafting a huaben. Caring too much made her agonize over every word, afraid a clumsy line might ruin the hard-won concession she had wrung from Chen Manor. After crumpling a small mountain of drafts, she finished at last, folded the sheet into an envelope, and handed it to Kuqiao.
"Go to Zhu Manor and place this straight into my father's hands."
Reading the gravity on her mistress's face, Kuqiao tucked the letter with care inside her collar. "Leave it to me, Miss. I am on my way."
Boarding the carriage, Kuqiao rode to Zhu Manor's side gate. Just as she stepped down, Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan alighted from their own carriage right beside her, plainly come to call on Master and Madam.
Knowing her mistress had stormed Chen Manor and won their consent to cancel the match—and knowing what the letter contained—Kuqiao figured the Chen elders had come for the exact same purpose. Thrilled for her mistress, she hurried forward and curtsied. "Master Chen, Madam Chen! My mistress wanted to come in person yet feared quarreling with Master, so she sent me with a letter. What luck running into the two of you! With you here in person, my mistress's wish is sure to come true!"
Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan blinked, then caught on at once: this maid thought they were here to call off the wedding.
"Are you Miss Zhu's maid?" Yao Qingyan asked. "What is your name?"
"I am Miss's personal maid, Kuqiao."
"Is that letter about breaking off the betrothal between our families?"
"Yes, Miss told me to deliver it in person."
Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan exchanged a glance. Thank goodness they had arrived in time, before Old Madam Xia's outburst reached Prefect Zhu's ears!
"No need to deliver it," Yao Qingyan said smoothly. "We came here to talk Prefect Zhu into canceling the match ourselves."
"No! My mistress gave me an order, and I have to carry it out."
Seeing the maid's stubbornness, Yao Qingyan put on a gentle smile. "Give the letter to me instead. I am about to see Prefect Zhu and can hand it to him on Miss Zhu's behalf."
Kuqiao hesitated, then shook her head. "Miss told me to hand it to Master myself. I have to obey her."
Chen Jianglin thought fast. "Kuqiao, we both know what Miss Zhu wrote—only that the Chens agreed to break off the match. Yet a single sheet of paper will never sway Prefect Zhu. Worse, if a maid walks in and hands him that letter, imagine how livid he will be! With Miss Zhu not around, he is bound to vent his wrath on you. The Zhu household's discipline is strict; a round of the rod would be getting off light. Let us carry it inside, and not only does the job get done, you spare yourself a beating. Think about it—am I right?"
His words hit straight on Kuqiao's secret dread; all the way over, she had fretted over Master flying into a rage and punishing her under house rules. Here was a chance to get Miss's task done and save her own skin—why not take it?
"Since you both came to discuss canceling the betrothal, matching my mistress's aim, letting you deliver it works fine." Pulling the envelope from her collar, she handed it to Yao Qingyan. "Much obliged, Master and Madam Chen."
Yao Qingyan accepted it with a beaming smile. "Rest easy. Go tell Miss Zhu we will handle everything without a hitch."
Thanking them once more, Kuqiao climbed back into Wangyue Pavilion's carriage, sighing to herself: Young Master Chen's parents are such kind folks! If only their son were half as decent, marrying into Chen Manor would not be bad at all. Shame Miss has no fate with in-laws this fine.
Servants had already swung Zhu Manor's side gate wide to receive the guests. Stepping inside, Chen Jianglin took the letter from his wife, tucked it into his robe, and whispered, "What timing! Intercepting this letter saved us. Hide it well—Prefect Zhu must never lay eyes on it."
"We will burn it the second we get home," Yao Qingyan nodded.
Guided by a servant, the pair walked toward Prefect Zhu's courtyard. Alerted ahead of time, Zhu Xiyan assumed his in-laws had come to finalize wedding details; summoning Madam Xiang, he waited at the main hall doorway with a broad smile.
Seeing Zhu Xiyan and Madam Xiang beaming with spring cheer, Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan traded a rueful glance—Prefect Zhu would not be smiling like that much longer. Masking their unease at once, Chen Jianglin hurried forward with cupped hands and a warm grin. "Forgive us for dropping in unannounced and troubling our in-laws to come welcome us."
"Nonsense! Zhu Manor's gates stand open to you both at any hour—no need for advance word," Zhu Xiyan laughed.
Madam Xiang took Yao Qingyan's hand. "Come right inside, Sister."
"After you, Sister," Yao Qingyan replied with grace.
Once everyone was seated and maids served tea, Zhu Xiyan got straight to the point after pleasantries. "Did our in-laws come regarding the children's wedding?"
Chen Jianglin put on an easy air. "Indeed, regarding their wedding."
Zhu Xiyan beamed wider. "With the date close at hand, Shuzhen's dowry is packed and ready, awaiting only your sedan. Arrange the ceremony however the Chen family sees fit—no need to consult us."
"Every detail of the wedding is already arranged," Chen Jianglin said smoothly. "It will not fall a hair behind the Qiao family's feast. We are just waiting to carry Shuzhen home so someone can finally rein in that rascal of ours."
Hearing the wedding would rival the Qiaos' set Zhu Xiyan and Madam Xiang chuckling with satisfaction. Today's Qiao wedding was rumored to be Lin'an's grandest in years, drawing every notable family in the city. Zhu Manor had received an invitation too, yet Zhu Xiyan had refused to go—even with Prince Yi attending—sending his son and daughter-in-law with gifts instead. Showing solidarity as double in-laws, Chen Jianglin and his wife had skipped the Qiao feast too, raising the Chens higher than ever in Zhu Xiyan's esteem.
Watching his host laugh in high spirits, Chen Jianglin hated bursting the bubble, yet postponing the wedding was why he had come. Lifting his bowl, he took a measured sip of tea, set it down, and waited for the laughter to ebb before diving in. "A major incident took place at Chen Manor today..."
Throat dry from laughing, Zhu Xiyan took a sip of his own tea and perked up. "Oh? What sort of incident? Let us hear it."
"First promise not to lose your temper," Chen Jianglin hedged.
Sensing trouble at once, Zhu Xiyan sobered. "Why would I lose my temper? Speak plainly."
Glancing at his wife and catching her nod, Chen Jianglin laid it bare. "Miss Zhu marched up to Chen Manor today with a maid, beat several of our servants, and smashed a stone block and a chair to pieces. By the look of her, she will not rest until the betrothal is broken."
Fury surged in Zhu Xiyan's chest, though he choked it down before his guests. "Pay it no heed, my dear in-law. I warned you those two brats would bring their act to your door—and sure enough, she did."
Chen Jianglin gave the full picture. "Qingyan and I paid it no mind and tried talking her down, yet we could not override our Old Madam. As you know, Mother dotes on Haoyu and hates seeing him suffer a scratch. Seeing how fierce Miss Zhu is, she fears Haoyu will know no peace married to her, and declared we will only welcome Miss Zhu home once she mends her temper. Rest assured, the betrothal stands—only the wedding must be pushed back a little."
Keeping quiet on the side, Madam Xiang felt a pang of envy hearing Chen Jianglin address his wife by her given name; her own husband had never once called her so warmly. Yet the next moment, envy vanished into sheer dread: her daughter had stormed Chen Manor and made them delay the wedding! What if Master stormed over to Wangyue Pavilion and whipped Shuzhen again?
Zhu Xiyan grasped the truth now: his in-laws had come about the wedding, only not in the way he had hoped. A postponed wedding stung Zhu pride, yet after his daughter's outrageous spectacle, it was already generous of the Chens not to cancel outright. "Blame my poor parenting for raising such an unruly daughter. I will discipline her until she mends that temper!"
Hearing him vow to discipline Shuzhen, Madam Xiang began dabbing at her eyes, filling Yao Qingyan with guilt.
Afraid his report might get Miss Zhu beaten, Chen Jianglin jumped in at once. "Truth be told, I like Miss Zhu's fiery spirit—no need to change a bit! Once she marries in, she will whip Haoyu right into line. Only... you understand my spot, in-law. I cannot go against Old Madam; she is frail and getting on in years, and if stress harms her health, I could never live with myself..."
That took half the fire out of Zhu Xiyan's rage. "What do you propose, then?"
Yao Qingyan spoke up without waiting. "Jianglin and I talked it over. Shuzhen's spirit is fine as it is—no need to change her. Just give us a little time to talk Old Madam around. We will push the wedding back a month at most; in one month, the ceremony goes on as planned."
Drying her eyes, Madam Xiang dared chime in. "That sounds like a sensible plan."
Zhu Xiyan shot her a sharp glare. "Who asked you to butt in?"
Madam Xiang shrank back into silence, envying Yao Qingyan more than ever: she spoke twice as much without a single sharp word from Chen Jianglin, proving how respected she stood in her own home.
Turning back, Zhu Xiyan nodded to Yao Qingyan's proposal. "That you two have not soured on Shuzhen is blessing enough. One month it is. Still, both our houses have faces to keep in Lin'an, and plenty of folks have been asking about the wedding date. Delaying out of the blue requires a decent pretext so neither family becomes a laughingstock."
Knowing well how tightly Zhu Xiyan guarded his reputation, Chen Jianglin had come prepared. "If anyone asks, say the Chen family prizes this marriage so highly that we mean to throw a grander feast than ever, our preparations fell short of the mark, and we pushed the date back to do it full justice."
Face preserved for both houses, Zhu Xiyan smiled in approval. "Thank you for thinking so thoroughly. If you ever need me to help persuade Old Madam, just say the word."
"You have prefecture affairs to manage; Qingyan and I can handle a little family matter like this. If we truly hit a wall, we will ask for your help." Wrapping polite refusal in smooth flattery, Chen Jianglin swept away the last trace of Zhu Xiyan's anger.
With both households aligned, Chen Jianglin and his wife took their leave and climbed back into their swaying carriage for the ride home.
Along the way, Yao Qingyan furrowed her brow. "Only one month's delay—I truly worry whether we can talk Mother around in time. And if the rest of the family asks, do we say it is postponed one month, or give Mother's version?"
"Tell the rest of the family only that it is delayed, without spelling out details," Chen Jianglin mused. "As for Mother, we do our best and leave the rest to Heaven. If she still will not budge, we send her out to the country villa for a spell and bring her back after the wedding is sealed."
"Ah, I suppose that is our only play," Yao Qingyan sighed. "Any other bride marrying into our house would never cause this much fuss. Miss Zhu refuses to marry, Haoyu refuses to wed, and we are the ones caught in the middle."
Chen Jianglin chuckled. "Never heard the saying that good things meet many twists? To my mind, marrying her into the house will prove a blessing for the Chens."
Yao Qingyan perked up. "How do you figure?"
"Did you not notice today?"
"Notice what?"
"She is not one bit afraid of Mother."
"True enough—stood right before Old Madam without flinching." Realization dawned on Yao Qingyan. "You are too filial to defy Mother, so I defer to her too, leaving nobody in the house brave enough to rein in Haoyu. Bring Miss Zhu in, and at last someone will! Put like that, it really is a blessing."
"And she is not afraid of you either."
"What is that supposed to mean?"
"Think it over."
Yao Qingyan mulled a second, caught the dig, and burst out laughing. "Saying I stop you from disciplining our son? If you ask me, Miss Zhu is not afraid of you either! Scared of nobody—will she not turn our whole house upside down?"
Chen Jianglin clasped her hand. "Long as she turns Haoyu's world upside down, that is our good fortune."
Riding home hand in hand, husband and wife pictured the bright day someone finally whipped their son into shape and let them taste the joy of having a decent heir.
#CH 172
#TITLE: Thugs Outside Wangyue Pavilion
Back at Wangyue Pavilion, Kuqiao gave a full account of meeting Master and Madam Chen outside Zhu Manor.
Qianli Xun brightened at once. "Storming Chen Manor turned out to be a masterstroke! The betrothal might be dead by tonight. Once it is settled, I will go talk my father into swearing off arranging any more matches for me."
In the old days, Kuqiao would have urged her mistress to drop such wild notions. Yet her mistress now had nerve and grit to spare—once she set her mind on something, no obstacle could shake her. "I believe Miss can talk Master around," Kuqiao said, then added with a soft frown, "Still, I would feel much easier if Miss found someone who loves you back and whom you can rely on for life."
Qianli Xun drifted into thought. Wenyuan had been such a man, yet today was his wedding day—a good fate cut clean in half. Who else could ever share mutual love with her?
Out of nowhere, Prince Yi's image surfaced in her mind. She shook her head hard to fling it away—and the instant she stopped, he popped right back.
Seeing her mistress fall silent, Kuqiao stepped over to the tea table to whisk tea.
It must just be Wenyuan's wedding leaving an empty space inside her, Qianli Xun told herself, making her mind reach for someone to fill it. Ever since learning Prince Yi was Shen Jianchen, her attitude toward him had been shifting inch by inch—probably because when she had first transmigrated here, Shen Jianchen had been the only man in her thoughts.
Then Mengrong crossed her mind, and shame stung her. Harboring thoughts for a man betrothed to her closest friend was inexcusable. Still, she could not stop a quiet question from rising inside: without Mengrong in the picture, would she have accepted Prince Yi's love? She forced herself to answer, "Maybe"—afraid that without forcing it, the honest word would be "Yes."
Before transmigrating, she had brushed off Shen Jianchen's confessions every time, even declaring, "When pigs can climb trees, maybe I will marry you!" Now in this world, for the two of them to end up together really would take a pig climbing a tree. Truly, a person needed to watch her words before careless boasts turned into prophecy.
Her thoughts drifted to Prince Yi's wish to take her as concubine. By the standards of this era, a woman of Zhu Shuzhen's standing becoming a prince's concubine was no disgrace—yet Qianli Xun could never cross that line. First, she refused to share a husband and spend her days scheming against other women for one man's favor; exhausting and degrading both. Second, she could never break Mengrong's heart. Mengrong's devotion to Prince Yi ran bone-deep, touching anyone who witnessed it; even entertaining a stray thought about him felt sleazy to Qianli Xun.
To clear her head, she sat at her desk and picked up her brush to work on her huaben. Yet the messier her heart grew, the harder plotting a story became. No fiction would come—instead, Zhu Shuzhen's own poems rose unbidden in her mind.
A faint smile touched her lips. Poetry truly was the vessel of feeling; with emotion pressing for release, Zhu Shuzhen's verses answered the call on their own. Dipping her brush, she wrote out the five poems of *"Resenting Spring"* (*Hen Chun*):
One:
Early cherries are offered, apricots and plums still sour;
Tender locust leaves meet high winds, heading wheat feels the chill.
How melancholy that the Lord of Spring is so fickle of heart—
Even begging him to linger a moment proves hard indeed.
Two:
One fleeting flash of bloom, and all this time slips by,
Tormented that no fine lines capture my longing.
Bright as spring light may be, wind and rain come thick;
Just as deep love takes root, parting tears us apart.
With tear-filled eyes I thank the blossoms pressing close;
My heavy heart leans solely upon wine for support.
Orioles and swallows, do not laugh in pairs—
Try nesting all alone, and each of you will know.
Three:
Listless from last night's wine as the noon sun stands high,
A single bird cries out from the flowering bough.
Startled awake from sweet dreams just budding into bloom,
Fresh sorrow stirs and bursts open from the sheath.
In secret I mark future trysts wherever I turn;
At leisure I borrow verse to mock my pure regret.
From this day on I learn how kindness turns to grief,
And hold my quiet converse with the orioles and flowers.
Four:
Slowly the blossom-dappled sun climbs the curtain hook;
All day alone with no one near, I lean upon the tower rail.
Butterfly envoys and bee go-betweens carry a traveler's ache;
Oriole songs and willow threads weave spring's distress.
No word arrives from azure clouds—only fragrant dreams remain;
Red leaves inscribed with verse already call up autumn's thoughts.
How many partings, how many tears—
Unbearable to recall, unbearable to shed.
Five:
A coil of incense fades beside the scented sleeve sachet;
At leisure I read from scrolls upon my ivory-inlaid couch.
Oriole notes drift softly into the deep courtyard;
Willow shadows darken the painted garden wall.
Before my eyes, ten million petals of fallen red;
Along my cheeks, two or three fresh trails of tears.
Pear blossoms in fine drizzle after dusk—
Even one who knows no sorrow would feel their heart break.
[TN: Translator's rendering]
While she wrote, Kuqiao brought over the whisked tea. Seeing her mistress absorbed, she set the bowl down without a sound and watched from the side. Line by line, poem by poem, Kuqiao marveled inwardly: since her "rebirth," Miss's poetry lost nothing to her old genius—five whole poems in a single sitting!
Finishing the last character loosened the knot in Qianli Xun's chest. Setting down her brush, she lifted the bowl—just the right warmth—and drained it in one go. Glancing around, she asked, "Where is Hongdou?"
"Did Miss forget? You told Hongdou to drill the other nine servants in fist techniques every day. She is in the back courtyard."
"Look at my memory."
A page boy called from outside: "Miss, Huayu from Young Master Dongfang's manor is here again."
Qianli Xun smiled. "Timely as ever. Give him all five poems."
Kuqiao balked. "Miss worked so hard writing those—why hand over all five? Two ought to be plenty!"
"Poems exist to be read and savored. Lock them up in my room and they will just gather mold."
Conceding the point, Kuqiao gathered all five sheets and walked out to the gate.
Peering inside with an eager grin, Huayu beamed the moment she appeared. "Youqi Jushi and Sister Kuqiao are the kindest souls alive—never keeping me waiting!"
Handing over the sheets, Kuqiao teased, "You sure keep track of the calendar, showing up every few days like clockwork. Today is the Qiao wedding—why did you not go with your master?"
Accepting the papers with a grin, Huayu replied, "My master meant to bring me, then remembered today was the day to call at Wangyue Pavilion for verses, so he sent me here instead."
"Did you not miss out on a grand feast?"
"No sweat! My master promised that if I bring back fresh masterpieces from Youqi Jushi, he will treat me at Juxiang Tower!" Feeling the stack thicker than usual, Huayu counted the pages—five full sheets! "What a haul today! Five whole poems! My master will be over the moon—Juxiang Tower is in the bag!"
After Huayu skipped off, Kuqiao glanced down the lane toward the spot where Bai Feng usually waited. Today he must be attending Prince Yi at the Qiao wedding, so he had not come.
Just as she turned to go back inside, carriage wheels rumbled up—a Chen family carriage! It rolled to a stop, and Chen Haoyu climbed out all by himself without a single attendant.
Assuming he had come bearing good news about breaking off the engagement, Kuqiao stepped forward with a bright smile.
Chen Haoyu spotted her and opened his mouth to call out—when out of nowhere, a gang of rough men surged from the shadows and surrounded him tight.
Frozen in fright, Kuqiao bolted back inside Wangyue Pavilion, slammed the gate shut, and—unable to resist peeking—cracked it open an inch to watch.
Through the gap, she saw a swarthy, pot-bellied man with narrow squinting eyes shoulder through the thugs toward Chen Haoyu. "Serves Young Master Chen right for never doing any good!" Kuqiao muttered to herself. "Trouble caught up with you now!" She kept peeking.
As the burly man closed in, Chen Haoyu plastered on a cheeky grin. "Master Jiang! What brings you out here with such a big crew?"
Master Jiang gave a cold snort. "Your Chen estate sits right next door, and you haunt this neighborhood all the time. Think I did not know?"
"Master Jiang truly knows everything that moves in Lin'an!"
"Never figured Eldest Young Master Chen knew how to butter folks up! Seeing your polite attitude, give me your word right now: stay away from Chun Jinmo. This boss has his eye on her."
Chun Jinmo had been the very first courtesan Chen Haoyu had broken his celibacy with after the rumor scandal broke; since then, she received only him, and Chen Haoyu showered her with silver and favors in return.
"Chunxiang Brothel is overflowing with beauties—why pick a fight with me, Master Jiang? Name whatever sum of silver you want and it is yours."
Unmoved, Master Jiang sneered. "Think a pile of stinking Chen silver makes you king? Today your granddaddy will teach you there are things in this world money cannot buy! I want Chun Jinmo, not your silver—what are you going to do about it?"
Regretting he had left home without bodyguards, Chen Haoyu sized up the mob. No getting out without a scrap—and he was not handing Chun Jinmo over either. "With your numbers, Master Jiang, I cannot stop you—yet I am not giving Jinmo up on a platter."
"Beat him! Beat him till he says yes!" Master Jiang barked, stepping back as his thugs piled onto Chen Haoyu.
Kuqiao slammed the gate shut. "Serves him right for chasing brothel girls!" She sprinted back to her mistress's chamber. "Miss! Trouble! A brawl broke out outside!"
With her heart cleared by the poems, Qianli Xun was back at her huaben and did not look up.
"Miss, there is a fight right outside our gate!"
"Let them fight. What is it to us?" Qianli Xun kept writing.
"The one getting beaten is Young Master Chen!"
"Even better. He has had a beating coming for ages." Finishing a character, she froze mid-stroke. "Chen Haoyu came to Wangyue Pavilion?"
Kuqiao nodded.
"Could he have come to tell me good news about calling off the wedding?"
Kuqiao nodded again. "Looked like it—only before he got a word out, a gang boxed him in, traded two lines, and jumped him!"
Tossing her brush aside, Qianli Xun sprang up. "If he might be bringing good news, I suppose I should bail him out. Run to the back courtyard and tell Hongdou to bring everyone—let us test our training on the thugs outside!"
Much as Kuqiao loathed Chen Haoyu, Miss's sister-in-law was a sweetheart; on Young Madam's account alone, he deserved a hand. She dashed to the back courtyard, rounded up Hongdou and the nine trained servants, and met her mistress in the front yard.
Hearing the scuffle beyond the wall, Qianli Xun ordered the gate swung open and led her crew charging out.
Alone against a mob, Chen Haoyu had tried holding his own for face, yet his shoddy kung fu lasted all of ten breaths before thugs pinned his arms and legs tight.
Striding up, Master Jiang slapped him hard across both cheeks. "I already paid for Chun Jinmo, and you outbid me so the madam handed her over! Today you learn what happens to fools who steal from me!"
Unbowed, Chen Haoyu spat back, "Blame the brothel madam for chasing silver! Go beat her if you have the guts—why jump me?"
"Once I finish tuning you up, she is next!"
Hardly had the words left his mouth when the gate behind him banged open. Spinning around, Master Jiang saw ten people—three women and seven men—charge out, followed by a striking young lady.
Master Jiang's eyes lit up into a greasy grin. "Where did a pretty little miss like you pop out from?" Glancing up at the plaque reading *Wangyue Pavilion*, he chuckled. "Are you the mistress of Wangyue Pavilion?"
Before Qianli Xun could speak, Chen Haoyu hollered as if spotting a savior, "Zhu Shuzhen! Save me! These guys are thugs!"
At the name "Zhu Shuzhen," a bulb lit up in Master Jiang's head. "Aha! So you are that famous Jiangnan poetess! Pretty as a picture too..." Sidling closer, he leered. "Word is you are engaged to Chen Haoyu. He is no prize—haunting Chunxiang Brothel day and night! Why not dump him and come with me instead? Heheh!"
Qianli Xun had zero tolerance for sleazy remarks, and his greasy smirk turned her stomach. Without a word of warning, she drove a fist straight into Master Jiang's face. "Get a taste of my fist—and learn to keep your mouth clean when you talk to me!"
Clapping a hand to his burning cheek, Master Jiang bared his teeth in fury. "What are you idiots standing around for?! Get them!"
#CH 173
#TITLE: Giving Up on Breaking the Engagement
The two sides clashed at once, leaving Chen Haoyu forgotten on the ground. Flexing his sore arms and legs, he felt for the very first time that having a fierce fiancée had its perks, and jeered aloud, "Your boys are outmatched, Master Jiang! Better quit while you are ahead!"
Nursing his throbbing cheek, Master Jiang edged clear of Zhu Shuzhen lest she swing again. Watching his thugs get flattened in moments by Wangyue Pavilion's ten servants, he cursed his luck for stepping out without checking the almanac and bumping into a brick wall. A wise man knew when to cut losses.
"Heroine, you and I have no grudge," Master Jiang called out. "If I gave offense today, please be magnanimous and forgive me."
Unsure who the man was and reluctant to blow things up, Qianli Xun held her stance. "You started a riot outside Wangyue Pavilion and spoke filth to my face—that is why I hit you. Since this is your first offense, I will let it slide. Pull another stunt on my turf and I will not be so lenient!"
"Never again, never again!" Master Jiang put on a placating grin, then caught Chen Haoyu's smug smirk and could not resist a parting jab. "Beats me why a heroine like you would marry Young Master Chen. He runs to Chunxiang Brothel every single day—has a sweetheart waiting there!"
Temper flaring, Qianli Xun snapped, "Which ear told you I am marrying him?!"
"If you are not marrying him," Master Jiang countered at once, "then why step in to help him?"
"Help him? What a joke! Are your ears just for show? You picked a fight outside Wangyue Pavilion's gate, so I stepped in. Take whatever beef you two have somewhere else—I could not care less about your sordid messes!"
"Then please tell your people to release my men, Heroine, and I will clear out right now."
Qianli Xun waved a hand. Hongdou and the other nine released their captives, who scrambled back behind Master Jiang.
Cupping his hands, Master Jiang asked, "May I take Young Master Chen with me?"
"Not yet," Qianli Xun said. "I have questions for him."
"Ask away, Heroine. I will wait up the road on his way home." Shooting Chen Haoyu a dark glance, Master Jiang marched his gang away.
Listening to her deny she had helped him had soured Chen Haoyu's mood. The instant the thugs cleared out, he complained, "My sister is your sister-in-law, Zhu Shuzhen! How could you leave her own brother hanging—especially when we are betrothed? Cold-blooded!"
"Watching you get thrashed makes my day—why on earth would I help you? I only stepped out thinking you brought good news about canceling the engagement. Spit it out: did your parents go see my father to break it off?" Certain of it after shredding Zhu Shuzhen's lady-like image to ribbons, she expected nothing less than total surrender from the Chens.
"Hmph! I will not give you the satisfaction! Breaking off the match is off the table till kingdom come—Grandmother says she will only welcome you through our doors once you mend your temper!"
