Having meant to treat her to Juxiang Tower in thanks for storming his house, her cold shoulder killed all his goodwill; now he just wanted to watch her stew.
It worked like a charm—her face darkened at once. "Are you out of your mind, Chen Haoyu? Feeding me that same stale line after the ruckus I raised at Chen Manor?! Did nobody tell you what I did?"
Her anger only puffed him up further. "Qinggua gave me the play-by-play the second I stepped through the door! Who do you think my parents and grandmother are? They saw right through your cheap tricks! Breaking off this engagement is nowhere in sight."
Dread pricked Qianli Xun. "Why are you grinning so smugly? Almost sounds like you do not want to break it off!"
"Of course I do not want to marry you!" Snapping back to his real goal, Chen Haoyu dropped the smirk. "Your little rampage was not totally useless, though. Before, stalling was just Grandmother's private idea; after your show today, she got an airtight excuse to tell your father outright that I will only wed you when you fix your temper."
"Wait—your parents went to Zhu Manor to tell my father THAT?" Qianli Xun realized at once that Chen Haoyu's parents must have intercepted her letter.
"That is what Grandmother told me," Chen Haoyu confirmed. "All you need to do now is refuse to mend your temper, and our wedding gets stalled forever."
Deflation washed over Qianli Xun; she had pulled out every stop and ended up right back at square one. "Is your whole family touched in the head? I put on a show like that and they still refuse to cancel?!" Remembering Old Madam Xia's slip earlier, she pressed before he could reply, "Your grandmother said my father forced you to marry me for a specific reason. What reason? Spill it right now!"
Terrified Zhu Shuzhen might tear him limb from limb if she ever learned he had spread those rumors, Chen Haoyu scrambled to cover. "A reason? How would I know? Grandmother was probably rambling, or talking about some old grudge between the elders..."
Eyeing his twitchy face, Qianli Xun narrowed her eyes. "Really do not know? Then why are your eyes darting all over the place? Lying through your teeth?"
Steadying his gaze, Chen Haoyu bluffed hard. "Do not flatter yourself—what makes you worth lying to? I told you all I know, and you know what to do. I have places to be; no time to waste jawing with you."
Turning to leave, he remembered Master Jiang waiting in ambush down the road—step out alone and he would end up beaten to a pulp. Glancing at Wangyue Pavilion's ten servants, he asked, "Can I borrow your crew for a bit?"
"What for?"
"Those thugs are waiting to jump me down the street! You going to stand by and watch me get crippled? Watch out, next time I see my sister I will tell on you!"
Qianli Xun had zero desire to lend them, yet her sister-in-law doted on this brother. If Chen Haoyu got maimed—and Chen Wenxin learned she had refused to help—her sister-in-law would be heartbroken. Loathing him as she did, she gave in. "Hongdou, take everyone and escort Young Master Chen. Just keep that gang from laying a hand on him."
"Yes, Miss!" Hongdou answered crisply.
Calling out a breezy "Much obliged!" Chen Haoyu swaggered back into his carriage while Hongdou and the nine servants fell in behind to guard him.
Qianli Xun had no energy to dwell on doing him a favor; her third attempt to blow up the betrothal had fizzled, leaving a hollow ache behind. Stall? Stall until when? Drag it out long enough and she might still end up shoved into a bridal sedan.
At last, she turned her mind to Matron Wei's warning. Walking back inside, she asked herself: if she truly had to marry into the Chen family, how would she live? Chen Haoyu's words from the teahouse echoed back—useless as the guy was, he had tossed out one practical blueprint: live separate lives under one roof and stay out of each other's way.
She let out a long sigh. Her romantic road since transmigrating had been pure disaster. She could not marry the man she loved, and breaking an unwanted betrothal was proving harder than her divorce from Lin Jue—so hard even Prince Yi could not untangle it.
Could Prince Yi really not help? She shot that thought down on the spot. The catch was that she would not accept his feelings—or rather, she could not. Thinking of Prince Yi brought back Shen Jianchen. Since parting at the chuiwan grounds they had not met; Bai Feng had passed along one message through Kuqiao saying the prince and princess welcomed her at Youlin Retreat anytime, and Qianli Xun had sent back an evasive reply Shen Jianchen could surely read between the lines.
Back in her room, unable to shake her gloom, she turned back to her huaben, hoping losing herself in a made-up world would sweep away real troubles.
Kuqiao knew her mistress had bailed Young Master Chen out yet had not heard the engagement was still intact; one look at her mistress's downcast face told her most of it. "Miss," she asked with care, "should I not have handed Master's letter to Master and Madam Chen?"
Sitting at her desk, Qianli Xun shook her head. "Handing it over or not makes no difference now. Breaking the engagement is pushed off with no end in sight—who knows how it will turn out. Forget it. I am done chasing an annulment. From now on, I live my life as usual."
Guilt tugged at Kuqiao; she blamed herself for letting the Chen parents intercept the letter. "It is all my fault, Miss! Young Master Chen's parents were so sly..."
"Enough, I told you it was not your fault. Grind the ink—I am writing my huaben."
As Kuqiao ground the ink, Qianli Xun took up her brush, shoving the stalled engagement out of mind to dive into her story.
Seven or eight pages later, Hongdou returned. Seeing her mistress absorbed in writing, she started to slip back out when Qianli Xun called her back. "Why so long?"
"Young Master Chen asked us to escort him all the way to the main avenue," Hongdou reported. "Seeing he is betrothed to Miss, I did not refuse."
Qianli Xun did not scold her. "Did that gang try anything?"
"They would not dare!" Hongdou lifted her chin with pride. "One look at us shadowing Young Master Chen, and the swarthy fat man pulled his crew back, muttering he would settle the score another day."
"Go tend to your duties. Kuqiao can wait on me here."
Dipping her brush once more, Qianli Xun went back to her manuscript as Hongdou withdrew.
#CH 174
#TITLE: Leaving the Wedding Banquet for Zhu Manor
When Qiao Wenxian's wedding day arrived, Shen Jianchen naturally attended; as the Qiao family's maternal grandson, Prince Yi owed them his presence. Skipping the bridal procession, he arrived straight for the banquet and watched the new couple bow to heaven and earth.
Throughout the rites, Wenyuan kept a calm, unreadable face—so steady that guests never guessed the misery beneath. Though the red veil hid Miao Zhiyu's features, Shen Jianchen could picture her quiet bliss. Thinking of Miao Zhiyu's joy drew his thoughts straight to Qianli Xun. Was she hurting today? Every time he invited her to Youlin Retreat lately, she sent polite excuses—feeling under the weather, or preferring not to go out and staying shut inside Wangyue Pavilion. Knowing she was dodging him on purpose only sharpened the ache in his chest.
Mengrong had come to Wenyuan's wedding too, keeping a tighter watch on her brother than ever and sticking close wherever he went. Once the ceremony ended and the feast began, however, she moved to the women's section. Surrounded by warm, deferential Qiao ladies, she had no time to monitor her brother.
Freed from Mengrong's watchful eyes, Shen Jianchen breathed easier and accepted every toast offered. By the time Wenyuan came around to toast him, Shen Jianchen already felt a warm buzz yet drained his cup in one swallow. Wenyuan matched him drop for drop. Prince Yi was the very first guest he toasted; still sober now, he had resolved to drink himself blind tonight so he could forget the bride waiting in his chambers was not the woman he loved.
As Wenyuan moved from table to table, Shen Jianchen kept knocking back cup after cup, the wine only making him miss Qianli Xun harder. Beckoning Bai Feng over, he murmured, "Go hitch the carriage outside."
Without asking questions, Bai Feng slipped out of the feast and exited Qiao Manor.
Glancing over to see Wenyuan busy toasting guests, Shen Jianchen leaned toward Qiao Shouren and murmured, "Grandfather, urgent business calls me away. Once the banquet ends, please have someone escort Mengrong home."
Qiao Shouren knew better than to pry. "Go right ahead, Your Highness. I will see Princess Jiahe safely home when it wraps up."
Slipping out unnoticed, Shen Jianchen left the hall. With Wenyuan wed, the Zhu-Chen wedding loomed right around the corner. Restless and emboldened by wine, he headed straight for Zhu Manor.
Dusk had fallen by the time the carriage rolled up. Alighting, Shen Jianchen marched straight to the side gate, with a baffled Bai Feng hurrying at his heels to knock.
Recognizing Prince Yi, the gatekeeper dropped to his knees—only for the prince to stride straight past into the courtyard, then halt after two paces unsure which path to take.
"Prince Yi is here to see Prefect Zhu," Bai Feng told the gatekeeper. "Lead the way."
"Right this way, Your Highness."
The servant ushered Prince Yi into the reception hall, saw him seated, and dashed off to summon Master Zhu.
Having finished supper, Zhu Xiyan was strolling in his courtyard when the report reached him. Without stopping to change robes, he hurried toward the reception hall, wondering what could bring Prince Yi knocking right after dusk when he ought to be at Qiao Wenxian's wedding feast—good news or bad?
Stepping inside, he caught the sharp whiff of wine and saw the flush across the prince's cheeks. Dropping into a deep bow, he said, "Keeping Your Highness waiting is this humble official's dereliction."
Seated in his chair, Shen Jianchen lifted a tea bowl and drank. Part of him tested whether a few sips of tea would sober him up enough to hold back what he was about to say. It did not.
Watching the prince drink in tight-lipped silence rattled Zhu Xiyan—had some blunder in prefecture administration reached his ears? "If this humble official has erred in any way, please speak plainly, Your Highness."
Setting the bowl down, Shen Jianchen raised wine-heavy eyes. "I came to ask Prefect Zhu for one favor."
A boulder rolled off Zhu Xiyan's chest—not official business, then! "Speak, Your Highness! If it lies within my power, I will go through fire and water without hesitation!"
"No need for dramatics, Prefect. It is neither huge nor hard—only a matter of whether you are willing."
"Willing, willing, of course I am willing! How could I ever refuse anything for Your Highness?"
"Good. Then I will not beat around the bush. I love Youqi Jushi and want her by my side. I ask Prefect Zhu to go to the Chen family tomorrow and call off their engagement."
Even though Zhu Xiyan had half-suspected Prince Yi's feelings for his daughter, hearing it laid out so bluntly stunned him speechless.
Bai Feng froze in shock too. Seeing them embrace beneath the Divine Willow had tipped him off to his master's heart, yet with both of them betrothed, he had assumed the prince would let go in time. Never had he imagined His Highness going this far—pressuring the Prefect to cancel a wedding on the eve of the ceremony! How was this any different from snatching a bride?
Reeling, Zhu Xiyan dropped straight to his knees and bowed his head in silence.
Shen Jianchen looked down through hazy eyes. "What? You refuse? Think I am not good enough for Youqi Jushi?"
If only Prince Yi had spoken up months ago, Zhu Xiyan lamented inwardly—yet now he could not bear another storm. "Your Highness is a dragon among men. It is Shuzhen who lacks the blessing to match Your Highness."
Emboldened by wedding wine, Shen Jianchen pressed his heart out into the open. "Yet Youqi Jushi suits my eyes."
Zhu Xiyan knocked his forehead to the floor. "Your Highness is His Majesty's own son; taking wife or concubine requires imperial assent. Not only has Shuzhen been divorced once, her name in Lin'an has been bruised. I fear His Majesty would never approve. I beg Your Highness to think thrice!"
Raising his eyes to see Zhu Xiyan's brow near the floorboards, Shen Jianchen frowned in irritation. "Think thrice? Sounds to me like Prefect Zhu simply refuses to give me your daughter."
Zhu Xiyan pressed his forehead flush against the floor. "This humble official would never dare! Only Shuzhen's road in marriage has been rocky from the start. After all this trouble, she is finally betrothed to the Chen family with the wedding close at hand. As her father, I want no fresh complications, nor dare I dream of climbing into imperial ranks. I wish only for her to live safe and smooth."
Shen Jianchen fell silent as Qianli Xun's sharp rejection rang in his ears: "I told you—when pigs can climb trees, maybe I will marry you. That still stands."
Letting out a long breath, he rose, stepped around the kneeling Prefect, and walked out the door, Bai Feng hurrying right behind him.
Only after Prince Yi had gone did Zhu Xiyan lift his head, climb stiffly to his feet, and drop into a chair with a deep exhale. "Ah, who knew Shuzhen was so irresistible that even Prince Yi fell for her? Shame our Zhu family lacks the destiny to become imperial kin."
Leaving Zhu Manor, Shen Jianchen did not head straight back to Youlin Retreat, steering instead toward Wangyue Pavilion. Arriving outside its gate, he stayed inside the carriage staring blankly into the dark. Bai Feng dared neither speak nor slip off to see Kuqiao, sitting silent on the box beside his master.
About half an hour passed before Matron Wei returned in a carriage with a maid and a page boy. After her young mistress had gone off to Chen Manor earlier, Matron Wei had collected shop rents and lingered over tea with old friends, arriving home late. Stepping down, she spotted Prince Yi's carriage parked outside and assumed Princess Jiahe was visiting inside while the prince waited. Walking over, she curtsied. "Your Highness, will you not step inside and wait for Her Highness over a bowl of tea?"
When no voice answered from inside, Matron Wei blinked and asked Bai Feng on the driver's bench, "Guard Bai, is Prince Yi inside?"
Seeing his master stay mute, Bai Feng scrambled to cover. "How could His Highness be inside? I only borrowed the carriage on the sly to see Kuqiao! Seeing how late it got, I was debating whether to knock when you showed up, Matron. Better I head back now!" Snapping the reins, he drove off in a hurry.
Matron Wei knew at once Prince Yi had been sitting inside—and Princess Jiahe was nowhere near Wangyue Pavilion. Prince Yi's feelings for her young mistress were far from ordinary. Resolving to bury tonight's sight deep in her chest, she decided not to breathe a word to Shuzhen and spare her fresh grief.
Inside the rolling carriage, Shen Jianchen had stayed silent not from fear of exposure, only from sheer weariness. He did not blame Bai Feng for driving away either. By this hour, Mengrong would be back at Youlin Retreat. One thought of her brought that suffocating weight crashing down on his chest harder than ever. Since the chuiwan grounds, he had tried telling her the truth more than once, only to choke every word back before her loving, vulnerable eyes. Now, with wine buzzing in his veins, the urge to lay the truth bare burned hotter than ever.
#CH 175
#TITLE: At Last Speaking the Love in His Heart
When her brother vanished early from the wedding banquet without a word, anxiety gripped Qiao Mengrong tighter than ever before. Once Qiao Shouren's carriage delivered her back to Youlin Retreat, her first question to Butler Li was whether Brother had returned. Told he had not, she paced her chambers unable to sit still. Where had he gone? Had he slipped off behind her back to meet some beauty?
Seeing her mistress on edge, Xilan offered comfort. "Master Qiao said His Highness went to handle official business."
"Official business? When have you ever seen Brother care about official business?" Qiao Mengrong dismissed Qiao Shouren's excuse on the spot. "Think back for me, Xilan—since coming to Lin'an, has Brother treated any other woman differently?"
Xilan thought hard. "Does Youqi Jushi count?"
"Shuzhen? I do not see Brother treating her specially."
"Yet aside from Your Highness, Youqi Jushi is the only woman His Highness talks to at length."
"Shuzhen and I are close friends, so of course Brother talks more with her. Think harder—anyone besides Shuzhen?"
Xilan racked her brain again. "I truly cannot think of another woman His Highness treats out of the ordinary."
"I must be imagining shadows. Go watch the front gate and tell me the instant Brother gets back." A flash of the chuiwan match crossed Qiao Mengrong's mind—something between Brother and Shuzhen had felt slightly odd that day. Could Brother like Shuzhen? She brushed the notion off at once: why would Brother fancy a betrothed woman? And Shuzhen was her dearest friend—she would never betray her.
"Yes, Your Highness." Xilan withdrew to wait by the gate.
Nearly an hour later, Prince Yi's carriage rolled up at last.
Xilan hurried out to meet him. "Your Highness, Her Highness has been worried sick and had me wait at the gate."
Bai Feng sighed to himself: if the princess ever learned His Highness loved Youqi Jushi, how shattered would she be?
Stepping down from the carriage without a word, Shen Jianchen strode straight into the retreat. Bai Feng moved to follow, only for Xilan to catch his sleeve. "His Highness is heading straight for Her Highness. Let us hang back and not interrupt."
Agreeing—and praying His Highness would cherish the princess—Bai Feng held back.
Shen Jianchen walked directly to Mengrong's courtyard. To onlookers, it looked like a devoted betrothed rushing to ease her worry; only he knew he was going there to lay bare his heart.
Stepping through her courtyard gate, he saw Qiao Mengrong waiting under the moonlight. Catching sight of him, she ran straight up and threw her arms around his waist. "Where did you run off to, Brother?" The smell of wine rose from his robes—he had drunk plenty at the Qiao feast.
Just as always, Shen Jianchen did not wrap his arms around her. "Mengrong, I have something to tell you."
Pressing her cheek against his chest, Qiao Mengrong closed her eyes to savor the solid comfort of holding him. "Tell me anything, Brother."
After a quiet pause, Shen Jianchen braced himself. "Mengrong, after all this time together, you must have sensed I am not the Zhao Pu from before..."
Her eyes snapped open, the warmth gone in an instant, yet she refused to let go, terrified he would vanish if she loosened her grip. "Why bring that up, Brother?"
"I do not want to keep lying to you..."
Sensing dread ahead, Qiao Mengrong hugged him tighter. "Stop talking, Brother! You are my brother from before—you are Zhao Pu, His Majesty's thirteenth son!"
"Why keep fooling yourself? This body is Zhao Pu's, yes, yet the heart inside is not. I... I already have someone I love." Getting the truth out at last lifted a crushing stone from his chest.
Hearing the exact words she had dreaded so long drained the strength from Qiao Mengrong's arms. She pried her hands loose, tears brimming before she could speak. "Who is the woman Brother loves?"
Shen Jianchen did not answer directly. "Mengrong, I will always treat you like my own little sister, and I hope you will see me only as your brother."
Tears spilled down her cheeks as she pressed on. "Who is she?"
"I cannot tell you yet."
Grief overwhelmed her, and she threw her arms around him again. "Tell me you were joking, Brother! Tell me every word was a lie!"
Her sob-choked plea tore at Shen Jianchen's conscience, yet he refused to lie against his own heart. "I have held these words back for so long. Tonight I had to say them."
Forced to accept he meant every word, Qiao Mengrong released him again, sobbing through tear-streaked cheeks as she searched his face. Under the silver moonlight, he looked calm and relieved.
"What good does saying all this do, Brother? His Majesty decreed our marriage—you have no choice but to marry me."
Transmigrated into an unwanted imperial betrothal with zero way out, Shen Jianchen felt even more trapped than Qianli Xun; at least she could fight hers, whereas he had no opening at all.
Seeing him silent, Qiao Mengrong sobbed on. "No matter who you are, Brother, no matter whether I am in your heart, you are the man Qiao Mengrong is going to marry. Worst comes to worst, after we wed, I... I will let Brother take concubines! I will live in peace with the woman Brother loves..."
Looking at her fragile, weeping face, Shen Jianchen cursed himself inside even as he forced out the rest of the truth. "With the decree in place, we have no choice but to wed. I have thought it through: even after marriage, I will still treat you as a sister and never cross that line. And if you ever love someone else, I will set you free at any moment."
Every syllable cut deeper. So Brother had already planned out their whole future without her. Fresh tears poured down her face. "I will only ever love Brother in this lifetime, and I will stay by your side forever! Tell me who is in your heart right now, or I will swear you are lying to me!"
Shen Jianchen wanted her to know his heart, yet he could not expose Qianli Xun—not when she kept rejecting him and their two betrothals stood like mountains between them. "It is late. Get some rest."
Turning on his heel, he walked out of the courtyard.
Heartbreak crushed Qiao Mengrong, making her miss her old brother with every fiber of her being. Dragging heavy feet back inside, she threw herself across the bed and wept aloud.
Xilan hurried in to comfort her. "Do not cry so hard, Your Highness. Maybe His Highness was only testing whether your devotion is true!"
Qiao Mengrong sat up. "You heard everything?"
Xilan nodded. "Bai Feng and I stood outside the courtyard gate and heard every word."
"Xilan, pry it out of Bai Feng in private—who is the woman Brother loves?"
"I already asked him, and Bai Feng swore he had no idea. How could a guard read a prince's mind? I still bet His Highness was only teasing you."
"Bai Feng knows! He follows Brother everywhere—how could he not know where Brother goes? He just will not tell you."
"Then I will keep pressing him," Xilan promised. "Still, Your Highness has no need to despair. His Highness is the Emperor's son—of course there will not be only one woman around him, and taking a fancy to others is ordinary enough. You hold His Majesty's marriage decree and are guaranteed to be Prince Yi's primary consort. Once you hold the keys to the inner quarters, every other woman will walk on eggshells around you."
Xilan's sensible words cleared a corner of Qiao Mengrong's mind. With things already at this pass, crying changed nothing. Dabbing her cheeks with a silk handkerchief, she murmured, "You are right. Shuzhen told me the same thing once, and I brushed it off. Looks like she noticed Brother had someone else long ago—maybe she even knows who it is! I will visit Wangyue Pavilion tomorrow and ask her."
For all Xilan's soothing, Qiao Mengrong remained obsessed with one question: who held her brother's heart?
#CH 176
#TITLE: Prince Yi Wants a Pig to Climb a Tree
Leaving Mengrong's courtyard, Shen Jianchen did not return to his own chambers, instead leading Bai Feng out toward the Divine Willow.
Having overheard his master's confession outside the gate, Bai Feng both pitied the princess and felt sorry for his prince. For all his master's royal blood and lofty rank, he was actually worse off than his own guard: Bai Feng could be with the girl he loved, while the prince could not.
Following his master through the shadowy woods, Bai Feng mulled over Prince Yi and Youqi Jushi's odds and hit the same dead end every time: zero chance. If they could never be together, why blurt out the truth and break the princess's heart? When had His Highness even fallen for Youqi Jushi? Probably when he learned she was Qianli Xun. Really, why fall for a woman just because you enjoyed her huaben stories? No—wait—ever since finding out Youqi Jushi was Qianli Xun, His Highness had barely visited a single storyteller! So it was not the stories after all; maybe he had just gotten hooked on the woman herself. Bai Feng used to think his prince had zero interest in women; turns out he wanted a loyal wife holding the fort at home while chasing bright flags outside—Youqi Jushi today, who knew who tomorrow? Poor Princess Jiahe, doomed to weep again and again.
Lost in thought, Bai Feng arrived with his master beneath the Divine Willow. Fresh leaves draped the boughs, dotted here and there with red ribbons whose color melted into the dark as they fluttered on the spring night breeze. Half-moon light grew dim whenever clouds drifted past.
Having spoken his mind to Mengrong, Shen Jianchen felt lighter than he had in months. The betrothal remained and he would still have to wed her, yet laying the truth on the table had snapped one heavy chain off his shoulders.
Gazing up at the swaying willow in the dark, he found himself smiling over memories of chasing Qianli Xun back in their own world—even her blunt brush-off sounded sweet now: "When pigs can climb trees, I will marry you—and I mean every word!"
A sly grin tugged at his mouth. Since you laid down that condition, let us see how you wriggle out once I get a pig up the Divine Willow!
"Bai Feng!"
"Here, Your Highness."
"I have a mission for you."
"At your command, Your Highness."
"Hunt down a smart pig. Starting tomorrow, spend part of every day here under the Divine Willow training it to climb this tree."
Bai Feng's jaw dropped. Had His Highness lost his mind? Everybody knew pigs could not climb trees! "Your Highness, I doubt I can pull that off. Pigs are not built to climb—training will not help."
"Nothing is impossible to a determined heart. Tell you what: I will raise your silver allowance and let you go visit Kuqiao whenever you please."
The bait struck dead center, leaving Bai Feng sheepish. "When Your Highness gives an order, I never refuse—no need to bribe me."
"So that is a yes?"
"Your Highness treats me well; how could I say no? Only nobody has ever trained a pig to climb a tree, so do not hold your breath on results."
"You said yes, so get to it and stop overthinking."
"May I ask one question?"
"Go ahead."
"Why does Your Highness want a pig climbing a tree?"
Shen Jianchen paused a beat. "I just want to turn the impossible into the possible."
Still bewildered yet knowing better than to pry further, Bai Feng shrugged. "Alright then—I will go crazy right alongside Your Highness and try what nobody has ever tried."
Well pleased, Shen Jianchen turned back to the willow and spoke in his heart: Help me out here, Divine Willow, and see Qianli Xun and me together at last.
Meanwhile, after Chen Haoyu borrowed Qianli Xun's servants to escort him, the squad really had scared Master Jiang off along the road, leaving him thrilled. Only once his carriage reached the main avenue and he spotted Scarface approaching with a pack of lackeys did he wave Wangyue Pavilion's crew off; disgusted, Hongdou led her team straight home without a word.
"Who was that crowd, Young Master Chen?" Scarface asked, jogging up to the carriage.
Poking his head out the window, Chen Haoyu snapped, "Quit asking questions! Go fetch Lu Yuanfeng for me on the double!"
Scarface rubbed his hands with a cheeky grin. "About the errand fee..."
"When have I ever been stingy with silver? Move!"
Scarface darted off. Climbing down, Chen Haoyu sent his driver back to Chen Manor and strolled toward Chunxiang Brothel with his cronies.
Inside, Chun Jinmo kept him company through song, dance, and a few rounds of dama—a Song board game—until night fell and Lu Yuanfeng arrived. This was the same martial expert who had sparred with Qianli Xun in the bamboo grove; Chen Haoyu had hired him purely to keep Master Jiang from crashing the brothel, and with generous silver on the table, Lu Yuanfeng had jumped at the work.
Weaving through the noisy ground floor, Scarface led Lu Yuanfeng up to the second floor and knocked on Chun Jinmo's door. "Young Master Chen, I brought Lu Yuanfeng!"
Having just finished fooling around with Chun Jinmo, Chen Haoyu let her straighten his robes before sitting primly at the table. Chun Jinmo opened the door, then returned to cracking melon seeds.
Stepping inside with Lu Yuanfeng, Scarface stole an appreciative look at Chun Jinmo—a real dish, no wonder Young Master Chen lived at the brothel these days—and held out a greedy palm. "Young Master Chen?"
Chen Haoyu slapped a silver ingot into his hand. "Scram!"
"Scramming right now!" Scarface pocketed it with a wide grin.
Next, Chen Haoyu set a heavy pouch of silver on the table. "This pouch is yours, Lu Yuanfeng. Starting right now, stay glued to my side and keep me safe."
Without standing on ceremony, Lu Yuanfeng tucked the pouch into his sash. "Good silver buys good work. With me around, nobody lays a finger on Young Master Chen."
"Now that is what I like to hear!"
Hardly had the words landed when Master Jiang's roar shook the floorboards from downstairs: "Chen Haoyu! Get your ass down here! Quit hiding like a turtle in its shell! Tonight I beat you into submission!"
Chun Jinmo froze mid-seed. "What do we do, Young Master Chen?"
With a master fighter right beside him, Chen Haoyu stayed cool. "Relax. Stay safe upstairs while I go meet Master Jiang."
"Master Jiang runs the underworld and kills without blinking—be careful!"
Though Chen Haoyu was not head over heels for Chun Jinmo, she suited him better than anyone he had met—neither coy and prissy like sheltered maidens nor throwing herself into every customer's lap like other brothel girls. Her loyalty sparked a rare protective streak in him. "Relax. With a master here, Master Jiang does not amount to squat!"
#CH 177
#TITLE: Master Jiang Fails to Pick a Fight
Chen Haoyu stepped out of Chun Jinmo's room with Lu Yuanfeng right behind him. Scarface and his lackeys hurried over. "Young Master Chen, Master Jiang even tied up the brothel madam! Looks like he will not leave without Miss Jinmo tonight."
Brimming with swagger, Chen Haoyu waved them off. "Stand aside! See the master standing behind me? If I do not flatten Master Jiang tonight, I will write my surname upside down!"
Scarface and the lackeys parted to clear his path.
Strutting to the top of the stairs, Chen Haoyu looked down onto a wrecked hall. Scared patrons had bolted, tables and chairs lay overturned, and the stage stood empty. The brothel madam sat bound to a chair with rope while the other courtesans had vanished upstairs, leaving the entire ground floor packed with Master Jiang's thugs.
"Failed to bully me in the street, so you crash Chunxiang Brothel instead, Master Jiang?" Chen Haoyu jeered as he descended. "Not scared my master here will plant another fist right on your face? Heh!"
The bruise Qianli Xun had left on Master Jiang's cheek still throbbed; Chen Haoyu's taunt pushed him over the edge. "You got lucky this afternoon, Chen Haoyu, yet tonight your luck runs dry! Watch your worthless neck! Be smart and call Chun Jinmo down to wait on me—catch me in a good mood and I might spare your hide."
"Please have pity on me, Young Master Chen, and give Jinmo up!" the bound madam wailed. "Blame my greedy eyes for chasing silver! Mercy, Master Jiang—I am not holding out, Young Master Chen will not let her go! Tell you what, I will find you a fresh young maiden instead—how about it?"
"I want nobody else—only Chun Jinmo!" Master Jiang barked.
The moment Chen Haoyu hit the ground floor, thugs closed a tight ring around him and Lu Yuanfeng.
Paid to clear trouble, Lu Yuanfeng murmured by Chen Haoyu's ear, "Relax, Young Master. Not one of these clowns is a match for me."
Chen Haoyu smiled easily. "Better call your boys off, Master Jiang, and let us talk like civilized men."
"Hand over Chun Jinmo and call me Granddaddy a few times, and I will talk civilized all night!"
"Silver you can have; the woman—wait for your next life! No, not even then!"
Fuming, Master Jiang backed off a step. "Take him! Tie Chen Haoyu up!"
As the mob closed in, Lu Yuanfeng eyed them with cold scorn. "Know what is good for you and back off. With me standing here, nobody touches a hair on Young Master Chen's head."
The thugs rushed in with raised fists. Shielding Chen Haoyu, Lu Yuanfeng met them head-on—one punch dropped a cluster, a sweeping kick sent another bunch flying, and within minutes every single one of Master Jiang's thugs lay groaning on the floor.
Dropping his fists without even breaking a sweat, Lu Yuanfeng declared, "Young Master Chen's safety is under my watch from now on. Keep your eyes open and stay clear of him—hear me?!"
Stunned, Master Jiang stared with fresh respect. "Hero, how have I never seen a fighter of your caliber before?"
Lu Yuanfeng answered with a cold snort.
Undeterred, Master Jiang pressed on. "No friendship starts without a bout! Let us get acquainted—I run Lin'an's underworld, folks call me Master Jiang. How do I address you, Hero?"
Spotting the poaching attempt, Chen Haoyu jumped in. "He is hired muscle on my payroll! Quit trying to butter up my bodyguard, Master Jiang!"
Ignoring him, Master Jiang pitched straight to Lu Yuanfeng. "Hero, wasting skills like yours trailing after Chen Haoyu leads nowhere. Why not throw in with me?"
"I never mix with underworld scum," Lu Yuanfeng scoffed.
Delighted, Chen Haoyu jeered, "Heard him! Now take your crew and get lost!"
Just then, boots thundered through the doorway as a squad of soldiers surged in behind Lin Jue, sword strapped at his hip. The instant the madam had been tied up, Chun Jinqiao had sent word to Lin Jue, who had arrived at top speed.
Face flipping straight to smooth courtesy at the sight of soldiers, Master Jiang greeted him, "Well now! What good wind blows Division Commander Lin here—and with such a big escort?"
Lin Jue swept a cold look over the thugs. "Instead of staying put at your Qiantang Gang, why are you tearing up Chunxiang Brothel?"
Unwilling to cross government troops, Master Jiang hurried to smooth it over. "A misunderstanding! Pure misunderstanding! I only came to settle a private score with Young Master Chen."
"Then why is the brothel madam tied to a chair?"
"What are you standing around for?!" Master Jiang barked at his men. "Untie Madam at once!"
Two thugs scrambled to undo the knots. Freed at last, the madam thanked Lin Jue profusely and scurried out of the way.
Lin Jue turned a disdainful gaze on Chen Haoyu. "Wedding right around the corner, Young Master Chen, and you are still haunting Chunxiang Brothel?"
Chen Haoyu sneered right back. "Brand-new groom yourself, Division Commander Lin, and here you are too!"
Both men let out a simultaneous "Hmph!" and looked away.
Turning back to Master Jiang, Lin Jue asked, "What private score do you two have? Let us hear it."
Master Jiang stepped close. "All over Chun Jinmo! I fancy her something fierce, yet Young Master Chen hogs her all to himself and blocks my path. Infuriating, right?"
"Infuriating indeed," Lin Jue agreed. "Still, settle your grudges out on the street—do not wreck Chunxiang Brothel, spoil everyone's fun, or drag bystanders in. Take Chen Haoyu outside and beat him to any shape you please, and I promise I will not lift a finger."
"With Division Commander Lin's word, my heart is at ease! Enjoy your night—see you around!" Master Jiang rounded up his battered crew and cleared out.
With order restored, Lin Jue dismissed his troops. With patrons long fled, the courtesans shut their doors for the night, leaving only Chen Haoyu, Lin Jue, and Lu Yuanfeng downstairs.
Trouble over, Chen Haoyu turned to head back up to Chun Jinmo—only for Lin Jue to stop him. "Not heading home yet, Young Master Chen?"
Chen Haoyu paused. "Division Commander Lin sure loves sticking his nose in."
Stepping closer, Lin Jue sneered. "How is Zhu Shuzhen marrying you any different from being with me? Just like me, you have zero taste for fancy literary games and live at Chunxiang Brothel—only your family has deeper pockets and you are a few years younger."
"Cannot help my irresistible charm," Chen Haoyu boasted. "Had Prefect Zhu desperate to shove his daughter at me."
Leaning in with a mocking smirk, Lin Jue murmured, "Watch out that rumor business does not blow open, or you will be eating more than you can chew."
"Save your concern for yourself, Division Commander Lin," Chen Haoyu murmured back. "Decide whether you are sleeping at Chunxiang Brothel tonight or crawling home to Lin Manor."
Just then Chun Jinqiao tripped down the steps and hooked her arm through Lin Jue's. "Stay the night with me, Commander?"
Without a second's hesitation, Lin Jue grinned. "Since I am already here, why not?"
Brushing past Chen Haoyu with Chun Jinqiao on his arm, Lin Jue started up the stairs.
Chen Haoyu hollered at his back, "Watch out, Division Commander Lin—you might end up divorced a second time!"
Pausing on the steps, Lin Jue tossed a laugh over his shoulder. "My current wife beats Zhu Shuzhen ten times over—gentle and sensible. Eat your heart out, kid!" Chuckling, he went upstairs with Chun Jinqiao.
Lu Yuanfeng looked at his employer. "Young Master Chen, am I staying the night at Chunxiang Brothel too?"
Already eager to get back to Chun Jinmo, Chen Haoyu halted. "Of course! What if that gang comes back after you leave?" Waving at the madam, he called, "Madam, send a girl down for my friend here!"
"Coming right up—plenty of beauties to spare!" Happy to earn back business, the madam shouted upstairs, "Jinchan!"
"Coming! What do you need, Madam?" a voice floated down.
"A gentleman downstairs needs company—get down here quick!"
Swaying her hips down the staircase, Jinchan took Lu Yuanfeng's arm. "Let me wait on you tonight, sir?"
Though unused to brothel ways, Lu Yuanfeng went along with his employer's treat and let himself be tugged upstairs.
Danger passed and everything squared away, Chen Haoyu whistled his way up the steps one by one.
#CH 178
#TITLE: Scheming to Make Master Jiang a Matchmaker
Having caroused late at Chunxiang Brothel the night before, Scarface still rose at dawn while Chen Haoyu slept off his night in Chun Jinmo's arms, slipping over to feed Chen Haodong fresh intel and pocket extra silver—playing both sides like a fish in water.
Hiding behind a thick tree outside Chen Manor, Scarface kept watch on the front gate. Eldest Young Master Chen juggled business and pleasure with equal ease, sneaking trysts with Chun Jinchan in spare moments yet always first out the door at dawn for family trade.
Sure enough, Chen Haodong stepped out first. Scarface poked his head from behind the trunk. "Eldest Young Master! Over here! Over here!"
Glancing around before walking over, Chen Haodong snapped, "You scruffy rat—making me walk extra steps? Show some respect next time."
"No offense, Eldest Young Master," Scarface grinned. "If anyone catches me talking to you, my cover as your eyes and ears is blown."
"More like you are just chasing silver."
"Whatever the motive, long as it helps Eldest Young Master, it is all good!"
Chen Haodong tossed him a silver ingot. "Your pay. People will be coming out soon—make it snappy."
Pocketing the ingot with a broad grin, Scarface reported, "Somebody tried to jump Young Master Chen at Chunxiang Brothel last night."
"Who?"
"Master Jiang."
"Master Jiang runs the underworld—how did my cousin cross him?"
"Over Chun Jinmo, who else? Master Jiang wants her too, yet she only takes Young Master Chen and shuts everyone else out."
"How did it end?"
"Young Master Chen hired a tough bodyguard who stays glued to his hip, so Master Jiang struck out. Then Division Commander Lin stormed in with troops and broke it up."
"Ah, that cousin of mine is good for nothing, yet he has the devil's own luck. Anything else?"
Scarface shook his head. "That is all."
"From now on, do not lurk outside Chen Manor," Chen Haodong warned. "Bring news to Yongtai Teahouse by West Lake—I stop by every few days."
"Who knew Eldest Young Master had such refined taste?" Scarface flattered. "I hear scholars and poets pack that place!"
"Save the lip and beat it before someone sees you."
Once Scarface slipped away, Chen Haodong lost all mind to visit the shops and turned straight back into the manor. His father had returned late from the Qiao wedding the night before, so he had not disturbed him with news of Miss Zhu's rampage; now, with Scarface's report sparking a bright scheme, he went straight to consult him.
Reaching his father's quarters, he found Chen Jiangshu already up and eating breakfast. "Eaten yet?" his father asked.
"I have. Meant to head to the shops, then something came up I need to talk over with Father."
"Sit down and talk," Chen Jiangshu said between spoonfuls of congee.
Taking a chair, Chen Haodong stayed silent and glanced at the maids. Catching the hint, Chen Jiangshu dismissed them. "Leave us—I need words with Eldest Young Master."
Once the maids curtsied out, Chen Jiangshu picked up his spoon again. "Speak freely. Nobody here but the two of us—your mother is still asleep inside."
"Father and Mother went to the Qiao wedding yesterday and missed quite a show."
"Miss Zhu storming the gates?"
"Father already knows?"
"A ruckus that loud, every servant in Chen Manor knows. Your Third Uncle dropped by last night and filled me in too."
"Saves me explaining, then. Did Third Uncle mention Grandmother wants Haoyu to inherit the family business?"
Chen Jiangshu froze, laying his spoon down inside the bowl. "He mentioned nothing of the sort. Where did you hear that?"
"I hid nearby during Miss Zhu's rampage yesterday and heard every word. Grandmother felt sorry for Haoyu and agreed to cancel the match. When Eldest Uncle and Aunt pleaded with her to reconsider, Grandmother snapped that Haoyu has heavy responsibilities ahead and cannot marry a bride with a bad name. Sounds clear as day her vote is going to Haoyu."
Pushing his bowl aside, Chen Jiangshu leaned back and mulled it over. "So Old Madam really wants Haoyu as heir—I misread her before. Looking back, she agreed to the Zhu match at first thinking Prefect Zhu would give Haoyu official backing; now that she sees Miss Zhu's tarnished name might hurt her precious grandson, she wants out."
"My thoughts exactly."
"So—do you want the match broken off, or kept?"
Chen Haodong weighed it a beat. "Since marrying Miss Zhu gives Haoyu zero edge for inheriting the business—and will even drag him down—letting him marry her is best. Besides, whatever Haoyu hates doing most is exactly what I want him stuck doing. I refuse to let him sail through life easy."
Chen Jiangshu nodded. "Then this marriage must not fall through. I wonder where your Eldest Uncle stands—is he still preparing the wedding?"
"I asked servants in Eldest Uncle's wing yesterday. Word is he claims he values the match so much he wants a grander feast than the Qiaos' and postponed the date to prepare."
Chen Jiangshu let out a dry laugh. "Pure pretext! From what I know, the wedding your Eldest Uncle already laid out beats the Qiaos' hands down. Looks like the match is stalling out."
"Father, why not give Haoyu a helping hand and make sure he marries that she-demon?" Chen Haodong leaned forward eagerly.
"What scheme do you have in mind?"
Rising, Chen Haodong leaned close and whispered his plan into his father's ear.
A wide grin spread across Chen Jiangshu's face. "Splendid! Set it in motion. Take whatever silver you need—only make certain you do not lose our Chen family fortune for real."
"Leave it to me, Father."
Taking his leave, Chen Haodong skipped the shops again and rode his carriage to the quietest edge of the eastern quarter, pulling up before Master Jiang's lair. Above the gate hung a plaque reading "Qiantang Gang"—named simply because Master Jiang liked the Qiantang River.
Two brawny guards stood outside. Alighting, Chen Haodong stated his business, and one guard led him in past yards full of men drilling weapons—quite a sprawling outfit, Chen Haodong marveled. After waiting briefly outside the main hall, he was shown inside.
Seated at a table gnawing a chicken leg with two mean-looking lieutenants flanking him, Master Jiang wiped his grease-stained fingers on a rag. "Chen Haoyu's cousin, eh? What brings you here?"
Cupping his hands, Chen Haodong bowed smoothly. "Long have I admired Master Jiang's fame. Family trade kept me tied up, or I would have called to make your acquaintance sooner. Today I finally carved out time—hope Master Jiang will not turn me from your door."
"I love making friends! You walk right into my hall—why would I turn you away? Bring a seat!"
A lieutenant set a chair down. Settling in, Chen Haodong smiled. "I brought a modest gift in my carriage outside. Master Jiang may have your men fetch it."
Eyes brightening, Master Jiang waved at his lieutenants. "Go haul it in for a look!"
Moments later, the two carried in a wooden chest and flipped the lid to reveal gleaming stacks of white silver.
Master Jiang grinned from ear to ear. "Shut the lid before it blinds me!" As the guards moved the chest aside, he eyed his guest shrewdly. "Spending this heavy, Eldest Young Master Chen—surely you are not here begging mercy for Chen Haoyu?"
"Far from it. I came to ask Master Jiang to help seal my cousin's marriage to Miss Zhu."
Master Jiang blinked. "When did I turn into the Old Man Under the Moon, playing matchmaker?"
Before explaining, Chen Haodong poured on smooth flattery. "Folks say Master Jiang is a stand-up hero who built the Qiantang Gang so his brothers could make a living. They say you aid the poor and commoners give you thumbs-up on the street. Above all, your word is solid gold—you never go soft on silver that is owed you, and never touch a single coin that is not."
Master Jiang blinked in mild amusement. "Am I really that saintly? One thing you got right, though: what is mine I take to the last coin, and what is not mine I leave untouched. Even our line of work has its code. Quit buttering me up and spit out the job—I do not take silver for free."
Reassured, Chen Haodong stood, stepped beside Master Jiang, and whispered his scheme into his ear.
Understanding lit up Master Jiang's face. "So THAT is how I play matchmaker! Aren't you scared I walk off with the entire Chen fortune for real?"
"I came to you precisely because I trust Master Jiang," Chen Haodong replied. "Once it is done, two more chests of silver come straight to your door—only I will need Master Jiang to put it down on a signed pledge."
Master Jiang scowled. "Sweet talk on your lips, distrust in your bones! Fine—seeing your plan lets me mess up Chen Haoyu, I will write your pledge. Bring brush and paper!"
One guard cleared the chicken bones while the other laid out writing gear. Master Jiang wrote out the pledge and pressed his thumbprint. "One lesson I learned walking the rivers and lakes: never get greedy for wealth not meant for you, lest you choke on it and lose your neck."
Tucking the pledge safely inside his robe, Chen Haodong praised him. "Master Jiang sees life clear as crystal. Respect!"
Master Jiang let out a sigh. "Clever plan—only a crying shame for that heroine. Pretty, packs a wallop, writes fine poetry, and stuck marrying Chen Haoyu. What a waste."
It took Chen Haodong a beat to catch who "that heroine" referred to. "Who knew Master Jiang had a tender streak for fair ladies? No need to pity Miss Zhu—fierce as she is, she will never take the losing end at Chen Manor. Best of all, my cousin will live in daily misery under a shrew's thumb, venting your grudge for you too."
Master Jiang brightened at once. "Good point! With a tigress wife watching him like a hawk, Chen Haoyu will never dare set foot in Chunxiang Brothel again—and Chun Jinmo falls straight into my lap! Heh, the more I chew on this plan, the sweeter it gets!"
With the conspiracy sealed, Chen Haodong exited the Qiantang Gang and headed off to manage Chen trade, waiting with quiet relish for good news.
#CH 179
#TITLE: A Dear Friend Calls for Help
After a sleepless night of grief, Qiao Mengrong rose at dawn, dressed, picked at her breakfast, and—for the very first time—skipped going straight to her brother's courtyard, heading instead out of Youlin Retreat with Xilan.
Passing the main courtyard, she caught the sound of her brother drilling Bai Feng in martial arts and let out a quiet sigh. Last night felt like a bitter dream.
"Xilan, how long since we sent that letter?"
Knowing she meant the letter to Imperial Attendant Liang, Xilan answered, "Over half a month now, Your Highness."
"Do you think His Majesty will agree to move the wedding up?"
Unable to know yet unwilling to see her mistress hurt, Xilan reassured her. "With Imperial Attendant Liang putting in a word, His Majesty is sure to agree."
"I worry Imperial Attendant Liang will not help me."
"Why would he not? You are a princess—and you slipped him plenty of silver over the years."
"I can only hope so. Let us go."
Exiting Youlin Retreat, Qiao Mengrong boarded her carriage for Wangyue Pavilion to see her closest friend. Since parting at the chuiwan grounds, they had not seen each other, and she had no idea how Shuzhen's efforts to cancel the Chen betrothal were faring—not that she had room to worry over anyone else's troubles today. She had far heavier questions to ask Shuzhen.
At Wangyue Pavilion, Qianli Xun had risen early, checked on the servants' morning drills in the back yard, and sat back down to her huaben. Lately she was adapting an online novel she had written before transmigrating, tweaking details to fit the Song setting. Halfway through a chapter, her brush stalled: the plot called for a gambling scene, and she knew next to nothing about Song gambling dens.
Remembering spotting Chen Haoyu strolling out of a gambling house once, she decided to visit one in person for field research—maybe even try a hand or two so the scenes rang true.
"Kuqiao, go fetch Hongdou and pull out my men's clothes. I am going out on an errand."
Bringing Hongdou made sense, yet disguising herself again puzzled Kuqiao. "Why dress as a man again, Miss? Nobody in the streets gossips about you anymore."
Qianli Xun made no secret of it. "My huaben needs a gambling scene, and I have no clue how those places work. Time for field research."
"Who knew writing huaben was tougher than writing poetry—even needs field research?! Don't tell me Miss means to gamble too?"
"If needed, sitting down at a table will not hurt."
Still baffled by her mistress's ways, Kuqiao turned to go fetch Hongdou—only for Princess Jiahe to hurry right through the doorway. Dropping into a curtsy, Kuqiao realized the gambling trip was off and stayed put.
Setting her outing aside at once, Qianli Xun hurried to welcome her friend. "Come sit inside, Mengrong! Kuqiao, clear the desk and whisk Her Highness a bowl of tea."
Catching the hint, Kuqiao stowed the manuscript pages safely away before sitting at the tea table.
Only once Qiao Mengrong sat down did Qianli Xun get a clear look at her: faint red rims around her eyes, pale cheeks. Alarm tugged at her. "You have been crying—what happened?"
Mengrong's eyes reddened darker, and fresh tears spilled over.
Qianli Xun pulled out a silk handkerchief and gently dabbed her cheeks. "Do not cry—tell me what happened."
"Brother... Brother told me straight to my face last night that he has someone else he loves, and only wants to treat me as a little sister..."
What she had dreaded most had come to pass—Shen Jianchen had told Mengrong the truth. Judging from Mengrong's manner, though, he had not revealed who that person was.
Qianli Xun froze, every soothing word feeling hollow and false on her tongue.
Over at the tea table, Kuqiao caught every syllable and panicked inside: Prince Yi's beloved was clearly Miss! If Her Highness found out, their friendship was toast!
Seeing Shuzhen silent, Qiao Mengrong went on. "You warned me once before to stay sharp, Shuzhen, and I brushed it off. Looking back, I trusted Brother too blindly. Help me think—what do I do now?"
Forced to speak, Qianli Xun said, "I already told you back then I misread him—Prince Yi is not that kind of man..."
"No! You read him dead right! Betrothed to me, yet his heart wandered to someone else! He even said after we marry he will still treat me like a sister, and if I ever love another man he will let me go! Listen to that—he never loved me at all! Wuuu!" She dissolved into sobs again.
So Shen Jianchen had mapped everything out: he would look after Zhao Pu's sister for him, yet he could never love her the way Zhao Pu had. Qianli Xun ached for Mengrong and understood Shen Jianchen both. Her only course was leaving Shen Jianchen zero scrap of hope; maybe once hope died, he would turn his full heart to Mengrong.
"Do not despair," she said. "Prince Yi is a man of duty. Even if another woman enters his life, he will never cast you aside. Keep faith in him."
Wiping her tears and choking back her sobs, Qiao Mengrong looked straight at her. "When you warned me about Brother before, you must have known he fancied someone. Tell me—who is the woman in Brother's heart?"
Before Qianli Xun could blink, a sharp clatter rang out from the tea table—Kuqiao had knocked the tea bowl clean over!
Listening hard while whisking, Kuqiao had flinched at the princess's question and tipped the bowl with her bamboo whisk. Flushing, she scrambled to mop it up and start a fresh bowl.
Glancing over at Kuqiao's panicked face, Qianli Xun knew the maid was sweating on her behalf and said nothing to scold her. Unable to dodge Mengrong's gaze yet unable to tell the truth, she chose a white lie. "I... of course I have no idea! Maybe Prince Yi was just teasing you—do not take it to heart and make yourself miserable. With your betrothal locked in, you are bound to marry him. Even if he truly fancies someone, she could only ever be a concubine. You hold His Majesty's decree; nobody can shake your seat as Prince Yi's primary consort. Set your heart at ease and get ready to be Princess Consort Yi."
Doubt flickered in Mengrong's eyes. "You truly do not know? Then why warn me like that before?"
"I told you, I misjudged Prince Yi," Qianli Xun pressed on with her lie. "Seeing him glance an extra second at pretty women gave me the wrong impression. I take back every word I said back then."
She looked so earnest that Qiao Mengrong had no choice but to believe her. "Alright, I trust you. Still, Brother having someone else in his heart is a hard fact, and I have to find out who she is. You have to help me, Shuzhen."
This time Kuqiao kept a death grip on the fresh tea bowl so it would not tip.
Qianli Xun forced a stiff nod. "I will help—what do you want me to do?"
"Help me track down the woman Brother loves."
"And once you find her... what will you do?"
"I... I do not know. Maybe... maybe offer her silver to leave Brother."
Mengrong was too decent to even imagine nasty schemes, piling fresh guilt onto Qianli Xun. "Alright, I will help you look—though I cannot promise I will find her."
"Thank you, Shuzhen." Gripping her friend's hands tight, Qiao Mengrong whispered, "When I feel most helpless, at least I still have you."
Qianli Xun gripped back, as if clinging to their fragile friendship. "Rest easy. I will help you keep your brother all to yourself."
Deeply touched, Qiao Mengrong counted herself blessed to have a friend like Shuzhen.
Kuqiao carried the fresh tea over with utmost care, set it before the princess, and watched from beside her mistress—sure enough, Her Highness had no inkling the rival was standing right in front of her.
Releasing Mengrong's hands, Qianli Xun asked, "Stay for lunch after tea, alright?"
Though no real solution had been found, Mengrong's mood had calmed noticeably since arriving. Taking a sip of tea, she shook her head. "I slipped out in a hurry without telling Brother. I ought to get back soon."
Seeing how her friend still worried over her brother, Qianli Xun did not press her to stay. Instead, she made up her mind to meet Shen Jianchen face-to-face and urge him to give up all hope of her.
Once Mengrong finished her tea, Qianli Xun walked her out to her carriage and watched it roll down the lane before turning to Kuqiao. "Is Bai Feng coming to see you today?"
"He meant to come yesterday, yet had to escort His Highness to the Qiao wedding," Kuqiao replied with caution. "Not sure about today."
"If he shows up, send word through him to Prince Yi: meet me at Yongtai Teahouse by West Lake at the Hour of the Rooster."
Turning back into Wangyue Pavilion, Qianli Xun had lost all appetite for disguising herself to research gambling dens; she needed the afternoon to decide what to say to Shen Jianchen.
#CH 180
#TITLE: Bai Feng and Sister-in-Law at Wangyue Pavilion
Sure enough, right after lunch, Bai Feng showed up looking for Kuqiao. The moment the gate boy brought word, Kuqiao skipped outside to their usual meeting spot.
"Yesterday I had to attend the Qiao wedding with His Highness—" Bai Feng began at once.
"No need to explain, I already figured."
"You are always so understanding." Taking her arm, Bai Feng pulled her deeper into the shadows.
"Nobody is out here—why drag me off to the corner?" Kuqiao asked, puzzled.
"I have something huge to tell you, and no other servant from Wangyue Pavilion can overhear."
Scanning the empty lane, Kuqiao dropped her voice. "What is it?"
"Yesterday, His Highness marched straight into Zhu Manor and demanded Prefect Zhu hand Youqi Jushi over to him..."
Kuqiao's jaw dropped before he finished. "What?! Really?!"
"Scared the daylights out of me too! That was when it finally hit me—Youqi Jushi is the woman His Highness really loves. Poor Princess Jiahe!"
Hearing him take the princess's side ruffled Kuqiao's feathers. "My mistress is the pitiful one, getting pestered by Prince Yi out of nowhere! If Her Highness ever learns Prince Yi loves my mistress, she will never set foot in Wangyue Pavilion again!"
"Wait—Her Highness came here?"
"First thing this morning! Cried her eyes out and asked Miss if she knew who Prince Yi loved! I was sweating buckets for Miss!"
"No need to sweat—I already figured out the whole puzzle for them."
Kuqiao gave a dry laugh. "YOU figured it out?"
"Underestimating me? Watch my brains at work! Think about it: can my prince and your mistress ever end up together?"
Kuqiao shook her head, then nodded.
"Why both shake and nod?"
"Shake because both of them have betrothals, so being together looks impossible. Nod because Miss hates marrying into the Chen family and fought hard to break it off—she eased up lately, yet I bet she tries again. If that match breaks, and Prince Yi pulls royal rank on Master, Master is bound to cave."
"Which means the whole thing hinges on Youqi Jushi! Long as she marries Young Master Chen like a good girl, problem solved!"
Temper flaring, Kuqiao snapped, "What kind of rotten talk is that, Bai Feng?! Miss cannot stand Young Master Chen—he is not even half the man Lin Jue was! Marrying him jumps from one fire pit into a deeper one! What was the whole point of her divorce?!"
"Keep your voice down before someone hears!"
"Beat it! I am not talking to you!"
"My fault, my fault—I apologize! Do not let other people's messes come between us."
"Who are you calling 'other people'? That is my mistress—closer to me than anyone outside my own family! Do you call Prince Yi 'other people' too?"
"What is wrong with my mouth today? Always saying the wrong thing! Forgive me, alright?"
Unwilling to stay mad at Bai Feng for long, Kuqiao softened. "Fine, forgiven."
Taking her hand, Bai Feng grinned. "That is my girl. We only see each other every few days—no sense wasting it fighting. Ask Youqi Jushi for the afternoon off and stroll the market with me?"
"No can do. Since Her Highness left, Miss sat staring blankly right through lunch in a foul mood and just picked up her brush to write her huaben. I cannot walk out on her now."
Bai Feng slumped in disappointment. "Alright then. Guess I am heading to the pig market alone."
"Pig market?! What for?"
"His Highness ordered me to buy a pig and train it to climb trees. Weird or what?"
"Are you sure Prince Yi has not bumped his head?"
Bai Feng put on a mock scowl. "Talk smack about my prince and watch out for a spanking!"
Kuqiao giggled. "Lay a finger on me—I dare you!"
"Like I could ever bear to."
Remembering her mistress's errand, Kuqiao added, "Tell Prince Yi when you get back: my mistress invites him to Yongtai Teahouse by West Lake at the Hour of the Rooster."
"Even after all this, Youqi Jushi still wants to meet him?"
"Stop underestimating Miss! She is going to lecture him into treating Her Highness right."
"Thank goodness for that."
Just then carriage wheels rattled up—Young Madam Zhu had arrived! Having seen Kuqiao already, Bai Feng bade a swift farewell and ducked around the corner.
Stepping down from the carriage behind Chen Wenxin, Yuegui teased, "Guard Bai bolts the second we pull up—up to something naughty?"
Kuqiao flushed bright red. "Quit teasing me, Sister Yuegui!"
"Who is teasing you? I meant Guard Bai!"
Blushing deeper, Kuqiao clamped her mouth shut.
"Enough teasing her," Chen Wenxin laughed. "What is Shuzhen up to, Kuqiao?"
"Miss is at her desk writing..." Catching her slip just in time—Yuegui's ribbing had dropped her guard—Kuqiao swerved smoothly: "...writing poems and lyrics!"
"A poetess through and through—not even resting after lunch! Let me go chat with her." Chen Wenxin started inside.
Kuqiao hopped in front to slow her down. "No rush, Young Madam! Let me dash ahead and tell Miss—walk slow!" Turning tail, she sprinted straight into the courtyard.
Chen Wenxin blinked. "What got into Kuqiao today? Acting so jumpy."
"Probably embarrassed from my teasing," Yuegui chuckled.
"Must be."
Bursting into her mistress's room out of breath while Qianli Xun scribbled away, Kuqiao panted, "Miss, Young Madam is here!"
Setting down her brush, Qianli Xun raised an eyebrow. "What are you panicking over? It is not like I am doing anything crooked."
Sweeping the huaben pages together, Kuqiao reminded her, "Miss does not want anyone knowing you write huaben!"
"Good thinking." Pulling out a fresh sheet of ornamental paper, Qianli Xun began copying one of Zhu Shuzhen's poems.
Just as Kuqiao slid the manuscript into a wooden box on the shelf, Chen Wenxin walked in with Yuegui. "Put the brush down, Shuzhen, and chat with your sister-in-law."
Guessing her sister-in-law had come over yesterday's raid on Chen Manor, Qianli Xun stood and took her hand. "You have not visited in ages, Sister-in-Law—stay and talk awhile."
"Listen to you blaming me—why do you never come visit me?"
Laughing as they sat down, Qianli Xun reminded her, "You know Father barred me from setting foot in Zhu Manor."
"More like you do not want to go back! Father-in-law talks stern, yet if you actually showed up, he would not say a word."
Her sister-in-law hit the nail on the head. Leaving Zhu Manor had hurt at first, yet living at Wangyue Pavilion had grown so sweet she had no wish to go back. The garden at Zhu Manor belonged to Zhu Shuzhen; Wangyue Pavilion belonged solely to Qianli Xun. Divorcing Lin Jue had been step one toward her own life, and moving out of Zhu Manor was step two.
"Why so quiet?" Chen Wenxin asked.
Pulling back from her thoughts, Qianli Xun switched to family chatter. "How is Mother?"
"Mother-in-law is fine—stop changing the subject and tell me why you stormed my parents' house yesterday!" Chen Wenxin cut straight to the chase.
Faced with her sister-in-law, Qianli Xun felt a twinge of sheepishness. "Why ask when you already know?"
"Honestly, you! A noble daughter and Jiangnan's celebrated poetess—smashing rocks and splintering chairs! What kind of spectacle is that? Word gets out and half the city will laugh!" Reproach mingled with genuine affection in her voice.
No need to hide behind polite masks with Chen Wenxin. "Let them laugh—I do not care, so long as I do not have to marry Chen Haoyu."
Chen Wenxin ached for her too. Her younger brother was a hopeless rake; what respectable lady in Lin'an would want him unless chasing Chen money? Shuzhen had beauty, brains, and family—marrying Haoyu was a raw deal by any measure. As a sister-in-law, she wanted Shuzhen to find true joy; yet as a Chen daughter, she still hoped Shuzhen would marry in and straighten Haoyu out.
"Shuzhen, some men are born steady and walk the straight path from day one; others have to wander down crooked roads before turning straight. I believe Haoyu is the second kind. Have a little patience with him—once you marry him, he might turn around faster than you think."
Her heartfelt plea bounced straight off Qianli Xun. "What kind of man Chen Haoyu is has nothing to do with me, and I have zero obligation to marry him just to fix him. Raising him is the Chen elders' job."
Seeing she could not budge Shuzhen an inch, Chen Wenxin dropped the persuasion. "Think what you like—I will save my breath. I went home to visit today, and my parents told me your wedding with Haoyu is postponed for a spell until preparations are perfected, then goes ahead on a new date. Father-in-law already agreed."
Qianli Xun sat bolt upright. "Wait—was your family not planning to stall forever? Why did it change?!"
"Stalling was Grandmother's notion. My parents only humored her to her face while settling the real plan with Father-in-law. Stop scheming, girl, and get ready to be a Chen bride."
Unable to stay mad at her sister-in-law's smug grin, Qianli Xun still lobbed a comeback. "Even if I marry him, so what? Your own brother told me we will live our own separate lives."
"Fine, fine—just as long as you walk down the aisle." Afraid pushing further might strain their sisterly bond, Chen Wenxin rose. "Keep busy, Shuzhen. I will head back and visit another day."
Watching her sister-in-law beat a hasty retreat, Qianli Xun chuckled—Chen Wenxin underestimated her if she thought a disagreement could sour their bond. Yet once the chuckle faded, realizing the wedding was only postponed wiped away her humor.
Oddly enough, though the news annoyed her, the panic and fury were nowhere near as fierce as before—because she had already mapped out how to survive if she ended up at Chen Manor. Respect for Matron Wei's wisdom deepened in her chest. She was tired of fighting the engagement. She had done all she could; let fate take the wheel from here. Staying unwed was great; marrying had its silver lining too—at least it would force Shen Jianchen to give up. Whichever way it fell, she would look on the bright side.
#CH 181
#TITLE: Buying Home a Little Dim-Witted Pig
After parting with Kuqiao, Bai Feng made a lap through the pig market, seeing nothing but huge, grimy, fat hogs that turned his stomach. Just as he turned to leave, a middle-aged farmer rolled up pushing a barrow full of piglets and began hawking: "Fresh piglets straight off our sow! Dirt cheap—eighty wen apiece!"
Bai Feng walked over and looked them over. Clean enough and easy on the eyes. "I will take one."
Delighted to score a sale the second he parked, the farmer grinned. "Take your pick, young man—home-raised, cheaper than anyone else on the street!"
Knowing nothing about pigs, Bai Feng pointed at random. "That one. Tie a rope on it so I can lead it home."
Looping a cord around the piglet's neck, the farmer lifted it off the barrow. Free on four hooves, the piglet tried bolting—only for Bai Feng to haul the rope taut. "Trying to run? Let us see how far you get!" Pulled short, the piglet flopped meekly onto the dirt.
"Eighty wen, young man," the seller reminded him.
"Do not worry, you will not be shortchanged." Gripping the rope in one hand, Bai Feng fished eighty copper coins from his pouch, paid the man, and led the piglet away.
Hiring a carriage to haul himself and the piglet back to Youlin Retreat, Bai Feng drew a cluster of curious servants the moment they stepped through the gate. "Guard Bai, what did you buy a pig for?"
"To teach it a skill," Bai Feng sighed.
"Not teaching it kung fu, are you?" a servant joked.
"Shoo! Beat it, all of you! His Highness ordered it—he has his reasons."
As the servants scattered, Butler Li walked up with a chuckle. "Well now! Guard Bai craving Dongpo Pork so bad you plan to fatten your own pig for slaughter?"
"Update your view of me, Butler Li," Bai Feng retorted, keeping a tight grip on the rope lest the piglet bolt. "Eating at His Highness's table every day, I got sick of Dongpo Pork ages ago—I prefer light dishes now."
"Then why buy a pig?"
"His Highness's orders. Quit prying and get back to work." Leading the piglet off to report, Bai Feng left Butler Li muttering after him, "Prince Yi's orders? What on earth does a prince want with a pig? Beats me."
Tying the piglet to a tree in the main courtyard, Bai Feng turned to knock—only to find Prince Yi already standing in the doorway, drawn out by the grunting.
"What does Your Highness think of this pig?"
Stepping close, Shen Jianchen looked it over. Clean and pale enough, yet still thoroughly pig-faced and unlovable. "One pig looks like the next—long as it is clean."
Right on cue, the piglet let loose a stream of urine followed by a steaming pile of dung, sending a sharp stench wafting across the yard.
Pinching his nose, Shen Jianchen barked, "Get this mess cleaned up and tie it somewhere else!"
Turning to fetch a servant, Bai Feng stopped at the gate and looked back. "Your Highness, Youqi Jushi invites you to meet her at Yongtai Teahouse by West Lake at the Hour of the Rooster."
Qianli Xun initiating a meeting?! Thrilled enough to forget the stench, Shen Jianchen dropped his hand. "Understood."
Seeing the undisguised grin light up his prince's face, Bai Feng fretted for Princess Jiahe and added, "Kuqiao told me Her Highness visited Wangyue Pavilion first thing this morning."
The grin vanished off Shen Jianchen's face at once—guessing immediately why Qianli Xun had called him out. "Cut the chatter and get someone to clean this up! Starting today, every spare hour outside fist drills, train that pig!"
"Your Highness has not forgotten your promise, right? Extra silver and visiting Kuqiao whenever I want?"
Without answering, Shen Jianchen spun around and went back inside.
Bai Feng kicked himself for mentioning the princess's visit and souring his master's mood—what if last night's promises went up in smoke? Then again, His Highness was generous and always kept his word. Cheering back up, he went to fetch a servant to scrub the courtyard and scouted out an empty corner in the back yard to pen the piglet under a servant's watch.
Once the yard was cleaned, Shen Jianchen picked up a book inside merely to kill the hours. Even guessing why Qianli Xun had asked to meet, anticipation still tugged at him. After laying his heart bare to Mengrong last night, he was done hiding his feelings. Part of him wondered if honesty was selfish—hurting Mengrong and risking Qianli Xun's friendship with her—yet he could not force himself to live a lie. He talked himself around: in this world, having him was enough for Qianli Xun; she did not need anyone else's affection, friendship included. They had known each other before transmigrating, crossed time together, and found each other again—if fate that deep did not unite them, what justice existed under Heaven? Today, he resolved to persuade Qianli Xun to choose him willingly. Say the word, and once Mengrong was provided for, he would cast off the title of Prince Yi, leave Lin'an with her, and never set foot in Bianliang again.
Back from Wangyue Pavilion, Qiao Mengrong felt marginally calmer, yet every thought of her brother loving someone else twisted a knife in her chest. Used to seeing him every day, staying away made the world feel drained of color. Tidying her makeup, she stepped out of her room and walked hesitantly toward his courtyard, Xilan trailing behind with a heavy heart—no words could fix this; her mistress had to find her own footing.
Reading inside, Shen Jianchen sensed a shadow at the door and looked up. Mengrong stood there, eyes faintly red, brows knitted in sorrow; dipping into a curtsy without a word, she took a chair.
Guilt pricked him, yet not regret. Setting his book down, he said, "Go out more often when you have time, Mengrong—get some air, take outings with friends. Do not waste this fine spring."
Hearing his breezy tone—without a shred of reluctance to part with her—deepened her ache. "Where Brother goes, I go. If Brother stays, I stay."
Catching the heavy hurt in her voice left him at a loss. Gauging the hour, he stood up. "I have an errand and need to head out."
He started toward the door, leaving her sitting there.
Qiao Mengrong shot to her feet. "Is Brother going to see the woman in your heart?"
Nailed on the spot, Shen Jianchen almost said yes—yet without Qianli Xun's consent, he still had to shield her. "Just heading out on business."
"Why will Brother not tell me who she is?"
"When the time is right, I will."
"Last night—could Brother have been teasing me?"
She hung on his words, praying for a yes, only to hear clear as ice: "Why would I joke about my own heart? Rest easy, Mengrong—I will take good care of you."
Breaking down, Qiao Mengrong lunged forward and wrapped her arms around him, tears pouring free. "I do not want Brother's care! I want Brother's love—just like when you were sick! Wuuu!"
After a beat of hesitation, Shen Jianchen pried her arms off him. "Do not throw yourself into my arms anymore, Mengrong. You are a young lady—if folks see, they will think you lack modesty."
His rebuff cut her to the bone. "I am about to marry Brother!" she choked out. "Hugging my own betrothed—why should I care what tongues wag?!"
The betrothal remained a wall he could not scale. Giving no answer and mindful of the hour, he strode straight out the doorway without looking back.
His cold retreating back opened the floodgates all over again. Sobbing uncontrollably, Qiao Mengrong gasped out, "Xilan... find a servant... give him plenty of silver... have him tail Brother... find out... who he is going to see!"
Heart aching for her mistress, Xilan dashed out at once, grabbed a sharp-eyed servant eager to win the princess's favor, pressed silver into his palm, and whispered instructions. Grinning ear to ear, the servant swore to pull it off. Leading him out to the front gate just as Prince Yi's carriage rolled away, Xilan flagged a coachman, bribed him for silence, and ordered him to trail Prince Yi's carriage without getting spotted.
Only after watching the tailing carriage pull out did Xilan return to her mistress's room. Back in her chambers weeping quietly, Qiao Mengrong merely nodded at the report and sank into bitter memory. Brother had once held her hand and sworn never to betray her devotion—yet he had betrayed her all the same. Better she had never prayed for his recovery! Sick, even dying, at least his heart had belonged wholly to her. Now he was cold to her and held another woman inside, shattering every dream she had cherished since childhood.
The longer she brooded, the sharper her hatred grew—hatred for the woman who had stolen her brother's heart. Track her down, and she would drive the interloper straight out of his life!
#CH 182
#TITLE: A Teahouse Meeting at the Hour of the Rooster
With Bai Feng busy getting acquainted with the piglet and plotting how to train it to climb trees, Shen Jianchen went to Yongtai Teahouse alone.
Stepping inside for the very first time, he saw how different it was from ordinary tea shops: refined antique decor, walls hung with calligraphy and painting, and lovely tea mistresses whisking tea with flowing grace. Patrons sipped tea while admiring scrolls, watched tea performances, or spoke in hushed clusters—every face looking scholarly and cultivated.
A waiter stepped up. "Are you taking tea alone, sir, or meeting someone?"
"Youqi Jushi," Shen Jianchen answered.
"Right upstairs to the second-floor private room, sir—the plaque reads *Could Such Feeling Wait to Become a Memory*."
Climbing the stairs, Shen Jianchen found the plaque and pushed the door open. The woman on his mind sat waiting at the table; spotting him, she rose and curtsied. "Greetings, Your Highness Prince Yi."
"You know who I am—why act so distant?" Shen Jianchen frowned.
Hardly had the words left his lips when Qianli Xun shot a fist straight at him. Shen Jianchen caught it on reflex. After trading several crisp exchanges, he blocked her wrist. "Can we talk like normal people instead of swinging fists the second we meet?"
"I am giving Mengrong's brother a lesson on her behalf!" Qianli Xun threw another punch.
Blocking two more blows, Shen Jianchen glanced at Kuqiao standing beside them. "What are you standing there like a post for? Your mistress and I need to talk—step outside!"
Curtsying, Kuqiao stepped out and pulled the door shut. She did not worry over their sparring: Miss was a strong fighter, and Prince Yi loved her too much to hurt a hair on her head.
When Qianli Xun raised her fists again, Shen Jianchen stayed her. "Call a truce. Let us sit down and talk this out."
Lowering her hands, Qianli Xun returned to the table. Like it or not, he wore Prince Yi's rank, so she left the seat of honor open. Watching him swagger straight into the host's seat drew a half-scoff, half-chuckle from her. "Sure know how to put on princely airs."
Sitting down and meeting her eyes, Shen Jianchen smiled back. "You strut around playing the great literary talent—cannot I put on a few princely airs?"
Something in his gaze pulled Qianli Xun back to the earthquake: him charging through falling rubble straight toward her without a thought for his own life, the exact second she grasped how deep this man's love ran—deep enough to lay down his life. Her nose stung, and moisture pooled in her eyes.
"What is wrong? Why do you look ready to cry?" Shen Jianchen asked in surprise.
Forcing a bright smile to beat back the tears, she murmured, "During the earthquake, running straight toward me through all that danger—how could you be that dumb, getting yourself..."
"Turned out great, did it not? We landed in the same dynasty and found each other again. Proof you can never slip out of my palm."
Unwilling to bark him down like before, Qianli Xun spoke in earnest. "Have you ever considered that fate only wrote us down as friends?"
"In my heart, you will only ever be mine."
"Hmph. Full of yourself."
"Not full of myself—dead serious."
His steady gaze stirred her chest; forcing thoughts of Mengrong back to the front, she steadied her voice. "I called you here today to make my heart plain. Before we transmigrated, I kept turning you down, and only when the earthquake hit did I regret never accepting you..."
"About time you saw how good I am."
"When I first arrived in this world, I missed you every single day, thinking I would never see you again. Starved for love, I met Wenyuan—so gentle, so wonderful—and fell for him before I knew it. Then you showed up with a betrothed little sister by your side. Then the Zhu and Chen families sealed my engagement, and ONLY THEN did you tell me you were Shen Jianchen..."
"What is the point of rehearsing all that?" Shen Jianchen cut in. "I already know every word."
"The point is for you to see how many walls block us from ever being together! Do you know what that proves?"
Afraid of the answer, Shen Jianchen stayed mute.
