"It proves we were never meant to end up together!" she answered herself. "Kill the hope right now and stop chasing illusions. If I could move on and love Wenyuan, why can you not love Mengrong?"
Cherry-picking only the pieces he wanted to hear, Shen Jianchen smiled. "Now I understand your heart at last! You DID fall for me—you only turned to Wenyuan thinking you would never find me again! And now you tell me to let go only because of our two betrothals—right?"
Qianli Xun almost had to hand it to him: grinning through impossible odds like that stripped away all her armor, softening her voice despite herself. "Without Mengrong—and without the lingering ache for Wenyuan—I would have said yes to you. You are the very first man willing to trade his life for mine, and my heart truly moved for you. Yet the walls between us cannot be crossed. Let it go, and let us be friends for life."
Unused to hearing her speak so gently, Shen Jianchen felt his heart melt soft. "Who says they cannot be crossed? Just say you will be with me, and leave the rest to me."
After all that breath, he had not budged an inch! Hardening her tone again, Qianli Xun snapped, "I told you before—when pigs climb trees, I will marry you! Did you forget?!"
Unable to keep his secret in, Shen Jianchen let it slip. "Not a word! I already had Bai Feng buy a piglet and start training it to climb trees. Once it makes it up, no backing out!"
"Are you sick in the head?! Could you not tell I was brushing you off?!"
"I do not care—I am stripping away every excuse you have to say no."
Just then, the door swung open and a tea mistress stepped in. "Youqi Jushi, I am here to prepare tea for you and this young gentleman."
Their bickering paused at once. "Much obliged, Tea Mistress."
Behind her entered a male qin player. Taking seats on the floor—she by the tea stand, he behind his zither—they began in unison: the crisp notes of the qin wove around the graceful sweep of her wrists as she prepared the tea. Dropping their standoff, both Qianli Xun and Shen Jianchen surrendered to the music and art before them.
Kuqiao slipped inside to stand behind her mistress, eager to steal pointers from a master. Every step matched Kuqiao's own routine—grinding tea powder, spooning it in, pouring hot water, whisking and whisking again—yet the tea mistress turned every motion into pure poetry, topping it off by painting a delicate scene across the creamy froth, a feat Kuqiao knew she could never match.
Right on the final chord of the qin, the tea was complete. An attendant set the two bowls before the guests; tea mistress and musician bowed and withdrew, and tactful Kuqiao stepped right back outside and closed the door.
"Sharp maid you have got there," Shen Jianchen remarked.
Lifting her bowl, Qianli Xun took a sip without hesitating over spoiling the tea painting like she had on her first visit. "Look who her mistress is."
Glancing down at his own bowl—where the foam depicted a faint range of distant hills—Shen Jianchen paid the artwork little mind and lifted his eyes. "I know you came today to lecture me for Mengrong's sake. My mind is locked in: nothing you say will sway me."
A tremor ran through Qianli Xun. What girl on earth could stay untouched when a prince pursued her with everything he had—especially when that prince was Shen Jianchen? Taking another sip to hide her face, she realized a truth she had been dodging: since learning Prince Yi was Shen Jianchen, he had crept back into her thoughts day after day. At first she had blamed rebound heartbreak over Wenyuan; now she saw that as Wenyuan faded, she had fallen for Shen Jianchen all over again. Was she fickle? No—Wenyuan was already married; she had every right to love again. Could she swallow her pride and be his concubine? In the face of love, part of her actually wavered—until Mengrong's tear-streaked face snapped her cold sober. If Mengrong ever discovered her dearest friend had stolen her brother, she would never forgive her, and Qianli Xun could never forgive herself either. Sharing a husband with her best friend and watching sisterhood rot into venom was a nightmare she refused to live.
Draining the bowl in one gulp, she set it down hard. "I have said my piece. Listen or not, your call—do whatever you please, I am done meddling, and I will never play along." Rising to the doorway, she glanced back with a dry scoff. "Heh—getting a pig up a tree? Wait for your next life! No, not even then!"
Pushing the door open, she walked straight out without looking back.
Far from angry, Shen Jianchen knew Mengrong was the sole wall holding Qianli Xun back. While Mengrong stood between them, they had no path forward. Lifting his tea bowl like a cup of hard liquor, he swallowed the distant hills in one big gulp.
Down on the street, the servant Xilan had bribed had trailed Prince Yi's carriage to West Lake and shadowed him on foot to Yongtai Teahouse. Hiding across the lane to watch the entrance, he saw Youqi Jushi exit with her maid about an hour later—followed moments after by Prince Yi himself.
"Wait a second," the servant muttered under his breath. "Was Prince Yi meeting Youqi Jushi in there? Oh boy, what a fireworks show!" Waiting until twilight fell and Prince Yi had ridden off, he hopped back into his carriage and hurried to Youlin Retreat.
Inside his own rolling carriage, Shen Jianchen wrestled with how to untangle his betrothal to Mengrong. Regretting telling her last night that he would still marry her, he decided to go back, convince her to accept he was not Zhao Pu and let go of marrying him, and then ride to Bianliang to ask His Majesty face-to-face to rescind the marriage decree.
#CH 183
#TITLE: One Step Short of Discovering Her Best Friend
Pulling up outside Youlin Retreat, Shen Jianchen spotted Lin Jue speaking with Butler Li at the gate. Already dark out—what brought Lin Jue knocking at this hour?
Hearing carriage wheels, Butler Li looked over. "What timing—His Highness is just back."
Stepping up to the carriage, Lin Jue bowed low. "Your Highness Prince Yi, this humble official has an urgent matter to report."
Sensitive business did not belong outside the gate. Alighting, Shen Jianchen ushered Lin Jue into his main courtyard chamber. Too respectful to sit—and treating the report with gravity—Lin Jue remained standing.
Setting his personal turmoil aside for the moment, Shen Jianchen asked, "What urgent matter? Speak freely, Division Commander Lin."
"This humble official has discovered Jurchens operating inside Lin'an lately."
"Jurchens? How did you learn this?" Shen Jianchen sharpened to full alert at once; if Lin Jue came in person, these were no ordinary Jurchen traders.
"Since last year, after the Jurchens seized the Sixteen Prefectures of Yanyun from the Liao, they offered to return only seven—Yan, Ji, Tan, Jing, Shun, Zhuo, and Yi—while demanding a million strings of cash from the Song as replacement tax for Yanjing AND stripping away the entire local population! Such shameless terms! I ordered my men to keep close tabs on foreign merchants, especially Jurchens. Sure enough, a band of six or seven Jurchens entered Lin'an—no merchants at all. They gallop horses through crowded markets, terrorizing citizens. On the night of the Qiao wedding, a group of Jurchens even raided the Shao Escort Agency, throwing the whole compound into chaos. My patrol discovered it while making rounds—yet oddly, the Shao family refused yamen involvement."
Shen Jianchen commended him first. "Your patriotism does you credit, Division Commander Lin. Shame that while you and I care for the empire, His Majesty keeps giving ground—feeding Jurchen greed without end! I hear the Jurchens just jacked up the price again, demanding two hundred thousand taels of silver in army provisions to return the Western Capital, and His Majesty paid without blinking. All His Majesty cares about is the hollow glory of 'recovering Yanyun.' Keep watching that Jurchen crew, and I will investigate them quietly from my end."
Flushed with pride at Prince Yi's praise, Lin Jue held his tongue over the prince's sharp criticism of His Majesty—though in his own chest, he too resented how softly the court coddled the Jurchens, fearing disaster down the road.
Not until after Lin Jue took his leave did the servant who had shadowed Prince Yi return to Youlin Retreat.
Inside her room, Qiao Mengrong had been pacing on pins and needles since her brother's cold departure, spinning darker and darker scenarios until dusk fell and Xilan brought word he was home. She had started out to confront him, only to pause when Xilan reported Division Commander Lin was inside with him. Just as she rose to go over, the tailing servant returned. Sitting back down, she demanded at once, "What did you see? Where did Brother go?"
Dropping to one knee, the servant reported, "I tailed His Highness straight to Yongtai Teahouse by West Lake..."
Yongtai Teahouse—at least it was a scholars' haunt, not a brothel! Wait: did that mean Brother's beloved was a literary beauty? Her chest tightened right back up. "What else did you see?"
"I hid outside watching the door. Right around dusk, I saw Youqi Jushi walk out—and moments later, His Highness walked out too. Your Highness, could Prince Yi have been taking tea with Youqi Jushi?" Head bowed low, the servant dared not look up at her darkening face.
Hearing Shuzhen had been at the teahouse—leaving right before her brother—sent a storm surging through Qiao Mengrong's chest. Could the woman Brother loved be Shuzhen?!
Seeing her mistress frozen mute, Xilan prompted, "See anything else?"
"That is all I saw."
"Dismissed." Once the servant exited, Xilan murmured with caution, "Your Highness... could it really be Youqi Jushi?"
Replaying every moment Shuzhen and her brother had shared in front of her, Qiao Mengrong clung to doubt and grabbed at a lifeline. "Maybe Shuzhen just happened to be at Yongtai Teahouse! Or even if they arranged to meet, maybe she went to talk sense into Brother on my behalf! I went crying to her yesterday, and Shuzhen is so loyal to her friends—she must have been trying to persuade him for me!"
"Put like that, it does make sense," Xilan agreed.
Unable to sit still another second, Qiao Mengrong rose and hurried toward the main courtyard to ask her brother straight to his face.
Stepping through the gate, she saw him practicing fist forms in the lamplit yard—lithe, crisp, brimming with power. Deep down, she already knew he was not her old brother—aside from the body, nothing matched—yet admitting her old brother was gone forever left her whole heart's devotion with nowhere to land.
"Bro... Brother..."
Hearing the tremor in her voice, Shen Jianchen halted his stance and turned. In the lantern light, her pale, tear-filled eyes gazed straight at him with raw pleading. Unable to bear meeting them, he turned his face away. "It is late, Mengrong. Get some rest."
Stepping close, Qiao Mengrong nearly reached out to hug him, remembered his cold warning earlier, and pulled her hands back. "Did Brother just come back from Yongtai Teahouse?"
Startled—so she had put a tail on him—Shen Jianchen saw no point denying it. "Yes."
"To meet Shuzhen?" she pressed, voice taut with dread.
"Yes. I went to meet Youqi Jushi."
He said it straight out and met her eyes; tears spilled down her cheeks as her lips trembled. "Could it be... could the woman Brother loves be... Shuzhen?"
Part of him longed to say yes, yet with their marriage decree unresolved, he kept Qianli Xun shielded. "Since you went to see her, you can guess why she called me out—only to lecture me on your behalf. Shame her powers of persuasion need work; my mind has not changed."
A rush of relief loosened the vice around Qiao Mengrong's heart. Dabbing her cheeks with her handkerchief, she steadied her voice. "I have thought it through, Brother. I accept that you have someone else in your heart. Once we marry, bring her into the manor—I will live in harmony with her and run your inner quarters well. Tell me who she is now so I can learn her tastes and get on good terms ahead of time."
Any ordinary man would have cheered at such generosity—yet this was Shen Jianchen, whose heart and side had room for Qianli Xun alone. "Mengrong, I see you as my own little sister, and I want you to see me only as your brother. Where in this world does a brother marry his sister? Can you let go of our betrothal?"
Even after she bent this low, he still hid the woman's name—and dared ask her to give up their betrothal! Pain curdled into bitter hatred, and strangely, hatred brought ice-cold calm. "I fear I cannot grant Brother's wish. His Majesty decreed our marriage—how could anyone cast it aside?"
"If you agree to let go, I will ride to Bianliang and beg His Majesty face-to-face to cancel the decree."
Last year, the morning after Shuzhen stayed overnight at Youlin Retreat, Brother had promised to marry her; just two days ago he had agreed to honor the decree—and now, straight after Yongtai Teahouse, he wanted to cancel it outright?! Whoever this faceless rival was, she had Brother wrapped tight around her finger! Mengrong swore she would dig three feet under the earth to drag her into the light.
"Canceling an ordinary betrothal is hard enough, let alone an imperial decree," she answered flatly. "Wake up, Brother, and stop wasting your breath."
Seeing her calm face despite the moisture in her eyes, Shen Jianchen mistook it for resignation and hoped she was coming around to staying brother and sister. Reaching out, he clasped her hands warmly like an elder brother. "Just make peace with staying brother and sister, Mengrong, and leave the rest to me. I will cherish, protect, and care for you like a true brother."
Warmth radiated from his broad palms, yet she wanted a lover, not a brother. Drawing her hands free, she dropped a crisp curtsy. "I am tired and going back to rest."
Without waiting for a reply, she strode swiftly out of the courtyard, reached her chambers, and threw herself sobbing onto her bed.
Xilan stood helpless—with His Highness talking about canceling the imperial betrothal itself, what comfort remained? How heartless a man could be! When he lay sick, he leaned on Her Highness and promised the moon; the second he got well, he tossed every vow aside with that ridiculous tale about not being the old Prince Yi—who on earth would buy that?!
Once her tears ran dry, Qiao Mengrong wiped her face and sat ramrod straight. "You saw and heard with your own eyes today, Xilan—Brother betrayed me first. From here on, whatever I do is justified. Am I right?"
"Of course," Xilan nodded.
"Good. Two tasks lie ahead of me now: first, wait for word advancing our wedding date; second, find out who holds Brother's heart—even if I have to dig three feet underground! I will confront her face-to-face and drive her clear of Brother!"
"Understood. I will see it arranged."
Meanwhile, back at Wangyue Pavilion after Yongtai Teahouse, Qianli Xun replayed her talk with Shen Jianchen and judged she had left no loopholes. Done meddling in Mengrong's love life—the more she tangled in it, the worse it knotted. Time to dive back into her huaben—starting with that field trip to a gambling house tonight!
"Kuqiao, fetch Hongdou and pull out my men's clothes."
"Going to a gambling den right now, Miss? Isn't it too late?"
"Nighttime is when the real action starts!"
Fetching the disguises and calling Hongdou in, Kuqiao soon watched the pair emerge looking like two fresh-faced young dandies.
"Bring a pouch of silver, Hongdou," Qianli Xun instructed. "Your mistress knows zero about gambling—we will head home the moment we lose the whole bag."
"Got it!" Hongdou stowed the silver.
After seeing the pair into their carriage, Kuqiao sighed aloud at the gate. "First fighting an engagement, then writing huaben, then lecturing Prince Yi, now off to a gambling den! Miss never sits still a minute! If writing huaben is this much fuss, why not stick to poetry—instead of only tossing off verses right before Huayu shows up?"
"Muttering to yourself again?" Matron Wei's voice floated from behind.
Spinning around with a sheepish grin, Kuqiao answered, "Nothing, Matron! What brings you out?"
"Where is Miss sneaking off to at this hour?"
"A gambling house," Kuqiao confessed.
"A young lady going to a place like that?" Matron Wei frowned.
"Not to gamble for real! She is writing stories that need a gambling scene, does not know how they run, so she went for 'field research.'"
"Always an excuse with you. Think I did not already figure out Miss writes huaben on the sly—and that the famed writer Qianli Xun the whole city raves about is probably our young mistress?"
"Hush, Matron! Miss does not want a soul knowing!"
"Nobody here but you and me—what is there to fear?"
Scanning the empty courtyard before looping an arm through Matron Wei's, Kuqiao marveled in a whisper as they walked inside, "Nothing ever slips past your eyes! Xiaguo worked right in Miss's room and never guessed, yet you barely step inside and know everything—uncanny!"
Matron Wei only chuckled all the way down the walkway.
#CH 184
#TITLE: Running into Yan Chi Outside the Gambling House
Arriving at the gambling house, Qianli Xun sent the carriage home rather than making the driver wait, then stepped inside with Hongdou. The cavernous hall buzzed with packed gaming tables and shouting bettors.
An attendant hurried over. "Two new faces—first time here, young masters?"
Pitching her voice low, Qianli Xun barked, "Less nosiness, more rundown! Tell this young master what games you run so I can pick one."
"Plenty of choices: ball wagers, chess wagers, dice, pai gow, cockfighting, cricket fighting, and guanpu! What strikes your fancy?"
"Ball wagers? What balls?" Qianli Xun asked.
"Cuju, polo, chuiwan—bet on any of them."
Glancing around the crowded hall, she asked, "You can hardly play cuju or polo in here—how do you bet?"
"Not right here, of course—want to wager, and someone escorts you straight to the pitch."
"And what is guanpu?" Here to learn, she asked freely.
Spotting a greenhorn who had never even heard of guanpu—a lamb begging to be sheared—the attendant actually warned her: "Young master, if you do not even know guanpu, better head home before you lose your trousers."
"Since when does a shop chase paying customers out? Quit stalling and explain guanpu!"
Before the attendant could speak, Hongdou chimed in. "Young Master, I know how guanpu works."
Qianli Xun grinned at the attendant. "Zip it—let my boy here explain."
The attendant sniffed. "Hmph, I already know what he is going to say."
Hongdou explained clearly: "You see it all the time on market streets—peddlers selling trinkets let you pay cash OR agree on a price and terms, then shake copper coins in a bowl. If the vendor wins, he keeps your silver; if the buyer wins, you walk off with the goods for free."
"Aha, so that is guanpu!" Qianli Xun glanced back at the attendant. "Don't tell me your gambling house hawks trinkets too?"
With no new patrons walking in, the attendant explained with patience. "Your lad only knows street-side guanpu. Inside our hall, we play guanpu for real stakes—people, houses, fields, shops, even whole family fortunes! Some walk out overnight tycoons, and others lose the shirt off their backs in one throw."
Note to self: steer clear of guanpu! Spotting a lively dice table nearby—easy rules—she ditched the attendant and squeezed up to the rail with Hongdou. A dealer rattled dice inside a wooden box while players bet high or low. Having Hongdou drop silver a few rounds, Qianli Xun bet high when the dice came up low and low when they came up high—zero gambler's luck in her bones.
Next they watched pai gow for a spell, yet the tiles looked too fussy to crack on the fly, so she waved the attendant over again. "Where are the cricket fights?"
"Follow me, young masters."
Pushing open a rear door, the attendant led them into a brightly lit courtyard packed with bettors clustered around cricket bowls and cockfighting pits. Thanking him, they squeezed into the cricket ring, watched two crickets lock jaws in an earthenware bowl, dropped another bet—and lost again.
Working free of the crowd to check out the cockfights, Qianli Xun froze: Chen Haoyu stood right across the pit cheering with his lackeys! Her interest died on the spot, along with any desire to be recognized. She already had more than enough material for her huaben anyway. "Time to head home."
Stepping out into the deeper night, they set off on foot; without curfew in the Song capital and prefectures, Lin'an's night markets glittered with life. Yet Qianli Xun paid zero mind to snacks or trinkets. Seeing Chen Haoyu had dragged back thoughts of the betrothal—which bounced straight to Shen Jianchen. The guy really was nuts, training a pig to climb a tree! What if it actually climbed one—would she have to marry him?
With a jolt, she noticed Wenyuan barely crossed her mind anymore, the old heartbreak fading bit by bit. In her heart she thanked Shen Jianchen: impossible as their future was, his fearless dash through the earthquake and stubborn devotion here had slipped quietly into her chest like medicine soothing an old wound. Reason told her thinking of Prince Yi was wrong, yet sneaking a quiet memory hurt Mengrong none. Letting her guard drop, she drifted through memories of him, a faint, unreadable smile playing across her lips.
Walking beside her, Hongdou watched her mistress ignore every lantern and stall as if wandering another world, smiling at thin air.
A harsh shout ripped Qianli Xun back to the street: "He ran that way! After him!"
Through the lamplit crowd, she saw a limping figure clutch a wound and duck into a side alley, shooting a panicked glance over his shoulder right at the mouth of the lane. Wait a second—that face! The "Delayed" guy from the market! So much for all his cocky swagger—running scared now! Moments after Yan Chi vanished into the dark, seven or eight men brandishing sharp blades charged past, led by a square-jawed young man with thick brows.
Curiosity winning out, Qianli Xun nudged Hongdou. "Let us tail them for a look."
Silently shadowing the blades into the pitch-black alley, they watched the pursuers wind through twist after twist until the quarry vanished clean away.
"Let him off easy tonight!" the leader barked. "Come near Shao Escort Agency again and he will never walk out alive! Pull back!"
Ducking into shadow while the Shao men marched back out, Qianli Xun waited for quiet before turning to retrace their steps—only to hit dead end after twisting fork in the dark.
"Hongdou, your mistress has zero sense of direction—don't tell me you do too?"
"I do not know this maze either, Miss. Kept my eyes glued on those men and forgot the turns."
Qianli Xun sighed and pointed at random. "Try down there."
Halfway down the next pitch-black turn, a hand shot out and grabbed her ankle! Any other lady would have screamed bloody murder; bold by nature and sharp with her fists, Qianli Xun whipped around, judged the target by feel, and fired a punch straight down.
The grip dropped instantly, followed by a groan: "Ow! So... it is you... what was it again? Right—Zhu... Shuzhen!"
Someone in the pitch dark knew her name—and recognized her fist?! "Hongdou, strike your fire-starter so I can see who this is!"
Blowing a flame alive on the tinder tube, Hongdou handed it over. Crouching down, Qianli Xun held the glow to a pale face—the "Delayed" guy himself! "Well, well—pretty slick dodging that mob."
"I took... a bad cut..." Yan Chi panted. "For old acquaintance's sake... give me... a hand?"
"Hmph! For all I know you are a crook—why should I help you?"
Reaching inside his robe, Yan Chi held up a beaded hair-pin. "Look... I still... kept this..."
Qianli Xun blinked. "Just a plain man's hair-pin—what is that to me?"
"The first time... we met... you dropped it... I kept it safe..."
Flashback to their street brawl when her disguised hair had come undone and lost that pin—she had forgotten it on the spot, yet this stranger had hoarded it?! Annoyed, she snatched it right back. "Since it is mine, no reason for you to hold onto it!" Tossing it to Hongdou, she added, "Yours now."
Seeing her mistress wanted zero strange men harboring notions, Hongdou pocketed it crisply. "Thank you, Miss!"
"Fend for yourself!" Blowing out the flame, Qianli Xun rose to leave.
"Never thought... you would leave a man to bleed out..." Yan Chi wheezed. "Guess helping folks on the street that day... was all just fake kindness..."
The barb hit square on Qianli Xun's pride. "Since you are playing the guilt card, fine—I will drop you at a clinic!"
Just as she turned to have Hongdou hoist Yan Chi up, a wooden courtyard door creaked open right beside them. Spilling lamplight lit up the man stepping out—the martial master who had dueled her in the bamboo grove! Jackpot! Dump the headache straight onto him!
"Disciple! Your master stands right here—why aren't you kneeling?!"
Hongdou stared in bewilderment at her mistress's sudden line, while Yan Chi groaned, "Are you... helping me... or not?"
Shutting his gate and squinting into the dark, Lu Yuanfeng called out, "Talking to me?"
"Back in the bamboo grove, you dropped to your knees begging me to be your master—forgot already?" Qianli Xun teased.
Recognition lit up Lu Yuanfeng's face as he hurried forward. "Master?! Really you?!"
"In the flesh—just wearing men's clothes for getting around. That your house?"
"Yes! Care to come inside for a seat, Master?"
"Pass!" No time for tea with a bleeding man at her feet. "Back then I had not accepted you yet. Since fate threw us together tonight, I will officially take you as my disciple..."
Lu Yuanfeng dropped straight to his knees in excitement. "Master above, accept your disciple's bow!"
Pulling him up, Qianli Xun grinned. "At ease, at ease. Since you are officially my disciple, you get a welcome gift—a bit of a hassle, though. Willing to accept?"
"When Master gives the word, hassle or not, I take it!" Lu Yuanfeng declared heartily.
"There is an injured man right here. Carry him inside your house and fetch a doctor to patch him up."
Agreeing without hesitation, Lu Yuanfeng paused to ask, "Master, may I lay him inside first, run a quick errand, and fetch the doctor right after?"
"Got an errand?"
"Young Master Chen hired me as his bodyguard. He went to the gambling house tonight and expects me there. I was just heading out when I bumped into Master."
"Chen Haoyu?"
"Yes."
"Skip it. I just walked out of that gambling house and saw him—healthy as a horse."
"I took his silver, though—bad form not to show."
"Do you listen to your master, or to Chen Haoyu?"
"Master, of course—still got to earn my pay."
"Listen to Master and ditch Chen Haoyu. If he gripes you pocketed silver for nothing, tell him to take it up with me!"
"With Master's backing, I will pocket his silver with a clear conscience!"
Thrilled to offload her problem so cleanly, Qianli Xun nudged the wounded man. "Hey, hear that? My disciple will patch you up."
"My name is not 'Hey'..." Yan Chi muttered. "It is... Yan Chi."
"'Delayed,' yeah, everybody knows—never on time!"
"It is 'Yan' (颜, as in color) and 'Chi' (池, as in pond)... got it straight, Miss?"
Ignoring him, Qianli Xun told Lu Yuanfeng, "Haul him inside, Disciple, then lead your master out of this twisting maze of an alley."
"Right away!"
Carrying Yan Chi indoors, Lu Yuanfeng came right back out, guided Qianli Xun and Hongdou back onto the main thoroughfare, and bade farewell to fetch a physician. With the hour late, Qianli Xun hailed a hired carriage straight back to Wangyue Pavilion.
#CH 185
#TITLE: A Rigged Wager to Force a Wedding
Back in the gambling house's rear yard, Chen Haoyu had won a few cockfights and lost a few, grumbling over Lu Yuanfeng's no-show. Nervous about bumping into Master Jiang unprotected, he called it a night. "Keep playing, boys—I am beat and heading home."
Scarface stepped in front of him. "Barely been here a spell, Young Master Chen—leaving so soon spoils the fun!"
"Move it! Quit blocking my way!" Pushing Scarface aside, Chen Haoyu stepped back into the main hall.
An attendant stepped up. "Pai gow or dice next, Young Master Chen?"
"Done for the night. Heading home."
Before he reached the front doors, they swung wide—talk about bad luck! Master Jiang strode in flanked by his gang.
"Leaving so early, Young Master Chen?"
With no Lu Yuanfeng in sight, Chen Haoyu forced a smooth grin. "Master Jiang arrived late—I have business and must run."
Two burly thugs stepped forward to block the exit, glaring murder.
Keeping his grin pinned on, Chen Haoyu said, "Tell your boys to step aside, Master Jiang—do not block my road."
With a wave, Master Jiang parted them—only to block Chen Haoyu himself. "Let us settle things between us once and for all tonight, Chen Haoyu. Deal?"
Playing dumb, Chen Haoyu blinked. "Settle what?"
"Short memory for a rich young master! Let me jog it: I still cannot get Chun Jinmo out of my head. Put her on the table tonight—you win, I steer clear of Chun Jinmo forever; you lose, she is mine and you never lay a finger on her again."
Fair enough, Chen Haoyu thought—he was sick of dodging Master Jiang too. "Deal! Only no betting Chun Jinmo herself—she is a living person, not chips."
Walking straight into Master Jiang's script! "Spoken like a man of honor! Fine—we leave Chun Jinmo off the slip. Still, given how high she ranks in my heart, whatever replaces her cannot be cheap, or no deal."
"Name your stake, Master Jiang!" Chen Haoyu agreed breezily.
At Master Jiang's flick of a wrist, his gang slammed the gambling house doors shut and blocked every exit. Gamblers dropped their dice and craned their necks for the show.
"Let me name the stake, and do not cry foul later," Master Jiang warned.
Just silver—the Chens swam in silver! "Spit it out already!"
"Bold! I like it! Then hear my terms:" Grinning as the trap snapped shut, Master Jiang declared, "Wager the Chen family fortune—and I swear off Chun Jinmo forever!"
Chen Haoyu's lackeys huddled close, one whispering, "Master Jiang's appetite is insane—even if he won the richest house in Lin'an, could he swallow it? Young Master Chen will never agree!"
"Just watch," Scarface murmured back. "The good part is coming."
Sure enough, Chen Haoyu barked, "No way! How could I bet the whole Chen family fortune?! Pick something else!"
Master Jiang flicked his eyes; two thugs grabbed Chen Haoyu on the spot, and one pressed a gleaming broadsword flat against his neck.
Pulling his chin clear of the cold steel, Chen Haoyu yelled, "Talk like civilized men—put the blade away! When my bodyguard gets here, you will regret this!"
Master Jiang pointed at the house attendant. "Go hang the 'Closed' sign outside. When his bodyguard sees it, he will turn straight around. Charge tonight's lost business to Young Master Chen's tab!"
"Yes, Proprietor!" the attendant obeyed.
Only now did it hit Chen Haoyu that this whole gambling house belonged to the Qiantang Gang—cursed himself for playing blind without checking who owned the roof!
Once the attendant returned reporting the sign hung, Master Jiang called to the room, "Keep playing, folks! Just a private matter between Young Master Chen and me—do not let us spoil your evening." Nobody went back to their tables; watching a rich heir get squeezed beat dice hands down.
Stepping right into Chen Haoyu's face, Master Jiang murmured, "The Chen fortune, or Chun Jinmo—pick one."
Chun Jinmo was nowhere near worth the Chen family empire, yet she was the one woman who suited him, handing his own girl over to another man would humiliate him across Lin'an, and Chun Jinmo had sworn she would smash her head against a wall rather than fall into Master Jiang's clutches.
"Neither!" Chen Haoyu snapped.
The broadsword nudged closer, slicing a thin red line across his skin.
Stinging pain flashed across his neck as his mind raced in panic.
"Unwilling to bet the Chen fortune?" Master Jiang cooed. "Then stick with Chun Jinmo—I win, and you never visit her again. Right enough—what is one brothel girl next to a family empire?"
Poor Chun Jinmo had no one to protect her—bet her and lose, and Master Jiang would break her. Meanwhile the Chen estate was so vast even Chen Haoyu could not tally it all; could a street boss actually collect with all the Chen elders standing guard? And besides—who said he would lose?!
Gritting his teeth, Chen Haoyu blurted, "I agree!"
"Agree to which?"
"Wager... wager the Chen family fortune!"
Gasps and whistles rippled across the hall. "True prodigal son!" someone muttered.
Exhaling in quiet triumph—bull's-eye!—Master Jiang clapped. "Willing to stake a whole empire for a woman! You opened my eyes today, Young Master Chen. Everyone here, stand witness!"
Regret slammed into Chen Haoyu two seconds later, far too late to swallow the words back.
Escorted across the gaming table from Master Jiang while the blade-wielding guard stood right behind his chair, Chen Haoyu stared as an attendant laid two pre-written contracts before him alongside brush and red ink paste. "Sign your name and press your thumbprint, Young Master Chen."
Pre-written contracts?! He had walked blind into an ambush! Casting a frantic glance at his lackeys for help, he saw Scarface and the rest shaking their heads and shrinking back like turtles. Useless fair-weather parasites! Boxed in by armed thugs inside a locked den, he had zero escape.
"Hold on—let us settle terms first!"
"What terms?" Master Jiang barked. "Written plain as day: you lose, Chen property is mine."
"I am adding one clause!"
"Speak!"
"If I win, you never bully me again—and whenever anyone messes with me, the Qiantang Gang steps in to back me up!"
Without blinking, Master Jiang nodded. "Done!"
Dipping the brush, Chen Haoyu signed both slips and stamped his red thumbprint.
"Ready to begin, Proprietor," the attendant announced as he collected the contracts.
"Pai gow or dice?" Chen Haoyu asked.
"Why waste all night? I prefer winning quick—guanpu, one throw takes all!"
"Guanpu it is—and you need nine or ten of a kind!" More coins meant slimmer odds for Master Jiang.
"Agreed!" Master Jiang answered without batting an eye. "If I throw nine or ten matching, you lose."
His readiness set off alarm bells. As the attendant set a small wooden box holding ten copper coins before Master Jiang, Chen Haoyu shouted, "Wait! Let me and everyone here inspect those coins for tampering!"
Master Jiang sneered. "Do not trust me? Fine—inspect away." Catching his boss's glance, the attendant deftly swapped boxes in one sleek sleight of hand before opening it in front of Chen Haoyu. "See for yourself, Young Master."
Stirring the coins with a finger—ten ordinary coins, heads and tails clean—Chen Haoyu breathed easier. "Start."
Master Jiang swept his eyes across the crowd and pointed straight at Scarface. "So Young Master Chen loses without a single gripe, let one of his own boys shake the box! You—step up and decide your master's fate!"
Thrilled—no way Master Jiang could rig his own lackey!—Chen Haoyu waved him over. "Get up here, Scarface!"
Grinning up to the table, Scarface asked, "If I fail to roll nine or ten matching, Young Master wins, right?"
"Damn right!"
"Then it is in the bag! I never even rolled five or six matching in my life—let alone nine or ten! Watch this!"
"Miss nine or ten, and I hand you ten taels of silver outside!" Chen Haoyu promised.
Easy silver! Scarface held out his hands. "Pass the box!"
Slipping the rigged box back in with seamless sleight of hand, the attendant placed it in Scarface's palms.
Gripping tight, Scarface rattled the box up, down, left, and right before popping the lid and dumping all ten coins across the table.
The entire hall gasped in one voice: "All ten matching!"
A roar echoed inside Chen Haoyu's skull; his knees nearly buckled.
"Scarface, are you helping Young Master or burying him?!" a lackey yelled.
"I shook it the exact loser's way I always do—who knew my luck would turn golden tonight?! Do not blame me, Young Master!" Scarface whined.
Dazed, Chen Haoyu muttered, "No way! Impossible! Let me check those coins again!"
Before his fingers touched the wood, the attendant swept all ten coins back into the box and handed it to Master Jiang.
Cradling the box in triumph, Master Jiang grinned. "Every eye here saw all ten matching! What excuse is left, Young Master Chen? Pay up like a man!"
"No! Shake again!" Panic sobered Chen Haoyu—he had just gambled away the entire Chen empire!
"One throw takes all—short memory again?" Master Jiang scoffed.
The attendant shoved one contract into Chen Haoyu's chest. "Keep your copy safe, Young Master."
Chen Haoyu gripped it ready to shred it to bits.
"I hold the twin copy—tearing yours changes nothing," Master Jiang warned. "Unless you want me marching my whole gang up to Chen Manor to collect, take it home and tell your elders everything the Chens own now belongs to Master Jiang!"
Stuck neither tearing nor folding, Chen Haoyu crushed it into a ball and jammed it inside his robe. "Hmph! Think grabbing Chen property is that easy?!" Turning toward the door, he raced to get home and confess to his parents—they would find a way out!
At the threshold, Master Jiang called out, "Hold on, Young Master Chen—one last word."
Halting without turning around, Chen Haoyu snapped, "What now?"
Stepping beside him, Master Jiang lowered his voice. "Truth be told, I have no appetite for Chen property—just wanted to knock that cocky grin off your face. Go tell your family: marry Miss Zhu, and I will not touch a single coin of Chen wealth."
A glimmer of air hit Chen Haoyu's lungs. Spinning around, he stared. "All this setup—just to force me to marry Miss Zhu?!"
"Sharp lad! I got a firsthand taste of Miss Zhu's fists. Once you marry her, you will never sneak near Chunxiang Brothel again—leaving Chun Jinmo free and clear for me!" Master Jiang tapped his chest. "You have three days to wed Miss Zhu. Miss the deadline, and I take this contract straight to Eldest Master Chen."
All this terror—just over Chun Jinmo! Burning with rage yet cornered tight, Chen Haoyu ignored his cowering lackeys and bolted out the moment the guards unlatched the doors.
#CH 186
#TITLE: At Last Setting the Wedding Day
By the time Chen Haoyu stumbled back into Chen Manor, night had grown deep and his parents were long asleep, leaving no chance to confess tonight. Heading straight to his own courtyard, where Qinggua and the other servants had turned in too, he pushed into his pitch-dark room—his servants must have assumed he was staying out again—felt his way to the bed, and collapsed fully clothed.
Half an hour later, his racing head cleared. Sitting bolt upright in the dark, he muttered aloud, "Wait a minute! What am I panicking over?! Marry Zhu Shuzhen and that contract turns to scrap paper! Nobody in the family even needs to find out I signed it! Parents already want the wedding, only Grandmother stalled—and Grandmother listens to me! Tell everyone tomorrow I want to wed within three days, and problem solved!"
Relief flooding his chest, he flopped back down and pulled up the quilt with a smug chuckle. "Master Jiang, oh Master Jiang—you get neither Chen wealth nor Chun Jinmo! Tomorrow I go straight to Zhu Shuzhen and fix the wedding! Heh!" Feeling downright brilliant—and convinced Master Jiang was an idiot—he drifted right off.
At dawn, Qinggua cracked open his master's door, checked the outer room for dust—clean enough to skip maids—and started to pull it shut when a voice drifted from inside: "Zhu Shuzhen! Fine, I will marry you—each lives their own life...!"
Blinking in surprise—when had Young Master come home?—Qinggua tiptoed into the bedchamber and found him sprawled in rumpled clothes, quilt kicked aside, muttering in his sleep. Worried he might catch cold, Qinggua reached down to tug the quilt free from underneath him.
The tug jerked Chen Haoyu awake. Blinking sleep away and spotting Qinggua, he lashed out a kick. "Who told you to wake me?!"
Dodging clear, Qinggua protested, "I was only covering you up so you would not catch a chill!"
Sitting up, Chen Haoyu grumbled, "Ruined a good dream—no point sleeping now. Fetch fresh clothes!"
"Calling Miss Zhu's name in your sleep sounds more like a nightmare," Qinggua remarked while pulling out clean robes.
"Quit yapping and dress me!"
As Qinggua helped peel off his outer robe, a crumpled paper ball hit the floor. Qinggua reached down—only for Chen Haoyu to bark, "Hands off!"
Scooping it up himself, Chen Haoyu flattened it out, scanned the ink lines once more, and ordered, "Bring the fire-starter."
Blowing a flame alive, Qinggua handed it over. Chen Haoyu touched the tip to the contract and dropped it to the tiles, watching it curl into ash—once the wedding wrapped up, he would go rip the second copy out of Master Jiang's hands too.
Snuffing the flame, he tossed it back. "Standing around for what? Finish dressing me!"
After getting dressed and washing his face, Chen Haoyu tossed the damp cloth straight into Qinggua's arms. "Are Father and Mother up?"
"Up ages ago at this hour."
Without another word, Chen Haoyu strode straight out, ignoring Qinggua's call about breakfast, and marched to his parents' courtyard.
Inside, Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan sat sharing breakfast—eating, chatting, laughing warm and easy. Growing up, Chen Haoyu had almost never seen them cross a harsh word, filling him with both comfort and quiet envy; how sweet it would be if he could share a bond like that with a wife someday. Then the thought of marrying Zhu Shuzhen inside three days wiped away the warmth. "Father, Mother, I have something to say."
Noticing him walk in, Yao Qingyan asked, "Eaten yet?"
"Not yet."
She signaled a maid to lay another bowl and chopsticks. Taking his seat as the maid ladled hot cardamom broth for him, Chen Haoyu heard his father remark without looking up, "The bamboo-shoot and pork buns are good today—try one."
With zero appetite, Chen Haoyu blurted straight out, "Father, Mother—I want to marry Zhu Shuzhen within three days."
Both parents froze in stunned delight at their son's sudden flip.
Yao Qingyan laughed and chided him, "Look at you—first fighting tooth and nail to wreck the match, now rushing headlong into a wedding! Give your father and me a second to catch our breath!"
Setting down his chopsticks to look squarely at his son for the first time, Chen Jianglin asked, "Everything is already prepared, so three days is no rush at all. Still, explain to your father why the sudden change of heart."
Thinking fast, Chen Haoyu spun a tidy tale. "I have fooled around outside long enough—time to settle down. Watching Father and Mother so devoted made me envious; I want a wife of my own to live happily with too."
Hearing sensible words from their son for the first time ever filled the couple with joy.
"Three days it is!" Yao Qingyan declared. "Your father and I will send word to Zhu Manor right away. Your job is convincing Grandmother and sharing the good news with your two uncles—tell them we set up today and welcome Miss Zhu home tomorrow!"
Agreeing to everything, Chen Haoyu downed his cardamom broth in one gulp. "Going to Grandmother right now!"
Finishing breakfast herself, Old Madam Xia beamed at her grandson's early visit and patted the couch beside her. Taking her hand, Chen Haoyu laid on the charm thick. "Whenever I am out, Grandmother never leaves my thoughts for a second!"
"Sweet-talking me again!" Old Madam Xia chuckled. "If you missed Grandmother so much, would you still run off to brothels and gambling dens?"
"Nothing gets past Grandmother!"—must have been Qinggua snitching!
"Old, not blind! Stay clear of those filthy places from now on, hear me?"
"Loud and clear—I obey every word Grandmother teaches!" Mindful he still had to track down Zhu Shuzhen, he cut straight to the chase. "Grandmother, I have decided to marry Miss Zhu. Father and Mother already agreed on tomorrow, so let us drop the stalling plan."
The smile vanished off Old Madam Xia's face. "After the riot she raised at our gate, you STILL want to marry her?!"
"I do! Suddenly hit me—a fiery girl like Miss Zhu is my exact type!"
Suspicion narrowed Old Madam Xia's eyes. "Did someone force you into this? Tell Grandmother who, and I will deal with them!"
Bull's-eye! Covering fast, Chen Haoyu waved it off. "Who could force me? Stop worrying, Grandmother—I came around all on my own! Miss Zhu has beauty, brains, and fierce fists: with her by my side, she can coach my reading and poetry AND protect me! Pure win!"
"Yet her reputation is bruised," Old Madam Xia fretted. "Marry her, and the Chen elders will never hand the family business to you."
So that was her hang-up! Seizing the chance to speak his true mind, Chen Haoyu laughed. "Grandmother gives me way too much credit! Look at me—could I ever run the Chen empire? Brother Haodong is a natural merchant; pin no hopes on me! As for Miss Zhu's reputation, that was all my blunder—cannot blame her."
Softened by his candor, Old Madam Xia relented. "If you have no wish to inherit the business, Grandmother will not force you. I only stalled because you hated the match; if you want to wed her, why would I stand in your way? Marry her, then! Only—isn't tomorrow terribly rushed?"
"Not rushed at all! Father already had everything lined up; decorate the manor today and we are set for tomorrow!"
Still uneasy yet unable to cross her beloved grandson, Old Madam Xia nodded with a worried smile.
Next, Chen Haoyu stopped by his second and third uncles to spread the news. Knowing his own son Haodong had engineered the whole turn, Second Uncle Chen Jiangshu glowed inside while putting on a warm congratulatory smile. Third Uncle Chen Jiangmu was utterly baffled why Eldest Brother still welcomed such a fierce bride, yet offered polite blessings anyway.
By the time Chen Haoyu grabbed Qinggua to head for Wangyue Pavilion, servants were already scrambling across Chen Manor hanging lanterns and red silk. Still heavy-hearted despite the bustle, he needed to lock down ground rules with Zhu Shuzhen before tomorrow so he kept his freedom afterward.
Once inside the carriage, seeing his master's tight frown, Qinggua ventured, "Young Master, you were the one who asked to wed tomorrow—why the gloomy face?"
"What do you know?! Shut your trap and do not say another word!" Slumping back against the cushions, Chen Haoyu barked, "Tell the driver Wangyue Pavilion!"
"East quarter—Wangyue Pavilion!" Qinggua relayed, keeping silent the rest of the way as he watched his master sigh over and over. Never had he seen Young Master look this miserable—plain as day how little he wanted this wedding.
Reaching Wangyue Pavilion, Chen Haoyu amazed everyone by walking straight inside to wait in the reception hall. Hardly had he warmed a chair when Qianli Xun walked in with Kuqiao.
Chen Haoyu shot to his feet and gave a deep, crisp cupped-hand bow.
Both Qianli Xun and Qinggua gaped—when had this pampered rake ever bowed with manners?!
"What act is this, Chen Haoyu?" Qianli Xun asked. "Bowing to me? Now I have seen everything."
Straightening up, Chen Haoyu spoke earnestly. "Tomorrow you marry into the Chen family. I bow to beg you to go easy on me once you move in and stay off my back."
Blindsided, Qianli Xun flared up at once. "What?! Tomorrow?! My sister-in-law JUST told me yesterday it was postponed! Now it is tomorrow?! Does your whole Chen clan just make things up on a whim, playing me and the Zhus for fools?!"
"Breaking off the match is dead anyway," Chen Haoyu urged. "Sooner or later you have to marry me—might as well get it over with. I came today to work out—" Catching sight of Qinggua standing nearby, afraid he might run straight to his parents, he swung a foot at him. "Scram! Quit standing around eavesdropping!"
"Scramming right now!" Qinggua hopped clear and darted outside.
Once Qinggua exited, Chen Haoyu looked at Kuqiao. "Step outside too."
Still fuming, Qianli Xun snapped, "Now you think you can boss my people around?!"
Backing down fast, Chen Haoyu got to the point. "Since marrying is unavoidable, let us agree how to live afterward. My offer: you live your life, I live mine, zero interference. Deal?"
Too livid over the ambush wedding date to care about terms, Qianli Xun snapped, "Who put you in charge of wedding dates?! Zhu Manor has not sent a single word—why would I bargain with you?!"
Unwilling to humiliate himself by confessing last night's gambling fiasco, Chen Haoyu hung stuck—when a servant's call rang from outside: "Miss, Madam, Young Madam, and Eldest Young Master are here!"
#CH 187
#TITLE: At Last Agreeing to Marry into the Chen Family
Before Qianli Xun could step out to greet them, Matron Wei ushered her family inside. Spotting Chen Haoyu right there, Chen Wenxin teased at once, "Seeing Qinggua outside, I guessed you sneaked over to see Shuzhen! Could not wait till tomorrow—had to whisper sweet nothings to your bride today?"
"Quit teasing, Sis—I came to discuss other business with her," Chen Haoyu muttered.
Her sister-in-law's words confirmed the shocking news. Keeping her temper reined in, Qianli Xun stepped forward and took Madam Xiang's hands. "Mother, what brings you, Brother, and Sister-in-Law here?"
"Your wedding, of course!" Madam Xiang smiled. "Your father wants you riding out from Zhu Manor tomorrow, so we came to fetch you home."
"I am not going back."
"Quit acting stubborn, Shuzhen," Zhu Jingyuan urged. "You are a Zhu daughter—not riding out from Zhu Manor will make us a laughingstock."
"I do not want to marry into the Chen family."
"Want to or not, you have to," Chen Wenxin said firmly. "Both families have already dispatched invitations—within two hours every notable household in Lin'an will have one in hand. Father-in-law even invited Prince Yi! Would you let guests gather with no bride and shame Father-in-law in front of Prince Yi? Be good and come home with us."
At the mention of Prince Yi, Qianli Xun fell mute. She had already thought of this: marrying into the Chen family would give Mengrong her brother back.
Noting her mistress's silence—meaning her refusal was no longer rock solid—and seeing Young Master Chen clearly eager for a private word, Matron Wei stepped in smoothly. "Madam, Miss and Young Master Chen seem to have matters to discuss. Let me walk you around Wangyue Pavilion for a bit; once Miss thinks it through, she will come home to Zhu Manor."
Trusting Matron Wei as always, Madam Xiang nodded. "Very well. I have visited several times without ever strolling the grounds—good weather to stretch our legs."
Taking Zhu Jingyuan's arm, Chen Wenxin pulled him along too. "Let us go stroll as well."
As the group trailed out with their maids, only Qianli Xun, Chen Haoyu, and Kuqiao remained in the hall—Kuqiao silently fretting over every twist landing against her mistress's wishes.
Watching Zhu Shuzhen sit brooding with knitted brows, Chen Haoyu chuckled. "Believe me now? No dodging this wedding, so let us figure out how we are going to coexist."
Ignoring him, Qianli Xun sank deep into thought. Facing this crossroads, what should she choose? Even having mapped out how to survive at Chen Manor, facing the actual wedding tomorrow rattled her—what if history repeated itself, turning all her struggle to divorce Lin Jue into a joke? Master Jingguang's "Right Thought and Right Mindfulness" echoed in her mind. Yield to please everyone else at her own expense, or bolt to the ends of the earth and throw both houses into chaos? Unlike letting go of Wenyuan or refusing Shen Jianchen for Mengrong's sake, where her gut knew the answer straight away, this choice felt foggy and impossible.
Watching her stay dead silent, panic crept up Chen Haoyu's spine. With her martial skills, skipping town tonight would be child's play—and if she vanished and broke the wedding, Master Jiang would march straight into Chen Manor with the contract, branding him the clan's eternal villain! Even if the elders somehow salvaged the estate, he would never lift his head in Chen Manor again.
"Go shut the door," Chen Haoyu told Kuqiao.
Kuqiao balked.
"Just shut it—scared I will hurt her? Like I even could! I have something dead serious to tell Zhu Shuzhen and cannot let anyone outside hear."
Struck by his sudden earnestness, Kuqiao walked over and pulled the door closed. When she turned back around, her jaw nearly hit the floor: Young Master Chen had dropped straight to both knees on the flagstones!
Desperate to get his contract back from Master Jiang, Chen Haoyu had thrown every ounce of pride out the window.
Startled clean out of her philosophical dilemma, Qianli Xun shot him a scornful glare. "Have you lost your mind, Chen Haoyu? Kneeling to me for what?!"
"Until you agree to wed me tomorrow, I am not getting up!"
"Hold on—weren't you fighting tooth and nail NOT to marry me? Why beg me now?!"
Cornered, Chen Haoyu spilled the entire story of last night's gambling den ambush from start to finish.
Qianli Xun's temper exploded. "YOU screw up and expect ME to wipe your backside?!"
"Have a heart and marry me!" Chen Haoyu pleaded. "I swear your life after marriage will be identical to right now! Do whatever you please inside Chen Manor—I will not stop you! Even if you fall for someone else, I will set you free whenever you want—deal?!"
Rising to circle around him, Qianli Xun scoffed, "Worthless as you are, you never looked half-wit stupid—how on earth did you fall into gambling with Master Jiang and stake your whole family's wealth?! Sharp on the surface, hollow inside!"
Swallowing the insult, Chen Haoyu begged on. "Had a short circuit in my brain and fell straight into Master Jiang's trap! Mock me all you want—I am past caring about face! Just agree to marry me! Name any terms you like and I sign every single one!"
Dropping back into her chair, Qianli Xun shook her head. "One thing baffles me: how does marrying ME equal the entire Chen family fortune? Is Master Jiang's brain flooded with water too?"
"Master Jiang figures you are so fierce you will lock me down and keep me clear of Chunxiang Brothel, handing Chun Jinmo straight to him!"
"A brothel girl outweighs the entire Chen family empire in Master Jiang's eyes?! His skull is not flooded with water—it is stuffed with junk!"
"Just say yes! Marrying me costs you not a hair! Tell you what—every scrap of property in my name now, plus anything I inherit later, I transfer straight into YOUR name!"
A spark of interest cut straight through Qianli Xun's reluctance. "How much property is in your name?"
"Never tallied it all—way more than yours! Pure profit, zero loss—turn down a deal this sweet and you will kick yourself later!"
"You fool around all day—who runs your properties?"
"My father's stewards. Zero hassle for you."
"Hand it all to me and what do you live on?"
"Which is why we negotiate: you take all the assets, and pay me a monthly allowance."
Odd how the prospect of serious wealth cheered her right up—she admitted to herself money beat relying on fickle men any day. At last she had found a deal that served her own interest while satisfying everyone else!
"If you knew today was coming, Chen Haoyu, why snatch my bamboo grove in the first place? Now you are begging to hand it right back!"
Catching the shift in her tone, Chen Haoyu lit up. "Does that mean you agree to marry me tomorrow?!"
"Get off your knees—let us negotiate how we live after the wedding."
Scrambling up with a grin, he repeated, "Same promise: name any terms you want!"
"Your words—no whining once I lay them down!"
"Not a peep!"
"First: once I enter Chen Manor, I do not share quarters with you. I get my own separate courtyard..."
Chen Haoyu winced. "That part is tricky..."
Qianli Xun's brow snapped together. "Said any terms, and you backpedal on item one?! Sincere my foot!"
"Calm down! As my wife you have to move into my courtyard—yet hear me out: the whole courtyard is yours, the main hall is yours, and I will fix up a side room for myself! Besides, I stay out most nights anyway, so the whole place is practically your private kingdom!"
"Fair enough—done. Second: zero interference with my attending banquets or poetry gatherings, zero blocking my friendships with scholars and poets, and pay no heed to outside gossip."
"I do not even care if you fall for another guy—why would I care about poetry salons? Agreed, agreed!"
"Third: the very day after the wedding, every asset under your name transfers into mine, and any future assets go straight to me too."
Wincing for half a beat before bowing to the inevitable, Chen Haoyu gritted out, "Agreed!"
She considered adding a fourth clause, then shrugged—freedom and wealth both locked down, what more did she need? "Three terms. Done."
"Your list is done—my turn!"
"Go ahead."
"Every month I get to draw one hundred taels of silver from the shops..."
Qianli Xun shot upright. "One hundred taels?! What on earth are you doing spending a hundred taels a month?!"
"Plenty of things! Less than a hundred barely covers pocket change!"
"No way—far too much! Cut it down!"
"Not much at all! Some months I spend two hundred!"
"Two hundred taels?! Textbook prodigal son!"
"Fine, ninety!"
"No!"
"Eighty!"
"No!"
Already feeling the squeeze before the wedding even started, Chen Haoyu forced himself to swallow it for the contract's sake. "You shoot down every number—how much will you give me?"
"Twenty taels!"
Chen Haoyu's eyes bugged out. "Twenty taels?! What am I, a beggar?!"
"Twenty taels, take it or leave it! Unhappy? I know ten ways to vanish before tomorrow's wedding!"
Cornered, Chen Haoyu figured he could sign now to snag Master Jiang's contract and wheedle more silver later. "Fine! Deal! Only you never pry into where I go or what I do, and you never meddle when I take concubines!"
"Could not care less about your sordid hobbies. Kuqiao, bring brush, paper, and red ink paste!"
Setting writing supplies on the side table, Kuqiao watched her mistress draft every clause cleanly and press her thumbprint.
Stepping up to press his own, Chen Haoyu paused. "Wait—can I add one tiny clause?"
"Add my foot! Everything is already inked—why slip in extras?!"
Putting on puppy eyes, he pleaded, "I just handed you every copper to my name—cannot I ask one tiny favor?"
"Spit it out quick."
"If anyone ever bullies me, you have to step in and protect me."
Qianli Xun shut him down flat. "Protect you, my ass! Drop that request before I march out to 'protect' you and end up pounding you myself!"
Giving up, Chen Haoyu stamped his thumbprint.
"Lock this up tight, Kuqiao," Qianli Xun instructed. "The second Chen Haoyu breaks a single line, pull it out."
"Same goes if you break it!" Chen Haoyu shot back. Crisis averted—all that remained was getting through tomorrow, grabbing Master Jiang's copy, and torching it! Calling out "See you tomorrow!" he darted out, scooped up Qinggua, and raced back to Chen Manor to play groom.
With her wedding set for tomorrow, Qianli Xun felt a quiet tug toward the Divine Willow—sitting beneath its boughs and talking things out might clear the heaviness in her chest. "Kuqiao, go tell Madam I agreed to marry into the Chen family and will return to Zhu Manor before dark. I am stepping out for a bit."
"Want me to come with you, Miss?"
"I want some quiet by myself."
"Alright, I will go tell Madam."
Once Kuqiao left, Qianli Xun stepped out of Wangyue Pavilion alone and rode a carriage toward the Divine Willow.
#CH 188
#TITLE: Conversations Beneath the Divine Willow
Finding Matron Wei and the Zhu family waiting in Wangyue Pavilion's garden kiosk, Kuqiao relayed her mistress's message.
Anxiety creased Madam Xiang's brow. "Could Shuzhen be tricking us? What if she runs away and hides?"
"Rest easy, Madam," Matron Wei reassured her. "Miss never goes back on her word. Once she promises, she follows through. Put a hundred hearts at ease—tonight I will deliver her safe and sound to Zhu Manor."
"Trust Matron Wei," Chen Wenxin agreed. "Nobody knows Shuzhen better."
Zhu Jingyuan sighed. "Wasted a whole trip over here, then. Where did Shuzhen even go, Kuqiao?"
"Most likely to see the Divine Willow outside town."
"The Divine Willow?" Thought flickered across Zhu Jingyuan's face before he nodded. "Folks say that willow governs marriage ties. With her wedding tomorrow, paying it a visit makes sense. Looks like she is fine, Mother—let us head back and wait."
Madam Xiang nodded. "Let us go home, then."
Out in the bright third-month spring, the mountains burst with fresh green life, roadside grass and wildflowers standing tall. Walking alone along the foothill path, Qianli Xun spotted the Divine Willow from afar, veiled in tender green shoots swaying in the slanted afternoon sun, dotted with red ribbons glowing like sunset clouds.
Approaching with a chest full of unspoken feelings, her quiet moment shattered at the sound of loud grunting.
There beneath the willow stood Bai Feng, both big hands propping a piglet's four hooves against the rough bark. "Climb! Climb already! Don't climb and you go hungry tonight!"
Snout shoved against the trunk, the piglet snorted in protest. Bai Feng slid it a notch higher; its little hooves scrabbled uselessly as its squeals grew louder. Undeterred, he kept inching it up like coaching a toddler to walk. "That is it—one step at a time! Believe in yourself! I am going to make you the first tree-climbing pig in history! Come on! You got this!"
Clueless as to his pep talk, the piglet squealed weaker and weaker until its voice cut off and its head sagged limp.
Bai Feng panicked. "Hey! Pigs slack off too?!" Sensing something wrong, he lowered it onto the grass, where it lay motionless. "Did you pass out—or die?!"
Qianli Xun walked straight up. "You hounded it to death! Pigs cannot climb trees—forcing it to do the impossible, what did it have left to live for?"
Spinning around in shock, Bai Feng spotted her. "When did Youqi Jushi get here?! Standing there silent watching me make a fool of myself!"
"If you know it is a joke, why keep at it? Beats me whether you or your prince is crazier."
Bai Feng glanced past her shoulder, spotting no Kuqiao.
Reading his face, Qianli Xun said, "I came alone. Kuqiao stayed back."
"Well, since Kuqiao is not here, better I bury the poor pig and go report to His Highness." Scooping up the limp piglet, he wandered off to find soft soil, drew a dagger from his belt, and started digging a hole.
Paying him no further heed, Qianli Xun raised her eyes to the Divine Willow and spoke inside her heart: *Divine Willow, tomorrow I marry a man I cannot stand. Did crossing over condemn me to marrying men I never love? Master Jingguang told me changing fate takes Right Thought and Right Mindfulness—yet every step of this road has been brutal, and it feels like Right Thought just means swallowing my own misery to make everyone else happy. I do not want to swallow misery—I want to be with the man I love...*
A familiar voice spoke behind her: "Patron, enduring wrong for yourself IS fulfilling yourself."
*Master Jingguang?* Turning around, Qianli Xun saw a gentle-faced elderly nun. "Does Master cultivate at the nunnery up the mountain?"
"Indeed."
*The exact same voice—it has to be Master Jingguang!* "Are you Master Jingguang?"
Unwilling to claim the title, the nun smiled. "I am not the abbess—merely someone with a similar voice."
A pang of disappointment flickered—yet clearly this nun had read the exact cry inside her heart, no ordinary monk at all. Stepping forward, Qianli Xun bowed. "Master just said enduring wrong for oneself is fulfilling oneself. I do not understand—please enlighten me."
Pressing her palms together, Master Jingguang chanted, "Amitabha. Mortals are trapped in passion and craving, sinking deep without escape. Why do they suffer unfulfilled love and unmet desire? Only because Heaven cherishes life and hopes people turn back from stray roads to their true nature. Yet mortals stay blind—calling unmet desire 'wronging oneself,' when getting what you crave is often the truest wrong."
Unable to grasp or swallow that, Qianli Xun shook her head. "By Master's logic, giving up the man I love to marry someone I despise fulfills me? Anyone hearing that would call it nonsense."
"Tell me, Patron—why can you not marry the one you love?"
Why? Her whole romantic road since arriving felt cursed: longing for Shen Jianchen with no trace of him; falling for Wenyuan only for rumors to rip them apart; healing just enough to realize Prince Yi was Shen Jianchen, only for Mengrong to block every bold step. Convinced she had done nothing wrong, she blurted, "Fate playing cruel tricks, nothing more."
"Every person is born with a destiny," Master Jingguang said, "yet destiny is not unchangeable—change lies in the single word 'course.' Pleasing yourself while wronging others is one course; pleasing others while enduring wrong yourself is another. Two courses plant different causes and yield utterly different fruits. Which does the young lady wish to choose?"
Qianli Xun let out a sigh. "My heart wants the first, yet reason forbids it, so I swallow the wrong—only because Master Jingguang urged Right Thought and Right Mindfulness. Tell me, Master: does Right Thought ALWAYS mean sacrificing myself for others?"
A compassionate smile warmed the old nun's face. "Buddhist wisdom is boundless, never one-size-fits-all. Right Thought shifts with circumstance—at times enduring wrong to spare others, at times letting others bear discomfort to save yourself. For the dilemma facing you today, enduring wrong is the sole path. I leave you one verse: *Wait through the clouds, and you will see the bright moon.* Amitabha. Affairs await at the nunnery; excuse me."
Watching the old nun vanish up the mountain path, Qianli Xun turned back to the willow with a heavy heart. Sunset faded as the pale moon climbed the sky, yet she lingered, turning those seven words over and over: *Wait through the clouds, and you will see the bright moon.* Did that mean true love waited AFTER marrying Chen Haoyu? Hardly—when did Buddhist precepts ever endorse adultery?!
"Still here, Youqi Jushi?" Having buried the piglet, Bai Feng strolled back over.
Turning to him, Qianli Xun felt an unexpected urge to talk. "Bai Feng, I have a riddle in my head—mind helping me answer it?"
Bai Feng grinned sheepishly. "Youqi Jushi is a great scholar, and I barely know a handful of characters—how could I answer riddles for you? Too much credit!"
"That nun just fed me Buddhist philosophy I can neither fathom nor stomach. Maybe an ordinary mortal like you will cut straight to the bone." Having already agreed to marry Chen Haoyu tomorrow, she still craved confirmation that stepping away was right.
"Ask away—I will give it a shot."
Hesitating for a smooth way to phrase it and finding none, she laid it bare. "Princess Jiahe came to see me and said Prince Yi has someone else in his heart. You know about this?"
"Of course I know," Bai Feng answered plainly.
"And you know who is in his heart?"
"Need you ask? Who else besides Youqi Jushi?"
"If... if I insisted on being with Prince Yi, how would you look at me?"
"I would think Youqi Jushi was a terrible person!"
His blunt reply sent a bitter pang straight through her. "Why?"
"Need you ask?! His Highness is betrothed to Princess Jiahe by imperial decree—jumping in between them, how could anyone call that decent? Besides, Xilan tells me all the time that when His Highness lay near death, Her Highness nursed him night and day—where were you then? What gives you the right to claim him now that he is well? These last two days Her Highness cannot eat, cannot sleep, crying her eyes red—all because of Youqi Jushi..."
Unable to hear another syllable, Qianli Xun cut him off. "Enough! Do not breathe a word to Prince Yi that you saw me beneath the willow today. Tell him pigs will never climb trees, and tell him to cherish the girl right beside him. And swear you will never let Mengrong know who he loves! Rest easy—tomorrow I marry into the Chen family, and I will not become the villain in your eyes."
A cool evening breeze rustled the willow branches. With zero heart left to enjoy the spring night, Qianli Xun walked away with a desolate chest.
Left alone under the moonlit boughs, Bai Feng scratched his head. "Did I lay it on too thick? Youqi Jushi looked pretty gutted. Whatever—long as you stop wrecking His Highness and Her Highness's marriage!" Starting off, he paused to squint up at the towering willow in confusion. "Why do both His Highness and Youqi Jushi love hanging around this tree?" Scanning the trunk and swaying branches under moonlight and spotting nothing special, he concluded, "Both His Highness and Youqi Jushi must be cracked in the head, running out here to stare at a tree like it grows gold!"
Riding home inside her carriage, Qianli Xun replayed Bai Feng's raw, resentful verdict. Through every outside pair of eyes, walking far clear of Prince Yi was the sole honorable choice. Down the lonely road of swallowing her own heart she went.
#CH 189
#TITLE: Storming Zhu Manor to Stop the Wedding
When Zhu Xiyan's messenger delivered the wedding invitation to Youlin Retreat, Shen Jianchen had been down beneath the Divine Willow watching Bai Feng try to hoist the piglet up the bark.
While Bai Feng fretted over the stubborn pig, Shen Jianchen stayed cool as breeze. "No rushing—take it slow."
Bai Feng grumbled inside: *Easy for His Highness to flap his lips! A dignified royal guard armed with top fist arts reduced to coaching a pig up a tree—if word leaks out, I will be a laughingstock!*
Bored after a short while, Shen Jianchen told him, "Keep drilling it. I am heading back to the retreat." Qianli Xun had arrived beneath the willow shortly after he left.
Back in his study, Qiao Mengrong stepped slowly inside holding a crimson wedding card. "Brother, while you were out, Prefect Zhu sent an invitation—tomorrow Shuzhen marries Young Master Chen and invites us both. Looks like... Shuzhen's push to cancel the betrothal ended in failure."
She weighted those last words on purpose, driving home the quiet warning that breaking his betrothal to her was just as impossible.
Staring at the red card in her hand, Shen Jianchen neither reached for it nor uttered a word—simply spun on his heel and bolted straight out the door.
Assuming her pointed phrasing had angered him, Qiao Mengrong sagged in dismay. "Xilan, is Brother mad at me?"
"Your Highness only mentioned Youqi Jushi's failed annulment—nothing aimed at His Highness. Why would he be mad?" Xilan tried soothing her.
"I did say it wanting Brother to see he cannot easily break an imperial betrothal..."
"Even I did not catch that meaning, so maybe His Highness missed it too."
"How can you compare your wit to Brother's?" Mengrong chided faintly.
"Forgive my loose tongue, Your Highness."
Softening at once, Mengrong sighed. "Enough. Come back to my room."
"Should I send someone to tail His Highness?" Xilan asked.
"No," Mengrong decided after a pause. "He was clearly furious catching a tail last time—sending another will only put him on guard."
"Ask the driver when he returns, then?"
"No. Servants love gossip; keep fewer tongues wagging." Noticing Bai Feng absent from the courtyard, she frowned. "Where has Bai Feng vanished to these last two days?"
"He bought a piglet, Your Highness—probably down under the Divine Willow right now training it to climb trees."
"Training a pig to climb a tree?! Whatever for?!"
"All I know is His Highness ordered it."
Sharp instinct flashed across Qiao Mengrong's face, icing her soft tone with resentment. "The instant Bai Feng gets back, summon him to my room. I have questions."
"Yes, Your Highness."
Qianli Xun had sworn she would never marry Chen Haoyu—so why was the wedding suddenly tomorrow?! Determined to get the truth face-to-face, Shen Jianchen whipped his carriage straight into Lin'an, racing through crowded lanes to Wangyue Pavilion.
Matron Wei met him at the gate; one look at his frantic face sealed her suspicions about his heart, and she sighed inwardly: *Kind-hearted Miss refuses to hurt the princess, leaving Prince Yi crushed—pray he does not tear the roof off.* Dropping a curtsy, she said, "Please step inside, Your Highness."
"Is Youqi Jushi home? I need to see her."
"My young mistress stepped out an hour ago."
"Where did she go?"
Matron Wei shook her head. "She left no word."
"When will she be back?"
Another shake. "Hard to say."
"Is she truly marrying into the Chen family tomorrow?"
Reading the desperate disbelief on his face, Matron Wei confirmed softly, "Indeed she is."
A hollow ache slammed Shen Jianchen so hard he could not even squeeze out a farewell; turning silently, he climbed back into his carriage as Matron Wei bowed after him.
Minutes down the street, realization struck: she had to be at the Divine Willow! Ordering the driver straight out the city gate toward the foothills, his carriage thundered out into the falling dusk at the exact moment Qianli Xun's carriage rolled in through the gate—neither glancing out the curtains, passing inches apart on opposite roads.
Reaching the Divine Willow under moonlight, Shen Jianchen searched every shadow and found emptiness. Cold dread crept down his spine—were they truly barred from ever being together? Laying a palm on the thick bark, he whispered, "Divine Willow, you dragged Qianli Xun and me into this era—why deny us our shot? Did you send me across a thousand years just to lose her for good?"
Only wind whispered through the leaves.
Dropping his hand in bitter frustration, he muttered, "Always silent, cold as stone—waste of breath talking to you! If she is not here, back to Wangyue Pavilion—I refuse to believe I cannot see her tonight!"
Back at Wangyue Pavilion, the gates stood barred tight. Pounding the wood himself until a sleepy servant cracked it open and dropped in shock before Prince Yi, Shen Jianchen demanded, "Has Youqi Jushi returned?!"
"Master ordered Miss to depart from Zhu Manor tomorrow, Your Highness—she must have reached Zhu Manor by now."
Hard lead at last! Back into the carriage and racing straight for Zhu Manor—criss-crossing the city all night just to see her face, get the truth, and smash this wedding!
Arriving at Zhu Manor, Shen Jianchen stormed straight through the side gate. Terrified servants dared not block him and led him into the reception hall, where he stood without sitting and barked, "Bring Youqi Jushi to me right now!"
Bewildered why a fuming prince demanded the daughter instead of the Prefect, the servant scurried off to fetch her—while another had already alerted Zhu Xiyan mid-dowry-inventory. Unsurprised yet panicked that Prince Yi meant to wreck tomorrow's ceremony, Zhu Xiyan dropped the lists and summoned the entire family to the reception hall to form a united wall.
Back in her old Zhu Manor courtyard, Qianli Xun sat staring at the crimson bridal gown hung by the wall in dull gloom. Was she really walking down the aisle tomorrow? *You already said yes—quit waffling!* Just then a servant called outside: "Miss, Prince Yi is here demanding to see you!"
Qianli Xun shot to her feet and stepped outside. "Where is he?"
"In the reception hall."
A sudden jolt of warmth spiked through her before worry drowned it out—had he come to stop tomorrow's wedding?
Hurrying out beside her, Kuqiao fretted, "What do we do, Miss?"
Letting out a long breath, Qianli Xun forced out words she hated speaking. "Meet soldiers with generals, block floods with earth. Even if he is Prince Yi, he cannot sway my decision."
Kuqiao's heart ached for her mistress: forced to marry a rake worse than Lin Jue, barred from every man she cared for—first losing Young Master Qiao, now losing a prince.
Crossing the Zhu garden to the reception hall, Qianli Xun froze: her father, mother, elder brother, and sister-in-law all knelt on the flagstones outside the hall doorway! So her family knew Prince Yi's heart too—and knelt here begging him to let her go. Bai Feng's harsh words beneath the willow echoed sharp in her ears.
Steadying her pulse, she walked straight to Madam Xiang and reached for her arms. "Rise, Mother—Father, Brother, Sister-in-Law, get up! I will handle my own mess."
"Kneel down too!" Zhu Xiyan barked. "Until Prince Yi promises to let you go, none of us rises!"
Refusing to defy her husband, Madam Xiang instead tugged her daughter's sleeve. "Kneel quick and beg Prince Yi not to ruin the wedding!"
"Kneel if you want—I am not kneeling for this!" Qianli Xun pulled free. "I will talk to Prince Yi face-to-face."
Just as she stepped toward the doorway, Prince Yi's voice rang out: "There you are at last—made me hunt half the night."
Lifting her eyes in the courtyard lantern glow, she saw his silhouette—no longer Zhao Pu, simply Shen Jianchen. Never had she wanted more to run straight into his arms, yet she choked it back, eyes stinging red behind a cold scoff. "What stunt is Your Highness pulling, making my whole family kneel in the dirt begging you?"
Wasting zero breath, Shen Jianchen cut straight to the bone. "I chased all over town tonight and still tracked you down—proof that if you fight hard enough, no wall holds! Why give up so easy and wed Chen Haoyu tomorrow? Say the word and fight alongside me to break this betrothal!"
Kneeling nearby, Chen Wenxin held her breath—tonight proved her chuiwan hunch dead right! When on earth had Prince Yi fallen for Shuzhen?! One wrong slip and the Chens would cross imperial wrath!
Keeping her frosty mask up, Qianli Xun retorted, "People change their minds! I refused before, now I accept—what is so weird? And why on earth should I team up with Your Highness?"
Zero heed for the kneeling Zhu family, Shen Jianchen declared, "Because I love you and want you by my side!"
Thrilled, flushed, panicked, and furious all at once, Qianli Xun snapped, "Watch your loose tongue, Prince Yi—eat my fist!"
Her punch flew straight out; Shen Jianchen caught it smoothly. Her blows held less sting than usual yet kept sharp force—pouring her silent plea through every strike for him to stop throwing himself against a stone wall. Meeting strike for strike, Shen Jianchen answered right back through his palms: *Never giving you up.*
Watching from their knees, the Zhu family trembled—terrified Shuzhen might offend the prince or get hurt, while forced at last to admit this fierce fighter was nothing like the frail daughter they used to know.
Hurrying into the courtyard and taking in the kneeling family and sparring pair at a glance, Matron Wei dropped straight to her knees too. "What good does trading fists with my young mistress do, Your Highness?!"
Never fighting in earnest, both dropped their hands at once.
Bowing low from her knees, Matron Wei spoke steadily. "Master, Madam, and my young mistress all know Your Highness's heart. With things at this pass, take one step back so everyone can find peace."
"Step back? YOU all find peace—what about me?!" Snatching Qianli Xun's hand, Shen Jianchen locked eyes with her. "Come with me right now! Tonight! We leave Lin'an, I ditch this princely title, and with our skills we will thrive anywhere in this dynasty!"
When Wenyuan had offered to elope, she had hardened her heart and walked away; hearing Shen Jianchen ask tonight made her heart stutter. Say yes, and the wide sky opened before them—none of these cages truly belonged to her anyway...
"Has Your Highness spared one thought for what happens to Princess Jiahe if you carry my mistress off?!"
Without Matron Wei's cutting question, Qianli Xun might well have nodded and bolted straight out of Zhu Manor with him. Hit dead center in her softest spot, she pulled her hand free. "Go back, Your Highness. My mind to marry into the Chen family is cast in iron."
