Shen Jianchen seized her hand back tight—and truth be told, she barely even tried pulling free. "Do not fret over Mengrong, Matron Wei—I will see her well cared for..."
Before he finished, a trembling, familiar voice shattered the night:
"Brother... so the one in your heart... really is... Shuzhen..."
Standing at the archway, cheeks glistening with tears, was Qiao Mengrong. Every drop of warmth drained from Qianli Xun's veins; ripping her hand free of Shen Jianchen's grip, she burned with shame for daring to dream of running away with him.
Swaying under the blow, Qiao Mengrong's knees gave out—
In one blur of motion, Shen Jianchen lunged forward and caught her before she hit the stones.
#CH 190
#TITLE: Seeing Off a Friendship with Tears
Returning to Youlin Retreat from the Divine Willow earlier that evening, Bai Feng had been summoned straight to the princess's chambers by Xilan. Guessing the reason on the walk over, he swore to keep his mouth clamped shut.
Sure enough, Qiao Mengrong opened straight away: "Bai Feng, you stay at Brother's side day in and day out and know everywhere he goes. Tell me the truth, and I will reward you handsomely."
Putting on an easy air, Bai Feng answered, "Aside from Youlin Retreat, His Highness visits Qiao Manor, the prefecture yamen, Zhu Manor on official errands—oh, and Wangyue Pavilion, all places Your Highness already knows."
Thinking she had been too subtle, Mengrong pressed straight out: "Brother told me he loves someone. Do you know who?"
"Xilan asked me before, and now Your Highness asks again, yet I truly do not know," Bai Feng lied against his conscience. "Stop tormenting yourself, Your Highness—in all my time serving His Highness, I have never seen him treat another girl half as warmly as he treats you."
"Tormenting myself? You heard him say it with his own mouth that night! Fine—if you refuse to talk, get out. I will uncover who holds Brother's heart sooner or later."
Left with no answers and no ally outside Xilan, Qiao Mengrong sank into gloom until her eyes landed on the red wedding card on the table. Shuzhen married tomorrow—why not go see her and talk her heart out?
Riding first to Wangyue Pavilion, where servants told her Shuzhen had returned to Zhu Manor, she steered straight there—and breathed a sudden sigh of relief spotting her brother's carriage parked outside! So he had only come to see Prefect Zhu; she could visit Shuzhen and ride home together with Brother!
Caught in a vise, Zhu Manor's servants dared neither blurt the truth nor block Princess Jiahe, saying only that Prince Yi and the Zhu family were at the reception hall. Following a guide there with Xilan, she walked straight into the sight of her brother clutching Shuzhen's hand and promising to elope with her!
Chest squeezed breathless and limbs turning to water, tears poured down Qiao Mengrong's cheeks as those broken words left her throat, and she crumpled toward the stones—only for Shen Jianchen's swift arms to catch her. Scooping her up, he glanced back at Qianli Xun with blazing eyes. "Do not marry him! Wait for me!" Then he strode rapidly out of Zhu Manor with Mengrong in his arms.
Qianli Xun fought back her tears; she had shed enough over Wenyuan and refused to weep over love again. She blamed only herself—if she had never let feelings for Shen Jianchen creep back in, none of this wreckage would exist.
Exhaling hard—Princess Jiahe's arrival and swoon could not have been timed better!—Zhu Xiyan led the kneeling line in a loud chorus: "Farewell, Your Highness!"
Once Prince Yi vanished beyond the gate, the Zhu family and Matron Wei stiffly clambered to their feet after kneeling so long.
Seeing Shuzhen standing frozen and mute, Chen Wenxin stepped beside her. "Treat what just happened like a bad dream, Shuzhen. Get some sleep tonight—you have a wedding tomorrow."
"A wedding... yes, tomorrow is the wedding." Coming back to herself, Qianli Xun murmured in quiet dread, "Sister-in-Law, I doubt Mengrong will ever count me a friend again."
"Stop dwelling on that—"
"Save your breath on her! Everyone back to your rooms!" Zhu Xiyan cut across, marching up to point a finger in his daughter's face. "Hear me well: once you enter Chen Manor, keep your head down and live clean! Stay clear of Prince Yi!"
Afraid his father might strike her, Zhu Jingyuan stepped in to take his arm. "Father, your knees must ache—let me help you back to rest."
With a dark snort at his daughter, Zhu Xiyan let his son lead him off.
Dabbing away tears on Matron Wei's arm, Madam Xiang approached once her husband left. "Shuzhen, Mother never asked for some lofty match—only a calm, safe life without storms. Why does nothing ever go smooth for you? Wuuu!"
"Smooth days are right around the corner, Mother-in-Law!" Chen Wenxin soothed her. "Once she marries Haoyu, it all settles down! Dry those tears—crying on wedding eve brings bad luck!"
"Young Madam is right," Matron Wei agreed. "A wedding calls for smiles, not tears."
Wiping her cheeks with a handkerchief, Madam Xiang nodded. "Kuqiao, walk Miss back to her courtyard."
Without a word, Qianli Xun curtsied to her mother and withdrew alongside Kuqiao.
"Pray tomorrow passes without another hitch," Madam Xiang fretted.
"Not a hitch, Madam—rest easy, Miss will marry cleanly," Matron Wei reassured her as they escorted Madam Xiang inside.
Silence settled back over Zhu Manor, yet Qianli Xun's mind churned nonstop. Every bridge behind her had burned to ash; going through with tomorrow's wedding cleanly was the sole way to prevent fresh disasters. Tossing on her bed, she worried over Mengrong—was she alright, and how would she look at her next time?—while Shen Jianchen's parting vow echoed loud: *Do not marry him! Wait for me!*
"All your fault for storming Zhu Manor," she whispered to the dark ceiling. "I am NOT waiting for you... Shen Jianchen, if I had known things would turn out like this, I should have said yes before the earthquake. Who knew life twists like this? Forget me—treat Mengrong well."
Inside the carriage racing back to Youlin Retreat, Shen Jianchen cradled Qiao Mengrong in his arms. He felt no regret for breaking the truth tonight—shock was inevitable, yet in time she would heal and move on.
Truth be told, Qiao Mengrong's faint had been half genuine shock at finding her best friend was her rival, and half sheer calculation—the quickest way to dodge humiliation and yank her brother straight back into her arms! Xilan had played along sharp as a tack, holding back instead of catching her so Brother would step in.
Nestling against his chest brought both comfort and stabbing agony. Replaying every scene between Brother and Shuzhen, she found zero slips—only cursed her own blind trust! How many secret glances had they traded right under her nose, laughing at her like a fool?! When had it started? Shuzhen had loved Young Master Qiao—when had she set her sights on Brother? Right after Brother's poetry salon at Youlin Retreat! That was the exact turning point when Shuzhen stopped badmouthing him and started singing his praises! Unable to win Young Master Qiao, Shuzhen had pivoted to seducing Brother—maybe those old street rumors calling her shameless had been dead right all along! How Mengrong loathed herself for befriending her, pouring out her heartbreak, begging her help—handing Shuzhen a golden excuse to meet Brother at Yongtai Teahouse and twist him into demanding an annulment from His Majesty!
Hatred surged so hot her fingers clenched tight into Shen Jianchen's robe.
Feeling the tug, he murmured, "Mengrong, you are awake?"
Dropping the swoon, she opened swimming eyes and clung to his neck. "Do not leave me, Brother! Without you, I would rather die! Wuuu!"
Staying quiet while her grief raged—reasoning now would bounce off deaf ears—Shen Jianchen made his own ruthless choice inside. Seeing the steel in Qianli Xun's eyes as he carried Mengrong out, he knew she meant to board that bridal sedan tomorrow. Only one card remained: use Prince Yi's rank to mobilize Lin'an troops tomorrow, raid the Chen bridal procession, wreck the wedding, and take Qianli Xun far away! He could only save one side; he would leave the bulk of Zhao Pu's wealth to Mengrong as his duty to the late prince. *Sorry, Zhao Pu,* he thought silently. *I cannot throw away the woman I love to play your sister's husband. I meant to keep her close as family, yet plans cannot outrun fate—I am taking my love far away, ditching this royal title and your betrothal for good.*
Feeling his silence, panic shook Qiao Mengrong harder as she clung tighter. "Why won't you answer, Brother?! Are you going to abandon me?! Wuuu!"
Hearing her pitiful sobs blend with the rumble of carriage wheels made Shen Jianchen feel like the lowest heel alive—yet his resolve stayed iron. "Now you know everything, Mengrong—know who holds my heart. Let me go and grant us our chance..."
Her sobs cut off dead. Releasing him, she sat bolt upright, wiped her eyes dry, and spoke in an ice-cold voice. "She marries tomorrow—what good does me letting you go do?"
"Just grant your blessing—leave the rest to me."
His unyielding tone pierced like a needle. Swallowing the stab, she kept her voice wintry. "Wake up, Brother. This is not up to me—His Majesty decreed our marriage, and canceling it is impossible."
Saying no more, Shen Jianchen steeled himself to gamble everything for his own happiness.
Back at Youlin Retreat, he carried her to her chambers, told Xilan to tuck her in, and walked out with a furrowed brow.
The second the door shut behind him, Qiao Mengrong sprang off the bed, snatched the red wedding invitation off the table, and ripped it to shreds with soft, venomous fury. "Zhu Shuzhen! I treated you like a true sister, and this is how you stab me?! From this hour on, we are enemies!"
Like their dead friendship, red paper confetti snowed across the floor. Stomping the scraps under her slippers, she hissed, "Dream on about getting Brother, Zhu Shuzhen! Even if you were NOT marrying tomorrow, I would never let you have him!"
Spent, she stumbled back to bed and buried her face in the pillows weeping aloud—sending off her dead friendship in tears. Xilan let her cry without interruption: learning her betrothed loved another AND losing her closest friend in one blow—letting it out was mercy.
#CH 191
#TITLE: The Princess Risks Her Life to Stop a Bride-Snatching
Returning late the night before, Bai Feng had missed reporting the piglet's death. At dawn he stepped outside his prince's door to call, "Your Highness, awake?"
Before he finished, the door swung open—His Highness already dressed, dark rings shadowing sleepless eyes.
"Your Highness, the piglet refused to climb and dropped dead from exhaustion."
A flicker of bleakness crossed Shen Jianchen's face. Was he truly barred from ever being with Qianli Xun? *Screw fate!* Drawing his Military Commissioner's command token, he slapped it into Bai Feng's palm. "Take my token to the Military Training Office, muster one thousand troops, and meet me at the city gate."
Bai Feng's eyes lit up. "Are we riding to war, Your Highness?!"
"No," Shen Jianchen said flatly. "I am snatching a bride."
The grin died off Bai Feng's face—today was Youqi Jushi's wedding to Young Master Chen! Dropping straight to both knees, he pleaded, "Think thrice, Your Highness!"
"You are my guard—obey orders, do not lecture me! Move!"
Forced to rise, Bai Feng clutched the token and dashed out of the main courtyard—paused three strides out in fierce conflict, then sprinted straight to Princess Jiahe's courtyard and pounded the door. "Xilan! Xilan! Open up! Quick!"
Yanking the door wide, Xilan snapped, "What are you yelling for at crack of dawn?!"
"Where is Her Highness?! I need to see her!" Brushing past Xilan into the yard, Bai Feng spotted Qiao Mengrong stepping to her doorway with swollen red eyes.
"What do you want?"
Skipping ceremony, Bai Feng blurted out, "His Highness has lost his mind! He sent me to muster a thousand soldiers to raid the wedding and snatch the bride! As his guard I have to obey—only Your Highness can stop him!" Spinning around, he raced off to fulfill his orders.
The shock nearly knocked Qiao Mengrong off her feet. Brother really had lost his mind—mobilizing Lin'an troops for Zhu Shuzhen?! Envy and jealousy erupted into scorching hatred. Catching her breath, she hissed, "Xilan, bring me a dagger."
Terrified, Xilan gasped, "Do not do anything rash, Your Highness!"
"Fetch it—NOW!"
Dashing inside, Xilan brought out a sheathed dagger with both hands. Drawing the blade just enough to flash bright steel in her eyes, Qiao Mengrong slid it back into its sheath and tucked it up her sleeve. "Come with me to find Brother."
Arriving at Prince Yi's courtyard clutching the hidden hilt—ready to put her own life on the blade if he refused to yield—she waited as Xilan called twice into the empty room, checked inside, and hurried back out. "Your Highness, His Highness is gone!"
Heart racing, Mengrong hurried straight to the front gate, bumping right into Butler Li.
"Heading to the Zhu-Chen wedding, Your Highness?" Butler Li bowed cheerfully, clueless to the storm. "Why did you not ride with His Highness? He just left—oddly enough on horseback instead of carriage."
Keeping her tone rock steady, Mengrong replied, "I told Brother to go ahead. Please tell the driver to bring my carriage—I am heading to the feast too."
"Already hitched and waiting outside!"
Thanking him, Mengrong boarded and had Xilan order the driver straight onto the bridal procession's main route—wherever Brother meant to strike, it had to be on that road.
Over at Zhu Manor, Kuqiao woke Qianli Xun early for a quick breakfast and bridal dressing. Sleepless half the night, Qianli Xun sat fighting off yawns as Kuqiao chattered nonstop to keep gloom at bay.
"At least the Chens have sense, Miss—sending crimson bridal robes!"
"Aren't wedding robes always red?" Qianli Xun murmured sleepily. "What else would they be?"
Tracing her brows gently with indigo eyebrow pigment, Kuqiao explained, "Never heard 'red for the groom, green for the bride'? Brides can wear green robes too!"
"Green wedding robes? Neat. Then why is mine red?"
"Miss is the Prefect's daughter, while the Chens—rich as they are—hold zero official rank! You are marrying down, so red honors you bringing luster to their house! Remember when Young Madam married into Zhu Manor, she wore green because she was marrying up." Stepping back, Kuqiao beamed. "My brow-painting keeps getting better—Miss looks lovelier than ever today!"
"This wedding was not my idea—just slap something basic on and skip the fuss."
Dusting powder across her cheeks, Kuqiao insisted, "Wanted or not, a wedding is a wedding! Starting today I live at Chen Manor with Miss—start day one cheerful so every day after stays bright!"
"Didn't know you were this superstitious."
"Not superstition—good wishes!"
Kuqiao's bright spirit nudged Qianli Xun loose from her funk. Since she had agreed to walk through Chen Manor's door, why drag a sour face along? Set last night aside and step in light.
Dressed in crimson robes and crowned with a heavy phoenix coronet, she walked to her parents' hall where her brother, sister-in-law, and Matron Wei waited. Hardly had she knelt to bow to her parents when festive pipes and drums struck up outside.
"Rise," Zhu Xiyan said. "The groom is at the gate. Walk out with your parents."
As Qianli Xun stood, Yinxing approached with a lacquer tray holding a square red veil, which Kuqiao draped over her head. Guided through a red haze by Kuqiao behind her parents, with her brother, sister-in-law, and Matron Wei following, she passed through Zhu Manor's wide-open main gate for the very first time.
Peeking up under the red hem, she spotted Chen Haoyu astride a tall steed in crimson groom's robes, grinning brightly as though yesterday's panic had never happened. Dismounting to bow to his in-laws, he reached out a hand to lead his bride—only for Qianli Xun, having already sighted the bridal sedan, to stride straight past him with Kuqiao and hop right inside!
The second the curtain dropped, she yanked the veil off and tossed it aside. "Suffocating thing!" Next came the phoenix coronet. "Weighs a ton! Why on earth do brides wear torture gear on their heads? Ancients love making themselves miserable!"
Unbothered by the snub, Chen Haoyu chuckled wryly and swung back onto his horse. When the sedan bearers lingered without lifting, Zhu Xiyan nodded to Zhu Jingyuan, who grabbed a tray of red gift packets from Lutao and stuffed them into the bearers' hands. Pipes blared back up, the sedan rose, and the procession marched off down Lin'an's widest avenues—picked by a geomancer for maximum fanfare.
Riding a palanquin for the first time made Qianli Xun's stomach pitch until her breakfast threatened to come back up—then without warning, the sedan lurched to a dead stop, the music snapped off mid-note, and hush fell over the street.
Cracking the curtain, Qianli Xun blinked at a wall of armed soldiers ringing the procession tight. "What is going on, Kuqiao?"
Leaning close, Kuqiao whispered in panic, "Miss, Prince Yi really lost his marbles—he brought an army to block the wedding!"
Dropping the curtain, Qianli Xun held her breath and listened inward: bolt with Shen Jianchen right now, or stay seated and ride on?
Outside, astride a tall warhorse barring the street, Shen Jianchen stared down. Dismounting to bow in confusion, Chen Haoyu called, "Your Highness Prince Yi—today is my wedding! What is the meaning of this?"
Shen Jianchen's voice rang calm and iron-hard: "Hold your wedding if you like—leave the woman inside that sedan to me."
Dim as he could be, the truth finally clicked for Chen Haoyu—Prince Yi wanted Zhu Shuzhen! No wonder His Highness had beaten him half to death over those rumors! Wait—marrying Zhu Shuzhen brought trouble THIS big?! Edging beside Prince Yi's stirrup, he whispered fast, "Your Highness does not know—I never even wanted to marry Zhu Shuzhen! Master Jiang forced me into it, holding a contract for the whole Chen fortune! If Your Highness gets that paper back from Master Jiang for me, I cancel this wedding on the spot and you can carry Zhu Shuzhen wherever you please—not a peep out of me!"
*Pure genius!* Chen Haoyu congratulated himself—get Prince Yi to crush Master Jiang AND offload Zhu Shuzhen in one shot!
"Your business with Master Jiang means nothing to me," Shen Jianchen answered coldly. "I want only the woman in that sedan."
Swinging off his horse without sparing Chen Haoyu a glance, he strode straight toward the bridal chair—only for Matron Wei to step into his path. "Does Your Highness mean to shred my mistress's reputation to bits all over again?!"
"Stand aside, Matron. I am listening to no one today."
Reaching past her, his fingers grazed the crimson curtain—when Qiao Mengrong's sharp cry pierced the street: "Pull back that curtain, Brother, and I die right before your eyes!"
Hand freezing midair, Shen Jianchen whipped around. There stood Mengrong, bared dagger pressed straight against her chest!
With a jolt, he realized Mengrong would never yield. Heart torn between the sedan behind him and the blade at her breast, he flicked a sharp look at Bai Feng—*Wait for an opening and disarm her!*—and tried distracting her with words. "Why push me like this, Mengrong?"
"YOU pushed me, Brother!"
"Be good—put the dagger down and let us talk."
Having heard every word inside the chair, Qianli Xun felt her heart wrench at Mengrong putting her life on the line. Unable to hide behind red silk another second, she kicked off with light-foot skill and burst straight out of the sedan!
Spectators gasped as a streak of scarlet flew through the air—*clang!*—and Qiao Mengrong's dagger spun clattering across the cobblestones! Floating down like a celestial maiden in crimson bridal silk, Youqi Jushi stood dazzling before the crowd.
Blind to everything save rage, Qiao Mengrong stepped straight up and cracked a ferocious slap across Shuzhen's cheek! "I hate you, Zhu Shuzhen!"
Before anyone moved, Mengrong's eyes locked onto a soldier's drawn sword beside her—and she threw herself straight toward the blade!
Stunned by the stinging slap, Qianli Xun hesitated half a heartbeat; Bai Feng stood frozen between awe at her flying leap and shock at the slap; Shen Jianchen flinched in worry over Qianli Xun—then snapped back with a roar: "Mengrong!"
Launching forward with light-foot speed, he arrived half a breath too late. Qiao Mengrong slammed into the blade's point; scarlet bloomed across her robe as she crumpled to the ground!
Dropping his sword in sheer terror, the soldier crashed to his knees. "Not my fault! Not my fault!"
Scooping Mengrong into his arms, Shen Jianchen sprinted down the street toward a clinic, Xilan racing weeping at his heels.
Left cleaning up the chaos, Bai Feng barked at the unit captain, "March the men back to barracks!" Once the troops pulled out, he cupped his hands to Chen Haoyu. "Apologies for the disturbance, Young Master Chen." Shooting one quick look at Kuqiao, he leaped onto a horse and galloped after his prince.
Still gaping at the whirlwind, Chen Haoyu walked over to where Zhu Shuzhen stood motionless. "Hey—that circus was all your mess, nothing to do with me. Still marrying or what?"
Her cheek still burned from Mengrong's slap—for someone so fragile, the blow packed pure venom, proving hatred had replaced every drop of friendship. Looking at what had just unfolded, how could she NOT marry?
"If you have the guts to marry me, I have the guts to marry you!"
Thinking of Master Jiang's contract, Chen Haoyu squared his shoulders. "If you have the guts to marry, I have the guts to take you!"
"Then what are we standing around for?!"
Turning sharply, Qianli Xun climbed straight back into the bridal sedan. Festive drums and pipes picked back up, yet not a shred of cheer lived in her chest—only heavy dread over Mengrong's wound. Even knowing she had seen the point miss vital organs, worry refused to let her go.
#CH 192
#TITLE: Xiaguo Visits After the Ceremony
At last the bridal procession reached Chen Manor's main gate, where musicians and banquet staff barred the doorway under Chen Haodong's lead, everyone beaming—none brighter than Chen Haodong, basking in his plot's success.
Speaking for the musicians, he chanted the *Gate-Blocking Poem*:
"Rich gifts to clear the gate count most of all,
Far above ordinary marketplace trade;
A hundred thousand cash strapped to the waist should satisfy—
Three thousand and five hundred strings—do not toss them lightly!"
[TN: Translator's rendering]
Chen Haoyu waved into the procession, and his fourteen- or fifteen-year-old cousin Chen Haofei trotted up. "Need me, Second Brother?"
"Haodong is blocking the gate—you know the drill."
Planting fists on his hips, Chen Haofei belted out the *Reply to the Gate-Blocking Poem*:
"True gentlemen never keep gold in mind;
True feeling sought grows deeper still with time.
We beg our worthy kin to ease the way,
And spare the go-betweens prolonged delay!"
[TN: Translator's rendering]
Laughing, the crowd parted. A geomancer bearing a wooden measure filled with grains, beans, copper coins, and festive fruits chanted blessings while scattering handfuls over the doorway.
When the moment came, Kuqiao called softly beside the curtain, "Time to step out, Miss."
Still haunted by Mengrong plunging onto the sword, Qianli Xun forced herself steady, settled the phoenix coronet back on her head, draped the red veil, and stepped out into Kuqiao's waiting hands. Soft blue-green brocade cushioned her slippers all the way forward. Guided beside Chen Haoyu, she walked into Chen Manor shoulder to shoulder with him, peering down under her veil to wonder why brocade lined only her path and not the groom's—until they crossed the threshold into a vast ceremony hall where red carpet replaced brocade.
At the front of the hall hung an ancestral portrait above an offering table bearing fruit and three burning incense sticks. To its left sat Old Madam Xia; to the right sat Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan, flanked by Second Uncle Chen Jiangshu, Third Uncle Chen Jiangmu, both aunts, and Chen Haodong's primary wife. A Lin'an official invited out of courtesy to Prefect Zhu stood in plain robes as master of ceremonies.
"Halt, bride and groom!" he announced with a warm smile.
Bracing to bow to heaven and earth—while still fretting over Mengrong—Qianli Xun heard instead: "First bow: to the ancestral spirits!"
*Wait—don't weddings bow to heaven and earth first? The Song bows to ancestors first?* Dutifully she bowed, then bowed to her in-laws and Old Madam Xia, then faced Chen Haoyu for the mutual spousal bow—a heavy lump rising in her throat at bowing as wife to the very man she despised most.
Escorted next to the bridal chamber, she sat on the wide wedding bed while maids and matrons scattered paired wedding fruits and coins across the quilt.
"Young Master Chen, time to lift the bride's veil!" an elderly woman announced.
Unwilling to let Chen Haoyu touch it, Qianli Xun reached up and pulled the red veil off herself.
"Oh, a bride cannot remove her own veil—unlucky! The groom must lift it!" The matron tried draping it back on, only for Qianli Xun's hand to block her solidly.
"Leave it," Chen Haoyu shrugged. "Her taking it off or me taking it off—same difference."
Flipping smooth as oil, the matron set the veil aside. "Look how the groom respects his bride—a sure sign of glowing days ahead!"
Next a maid clipped a tiny lock of hair from each of their heads, bound the two locks together with a red cord, and laid them in a brocade box; another brought forward two split gourd halves linked by red silk and filled with wine. Guessing this was the nuptial gourd-cup rite, Qianli Xun glanced sideways to see Chen Haoyu already smiling and reaching for his half.
"Hurry with the nuptial gourd rite, bride—even the groom cannot wait!" the matron nudged.
"No fuss—I can drink mine solo," Chen Haoyu offered.
"Impossible!" Pressing the gourd into Qianli Xun's hand, the matron urged, "Quick now, bride!"
Qianli Xun tossed the whole gourd down in one swallow and set it back on the tray.
"Not like that!" the matron yelped. "Drink half and swap gourds with the groom!"
"Quit fussing over formalities—I do not mind," Chen Haoyu said, draining his own half clean.
Defeated, the matrons and maids cleared the tray and withdrew.
Once the room emptied, Chen Haoyu stood up. "Sorry to leave you sitting here, yet I have guests to greet outside."
Glad to see the back of him, Qianli Xun said nothing as he left, lifting off the heavy coronet and handing it to Kuqiao. "Put this away."
Setting it on the table, Kuqiao saw her mistress's glum face and guessed she was brooding over Princess Jiahe's injury. Casting about for a distraction, she chatted, "Miss, I never saw a banquet hall as massive as the Chens'! Wonder if His Majesty's own hall is even bigger?"
No response.
Kuqiao tried again. "So many guests today—half of them strangers! Oh, I spotted Xiaguo too! Can you believe she never once dropped by Wangyue Pavilion to visit you after getting married?"
Mention of Xiaguo brought Wenyuan briefly to mind. Counting back, her whole romance and breakup with Wenyuan had spanned barely seven months, yet it felt lifetimes ago. Back at the chuiwan grounds the ache had still lingered; then bit by bit it vanished as Shen Jianchen moved back into her heart—these past days she had hardly spared Wenyuan a single thought.
Not wanting to leave Kuqiao hanging, she asked idly, "Did Wenyuan come?"—then realized how pointless the question was if Xiaguo was here.
"I peeked into the men's section," Kuqiao reported. "Too crowded to spot Young Master Qiao, yet I saw Nan Shuang standing tall—if he is here, Young Master Qiao definitely is."
With nothing more to ask about Wenyuan, Qianli Xun drifted back to worrying about Mengrong, while Kuqiao ran out of topics and stood quietly beside her.
Bai Feng's sharp condemnation surfaced again. "Kuqiao, do you think I am a bad person?"
"Why on earth ask that, Miss?"
"You saw what happened today."
Catching on, Kuqiao protested at once, "How could anyone blame YOU?! You turned Prince Yi down nonstop! Actually... one thing still puzzles me, Miss: why not just agree to be Prince Yi's concubine? You and Her Highness are best friends—serving Prince Yi side by side would be lovely!"
"If only life were that tidy. Mengrong was ready to die for Prince Yi—sharing him would tear her apart. Precisely because I count Mengrong a real friend and saw how deeply she loves him, I could never stay by his side."
Kuqiao sighed silently at Heaven's injustice toward someone so kind.
"Besides, I refuse to share a husband with anyone," Qianli Xun added. Thinking how furious she would be sharing Bai Feng, Kuqiao got it completely.
Standing up with a big stretch, Qianli Xun shook off the gloom. "Enough moping—what does brooding fix? New chapter starts today, time to organize my turf!" Scanning the ornate bridal suite, she wrinkled her nose. "Flashy luxury without a shred of literary taste! Go call Matron Wei."
Kuqiao hurried out to the banquet hall to find her.
The Chens had spared zero expense, clearing out their prized Xinglong Pavilion for the feast—normally the nerve center where Chen elders and Lin'an shop managers met every few days to decide major trade moves and gather each year-end to map out the next year's commerce. Too busy guiding Miss earlier to look close, Kuqiao stepped through the doors now onto a dazzling scarlet world: twin drum-shaped lanterns carved with giant double-happiness characters flanking the doorway, blooming spring potted flowers lining the crimson carpet, joy stickers on every pillar, and colored lanterns dangling from the rafters like a sky full of stars. Turning right into the women's wing, she spotted Matron Wei returning from a toast with Madam Qin, caught her eye, and waited while Matron Wei whispered an excuse to Madam Xiang before hurrying over.
"Miss sent you for me?"
"Yes!"
Slipping back to the bridal chamber, Matron Wei pushed open the door to find her mistress already stripped out of wedding crimson into everyday clothes. "The feast is not even over and you changed out of your bridal gown! The Chens will call you ill-mannered!"
Taking her arm lightly, Qianli Xun brushed it off. "That gown felt like armor—give me everyday clothes any day. Sit down, Matron; I have errands to go over."
Sitting at the table, Matron Wei nodded. "I am all ears."
Pushing Mengrong's injury aside for now, Qianli Xun ticked off her list. "First, tomorrow please transfer all of Chen Haoyu's assets into my name."
"Kuqiao already told me," Matron Wei nodded without blinking. "I will handle it first thing tomorrow."
"Second..." Qianli Xun gestured around the gaudy room. "This decor drives me crazy. Chen Haoyu promised me this whole courtyard and main hall—please have someone redecorate to my taste."
"Two days tops, and it will match your chamber at Wangyue Pavilion down to the brush-rest."
"And my books..."
"Before nightfall, Hongdou will bring every volume over by carriage."
Everything ran like clockwork with Matron Wei. Mention of Hongdou reminded the matron of one more item. "Walking through this courtyard, I noticed Young Master already has plenty of attendants. No sense hauling all of Wangyue Pavilion's staff here—keep just the ten trained in fist arts, send the rest off with generous severance silver, and sell Wangyue Pavilion. Agree?"
With a new chapter open, no need to cling to the old shell. "Agreed on every count."
"If nothing else, I must head back to the feast before Madam gets overwhelmed."
"Much obliged, Matron."
Just as Kuqiao opened the door for Matron Wei to exit, Xiaguo stepped into view.
"Well now—Concubine Xia of the Qiao house finally remembers her old mistress!" Matron Wei teased.
"Save the barbs, Matron—only been at Qiao Manor a few days!" Sliding past Matron Wei into the room—while her maid Aimo stepped aside to bow respectfully before following—Xiaguo curtsied warmly. "Please do not blame me for not coming back to visit sooner, Miss."
With zero mood for fake warmth, Qianli Xun replied evenly, "Wangyue Pavilion is hardly your maiden home, so skip talk of post-wedding visits. Both of us live in Lin'an—plenty of chances to cross paths."
Without waiting for an invite, Xiaguo perched straight down beside her former mistress—no longer a standing maid, even as a concubine she sat level now! Throwing a smug glance at Kuqiao still standing in servant attire, she chuckled, "The Chen wedding really is grand—blew the Qiaos' clean out of the water! You should have seen the sour faces on Miao Zhiyu and Madam Qin! Heh!"
Kuqiao frowned sharply. "Why mock Young Madam Qiao, Concubine Xia? You entered Qiao Manor the exact same day—her wedding was your wedding too."
Xiaguo's face fell. "I am only a concubine—what right do I have to call it a wedding?"
"At least you know your place," Kuqiao muttered.
Stepping in to shift topics, Qianli Xun asked, "Did you bring your family into town?"
Gratitude softened Xiaguo's eyes. "I wanted to—with the dowry Miss gave me, we could live well in Lin'an—yet my folks refused, too used to country life. I plan to send silver home regularly so Grandfather never has to wander singing for coin."
"How is the old gentleman?" Remembering the blind drummer, Qianli Xun smiled. "I would love to hear him sing Cai Bojie's ballad again sometime."
"Healthy enough! In a while I will bring him into town to sing privately for Miss."
"That would be lovely!"
Then the smile slid right off Xiaguo's face. "Miss, you have no idea—all these days at Qiao Manor, Wenyuan has not consummated our marriage yet. Nor with Miao Zhiyu either."
#CH 193
#TITLE: A Lively Chen Family Wedding Feast
Blindsided by the bedroom confession, Qianli Xun had zero idea how to answer—wanting no part of anyone's private laundry.
Kuqiao spoke up sharply. "How can Concubine Xia spill bedchamber secrets in front of Miss?! Have you no shame?!"
"Mind your tongue when I am speaking with Miss!" Xiaguo snapped back.
Kuqiao pursed her lips and fell quiet.
"Do not take offense, Miss," Xiaguo said softly. "I only speak freely because I think of you as family—and wanted you to know Wenyuan still has not let go of you..."
Catching the subtle probe—and hating being tested—Qianli Xun answered smoothly, "I am glad you think of me as family. As for Wenyuan and me, that chapter is closed. As you saw today, I just got married myself—what is left to fret over?"
Relief washed over Xiaguo's face. "Truth be told, I envy you winning Wenyuan's real love. I dare not ask for love—just waiting on him and chatting with him makes me content." A small triumphant smile curved her lips. "One thing does cheer me up, though: Wenyuan has dropped by my room to chat several times, yet he never spares Miao Zhiyu a glance."
Qianli Xun thanked her lucky stars she had never accepted concubinage—not for Wenyuan, not for Prince Yi! Measuring petty victories against rival wives until hearts rotted with schemes sounded like pure hell.
Seeing her former mistress stay silent with obvious boredom, Xiaguo rose to excuse herself. "This is the bridal chamber, and the groom might return any minute—I should head back to the feast and visit again when you have time."
"Take care," Qianli Xun offered two cool words as Xiaguo curtsied out with Aimo.
Latching the door once they cleared the yard, Kuqiao wrinkled her nose. "I do not like the new Xiaguo, Miss."
"Why?"
"Hard to put my finger on—just rubs me the wrong way."
"Leave her be—nothing to do with us. I am exhausted and need a nap. When Chen Haoyu gets back, send him to find a bed in the back courtyard."
"Wait—didn't Miss tell Young Master he gets the side room?"
"Changed my mind. Side room is too close—last thing I want waking up is staring at Chen Haoyu's mug." Lying down fully clothed—first night in a strange house, what if Chen Haoyu staggered back drunk into the wrong room?—she let Kuqiao draw the bed curtains and slip out to ready a back-courtyard room before the groom griped.
Out in the men's banquet wing, Qiao Wenxian drank cup after bitter cup. Heartbroken at receiving the invitation, he had wanted to stay home, yet Qiao Shouren insisted he represent the Qiaos, so he arrived dutifully with wife and concubine—glad at least to split off with Dongfang Chenze's table while the women headed to their own wing. Watching Shuzhen walk past in bridal crimson beside Chen Haoyu stoked blazing jealousy; he gripped his wine cup so tight his knuckles ached. Once the bride withdrew, his soul walked out with her, and he ignored every friend's plea to slow down.
Worried for his health, Nan Shuang stepped up. "Go easy, Young Master."
"Stay out of it! Back off!" Qiao Wenxian shoved him away.
Soon Chen Haoyu strolled over to make toasts with Qinggua carrying the wine jug. Ignoring Qiao Wenxian's black cloud, Chen Haoyu raised his cup to the table. "Thank you all for gracing my wedding—a toast to you, gentlemen!"
Dongfang Chenze, Cao Ban, An Mingxuan, and the others rose politely to drink back—much as they scorned this rake and pitied Youqi Jushi, wedding courtesy came first. Only Qiao Wenxian stayed seated without lifting a finger.
As Chen Haoyu turned to move to the next table, wine-bold Qiao Wenxian called out, "You have not toasted me yet."
"I just toasted the table—you refused to drink," Chen Haoyu shot back.
Filling a cup to the brim and rising, Qiao Wenxian sneered, "What kind of toast lumps a whole table together? Why not toast man to man?"
"Cannot help being popular—half Lin'an showed up. Toast one by one and we would still be drinking tomorrow!"
With one flick of his wrist, Qiao Wenxian flung his wine straight into Chen Haoyu's face! "You are not fit to marry Shuzhen!"
Dropping the jug, Qinggua scrambled to dab his master's wet face with his sleeve. "Young Master Qiao had too much wine—do not mind him, Young Master!"
Shoving Qinggua clear, Chen Haoyu snatched Qiao Wenxian by the collar. "Looking for a brawl?!"
Springing up, Dongfang Chenze stepped between them. "On your wedding day, Young Master Chen—do not trade blows with a drunk!" With Cao Ban and An Mingxuan hauling Chen Haoyu back, the flare-up cooled.
Watching from a nearby table, Lin Jue scoffed silently: *Look at them fighting over a woman I already ditched—hilarious!* Yet an instant later, a dull hollowness hit him; Zhu Shuzhen had been his love at first sight, a rush he could never erase, and he knocked back a full cup himself.
Slumping back into drinking, Qiao Wenxian suddenly blinked—where was Prince Yi?
At the head table, Chen Jianglin wondered the same thing, leaning to whisper to Zhu Xiyan, "Any idea why Prince Yi has not arrived?"
Recalling last night's storm, Zhu Xiyan answered smoothly, "Likely tied up with urgent affairs. If His Highness missed today, you and I will call on him together tomorrow to pay respects."
"Fair enough," Chen Jianglin nodded, smiling back into his toasts.
At another table, Chen Jiangmu already knew about Prince Yi's raid on the bridal procession—his son Haofei had walked with the escort and whispered every detail—and planned to tell Eldest Brother after guests cleared out. Second Brother Chen Jiangshu knew nothing of the raid yet, still basking in his son's triumph while marveling at why Eldest Brother looked so thrilled throwing a fortune at a bride with a divorced past and messy name.
Over in the women's wing, packed with Lin'an's elite ladies, Yao Qingyan circulated table to table with stout-drinking maids covering for her weak tolerance—swapping in plain water whenever trapped. Completing her rounds, she noticed Princess Jiahe absent too and queried Madam Xiang. Uninformed by Matron Wei about the street ambush yet haunted by last night's scene at Zhu Manor, Madam Xiang deflected mildly: "Royals stay busy—probably delayed." Asking Chen Wenxin brought only a discreet "No idea," so Yao Qingyan dropped it and kept hosting.
Meanwhile, guests mingled between tables. Spotting Madam Qin step away, Bai Meiruo carried her cup over and slid into the empty seat beside Miao Zhiyu. "Why does Young Madam Qiao look so downcast?"
Miao Zhiyu glanced at her. "Just call me Zhiyu."
"Oh? Not fond of 'Young Madam Qiao'?" Bai Meiruo probed lightly.
"We know each other well enough, and I actually find your company pleasant—no need for stiff titles. Call me Zhiyu like a friend."
New to Lin'an with few acquaintances, Bai Meiruo had hoped to build her own circle. Approaching Youqi Jushi felt awkward given her past as Lin Jue's wife; after chatting briefly with Princess Jiahe at the Qiao wedding, she had even tried calling at Youlin Retreat once—only to learn the princess was out at Wangyue Pavilion, Youqi Jushi's home! Figuring the two were thick as thieves, Bai Meiruo had backed off—until today's bombshell tore that friendship to ribbons! With the princess absent today, Miao Zhiyu made the ideal ally—especially after Bai Meiruo overheard noblewomen whispering about Youqi Jushi's old romance with Young Master Qiao.
Warming at once to Miao Zhiyu's invitation, she raised her cup. "A toast between us, Zhiyu."
Clinking cups, they sipped—just as Xiaguo strolled back into the hall toward the concubines' table.
"How men envy Young Master Qiao," Bai Meiruo murmured slyly, "marrying a talent like you AND taking a pretty maid as concubine! Yet funny—deck Concubine Xia in fine silks, and she still reeks of servant ways."
Hearing Xiaguo jabbed delighted Miao Zhiyu. "Pour another—let us drink again!"
Finishing the second cup, Miao Zhiyu suggested, "A few of my close friends skipped today because their families keep them out of crowds. Another day, let us all meet for tea and talk at Yongtai Teahouse!"
"I would be honored!" Leaning closer, Bai Meiruo dropped her voice. "Do you know why Princess Jiahe missed today?"
Miao Zhiyu shook her head.
"Word is Her Highness took a sword wound..."
"What?! Who dared stab royalty?!"
"My nosy maid chased after the bridal procession for fun. When she ran back, she told me Prince Yi tried snatching the bride mid-street, and Princess Jiahe stepped in threatening her own life..."
"WHAT?!" Miao Zhiyu's gasp drew puzzled stares across the table.
Flushing, she forced a polite smile. "Nothing—carry on!" Pulling Bai Meiruo aside to a quiet corner, she whispered urgently, "You mean Prince Yi tried snatching the CHEN bride?!"
Bai Meiruo nodded. "Who knew Youqi Jushi even hooked Prince Yi?!"
"Impossible! She loved Wenyuan!"
Bearing quiet resentment toward her husband's ex-wife, Bai Meiruo spun freely. "Once you married Young Master Qiao, she had no shot left—so to spite you she shoved her maid into his house, pivoted straight around, and set her hooks in Prince Yi!"
Pleasant as it felt hearing Youqi Jushi torn down, Miao Zhiyu kept her refined mask intact. "Youqi Jushi hardly seems that sort..."
"Hard to say—what IS certain is she crossed Princess Jiahe! If the princess recovers, Youqi Jushi is in for stormy weather."
"She is already married into the Chen family—what can the princess do to her?"
"Wait and watch the fireworks! Right now, let us both keep an ear out for Her Highness's condition so we can call on her when the moment is right."
Miao Zhiyu nodded, then asked curiously, "Why do you dislike Youqi Jushi so strongly?"
A flat calm settled over Bai Meiruo's face. "She never wronged me directly—yet she is my husband's former wife. He says nothing aloud, yet his heart still clings to her! Always sitting around holding *Collected Poems of Wang Youcheng*. At first I thought he loved poetry—until I caught him grinning at it while holding the book UPSIDE DOWN! Asking his servants gave me the answer: he only holds it because of Youqi Jushi."
Miao Zhiyu let out a quiet sigh. "Looks like we are two sisters sharing the same ache."
Wary of drawing stares, both returned to their seats and resumed polite small talk.
#CH 194
#TITLE: Bullying the Groom After the Feast
Once the banquet ended and the last guests departed, Chen Jiangmu drew Second Brother Chen Jiangshu aside into a spring-blooming garden pavilion.
"What is the secret, Third Brother?" Chen Jiangshu asked.
"Huge trouble, Second Brother—the Chens might have crossed royalty!"
"Prince Yi?"
"Yes! Haofei marched with the bridal escort and watched with his own eyes as Prince Yi brought soldiers to snatch the bride mid-road! Had Princess Jiahe not put her own life on the line, Miss Zhu would never have reached Chen Manor!"
Alarm shot through Chen Jiangshu—offending royalty was no joke, and now Prince Yi's absence made total sense!
Honest and cautious by nature, Chen Jiangmu always leaned on his elder brothers. "I froze when Haofei told me—was not sure whether to tell Eldest Brother, so I came to you first."
"No hiding something this huge—Eldest Brother will find out sooner or later, maybe Haoyu already told him. Come with me to see Eldest Brother right now."
In the main courtyard, Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan had just sat down for quiet tea when the two brothers arrived; sensing serious talk, Yao Qingyan excused herself to the inner room.
Pouring tea and inviting his brothers to sit—Chen Jiangshu opposite on the couch, Chen Jiangmu on a chair—Chen Jianglin had barely handed cups out when Chen Jiangshu cut straight in. "Big trouble, Eldest Brother—did Haoyu tell you?"
"Tell me what?"
"Did you not wonder why Prince Yi skipped the feast?"
"Heard something?" Chen Jianglin tensed.
Chen Jiangmu spoke up straight. "Haofei whispered to me that Prince Yi raided the bridal procession today! Princess Jiahe forced him back at risk of her own life and got wounded before he withdrew."
Reaching for his tea bowl, Chen Jianglin jerked in shock, tipping the cup clean across the tabletop. As a maid scrambled to wipe the spill, he barked, "Go fetch Haoyu this instant!"
A servant sprinted off.
Meanwhile, after the feast wrapped up, Chen Haoyu figured Zhu Shuzhen had not eaten all day and had Qinggua pack a food carrier—not from romance, purely keeping peace under one roof. Glancing at the sinking sun reminded him of his wedding night; no way he dared lay a hand on that tigress, so he planned to drop off the meal and head straight to Chun Jinmo.
Reeking of wine as he stepped into his courtyard, he stopped dead: twenty or thirty maids stood lined up in formation while Zhu Shuzhen—already out of her bridal gown—addressed them like a general!
Waking from a one-hour nap hungry, Qianli Xun had eaten the meal Matron Wei sent over, then decided to inspect the household staff to shake off thoughts of Prince Yi's ambush. Strolling the courtyard with Kuqiao revealed an absurd crowd of pretty young maids—even the sweepers looked handpicked for looks! Disgusted by this rake keeping a private harem while never even sleeping home, she summoned all twenty or thirty maids alongside Chen Manor's Butler Wang to clear house.
Spotting Chen Haoyu walk in flushed from wine, she greeted him briskly. "Good timing! Just letting you know I need zero of these maids and am clearing them out. Already asked each one: half want their deeds of sale back to leave, half want to stay on at Chen Manor, so Butler Wang will reassign those to other courtyards."
Half his wine buzz evaporated in sheer outrage as Chen Haoyu stabbed a finger at her. "You—you have gone way too far!"
Rising to her feet, Qianli Xun snapped, "Point that finger somewhere else! This courtyard belongs to me now, and it runs my way!"
Unable to contradict her, he tried bargaining. "At least leave me a couple pretty ones!"
"You barely sleep here—what for?" Flicking a look at Qinggua, she asked, "Your name?"
"Qinggua, Young Madam."
"Qinggua looks handy enough. One attendant is plenty for you."
Choking down fury, Chen Haoyu sneered, "A real man does not wrestle women—have it your way! Still, boot everyone out and who sweeps the courtyard? Working Kuqiao to death?!"
Right on cue, ten familiar figures marched through the courtyard gate—the exact crew who had flattened Master Jiang's gang outside Wangyue Pavilion!
Hongdou stepped forward to report: "Miss, Matron Wei sent us ahead. Carts loaded with all your books are waiting outside the gate—move them in now or wait till tomorrow?"
"See? Plenty of help!" Qianli Xun smirked, then told Hongdou, "Park the carts inside the manor and wait until Matron Wei finishes redoing the hall before unloading the books."
"Yes, Miss!" Dismissing the nine others to settle in, Hongdou turned back out to direct the carts.
Chen Haoyu turned purple. "Zhu Shuzhen! Every stick of furniture in that hall is rare hardwood! Redoing the room—are you hauling MY furniture out too?!"
"Whatever I do not want goes; whatever fits stays. Oh, one more thing: the side room sits close to me, so Kuqiao and Hongdou move in there. Out back sit two rooms—Matron Wei takes the left, you take the right. Kuqiao already made your bed nice and cozy, just a bit plain."
Chen Haoyu's lips trembled with rage. "Zhu... Zhu Shuzhen! Too far! Here I felt sorry for you and brought you dinner—I would rather feed it to pigs than give you a bite!"
Glancing at the food carrier in Qinggua's hand, she smiled coolly. "Thanks for the thought, yet I already ate. Wait for your leftovers and I would starve to death."
Just then a servant hurried in from the master's wing: "Young Master, Master summons you!"
Desperate to vent, Chen Haoyu swung a kick straight into Qinggua's food carrier, sending bowls and dishes smashing across the flagstones—and stubbing his own toes so hard he hopped yelling "Ow! Ow!" Starting out the gate, he slammed straight into Qianli Xun blocking his path.
"Clean it up before you leave!"
Terrified a brawl was about to erupt, Qinggua scrambled onto his knees. "I will clean it, Young Madam! Leave it to me!"
Only then did Qianli Xun step aside; fists clenched tight, Chen Haoyu stormed out. Sweeping shattered bowls into the tray, Qinggua mused to himself: *Young Master finally met his nemesis—wonder if that is blessing or curse!*
Swallowing his anger, Chen Haoyu reached his father's hall, saw both uncles seated inside, and knew at once word of the ambush had leaked. Bowing to all three, he braced himself.
"Tell me straight," Chen Jianglin demanded. "Did Prince Yi try to snatch your bride?"
"Yes, Father."
"How bad is Princess Jiahe's wound?"
"Missed vital organs—she will pull through."
"Thank Heaven. Do you think this spells trouble for the Chen family?"
Fresh off being steamrolled by Zhu Shuzhen, Chen Haoyu leaped at the chance to paint her black. "None of this is the Chens' fault! Blame Zhu Shuzhen—carrying on with Prince Yi behind closed doors, then dragging that disaster into Chen Manor onto my head!"
Privately thrilled—the messier Haoyu's marriage, the cleaner Haodong's path to the empire!—Chen Jiangshu chimed in smoothly, "Haoyu speaks true, Eldest Brother. None of this falls on us. Besides, Prince Yi is known for fairness and will hardly punish the Chens over private heartache."
Steering clean clear of gossip about his brand-new daughter-in-law, Chen Jianglin nodded. "Fair point. Wait a beat, then we call on Prince Yi in person to apologize."
"Apologize for what?!" Chen Haoyu balked. "What crime did we commit?!"
"Crime or not, it unfolded during our bridal escort," Chen Jianglin explained. "Showing humility saves royal face, and a fair prince will not hold a grudge once he sees our respect."
Both uncles agreed. Chen Haoyu opened his mouth to trash Zhu Shuzhen further when his father waved him off. "Your wedding night—do not leave your bride waiting. Head back."
Bowing out into the dark, Chen Haoyu had zero desire to crawl back to his hijacked courtyard and steered straight for the front gate toward Chunxiang Brothel—only to get boxed in halfway across the grounds by Hongdou and two sturdy maids!
"Got a death wish, blocking this young master inside his own manor?!"
"No offense, Young Master," Hongdou answered politely. "Matron Wei reminded us you must accompany Miss at dawn to serve morning tea to the Chen elders. Skipping out breaks rite, so Miss sent us to escort you back."
"And if I refuse?!"
"Miss said carry you back over our shoulders if we have to."
Before he blinked, the two martial-trained maids hooked his arms from either side and frog-marched him straight back despite his thrashing! Dumping him inside the yard, Hongdou latched the gate before letting him go.
"Zhu Shuzhen! Oath-breaker!" Chen Haoyu roared. "Signed in black and white zero interference with my freedom—going back on your word night one?!"
Stepping onto the porch, Qianli Xun shot back, "Quit howling! Like I care about your freedom! Look at what you are wearing—a groom bolting on his wedding night makes both of us a joke!"
"Oh?" Chen Haoyu leered slyly. "Hoping to consummate the marriage after all?"
"Consummate my foot! Tomorrow morning you escort me to greet your elders—leave me going solo? Relax, just tonight! Starting tomorrow afternoon, go rot in any gutter you want and I will not lift a finger!"
Turning back inside, she shut the door, leaving Chen Haoyu standing lonely under the stars. "Thank heaven I had the brains to sign a contract first, or living under this roof would be hell!" Muttering, he shuffled off toward the back courtyard.
#CH 195
#TITLE: Follow Fate, Never Force It
Reaching the back courtyard and seeing lamplight glowing across from him in Matron Wei's room, Chen Haoyu grumbled, "Shoving me right across from an old matron—she really has nerve!" Still powerless against Zhu Shuzhen, he pushed into his assigned room, where Qinggua was patting down the bedding.
"Young Madam had this room scrubbed spotless and I made your bed fresh, Young Master," Qinggua greeted him.
"Look how pathetic your master has been brought—banished to a plain shack in the back yard!"
"On your wedding night too—barred from your own bridal chamber!" Qinggua sympathized. "Why not tattle to Master and Madam tomorrow and let them put Young Madam in her place?"
Chen Haoyu kicked out at him. "What do you know?! Like I even WANT to share a bridal chamber with her!"
Dodging aside, Qinggua scratched his head. "Beats me why you even got married if you refuse the bridal chamber."
Ignoring him, Chen Haoyu spotted an extra cot against the wall. "Why is there a second bed in MY room?!"
Backing out of kicking range with a cheeky grin, Qinggua chuckled, "Heh, Young Madam ordered me to bunk in here so I can wait on you handy!"
"Zhu Shuzhen! You pushed me too far! Wait until I wrap up my business—I will slap you with a charge of abusing your husband and divorce your hide!" Slamming his fist onto the tabletop, Chen Haoyu winced—not a scratch on the wood, while his knuckles throbbed red. "Ow! Damn, that hurts!"
Rubbing his master's hand, Qinggua murmured, "You keep injuring yourself today—take it easy and stop feuding with Young Madam."
"Hands off—help me undress for bed!"
Peeling off his master's crimson groom robes and climbing into his own cot once Chen Haoyu lay down, Qinggua grinned into the dark—sharing a room with his master felt downright novel, and watching Young Madam tame him flat on day one finally explained why Master and Madam had fought so hard to bring a divorced Miss Zhu home!
Meanwhile, back when Shen Jianchen had carried Qiao Mengrong to a clinic, the physician bandaged her wound and reassured him it was shallow and posed no danger. Exhaling hard, Shen Jianchen carried her back to Youlin Retreat, cursing how hollow his princely title had proved—in every costume drama back home, princes took wives and concubines at will, yet one Qiao Mengrong had tied his hands and cost him the woman he loved!
Laying her onto her bed at Youlin Retreat and watching tears gleam on her lashes, he murmured softly, "Never pull a stunt like that again."
"Only if Brother stops pulling stunts first."
When he moved to rise, she caught his hand. "Stay with me a little while, Brother."
Weighed down with guilt, he sat on the bedside. "Alright. Brother will sit with you."
Closing her eyes, Qiao Mengrong let silent tears roll down her temples. Taking a silk handkerchief from Xilan, Shen Jianchen gently blotted them away. Before he drew back, her fingers closed around his wrist. "Tell me, Brother—when did it start?"
Dreading fresh grief, he hedged. "Wait until your wound heals, then I will answer."
Opening swimming eyes, she shook her head. "No. I want to know right now."
Glancing over, Shen Jianchen told Xilan, "Step outside with Bai Feng." Both attendants exited and closed the door behind them.
Freeing his wrist to wipe another tear, Shen Jianchen spoke the raw truth. "I am not Zhao Pu, and she is not Youqi Jushi. We knew each other in another age altogether. I loved her long ago, and after finding her again in this world against all odds, I could not just walk away..."
Flashes returned to Qiao Mengrong—Shuzhen claiming amnesia, Brother remembering nothing of his past—could it really be true? Was THAT why Brother had cast lots on the street and stopped hunting the writer Qianli Xun? Still, she refused to believe it. "If Brother's heart wandered, why invent some tall tale nobody could swallow?! Shuzhen is gorgeous and brilliant, turning every man's head—I just never thought even my own brother would fall for her spells! Shameless seductress..."
Fury triggered a fit of coughing that pulled sharp pain at her bandaged side—still nothing next to the agony tearing her heart.
Helpless, Shen Jianchen patted her shoulder. "Stop dwelling on it, Mengrong. You wrecked my last push—there is no road left between her and me..."
Hearing "no road left" calmed her ragged breathing. "Does Brother blame me?"
Sighing under life's crushing ironies, Shen Jianchen shook his head. "No—I blame myself, unable to bring joy to you or to myself."
Spent, Qiao Mengrong closed her eyes. "I want to sleep a bit, Brother. Stay beside me—do not leave, alright?"
Saying nothing aloud, he stayed put, dabbing the last tears from her lashes until, half an hour later, exhaustion pulled her into sleep. Setting the handkerchief by her pillow, he slipped quietly out and shut the door.
Gloom pressed heavy on his chest. Mengrong's desperate gamble had walled off every escape—yet his heart still tugged toward Qianli Xun. Glancing at the afternoon sun still high in the sky, he knew she must be bowing beside Chen Haoyu right now. Actually envying that worthless playboy Chen Haoyu! How unfair Heaven could be.
Feet carrying him on autopilot, Shen Jianchen wandered through the blooming woods out toward the Divine Willow. Reaching the pond's rim, he saw the willow draped in fresh green shoots glittering like jade in the sunlight. Lines from He Zhizhang's *The Willow*—memorized back in grade school—slipped off his tongue unbidden:
"The slender beauty's dressed in emerald all about;
A thousand branches droop like fringes made of jade.
But do you know by whom these slim leaves are cut out?
The wind of early spring is sharp as scissor blade." [TN: Translated by Xu Yuanchong]
Strange how such bright poetry surfaced while his chest felt hollowed out—maybe the Divine Willow was nudging brighter lines into his head to soften the blow. As red prayer ribbons fluttered on the breeze like scarlet bridal silk, Qianli Xun's dazzling leap from the wedding sedan flashed back before his eyes, fanning longing he dared not feed lest it cost Mengrong's life.
"Tell me, Divine Willow," he whispered upward, "what am I supposed to do? Marry Zhao Pu's sister in his place, or use Prince Yi's power to pry her back from the Chens?"
Only wind answered, blowing one red ribbon loose onto a nearby briar bush where thorns snagged it fast. Too drained to care, Shen Jianchen stood still—until a weathered hand plucked the ribbon clear of the thorns and tied it neatly back onto a willow bough.
Turning, he recognized the kind-faced elderly nun with a cloth bundle slung over her shoulder—Master Jingguang!
"Amitabha," she greeted him.
"Peace to you, Master. What fate meeting you again."
"Hearing Lin'an's wealthiest Chen family held a wedding today, I came down the mountain for alms—and generous they were, gifting fine teas and herbs! Coming round the back hill, I bump into Patron too. Since I came down for alms, surely Patron owes me a donation?"
Having rushed out empty-handed, Shen Jianchen patted his sash, untied a fine jade pendant from his belt, and held it out. "This trinket is the only thing of value on me—if Master does not mind it, take it."
Accepting it, Master Jingguang smiled. "Not worried I am a swindler?"
"Wealth is external—you bring none at birth and carry none in death. On my waist it is just ornament; sold for coin to mend your nunnery or cover daily needs, it earns merit. Why fear being swindled?"
"Patron sees clean through worldly vanity," Master Jingguang praised. "In return for your jade pendant, I gift you seven words: *Wait through the clouds, and you will see the bright moon.*"
Puzzled, Shen Jianchen asked, "Could Master put it plainer?"
Smiling gently, she offered another phrase: "Follow fate; never force it."
"Please enlighten me further, Master."
"Following fate moves with wind and current, carrying life smooth; forcing fate rows upstream against headwinds, hitting snag after snag. Better to follow fate with the longing in your heart—only then will the clouds part to reveal the bright moon."
A quiet click settled in his mind. "Thank you for your guidance, Master."
"Amitabha." Joining her palms, Master Jingguang turned back up the mountain trail.
Watching her vanish among the trees, Shen Jianchen mulled it over: chasing Qianli Xun felt like clawing up a sheer cliff against every wind—was following fate telling him to accept his tie to Mengrong? Deep down, he still hated bowing to it.
"Your Highness! Imperial Attendant Liang arrived from Bianliang bearing an imperial decree!"
Spinning around at Bai Feng's voice, Shen Jianchen ignored the decree and snapped, "Was it you who tipped Her Highness off about the raid?"
Dropping to both knees, Bai Feng owned up straight. "It was me! For Your Highness's and Her Highness's own good—I could not watch you betray the princess! Besides, Youqi Jushi was marrying today—why keep clinging?"
Stepping past him, Shen Jianchen snapped, "Stay out of my private affairs! For meddling, kneel right here under the Divine Willow until dawn tomorrow!"
Indignant once his prince walked out of earshot, Bai Feng muttered, "HE messes up and punishes ME?! Totally unfair!" Kneeling five minutes without a soul watching felt idiotic—why not take a nap, hike to the nunnery, or stroll into town to see Kuqiao until morning? Choosing town, he hopped up, dusted off his knees, and headed around the foothills toward Lin'an.
Inside Youlin Retreat's reception hall, Shen Jianchen received Liang Shicheng, escorted by Zhu Xiyan now that the Chen banquet had wrapped up by mid-afternoon.
"When did Imperial Attendant Liang reach Lin'an?" Shen Jianchen asked.
"Just today, Your Highness! Shame I missed Prefect Zhu's daughter's wedding—came straight from the yamen without resting to deliver the decree."
"Much obliged, Imperial Attendant."
Unrolling the yellow silk scroll, Liang Shicheng announced, "Prince Yi, kneel to receive the imperial decree!"
Shen Jianchen and Zhu Xiyan knelt together.
Liang Shicheng read aloud in sonorous tones: "By the Mandate of Heaven, inheriting ancestral virtue, We ponder the realm's welfare and decree a joyous matter. Our son Zhao Pu, gifted in talent and upright in character, stands as a future pillar of Our Song and ought to wed early and establish his household. Though his wedding date was set, We yearn to witness the blissful union of fine son and fair bride sooner, and hereby command the date advanced: return to the capital within half a month of receiving this decree, complete the wedding, and thereafter resume your post in Lin'an. Respect this!"
Pinned between Mengrong's threat on her own life and His Majesty's decree, every exit slammed shut—he had no choice left but to follow fate and marry Zhao Pu's sister.
Forcing out the heaviest words on his tongue, he bowed. "Your son receives the decree."
Placing the scroll into his hands, Liang Shicheng bade both men rise. The silk weighed like iron in Shen Jianchen's palms. "Since Imperial Attendant Liang traveled all this way, stay a few days to enjoy Lin'an and travel back to Bianliang with me."
"Grateful as I am for Your Highness's hospitality, court business presses—especially ongoing talks with the Jurchens over returning Yanjing. I must set out on the return road today; please excuse me."
Words criticizing the court's appeasement rose to Shen Jianchen's lips, only to die unspoken—history would not bend, and neither emperor nor sycophant wanted hard truth. "In that case, safe journey, Imperial Attendant."
Escorting the emperor's favorite eunuch out to his carriage and watching it roll away, Zhu Xiyan did not leave; lifting the hem of his official robe, he dropped straight to his knees outside Youlin Retreat's front gate.
#CH 196
#TITLE: Paying the Shao Escort Agency a Foul-Mooded Visit
Startled to see the Prefect of Lin'an kneeling outside the front gate, the gatekeepers dared not budge him and sprinted for Butler Li, who hurried straight to the main courtyard. "Your Highness, Prefect Zhu is kneeling outside our gate for some reason, and the guards dare not question him!"
Still sitting grimly with the yellow decree in hand, Shen Jianchen called out, "Invite Prefect Zhu inside."
Right as Zhu Xiyan had calculated, Butler Li emerged moments later to summon him. Rising and following him to the prince's quarters, Zhu Xiyan saw Prince Yi holding the imperial scroll with an unreadable face and dropped straight back to his knees. "This humble official Zhu Xiyan begs Your Highness's forgiveness!"
"What offense have you committed, Prefect Zhu?" Shen Jianchen asked evenly.
Bowing his head low, Zhu Xiyan answered, "My daughter caused a grave incident—all due to my poor parenting. I beg Your Highness's pardon!"
Realizing he meant today's raid, Shen Jianchen replied, "Today's commotion was my own doing and involves no one else. Do not blame yourself, Prefect."
A huge breath of relief eased Zhu Xiyan's chest—thank heaven for a fair-minded prince! "Your Highness's graciousness shames me even more. Aside from today's apology, I will return another day with the Chen family to offer formal amends."
Catching the hint that the Chens might try smoothing things over with lavish gifts, Shen Jianchen's face darkened. Could silver measure his heart for Qianli Xun?! "I already said no one else is to blame. If the Chens truly wish to make amends, hand Youqi Jushi back to me! If they cannot, keep out of my sight—looking at them only sets my teeth on edge."
Startled by the sudden chill, Zhu Xiyan pressed his brow down. "Forgive my presumption, Your Highness!"
"Go. I want peace and quiet—do not disturb me."
Bowing out backward, Zhu Xiyan straightened outside the gate, swiped sweat off his brow, and smiled in sheer relief. False alarm! Not only did Prince Yi take full blame without dragging others down, His Majesty's decree had arrived right on cue to calm the storm for both the princess and the Zhu and Chen families. If Zhu Xiyan had felt a brief pang over missing royal in-laws before, that regret died dead today: hearing from escort servants how fragile-looking Princess Jiahe had thrown herself onto a sword proved what a ruthless mistress she would make—marrying Shuzhen into the Chen household had been far wiser.
Hearing from Butler Li that Imperial Attendant Liang had arrived from Bianliang with a decree, Xilan guessed Her Highness's letter had worked and dashed straight into her courtyard shouting, "Good news, Your Highness! Imperial Attendant Liang came!"
Energy surging back through her, Qiao Mengrong sat straight up ignoring her bandage. "Quick, dress me so I can see Imperial Attendant Liang!"
Steadying her, Xilan shook her head. "Butler Li says he delivered the decree and rode right back to Bianliang."
"Gone already?" Disappointment flashed before eagerness took over. "What did the decree say?"
"Need you ask? Advancing the wedding date, of course—Your Highness gets your wish at last!"
Xilan's words worked better than any tonic; pain faded and appetite roared back. "I am starving—bring food!"
"Right away! Lie back a minute while I fetch dinner."
"No, I want to sit up." Yielding, Xilan hurried to the kitchen.
Meanwhile, after wandering Lin'an, Bai Feng had stopped by Chen Manor hoping to see Kuqiao—only for the gatekeeper to neither know Kuqiao nor let any servant out on wedding day, slamming the gate in his face. Looking into inns, fifty copper coins a night felt outrageous when he was pinching every copper to marry Kuqiao, so by dusk he crept back to Youlin Retreat torn between kneeling at the willow or gambling His Highness had forgotten the punishment.
Approaching the prince's yard and hearing fist drills inside, he eased open the gate—only for Prince Yi to spot him at once. "Where did you vanish to all day, Bai Feng?"
*Jackpot—he forgot!* "Your Highness asks me?! You ordered me to kneel beneath the Divine Willow!"
Remembering now, Shen Jianchen threw another crisp punch. "Then why crawl back here? March right back to the willow!"
Kicking himself for opening his big mouth, Bai Feng pleaded, "Spare me, Your Highness! Let me kneel right here in the yard all night—beats the willow!"
With bigger storms roaring in his chest, Shen Jianchen had zero appetite for punishing his guard. Bottled-up rage over the failed raid and the forced decree had nowhere to go; hitting air wasn't cutting it. Dropping his stance, he asked, "In the mood for a real brawl, Bai Feng?"
Grinning wide at being let off the hook, Bai Feng cheered, "Always! Point the target, Your Highness!"
Remembering Lin Jue's report about Jurchen raiders hitting the Shao Escort Agency—something he had sidelined over personal drama—Shen Jianchen decided tonight was perfect for paying them a visit. "Shao Escort Agency."
"I pass their gate all the time!" Bai Feng lit up. "Folks say everyone inside is a blade master—always wanted to test them! Do we slip into black stealth suits and masks and sneak over the wall?"
"What nonsense! Are we sneaking thieves? No masks, no black suits—we walk straight through the front door!"
Heading out, they ran right into Qiao Mengrong leaning on Xilan's arm outside the gate.
"Your wound is fresh—why are you out walking?" Shen Jianchen asked gently.
"It barely hurts—just worried about Brother," Qiao Mengrong murmured. "Where are you heading?"
"Errands," he deflected, keeping the escort agency to himself.
Knowing Zhu Shuzhen had married into the Chen family today, Mengrong breathed easier—at least he wasn't chasing her! "I heard Imperial Attendant Liang came from Bianliang today—what was the decree?"
"His Majesty commanded us back to Bianliang within half a month to complete our wedding," Shen Jianchen answered plainly.
Quiet triumph glowed across both princess and maid. "How eager His Majesty is—shows how deeply he holds Brother in his heart."
Unwilling to linger, Shen Jianchen instructed Xilan, "Help Her Highness back inside to rest and stop her from wandering."
Docile as a lamb now, Qiao Mengrong turned back inside while Shen Jianchen and Bai Feng drove their carriage out into the night toward Shao Escort Agency.
The biggest escort outfit in Lin'an boasted two to three hundred armed guards under Master Shao Zhengye, renowned for his broadsword skill, alongside his seventeen- or eighteen-year-old handsome son Shao Zhize, heir to the family blade. Proud of standing on pure martial grit without sucking up to officials or tycoons—even skipping today's Chen wedding—the Shaos stood aloof from Lin'an's elite circles, happy to be left alone to build their trade.
Pulling up outside their gate, Bai Feng pounded the wood and barked at the guard who opened up, "Go tell your boss my young master is here to challenge Shao Escort Agency!"
Unfazed, the guard hollered inward, "Brothers! Challengers at the door—come greet them!"
Within moments, a dozen broadsword-wielding escorts blocked the entrance. "Looking for a fight?" their leader sneered. "Get past us first!"
More than enough for Bai Feng—Shen Jianchen folded his arms to watch. Eager for action, Bai Feng flashed forward and planted a punch dead on one guard's jaw before anyone blinked, dropping man and blade to the stones. As the rest ringed him with gleaming steel, Bai Feng laughed, "Think bare fists lose to blades? Come all at once!"
As blades slashed inward, Bai Feng vaulted with light-foot skill straight onto the flat of their broadswords, pinning their arms down before unleashing a blur of sweeping kicks that sent men and steel clattering everywhere. "Just you bunch? Save your breath!"
Seeing Prince Yi stroll casually inside the main yard, Bai Feng followed straight into a wider ring of dozens more armed escorts. Outnumbered now, Bai Feng welcomed his prince stepping beside him. Bare fists against flashing steel, both dodged razor edges by a hair and struck crisp non-lethal knockout blows. With every guard dropped, the knot of fury in Shen Jianchen's chest loosened notch by notch until the last man fell and real clarity washed over him: Master Jingguang had been right—follow fate, never force it. Qianli Xun was married now, maybe already in Chen Haoyu's chamber; railing against destiny changed nothing. Accept the hand dealt him, wish her well from afar, and hold his true love quietly in his heart.
Heart settled, time for business.
Watching from the porch throughout the melee, Shao Zhengye and Shao Zhize had noticed two vital clues: first, neither intruder aimed to maim; second, their fist style looked uncannily like Yan Chi's! Were they his allies?
Stepping forward with cupped hands as the last guard groaned on the stones, Shao Zhengye spoke evenly. "We have never met nor crossed paths, Young Master. Why raid our Shao compound?"
"Not raiding—just testing Shao martial skill," Shen Jianchen answered coolly. "Turns out it is nothing special—beats me how you built such a big name in the martial world."
Shao Zhize bristled ready to charge, yet his father raised a hand to stay him. "The rivers and lakes teem with masters; beating us today means someone stronger awaits you tomorrow. We practice blades to guard caravans and keep fit, not chase hollow fame."
A poised answer that took the edge off further taunts. "Smoothly spoken, Master Shao—respect. Enough banter; I came to ask about something serious."
"Ask freely, Young Master."
"I hear a band of Jurchens raided Shao Escort Agency. Where are they now, and why did they come looking for trouble here?"
Shao Zhengye blinked inwardly—so this man wasn't allied with Yan Chi after all! Then how on earth did they share the exact same fist art?!
#CH 197
#TITLE: Qiao Wenxian After the Wedding Banquet
Studying the tall young man before him—fine robes, noble bearing—Shao Zhengye guessed he was no common citizen, perhaps a high Lin'an official sent after Division Commander Lin reported the Jurchen raid. Cupping his hands, he asked politely, "May I ask Young Master's name?"
Seeing no need to hide, Shen Jianchen nodded to Bai Feng, who announced with pride: "Standing before you is His Majesty's thirteenth son, Prince Yi!"
Neither father nor son had ever dreamed Military Commissioner Prince Yi himself would step through their gate—let alone pack martial skill like that! Thrilled and awed, both dropped to their knees. "Ignorant of Your Highness's identity, we gave grave offense—please pardon us!"
"Sparring with your men stretched my stiff limbs—no offense taken. Rise and tell me about those Jurchens."
Rising at once, Shao Zhengye ushered Prince Yi into the main hall, served fine tea, and stood respectfully alongside his son.
Once Prince Yi sipped, father and son traded a quick look before Shao Zhengye answered, "We do not know those Jurchens' true backing either—only that they were no ordinary traders. Likely they got wind of our Shao family's anti-Jurchen sentiment and a few Jurchens we killed in the past, and came for revenge."
At the words "anti-Jurchen," Shen Jianchen's eyes lit up. "Patriots after my own heart—consider yourself my friend!"
A royal prince calling him friend thrilled Shao Zhengye—even as guilt pricked him over telling only half the truth about a family secret best kept quiet. Bowing alongside his son, he said, "Your Highness's favor honors our entire house. One more detail I must mention..."
"Skip the formal reporting—friends sit and talk!" Having longed to befriend bold heroes—failing to meet Mount Liang's outlaws, anti-Jurchen fighters were next best—Shen Jianchen waved him to a chair while young Shao Zhize remained standing respectfully behind his father.
"Seeing Your Highness's extraordinary fist technique tonight amazed me," Shao Zhengye said carefully. "What struck me strangest is that your fist style matches the Jurchen leader who raided us almost stroke for stroke."
"Wait—really?!" Shen Jianchen froze. He was the 360th-generation heir of Shen Fist—nearly a thousand years separated the Northern Song from his own era, so how could a Jurchen here know Shen Fist?! "Where are those Jurchens now?"
"He led seven or eight men raiding us twice," Shao Zhengye explained. "The second time, my son slew all seven or eight in the fight. I sliced the leader myself, yet his light-foot skill was astonishing—he escaped, and my son chased him into a maze of lanes where he vanished."
"Which alley?" Shen Jianchen meant to track him down himself—wounded, the man could not have run far.
"Mihun Alley—literally 'Bewildering Alley'—not far from Master Jiang's gambling house. By the name alone, Your Highness can tell it is easy to enter and brutal to escape, especially after dark. That Jurchen stumbled inside at night and my son had to call off the hunt."
"People live in Mihun Alley?"
"Every walk of life—only longtime locals know their way through its twists."
Shao Zhize chimed in, "That night I chased him inside, came up empty, and even got lost trying to back out—had to knock on a random door and bribe a resident with silver to lead us out."
Making a mental note to check Mihun Alley—and catching the mention of Master Jiang! Twice in one day that underworld boss's name had crossed his path, the exact bastard who forced Chen Haoyu into rushing today's wedding and ripping Qianli Xun away! That man was definitely catching his fists. "Who is this Master Jiang you mentioned?"
"Boss of the Qiantang Gang over on the far east side," Shao Zhengye explained. "Half villain, half rogue—owns over half Lin'an's gambling dens and takes dirty jobs for silver, yet sometimes hands out relief to the poor."
Rogue or saint, anyone who messed up Shen Jianchen's life was getting tuned up. With night deepening, he rose to take his leave. "Much obliged for the briefing, Master Shao. Forgive the late intrusion—drop by Youlin Retreat another day for a real drinking session!"
"Honored beyond words, Your Highness! I will call with proper gifts soon—and if Your Highness ever needs the Shao Escort Agency, just send word."
Walking out to his carriage, Shen Jianchen felt rare warmth over his blunt new friend—after months surrounded by dreamy poets, spineless emperors, and corrupt ministers, a straight-shooting martial man felt like fresh mountain air.
Watching the carriage roll away, Shao Zhize murmured uneasily, "Father, hiding the full truth from Prince Yi—won't he be furious if he finds out later?"
Shao Zhengye let out a heavy sigh. "Hide it while we can. That brat Wanyan Chi should stay clear for a spell—out of regard for your aunt, I let him off light. Dare show his face here again, and my blade ends his life!"
