Meanwhile, after the Chen wedding banquet ended, Qiao Wenxian rode back to Qiao Manor sharing a carriage with both wife and concubine—looking enviable from outside, hollow to the bone inside. On his own wedding night, grief had kept him out of both women's rooms, leaving them both sleepless till dawn.
Flushed red with cloudy eyes, Qiao Wenxian let out a loud hiccup, filling the cab with stale wine fumes. Miao Zhiyu pinched her nose while Xiaguo lifted the window curtain for fresh air.
Slurring bitterly, Qiao Wenxian chuckled, "Now you two see... I am no prize... still time to regret... *hic*..."
Dropping her hand politely at his second hiccup, Miao Zhiyu replied gently, "What talk is that, Wenyuan? I am already your wife—no matter how you are, I would never look down on you."
Hiding a brief smirk, Xiaguo beamed right back. "Stop talking nonsense—Sister Miao and I could never turn up our noses at you!" Sliding straight against him, she tilted her nose right near his mouth. "See? Not disgusted one bit—hiccup again so I can smell it!"
Through wine-blurred vision, Xiaguo's face pressing close shifted into Shuzhen's features! A soft smile broke over Qiao Wenxian's face as he raised a hand to touch her cheek—only for her face to vanish with a jolt!
Disgusted by Xiaguo flaunting herself shamelessly right before her eyes, Miao Zhiyu had forgotten lady-like restraint and shoved Xiaguo sideways! Xiaguo's forehead bumped the cab wall hard enough to rock the carriage. Rubbing her bruised temple, she snapped, "What is wrong with you, Sister Miao—trying to cripple me?!"
Sitting stiffly upright, Miao Zhiyu rebuked her. "As a concubine, know proper conduct outside the house!"
Rubbing away the sting, Xiaguo shot right back, "We are inside a closed Qiao carriage with zero outsiders—just the three of us! Cannot I show a little affection to my own husband?!"
"Since when do you have the right to call him 'husband'?!" Miao Zhiyu snapped.
Eyes squeezed shut against the noise, Qiao Wenxian barked, "Shut up, both of you!"
Switching tones on a dime, Xiaguo cooed, "I listen to Wenyuan—I will not argue with Sister Miao, long as she stops picking fights with me."
Clamping her lips shut, Miao Zhiyu seethed inwardly—she could never stoop to that cheap flirtiness! Back when Xiaguo served Youqi Jushi she had looked quiet enough—how fast people showed their claws! Wait: thinking of Youqi Jushi brought back Prince Yi's raid on the wedding today! Telling Wenyuan would kill his lingering devotion to Youqi Jushi AND rub salt in Xiaguo's pride over her former mistress!
Steadying her voice, Miao Zhiyu spoke smoothly. "Wenyuan, I heard astonishing news today about Youqi Jushi—still can hardly believe it."
At the name "Youqi Jushi," Qiao Wenxian's eyes snapped open, sobering half a shade. "What news?"
Curious yet unbothered, Xiaguo listened too.
"During the Chen bridal procession today, someone raided the street to snatch the bride..."
Sobering fast, Qiao Wenxian demanded, "Who?!"
A guess flashed in Xiaguo's mind before she voiced the same word: "Who?!"
Reading his utter disbelief that any other man could love Shuzhen like he did, Miao Zhiyu dropped two crisp words: "Prince Yi."
"Rubbish!" Qiao Wenxian barked at once. "How could Prince Yi snatch a bride when he is betrothed to Princess Jiahe?!"
Suspicions confirmed, Xiaguo held her tongue and watched Wenyuan close—hoping this bomb would blast her old mistress clean out of his heart so she stood a real shot!
"Half the street saw it!" Miao Zhiyu pressed smoothly. "Word is Princess Jiahe put her own life on the line and took a serious blade wound before Prince Yi backed down—otherwise the Chens would have had a groom with no bride! If you doubt me, ride to Youlin Retreat and ask Prince Yi yourself, or check Princess Jiahe's wound."
The shock hit like a bucket of ice water, wiping every drop of wine from Qiao Wenxian's brain. Speechless, he remembered how detailed Miao Zhiyu's tale sounded—far too specific to invent! When had Prince Yi fallen for Shuzhen?! Before he and Shuzhen broke up, or after?!
Snapping his eyes onto Xiaguo—her former personal maid—he demanded, "You stayed by Shuzhen's side all day! Tell me straight: when did Prince Yi start chasing Shuzhen?!"
Seeing no point hiding now, Xiaguo laid it out. "I do not know the exact day—only back when rumors flooded the city and the Qiaos stopped calling, leaving Miss devastated right before breaking up with you, Prince Yi visited Wangyue Pavilion several times—sometimes with Princess Jiahe, sometimes alone—and I caught on he had fallen for her..."
Disappointed she hadn't rattled Xiaguo, Miao Zhiyu nonetheless felt unexpected warmth toward the girl—shared enemies made smooth allies, and Xiaguo clearly wanted Youqi Jushi wiped out of Wenyuan's mind just as badly!
Having prayed Xiaguo would deny it, Qiao Wenxian froze as she pinned the exact timeline to his own hunger strike—the very days he had begged Prince Yi to carry messages to Shuzhen! Playing messenger on the surface while harboring private schemes underneath?!
Unable to sit still another breath, Qiao Wenxian yelled out the window, "To Youlin Retreat!"
Stepping in smoothly, Miao Zhiyu urged, "No need to rush tonight—Princess Jiahe just got wounded so Youlin Retreat is in chaos, and dusk is already falling. Go tomorrow morning instead."
Burning inside yet seeing her logic, Qiao Wenxian stayed silent. Catching his mute consent, Miao Zhiyu called out, "Back to Qiao Manor."
Leaning back against the swaying cab amidst street clamor, Qiao Wenxian replayed every favor Prince Yi had done for him and Shuzhen, tormenting himself with one question—*When did it start?*—and finding zero clues.
#CH 198
#TITLE: An Early Morning Visit from Young Master Qiao
At first light the next morning, Qiao Wenxian hurried to Youlin Retreat—his very first visit since his wedding—and strode straight into Prince Yi's courtyard. Inside, maids were clearing breakfast dishes while Prince Yi sat sipping morning tea at ease.
Shen Jianchen greeted him warmly. "Thought wife and concubine had wiped me clean out of your mind and you would never darken my door again!"
Giving a stiff, formal bow without a word, Qiao Wenxian took a side seat with an expressionless face.
"Pour tea, Bai Feng," Shen Jianchen instructed. Serving a bowl to Young Master Qiao, Bai Feng caught Nan Shuang's discreet signal and stepped outside with him to catch up after days apart.
"Why the silent treatment?" Shen Jianchen asked.
Fixing him with a steady stare, Qiao Wenxian countered, "Why did Your Highness skip the Chen wedding feast yesterday?"
Word of the raid was clearly all over town—and by Wenyuan's face, that was why he came. Bold enough to snatch a bride, Shen Jianchen saw no reason to dodge now. "Since you already know, why make me repeat it?"
"When did it start?"
"Saying a long, long time ago is not quite right—more like a long, long time in the future. I fell for her—for that bright soul inside."
Baffled, Qiao Wenxian frowned. "Are you telling me you loved Shuzhen in another world?"
"You could put it that way."
Dismissing it as another slick fairy tale—just like the yarn Prince Yi once spun mocking His Majesty—Qiao Wenxian felt bitter grief rise: plain as day, Prince Yi had fallen for her BEFORE they even broke up! "When you offered to help Shuzhen and me, were you ever sincere?"
"Before I realized she was Youqi Jushi, yes. Afterward... not so much."
Confused by his distinction between "her" and Shuzhen, Qiao Wenxian pressed past it. "When did you stop being willing?"
"The very first time you and Youqi Jushi met secretly by West Lake, I realized she was the woman I loved."
Refusing to buy the tall tale, Qiao Wenxian scoffed painfully. "Always spinning fancy stories to dress up stealing a friend's girl! Better just admit straight out you fancied her before we broke up—at least I would respect your candor!"
"Fine. Yes—I loved her before you two broke up."
Hearing the flat admission twisted the blade deeper. "Then why did you still try talking my father into proposing to the Zhus?!"
Shen Jianchen let out a low sigh. "Even though I loved her, her whole heart and eyes belonged to you back then. I wanted her happy—gritted my teeth and leaned on Grandfather anyway."
"Tell me the truth," Qiao Wenxian pressed, dread burning in his eyes. "The second time you spoke to my father—did you hold back?"
"Not entirely..."
Squeezing his eyes shut against the stab—losing his love AND his friend both?!—he snapped them open. "So... you DID hold back?!"
"You saw Grandfather threatening to kill himself—what could I do? And yes, loving her as I did, part of me thanked Heaven for handing me an excuse not to help you..."
Snapping at last, Qiao Wenxian leaped up, strode straight across, and seized Prince Yi by the collar! "While I was starving myself fighting my family—did you deliberately refuse to carry messages between Shuzhen and me?!"
Rising calmly to meet his bloodshot gaze—feeling barely a tug from the scholar's grip, letting him vent—Shen Jianchen owned it flat. "Yes. Your road with her was dead, and I hoped I would be the man who ended up with her."
"You have an imperial betrothal—you are no better off than me! What right did you have to be with her?!" Qiao Wenxian twisted the silk tighter.
"Right or no right means nothing to either of us now," Shen Jianchen replied quietly. "She married Chen Haoyu. All either of us can do is bow to fate." Comforting Wenyuan and himself in the same breath.
"Bow to fate" hit like cold water. Shuzhen was gone—zero hope left—so what joke was he playing brawling with Prince Yi? Loosening his fists from the collar, Qiao Wenxian collapsed against Prince Yi's chest and wept aloud: "Wuuu! Even YOU carrying her off would have been better than handing her to Chen Haoyu! Wuuu!"
Patting his shaking back gently, Shen Jianchen murmured, "Real men do not shed tears easy—stop bawling before someone laughs. Circumstances forced her to leave you, then barred her from choosing me—fate toys with us all. I threw everything I had at it, nearly cost Mengrong her life, and still came up empty. Right on cue yesterday arrived His Majesty's decree ordering me back to Bianliang within half a month to wed—like invisible strings pulling every move. The nun up the hill told me: *Follow fate, never force it.* I forced things too hard before; maybe letting fate steer will turn a dream true someday—she also told me *Wait through the clouds, and you will see the bright moon.*"
Half the speech drifted past Qiao Wenxian, yet it dried his tears—and one phrase snagged sharp in his ears: "Wait—what did you mean saying Shuzhen was 'barred from choosing you'?!"
Guessing Wenyuan dreaded hearing Shuzhen had truly moved on, Shen Jianchen figured a clean cut would finally set him free. "Because she and Mengrong are closest friends. Refusing to hurt Mengrong, she turned me down over and over and willingly married into the Chen family..."
Pulling back with pain flashing through wet eyes, Qiao Wenxian whispered, "You mean... she fell in love with you?"
Qianli Xun had only admitted feelings right after crossing over; how deep they ran today, Shen Jianchen could not be 100% certain—yet he hoped so, and wanted Wenyuan to stop chasing ghosts. "Yes. She fell in love with me."
Shoving Prince Yi back hard, Qiao Wenxian choked out, "Liar!" Spinning away, he stumbled blindly out the doorway muttering "Liar! Liar!" under his breath—forgetting even Nan Shuang as he boarded his carriage in a daze and rattled out of Youlin Retreat. Shen Jianchen did not chase him; sooner or later Wenyuan had to face reality and move forward.
Up on the wooden watch-platform outside, Bai Feng and Nan Shuang had been trading updates on Kuqiao and Youfan ("Quit eyeing Xiaguo—she is your master's woman now!" / "Don't worry, I am not that stupid!") when Nan Shuang spotted his master bolting out the gate solo. Sprinting down the stairs too late to catch the departing carriage, he hitched a ride from a friendly Youlin Retreat driver to chase him down.
Coming down to find his master, Bai Feng saw Prince Yi stepping out toward Princess Jiahe's courtyard and fell into step behind him.
Inside her quarters, the imperial decree had acted like miracle medicine on Qiao Mengrong: her wound felt worlds lighter, breakfast went down clean, and while waiting for Qiao Wenxian to leave before seeing Brother, she browsed a volume of contemporary Song verse at her desk.
Flipping pages, her eye caught a quatrain titled *"Impromptu Upon a View"* (*Duijing Mancheng*). Drawn by its charm, she read it aloud:
"Half a window of dying light, a single curtain of wind;
A modest pond pavilion where a bamboo path leads through.
Maple leaves flush drunk with red amid the autumn hues;
Two or three lines of wild geese cross the evening sun."
[TN: Translator's rendering]
Savoring every character, Qiao Mengrong praised it brightly: "What a crisp autumn sunset picture! 'Curtain' links the autumn breeze right to a lady's boudoir longing; 'leads through' threads pond, pavilion, and bamboo grove together while sketching a straight, deep trail; 'drunk' brings the maple leaves alive like a shy maiden blushing in love; and 'two or three lines' sweeps wide, airy height across the sky!"
"Hearing the lines alone I missed half of it," Xilan marveled, "yet once Your Highness unpacks them, it glows!"
"Only fine poetry yields fine reading—wonder who wrote it?" Glancing at the signature line, four tiny characters jumped out: *Youqi Jushi*.
Every spark of delight died on the spot as she flung the book aside! "Even my eyes go blind sometimes—wasting praise on verse she does not deserve!" Rage pulled sharp pain across her bandaged side; clutching her left flank with knotted brows, she barked, "Xilan, flip through that book and tear out every single poem by her—no! Take the whole book outside and burn it!"
Guessing the author at once, Xilan scooped the volume out into the yard, struck a fire-starter, and watched it curl to black ash before reporting back to find her mistress's breathing calmer.
Dropping her hand from her side, Qiao Mengrong ordered coldly, "I collected stacks of her poems and lyrics before. Search my entire room today, pull out every single one, and burn the lot! I refuse to look at a single brushstroke tied to her under my roof!"
"Yes, Your Highness."
Just as Xilan began rummaging shelves, Shen Jianchen walked in. Stopping her search to curtsy politely, Xilan stepped back in silence lest His Highness ask what she was clearing out.
Brightening at her brother's arrival—staying seated lest rising awkwardly tempt him to delay their trip to Bianliang—Qiao Mengrong smiled. "I was just about to visit Brother, and here you are!"
"How does the wound feel today?"
"Much better! Cannot wait to ride back to Bianliang with Brother."
Heart settled on following fate, Shen Jianchen nodded. "Once you mend, we head back to Bianliang for the wedding."
"It barely hurts—we can leave anytime!"
After a brief pause, he said, "His Majesty gave us half a month; plenty of time. Rest two more days—we depart the day after tomorrow."
"Two days from now I will be good as new!" Mengrong beamed.
Forcing a thin smile, Shen Jianchen told Xilan to tend her well and turned to go—only for Mengrong to call out, "Where is Brother heading?"
Without turning back, he answered, "There is an underworld boss in Lin'an called Master Jiang who crossed me. Going to pay him a visit."
Long as he wasn't sneaking off to Zhu Shuzhen, she felt at peace. "Be careful and come back early, Brother."
Once he left with Bai Feng, Mengrong turned ice-cold to Xilan. "Keep searching—even if a sheet only bears the four characters 'Youqi Jushi,' burn it! Wipe her clean out of my life!"
"Yes, Your Highness."
Left brooding, memories of sharing a bed whispering secrets and warming hands by a brazier with Shuzhen flooded back—never again would she trust a friend like that. She hated Zhu Shuzhen not merely for stealing Brother's heart, yet for murdering their cherished sisterhood.
Soon Xilan hauled out armfuls of volumes with several servants and set a tall bonfire in the yard. Watching flames dance through her window, others saw burning paper; Qiao Mengrong watched her friendship turn to ash.
#CH 199
#TITLE: Serving Morning Tea on the First Day of Marriage
On her first morning as a Chen bride, Qianli Xun rose at dawn, dressed cleanly, finished breakfast, and waited to go pay respects to the family elders—waiting and waiting with zero sign of Chen Haoyu.
"Kuqiao, check the back courtyard and tell Chen Haoyu to shake a leg."
Rounding into the back yard to knock on the right-hand door, Kuqiao called out, "Young Master, awake yet? Miss wants you to greet the elders together."
Qinggua cracked the door wide with a grimace. "Dead to the world—cannot drag him up!"
"Alright, I will tell Miss."
Moments later, Qianli Xun marched straight into the back yard and slammed the door wide open! "Chen Haoyu! Out of bed NOW—stay down and I punch you awake!"
Chen Haoyu shot bolt upright clutching the quilt to his chest. "Back off! I am naked under here—do not want a tigress eyeballing me!"
Beside the bed, Qinggua fought back laughter—he had yelled and even kicked the bed to no avail, yet one bark from Young Madam jolted him wide awake!
Spinning around to face the wall, Qianli Xun scoffed, "Like I care to look at your scrawny hide! I count to ten—be dressed and standing before me. One... two... three..."
With Qinggua shoving robes onto him at lightning speed, Chen Haoyu stood fully dressed at "nine."
"Good," Qianli Xun nodded. "I do not know Chen Manor's layout—lead the way to the elders."
"If I knew marriage meant losing my beauty sleep, I would never have bothered!" Chen Haoyu whined.
"Think I wanted to marry you?! You knelt begging me—only pitied you enough to agree!"
Catching Qinggua snickering on the side, Chen Haoyu snapped, "Quit airing my dirty laundry in front of servants!"
"No need to air what hangs out in plain sight—every servant here sees it, just too polite to say! Move it!"
"Wait, Young Madam—Young Master has not had breakfast!" Qinggua piped up.
"Skipping one meal will not kill him."
Shutting his mouth, Qinggua trailed behind them. Stepping past Matron Wei's shut door across the yard, Chen Haoyu jeered, "Look—your precious matron is still sleeping in! Want me to go bang her door down too?"
"Think everyone is a lazy hog like you? Matron Wei was up before dawn heading off to transfer all your assets into my name!"
Regret pricking him all over again over signing away his fortune, Chen Haoyu swallowed his grumble and kept walking.
Picking up Kuqiao at the main hall, they reached the courtyard gate just as Yao Qingyan's personal maid Yingxue curtsied up. "Young Master, Young Madam—Madam sent me to guide you."
"Mother really loves me—sparing me even guide duty!" Chen Haoyu cheered.
Ignoring him, Qianli Xun nodded politely. "Much obliged, Yingxue."
Under her red veil yesterday she had seen zero of Chen Manor; walking through now, she admired its bright, open elegance—best of all, main paths ran dead straight in neat squares, curving only inside gardens, pure mercy for someone with terrible sense of direction. Yet the quiet stroll pulled yesterday's nightmare back: Mengrong's stinging slap, lunging toward steel... One slap had declared their friendship dead. Bearing no grudge, Qianli Xun swore silently never to harm Mengrong again; live calmly inside Chen Manor, stay clear of Prince Yi, and maybe someday their sisterhood would heal. In six months or a year, let Chen Haoyu take a concubine to bear Chen heirs while she enjoyed peace and total freedom!
Arriving at her in-laws' courtyard, where a smiling door boy announced them inside, Yingxue gestured ahead—prompting Chen Haoyu to snap, "Walked into my parents' house a thousand times—say 'after you' to HER, not me!" Smiling smoothly, Yingxue pivoted to usher Young Madam across the threshold.
Inside sat more elders than expected: Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan in the seats of honor, two men to the left, three women to the right. Stepping forward, Qianli Xun sank into a crisp, graceful curtsy. "Your daughter-in-law pays respects to Father-in-Law, Mother-in-Law, and all elders."
Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan beamed in quiet delight—every inch a polished noble daughter today, worlds away from the rock-smashing terror at their gate! Catching his son standing slack beside her without bowing, Chen Jianglin's face went stone cold; Yao Qingyan shot her son a frantic glance until Chen Haoyu muttered a hasty bow: "Father, Mother, your son greets you."
Softening at once lest his bride feel unwelcome, Chen Jianglin smiled and introduced the room. "Meet the Chen elders: on your left sit your Second Uncle and Third Uncle."
Turning left, Qianli Xun greeted them smoothly. "Greetings, Second Uncle and Third Uncle." Both smiled back.
"And on the right," Yao Qingyan added, "your two aunts and Eldest Sister-in-Law."
"Shuzhen greets both aunts and Eldest Sister-in-Law," Qianli Xun curtsied cleanly.
Both aunts nodded while Eldest Sister-in-Law Ye Anya rose warmly to take her hands. "Long have I heard of Zhu Manor's celebrated poetess and hoped to meet you—now we are sisters under one roof! Whenever you pen fresh poems or lyrics, you must let me admire them!"
Giving the textbook modest answer, Qianli Xun smiled. "Merely empty fame outside, Sister-in-Law. If you do not mind my clumsy brush, I will send a poem over soon—hardly worthy of admiration, yet worth merit if it eases a dull afternoon."
Watching from the side, Chen Haoyu rolled his eyes inside: *What a phony! Acting sweet, refined, not one swaggering boast out of her mouth—total hypocrite!*
"Gorgeous, gifted, and silver-tongued—Haoyu struck gold!" Ye Anya laughed before returning to her seat for the tea rite.
Briefed by Matron Wei the night before, Qianli Xun accepted a lacquer tray holding two bowls of tea from a maid, knelt gracefully, and raised it. "Please take tea, Father-in-Law and Mother-in-Law." Maids conveyed the bowls to Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan, who took ceremonial sips. Following suit, she presented fresh bowls to both uncles, then both aunts—skipping peer-generation Ye Anya.
Rites finished, Yao Qingyan said, "Haoyu, take Shuzhen to pay respects to Old Madam."
Remembering Old Madam Xia's fury on her earlier raid—unlikely to go as smooth as her in-laws—Qianli Xun resolved to keep polite and low-key on day one.
Hardly had they cleared the courtyard gate before Chen Haoyu sneered, "Quit the fake act, Zhu Shuzhen! Got guts? Pull your tigress act in front of the elders so they see the real monster!"
Stopping dead, Qianli Xun tilted her head and glared sideways. "Nobody thinks you are mute if you keep your jaw shut! Hear me straight: BOTH faces are real! Treat me warm like your family does, and I return every ounce of grace; act like slime like YOU do, and out comes the tigress! Besides, your parents and grandmother already saw me wreck the gate—who am I fooling?!" She marched right ahead.
"Slime like me?!" Chen Haoyu yelled after her. "What kind of man do you think I am?!"
Spinning on her heel and stepping right into his nose, fists planted on hips and bright eyes blazing wide, Qianli Xun unloaded every single scathing four-character epithet in her vocabulary in one breathless volley: "A spoiled dandy! Libertine rake! Bully of decent women! Brothel hound! Chief of scoundrels! Tyrant of the streets! Compulsive gambler! Sanctimonious fraud! Habitual crook! Arrogant fool! Idle loafer! Gluttonous sluggard! Shameless creep! Shortsighted dimwit! Slick-tongued liar! Pompous peacock! Smug idiot! Ignorant dunce!..." Stopping only when out of breath, she gasped, "Done!"
Hit by a lexical avalanche, Chen Haoyu blinked half-furious, half-awed by a poetess's insult range—*Am I REALLY that awful?!*—while Qinggua and Kuqiao burst into muffled giggles behind them.
Stung by the servants laughing too, Chen Haoyu lashed back where it hurt: "Call me all that trash—and yet YOU STILL MARRIED ME! Didn't see Qiao Wenxian or Prince Yi marrying you, did I?!"
The cheap shot landed dead on the raw bruise. Dropping her hands without another syllable, Qianli Xun turned sharply and strode off ahead, Kuqiao hurrying right behind. Scoring one win at last, Chen Haoyu whistled triumphantly down the path—pausing only at a fork when she steered left: "Hey! Left goes to Second Uncle's—right for Grandmother!"
Turning right without a word, Qianli Xun paused outside Old Madam Xia's gate to enter together, only for Chen Haoyu to brush straight past her and dash inside putting on a syrupy baby voice: "Grandmother! Your grandson missed you SO much!"
Goosebumps prickling up her arms, Qianli Xun gritted her teeth and walked inside to see Chen Haoyu throwing himself into Old Madam Xia's lap on the couch—gross enough to turn her stomach! Dropping into a curtsy, she said evenly, "Shuzhen pays respects to Grandmother."
Beaming over her grandson while intentionally freezing out the bride to knock her down a peg, Old Madam Xia ignored her completely. "What a sweet boy, coming bright and early! Sit right down and share tea with Grandmother."
Delighted by the snub, Chen Haoyu perched opposite and laid it on thick. "Heard on the street about a miracle doctor for sore legs—I will bring him over for you soon, Grandmother!"
"My boy is always so thoughtful!"
"Only because Grandmother spoils me best!"
Leaving Qianli Xun standing like a lamp post in the middle of the floor, the two cooed back and forth—killing every flattering phrase Qianli Xun had rehearsed on the walk over. Steadying her breath, she chose patience while Kuqiao fretted silently beside her: lovely in-laws, terrifying grandmother!
"Lean closer—let Grandmother see if you lost weight," Old Madam Xia cooed.
Chen Haoyu leaned in puffing his cheeks out; she tapped them chuckling. "Quit puffing—Grandmother's eyes are sharp!" Dropping his cheeks, he said, "Not thinner—plumper!" Touching his face again, she fretted, "Lying to Grandmother—definitely thinner!"
Seeing a golden opening to sick Grandmother on Zhu Shuzhen, Chen Haoyu sighed dramatically. "No wonder I look gaunt, Grandmother—marched straight over here on an empty stomach without a bite of breakfast!"
Old Madam Xia snapped straight up. "No breakfast?! Qinggua! How are you looking after Young Master, letting him starve?!"
Qinggua stammered helplessly.
"Do not blame Qinggua," Chen Haoyu sighed like a martyr. "Blame me for marrying a terrifying wife—SHE refused to let me eat!"
At last, Old Madam Xia whipped a blistering glare onto Miss Zhu and barked: "KNEEL!"
#CH 200
#TITLE: A Morning Call at the Qiantang Gang
Frozen out for half an hour and barked to kneel on her first line? No way Qianli Xun was eating that! Staying upright, she offered a calm second bow. "Respectfully, Grandmother, do not take only Chen Haoyu's side of the story. I did not deny him breakfast—he slept past the hour for greeting the elders, refused to leave bed, and by the time he dressed, breakfast hour had long passed."
Dead set on breaking the new bride's pride on day one, Old Madam Xia refused to hear reason. "An elder orders you to kneel, and instead of dropping down you talk back?! Hear me plain: inside Chen Manor, my grandson sleeps late when he wants and does as he pleases—who dares police him while I breathe?! Bullying my grandson day one—what next?! I sentence you to kneel in this courtyard for the entire day!"
Chen Haoyu beamed—with Grandmother backing him, Zhu Shuzhen was cooked!
Disgusted by the blind coddling that had rotted Chen Haoyu into a useless rake, Qianli Xun dropped every ounce of meekness. Spotting teapot and bowls on a side table, she strolled calmly over, poured one cup, and carried it in both hands before Old Madam Xia. "Having married into the Chen family and called you Grandmother, I still owe you morning tea."
Old Madam Xia jerked her face away without reaching out.
Setting the bowl calmly on the low table beside her, Qianli Xun said, "Tea is served. Since Grandmother cannot stand the sight of me, I will excuse myself rather than spoil your mood."
With one sharp sweep of her hand, Old Madam Xia knocked the tea bowl crashing off the table, shattering it across the tiles in a splash of hot tea! "Did you go deaf?! I ordered you to kneel in my courtyard all day!"
"Heard you loud and clear, Grandmother—yet I committed zero wrong, so why kneel? Does seniority hand out a blank check to bully juniors at will?!"
Purple with fury, Old Madam Xia bellowed, "Servants!" Four or five maids rushed in. "Hold her down! Make her kneel!"
Remembering the splintered chair at the front gate, every maid froze in her tracks, not one daring to step forward.
Sweeping a cool look over the trembling girls, Qianli Xun scoffed, "Save the show, Old Madam! You already saw what my fists do—one punch drops all five of them, and you expect them to pin ME down? Laughable!" Spinning around, she snapped, "Come on, Kuqiao!"
Pausing at the threshold, she tossed one last volley over her shoulder: "Take three truths from me, Old Madam: ventures fail through laziness, men rot through idleness, and houses ruin through indulgence. If you truly love your grandson, quit spoiling him—lest the day he burns the entire Chen fortune to ash, you have nowhere left to cry!"
Striding out with head high, she left silence behind her.
Pointing at the empty doorway, Chen Haoyu cried, "Look how cocky she is, Grandmother—ignoring even YOU!"
To his utter shock, a warm chuckle broke across Old Madam Xia's face. "Weren't you the one begging to marry her yesterday? Turning sour already?"
Chen Haoyu blinked in total confusion. "Grandmother—you aren't mad?! You are LAUGHING?!"
"The whole tantrum was an act—just wanted to test her nerve and knock her down a peg so she goes easy on you! Listening to those sharp words, she has a clear head and will not bully you without cause. Better still, standing dead steady under my bullying—spitting image of me in my youth! I actually like her! Since you wanted her, settle down and live right with her, and stay out of filthy haunts!"
Floored that Zhu Shuzhen had won Grandmother over by wrecking her tea ritual, Chen Haoyu whined, "Got a granddaughter-in-law and stopped loving your grandson already?!"
"Nonsense—Grandmother loves you most."
"If she hits me, whose side are you on?"
"Yours, of course."
Chatting a few minutes more, Qianli Xun's warning about burning the Chen fortune jolted him back to Master Jiang's contract! Bidding Grandmother goodbye, he marched straight out to settle with the Qiantang Gang.
At Chen Manor's gate, spotting Qinggua trailing him, he whirled with a sudden kick straight into his belly! "Who asked you to tail me?! Beat it—important business!"
Doubled over clutching his gut, Qinggua wheezed, "Ow! Young Madam ordered me to stick with you—got to obey her!"
"Say that again! WHOSE orders do you take?!"
"Young Master's too, of course!"
"Then SCRAM!" Ditching Qinggua behind, Chen Haoyu swaggered out the gate straight into Scarface and his lackeys lurking outside.
Remembering them turning tail like cowards in the gambling house, Chen Haoyu snapped, "Got the nerve to show your faces after ditching me?! Find another boss!"
"Mercy, Young Master Chen!" Scarface grinned sheepishly. "Master Jiang had a whole army that night—we wanted to jump in yet dared not! Afterward we kicked ourselves and swore: from now on, say jump and we dive straight into fire!"
Unwilling to walk into the Qiantang Gang solo in case blows flew, Chen Haoyu barked, "Fine—earn back your spots! Right this second, drag Lu Yuanfeng PLUS a few extra musclemen to meet me at the Qiantang Gang gate!"
"On it!" The lackeys bolted in all directions while Chen Haoyu boarded his carriage for the eastern edge of town.
Pulling up outside the Qiantang Gang, Chen Haoyu squinted out the window in surprise—a second Chen Manor carriage already sat parked outside! Moments later, Chen Haodong strolled straight out of the gang's gates!
Panic shot through Chen Haoyu—had his gambling disaster leaked, sending Father's right-hand man Haodong here to mop up?! Hopping down, he braced himself as his cousin approached.
Spotting Haoyu gave Chen Haodong an instant start—must not let his cousin sniff out his secret deal with Master Jiang!—yet prepared with a ready cover, he flashed a smooth smile. "Haoyu? What brings you to the Qiantang Gang?"
*Smiling warmly—phew, he has no clue!* Relaxing at once, Chen Haoyu countered, "I was about to ask what brings Brother Haodong out here!"
Putting on a weary elder-brother sigh, Chen Haodong explained, "You stay clear of Chen commerce, so you miss the headaches of trade—got to stay friendly with officials on the bright side AND underworld bosses in the shadows, or trouble hits our shops. Dropped by to keep Master Jiang sweet so our business runs smooth."
Relieved beyond words, Chen Haoyu praised him. "Hard work on your shoulders, Brother Haodong—spares Father tons of grief."
"Do not flatter me—an empire this vast is too heavy for one pair of hands. Join in someday to share the load; Eldest Uncle and Grandmother would be thrilled!" Chen Haodong probed smoothly.
Totally blind to his cousin's guile, Chen Haoyu waved it off. "You know me—zero taste for trade! Long as silver keeps rolling and fun stays hot, I am set."
Satisfied completely, Chen Haodong smiled. "Still didn't say why you came out here."
"Ran into Master Jiang at a gambling den a few days back and he invited me to drop by—figured I had a free morning," Chen Haoyu fibbed.
Sneering inside yet nodding smoothly, Chen Haodong replied, "Good for the Chens if you stay chummy with Master Jiang. Carry on—I have books to check at the tea shop." Watching his cousin drive away, Chen Haoyu shook his head in awe—he could never match Brother Haodong in a million years.
With no hired muscle arriving yet, he lingered outside eyeing the burly gate guards—when another familiar carriage rolled up: Prince Yi! Elation surged in Chen Haoyu's chest! His tip during yesterday's raid had paid off! With Prince Yi's lethal guard—and surely an even deadlier prince—backing him, Master Jiang would cough up that contract in seconds!
Hurrying forward, he greeted him eagerly: "What luck running into Your Highness here!"
Glancing out at the worthless rake who had somehow married the woman he loved, Shen Jianchen felt pure bile rise and ignored him flat, stepping down with Bai Feng straight toward the gang's gate.
Guessing the prince held a grudge over the wedding, Chen Haoyu hustled alongside him. "Your Highness, one private word—about Zhu Shuzhen!"
Magic words—Shen Jianchen halted, eyed him once, and stepped aside out of Bai Feng's earshot.
Lowering his voice, Chen Haoyu spilled fast: "Like I told you yesterday, Master Jiang forced me into marrying Zhu Shuzhen—so our marriage is completely fake!"
"Invitations across Lin'an and a massive feast—and you expect me to call it fake?" Shen Jianchen scoffed.
"The feast was real, yet the marriage is pure paper! We signed terms beforehand—total non-interference! Last night I never even entered the bridal chamber; Zhu Shuzhen booted me straight to a back shack! Doubt me? Have Guard Bai ask Kuqiao!"
Relief spiked through Shen Jianchen alongside a sharp pang of pity for Qianli Xun: freed from one loveless cage only to climb into a sham marriage out of necessity. "Stubborn as she is, why would she marry you without caring for you?"
Recounting the entire gambling ambush, Chen Haoyu added, "Master Jiang rigged a bet to force me to wed her. Desperate, I dropped to my knees begging her—maybe she pitied me, plus I promised her sweet perks and we signed a written contract."
"A written contract?"
"Spelled out in black and white how we live apart—and transferring all my assets to her."
Pitying Qianli Xun yet suspicious of Chen Haoyu's sudden candor, Shen Jianchen narrowed his eyes. "Why tell me all this?"
Emboldened by the warmer reception, Chen Haoyu pitched straight: "Marrying the woman Your Highness loves made me sweat bullets! Wanted to clear the air—and beg Your Highness to help pry my contract out of Master Jiang's claws! I swear on my life I will never touch a hair on Zhu Shuzhen's head, and the day the moment ripens, I hand her straight back to Your Highness!"
Something smelled fishy as hell—an underworld boss turning down the whole Chen fortune? Definitely a scheme underneath worth digging out! A thin smile touched Shen Jianchen's lips. "For being so sensible, I will get your contract back."
*Pure genius!* Chen Haoyu beamed—flattered Prince Yi, crushed Master Jiang, AND laid a clean escape hatch from Zhu Shuzhen!
Leaving him behind, Shen Jianchen strode up to the gate. "Who are you? What business at Qiantang Gang?" the two guards barked.
Before the echoes faded, Bai Feng dropped both with two crisp punches. "Watch your mouths! Young Master Shen is here to see your boss!"
Strutting past the groaning guards behind them, Chen Haoyu crowed, "Heard that?! Young Master Shen! Going to wipe the floor with you lot!"
Stepping into the main yard, a ring of armed gangsters closed around all three with gleaming steel.
#CH 201
#TITLE: Thrashing Master Jiang and Hunting the Jurchen
Bigger crowds only fired Bai Feng up. "Do not steal my fun, Young Master!"
Shen Jianchen folded his arms in silence alongside Chen Haoyu to watch. Early exchanges went smooth for Bai Feng, yet sheer numbers slowly pressed him tighter; nimble footwork kept him dodging blades cleanly without letting them pin him down. Watching nearby, Chen Haoyu wondered why on earth Prince Yi did not just drop his royal title to flatten Master Jiang instead of playing "Young Master Shen."
Mid-brawl, Master Jiang strode into the yard. Sizing up one guard holding off dozens to a standstill—and spotting the tall, aristocratic stranger watching calmly while normally cocky Chen Haoyu stood tucked behind him—Master Jiang smelled real trouble at once.
"Enough! Hold back!"
At his bark, gangsters sheathed steel and parted. Floating lightly back to ground, Bai Feng scoffed, "Just warming up—talk about spoilsports!"
Ignoring the guard, Master Jiang addressed the poised nobleman. "Never met before, Young Master—why bring trouble to Qiantang Gang?"
Before Shen Jianchen opened his mouth, Chen Haoyu crowed, "Young Master Shen is my backup, Master Jiang! Be smart and cough up my contract!"
Hardly buying that Chen Haoyu commanded a man like this, Master Jiang still kept his swagger. "In the underworld, my word is iron—since you wed Miss Zhu, your contract is yours anyway. Why fight through my gate?"
Hearing it confirmed straight from Master Jiang's lips—using Qianli Xun's marriage as a pawn!—lit the fuse under Shen Jianchen's temper. In one blur of light-foot speed, he crossed the yard and seized Master Jiang by the throat of his robe. "Hand over the contract!"
Gangsters surged forward, only for Bai Feng to flash right beside his prince, freezing them dead.
"Hold off—do not scratch the hero!" Master Jiang grinned easily despite the fist at his collar. "Fierce skill, Hero—beats even Chen Haoyu's Lu Yuanfeng! Contract coming right up!" Signaling a lieutenant to fetch it, he held the paper up. "Inspect away, Hero."
Taking it with one hand and releasing Master Jiang with the other, Shen Jianchen scanned every line—no mention of Miss Zhu anywhere on the page, cooling half his wrath. Handing it toward Bai Feng—"Take it"—he watched Chen Haoyu snatch it midair: "Mine anyway—I will hold it!"
Checking every word clean, Chen Haoyu bowed hastily. "Much obliged, Young Master Shen—got errands, will repay you another day!" And bolted straight out the gate—running smack into Scarface arriving late with three middling fighters.
"Late as usual!" Chen Haoyu scoffed, waving the slip. "Already got it! Waiting on you lot, dinner would freeze over!"
"Late, sure, still earned sweat bringing them!" Scarface whined.
Eyeing the three passable hires—nowhere near Lu Yuanfeng's league—Chen Haoyu frowned. "Where is Lu Yuanfeng?"
"Hit his house first—big padlock on his gate! Probably took your silver and skipped town!"
Careless with silver as ever, Chen Haoyu shrugged. "You three shadow me everywhere from now on—good pay!" Accepting at once, the three fell in behind as Chen Haoyu glared at Scarface's crew. "Quit leeching off me—useless when blades flash!" After frantic begging and oaths of loyalty, Chen Haoyu let them off with zero errand pay—"Pass me a fire-starter!"
Touching flame to the contract, Chen Haoyu watched it burn to ash on the dirt—a mountain lifted off his chest! "To Chunxiang Brothel!" Starved from a wasted wedding night and safe while Prince Yi tied Master Jiang up, he strutted off toward Chun Jinmo.
Back inside the yard, paying zero mind to Chen Haoyu's exit, Shen Jianchen turned icy eyes back onto Master Jiang. "Two errands brought me here: first, that contract; second, tell me WHY you rigged a trap for Chen Haoyu using Miss Zhu's wedding as the condition!"
Outmatched physically yet bound by underworld code, Master Jiang tried talking his way clear. "Admire top fighters like you, Hero—why not trade blows for drinks and become friends? If folks say one good thing about me, I treasure true talent..."
"Cut the crap and answer my question."
"A man of iron like you understands my spot," Master Jiang cupped his hands. "Took pay to do a job—cannot sell out a client. Stay and drink with my brothers instead?"
"Tell your boys to clear out—far as they can!" Shen Jianchen snapped.
Seeing no talking his way past a beating, Master Jiang barked, "Jump them!" and backed clear as armed gangsters swarmed in.
Paper tigers all—with Prince Yi stepping in beside Bai Feng, the yard emptied in minutes, groaning thugs littering the dirt while survivors scrambled back in terror. Spinning to bolt, Master Jiang felt Bai Feng's iron grip lock onto his collar. "Not going anywhere!"
"Beat him!" Shen Jianchen ordered.
A crisp storm of punches and kicks echoed across the yard—Bai Feng striking lightning-fast only on flesh that hurt like hell without killing, leaving Master Jiang howling until Bai Feng paused panting. "Enough, Young Master?"
Eyeing Master Jiang swollen purple head to toe—worse than Chen Haoyu had looked—Shen Jianchen nodded, crouched down beside him, and murmured, "Ready to tell me why you dragged Miss Zhu into your rigged bet?"
Grasping at last who this terror fought for, Master Jiang wheezed through split lips, "Walk... the underworld... word is everything... cannot tell you... who... Yet rest easy, Hero... aimed only at... Chen Haoyu... nothing against Miss Zhu..."
Admiring the boss's stubborn grit despite himself, Shen Jianchen stood up coldly. "Keep quiet—I will dig it out anyway. Aimed at Chen Haoyu or not, using Miss Zhu as bait to trap her with trash like him earned every bruise you got. Pull her into another stunt and I take your life!"
Striding out with Bai Feng through a silent lane of terrified thugs, they left Master Jiang groaning as his men rushed to carry him inside and smear salve across his welts.
"Go... dig up... who that man is!" Master Jiang hissed.
"Recognized him, Boss!" a lieutenant spoke up. "That is the exact guy who tried snatching Young Master Chen's bride yesterday!"
"Bride-snatcher?!" Everything clicked—no wonder he came tearing in for Miss Zhu! "Who IS he?!"
"No clue, Boss!"
"Find out—NOW!" Familiar with every underworld face and half the yamen too, Master Jiang sensed cold sweat—he had stepped squarely on a hidden dragon's tail!
Venting his fury done, Shen Jianchen skipped Youlin Retreat and ordered Bai Feng straight to Mihun Alley to track the Jurchen.
Driving along, Bai Feng called back, "Why not hand this to Division Commander Lin? One ring of troops around the alley and that Jurchen has zero escape!"
"Court talks with the Jurchens over returning the lost territories are delicate—keeping surface peace," Shen Jianchen explained. "Mobilize troops over one Jurchen and give them a pretext to tear up talks? Better just you and me scout quietly." *Not that one transmigrator could shift history's avalanche anyway.*
"Sharp thinking as always, Your Highness!"
Hitching the carriage outside Mihun Alley, they stepped inside trying to memorize turns—and within ten minutes wound thoroughly turned around inside a living labyrinth. Spotting a passerby, Shen Jianchen nodded to Bai Feng, who stepped forward politely asking directions out.
"Easy—follow me!" the local agreed cheerfully.
Walking alongside, Shen Jianchen dropped a casual probe. "Live in Mihun Alley long?"
"Born and bred—only locals survive these twists."
"Seen anyone around lately who looks different from ordinary Lin'an locals?"
Pausing mid-stride, the man snapped his fingers. "Now that you mention it—one guy! Dropped by a friend's house yesterday and saw a relative staying there whose features did NOT look Han at all!"
Talk about striking gold! At Shen Jianchen's nod, Bai Feng pressed a tael of silver into the man's hand. "Guide fee—take us to your friend's door."
Eyeing the silver warily, the local hesitated. "Who are you two, chasing my friend's kin?"
"Relax—that non-Han man may be an old friend of ours who took an injury and ducked in here, so we came looking," Shen Jianchen reassured him smoothly.
Weighing their honest faces against real silver, the local pocketed it. "He really WAS hurt! Come on—and try any tricks, my friend packs mean fists that will flatten you both!"
Winding through four more tight twists, they stopped before Lu Yuanfeng's gate—sealed shut with a hefty iron padlock.
"Bad luck—my friend left town," the local said.
"How do you know?"
"Habit of his: stays in Lin'an, never locks up; slaps the big padlock on only when traveling far."
"Nobody else home?"
"Lives solo—one mouth to feed."
Disappointment clouded Shen Jianchen's face—missed the Jurchen AND the mystery behind his Shen Fist! "What is your friend's name, in case we cross paths someday?"
"Lu Yuanfeng! Killer fists—only ever heard of him losing once, to his own master."
Name noted, Shen Jianchen asked no more. Guided back out to the avenue, he had zero appetite to return to Youlin Retreat—with his wedding trip to Bianliang looming in days, he needed one last sight of Qianli Xun! Impossible to barge into Chen Manor—wait: one man could pull it off! "Bai Feng—to the Dongfang Manor!"
#CH 202
#TITLE: A Smooth Property Transfer
After Qianli Xun finished serving morning tea and withdrew, the Chen elders chatted briefly before dispersing. Moments later, Butler Wang came to Chen Jianglin's hall just as he and Yao Qingyan were setting up a chess game.
"Master, Madam, one delicate matter calls for your ruling," Butler Wang bowed. "Young Madam ordered every single maid in Second Young Master's courtyard cleared out to use her own people. Being her first day, I dared not cross her on the spot, yet thought it best to ask Master and Madam."
Placing a white stone casually on the board, Yao Qingyan snorted. "I could never stand that pack of maids Haoyu dragged in from who-knows-where anyway! Meant to tell him to clear them out ages ago—good riddance!"
Dropping a black stone, Chen Jianglin agreed. "Madam is right. Do exactly as Shuzhen wishes."
"What did Haoyu say about it?" Yao Qingyan asked.
"Naturally displeased, yet clearly helpless against Young Madam—gave in at the end."
"Proves we picked the right bride!" Chen Jianglin chuckled. "Obey Shuzhen's instructions, Butler Wang."
"One more tricky detail, Master," Butler Wang hesitated. "Half those maids want to stay inside Chen Manor. On the walk over I mentioned reassigning them to Second Master and Third Master—who jumped at the chance—then bumped into Second Madam and Third Madam, who flatly refused, calling Second Young Master's maids far too coquettish for their yards."
Holding a chess stone poised, Yao Qingyan slapped it decisively onto the board. "Dismiss the whole lot cleanly! Saves stirring trouble in every other courtyard!"
"Dismiss them all," Chen Jianglin backed her.
"Yes, Master." Butler Wang withdrew.
Playing on in relaxed warmth, Yao Qingyan teased him for letting her win every game, while Chen Jianglin grinned that watching her beam in victory was half the fun. Mentioning Haoyu, Yao Qingyan wondered aloud if Old Madam might kick up a fuss once she heard his maids were cleared—Chen Jianglin merely shrugged to wait and see.
After Yao Qingyan won, the pair strolled through Chen Manor's spring-bright gardens and rested in a kiosk—until Steward Fang, chief accountant of Chen commerce, came hurrying up. "Master! First thing this morning, Young Madam's Matron Wei came demanding every asset under Second Young Master's name be transferred into Young Madam's name! Too huge for me to decide solo, so I put her off to consult Master first."
Dismissing maids was peanuts; moving Chen assets required real thought. Telling Steward Fang to hold tight, they headed straight back inside and sent Yingxue to summon Matron Wei.
Entering moments later, Matron Wei found Yao Qingyan rising warmly to clasp her hands. "Now that you live at Chen Manor, Matron, drop by my rooms often!"
Curtsying smoothly despite her held hands, Matron Wei declined the seat of honor Chen Jianglin offered, settling politely on a lower chair even as both masters praised her fame—saying half Youqi Jushi's renown belonged to Matron Wei managing daily burdens so her genius could bloom. Chuckling warmly over tea, Matron Wei saved her hosts awkwardness by opening the topic herself: "Master and Madam summoned me over transferring Young Master's assets to my mistress, I assume?"
"Sharp as ever, Matron Wei," Chen Jianglin praised. "Nothing major, only sudden—we hoped to learn the background."
Drawing a folded sheet from her sleeve, Matron Wei let Yingxue pass it to Yao Qingyan, who read it with a sigh before handing it to Chen Jianglin. Knitting his brows as he read, Chen Jianglin exhaled. "Ah—so these terms are why Miss Zhu agreed to marry into our house."
"Do not feel saddened, Master and Madam," Matron Wei soothed smoothly. "Marriage follows Heaven's design—whatever brought her through your gate, Miss is a Chen now."
"Did they consummate the marriage last night?" Yao Qingyan asked softly.
"Neither wanted the match at first, yet Heaven still bound them together," Matron Wei answered tactfully. "Give them patience—nobody can foresee every twist ahead, yet good things will come."
Plainly not consummated yet—still, Matron Wei's steady calm smoothed Chen Jianglin's brow. "Once under one roof, consummation comes in due time. Since Haoyu signed this prenuptial pact himself, we elders cannot break his word—and besides, whether under Haoyu's name or our daughter-in-law's, Chen wealth stays inside Chen gates!" Glancing back at the contract, a wide grin broke across his face. "One clause here delights me most: allowing Haoyu only twenty taels of silver a month—and he STILL signed and thumbprinted it! Miracle!"
Handing the sheet back via Yingxue for Matron Wei to stow away, he heard her add smoothly, "Exactly as Master says—wealth stays in the family! Miss is no gold-digger; using property simply gave her peace of mind to stop fighting fate. Master's sharp eyes nailed the real truth: my young mistress is Young Master's sole match, and her presence brings pure gain to the Chens."
"Matron saw straight through why Jianglin and I insisted on this bride!" Yao Qingyan laughed at last. "Nothing left to hesitate over!"
Chen Jianglin nodded crisply. "Go back to Xinglong Pavilion and finalize it with Steward Fang, Matron—I will send word right ahead."
With yesterday's crimson wedding drapes cleared away, Xinglong Pavilion stood back in full commercial swing—hub of over two hundred managers and clerks housed along Chen Manor's west side with their own west gate, rarely crossing paths with Chen family members residing north and east. Stepping back onto the vast ground floor lit by daytime lanterns, Matron Wei walked past busy desks of copyists, runners, and ledger binders toward the innermost accounting bay humming with crisp abacus beads, where Steward Fang sat reviewing books.
"Back again, Steward Fang!"
Rising with a cheerful grin, Steward Fang greeted her: "Master's word just arrived—let us get this done right away!"
"Wait—I walked straight here without stopping and saw zero runners! Do you and Master speak through thin air?" Matron Wei teased.
"Just missed our fleet-footed lad by a hair!" Setting a chair out, Steward Fang pulled a thick ledger labeled *Property Registry* onto the desk and chatted while opening it. "Young Madam truly struck rare fortune marrying Second Young Master! By Song statute, a household's scattered properties register under a single household head—so all Chen assets sit in Eldest Master's name. Only because Old Madam dotes so fiercely on Second Young Master did she pull strings with Prefect Zhu to carve several holdings into his name—and now he hands the whole lot straight to his bride! Rare devotion!"
Already familiar with the single-household statute, Matron Wei felt deeper respect for the Chens' generosity. "Is transferring title into my mistress's name complicated?"
"Not a bit! Log it in this registry, stamp the Chen Firm grand seal plus Eldest Master's and Second Young Master's private seals, then register it down at the prefecture yamen." Dipping his brush, he recorded the entries cleanly and set the pen down. "Done on paper—I will head upstairs to the seal-keeper for the three stamps next." Glancing around, Matron Wei spotted a long wooden staircase climbing into upper floors she had never noticed before.
"One last detail to confirm, Matron—should Eldest Master's stewards keep managing those holdings, or will Young Madam install her own people?"
Smart enough to keep Chen Jianglin comfortable—leaving his stewards in charge ensured he felt assets remained safely in the family while sparing her own overburdened hands—Matron Wei smiled. "Keep the same stewards. I will drop by to look over accounts when free, and trust them when busy."
"Splendid!" Steward Fang beamed.
#CH 203
#TITLE: Two Requests for a Poetry Gathering
Property transfer wrapped up, Matron Wei returned to the newlyweds' courtyard to find her mistress standing outside looking glum.
"Transfer is done, Miss—smooth sailing, just waiting on Steward Fang to register it at the yamen."
Weighed down by unfamiliar Chen walls, Old Madam Xia's morning ambush, and yesterday's heartbreak, Qianli Xun brightened a shade at the smooth news—at least Chen Haoyu's parents treated her well. "Thank you, Matron."
"Did morning tea go smoothly?" Matron Wei asked gently.
Kuqiao blurted straight out, "Father-in-Law and Mother-in-Law were lovely, Matron, yet Chen Old Madam ordered Miss to kneel and smashed her tea bowl on the floor! Outrageous!"
Far from bristling, Matron Wei let out a warm chuckle. "Thought it was real trouble! Just Old Madam Xia? Trivial—coaxing an elderly lady is child's play for Miss!"
That single word—"coaxing"—clicked a light straight on in Qianli Xun's head, chasing the clouds off her brow. "Trust Matron to nail it in one word! Elders are just big kids craving sweet talk—whoever coaxes them wins them over! Now I get why Old Madam Xia adores Chen Haoyu: the guy pours out syrupy butter nonstop!"
Pleased to see her cheer return, Matron Wei nodded warmly. "Even if Miss did not marry for love, putting thought into winning over Chen elders shows you accept your new home and mean to live well here. Wherever your heart settles, no wall holds."
Touched yet skipping sappy thanks, Qianli Xun pivoted to decorating. "This courtyard is smaller than Wangyue Pavilion yet bigger than my old Zhu Manor garden. Walking around, I saw plenty of flowers yet none I like! Besides redoing the hall, I want my favorite blooms planted—and I named the whole courtyard and every single room! Written on paper for Kuqiao to hand you."
"Just heading to Wangyue Pavilion to haul your furniture over," Matron Wei agreed effortlessly. "Once the hall is set, I will call Chen Manor's gardeners to redo the yard just how you like."
Hugging her arm, Qianli Xun smiled. "Matron never turns down a single wish of mine!"
"Reasonable wishes—and living in a house that feels wrong never brings peace." Accepting a thick stack of room names from Kuqiao ("Even the servants' quarters got fancy names!" Kuqiao grinned), Matron Wei skimmed them. "I will swing by a plaque carver on the way to Wangyue Pavilion—hung by tomorrow!" Rounding up Hongdou and the nine trained hands, she set off to haul furniture.
Meanwhile, bolting from Youlin Retreat earlier that morning, Qiao Wenxian had refused to go back to Qiao Manor—driven by one burning need: see Shuzhen face-to-face and ask straight out! Once Nan Shuang caught up to his carriage, they stopped briefly outside Chen Manor's closed gate before turning straight for Dongfang Manor.
Inside his study, Dongfang Chenze sat at his zither setting one of Youqi Jushi's ci lyrics to music for Xiangrong to sing at his next gathering, strumming and singing aloud: *"Slanting winds and fine rain bring a spring chill. / Before the wine cup, / I recall old joys. / Once beside the pear blossoms, / Lonely tears streamed down..."*
Stepping inside, Qiao Wenxian recognized Shuzhen's *"Jiangchengzi: Admiring the Spring"*—the exact lyric that had filled him with bright dreams back in Bianliang, now ashes in his mouth.
Alerted by Huayu, Dongfang Chenze lifted his hands from the strings and saw his friend's red-rimmed eyes. "Brother Qiao! Rare honor—sit while I whisk tea myself!"
Catching his arm, Qiao Wenxian shook his head. "No mood for tea. Sit and listen to business."
Seated together, Dongfang Chenze braced himself. "Speak."
"Host a poetry gathering tomorrow."
Much as Dongfang Chenze loved salons, tomorrow?! "Planned one soon, yet tomorrow is wildly rushed—push it back a few days?"
"No. Tomorrow. I cannot wait another day."
Reading the raw storm on his face, Dongfang Chenze asked softly, "Why the urgent rush?"
"I need to see Shuzhen."
Blunt honesty put Dongfang Chenze in a tight bind. "You and Youqi Jushi both have new lives now, Brother Qiao—better not stir old embers."
"Do not misunderstand—I only want ONE last private talk to ask a few questions, then I let go for good."
"Ask questions, and I fear your heart only burns hotter."
"My word on it—the very last time. After tomorrow, I never disturb her again."
Seeing genuine finality beneath the ache—maybe closure really would set both free—Dongfang Chenze relented. "First time helping you like this, and the LAST—never again!"
A tired smile touched Qiao Wenxian's face. "Thank you, Brother Dongfang." Taking his leave shortly after, he left Dongfang Chenze dropping his zither to seek counsel from An Mingxuan and Cao Ban on pulling off a discreet salon overnight.
Stepping out his front gate with Huayu, he froze at a carriage pulling up driven by Bai Feng—Prince Yi! Having struck out in Mihun Alley, Shen Jianchen had come straight here seeking the exact same pretext to see Qianli Xun one last time before leaving for Bianliang!
Bowing outside the cab, Dongfang Chenze asked, "Calling on my father for official business, Your Highness?"
"Not Master Dongfang—YOU, Young Master Dongfang. Step inside for a word."
Climbing in without surprise, Dongfang Chenze sat opposite the prince. "At Your Highness's service."
"Simple task," Shen Jianchen smiled smoothly. "Host a poetry gathering tomorrow—keep the guest list small, yet make certain Youqi Jushi attends."
Having heard all about yesterday's raid—and secretly understanding, having briefly admired Youqi Jushi himself before backing off—Dongfang Chenze blinked at getting the EXACT SAME demand twice in one hour!
Reading his hesitation, Shen Jianchen tossed a heavy silver pouch onto his lap. "Expenses are on me. Word is you want your poetry salon famous across Lin'an—help me tomorrow, and once I return from Bianliang, I will put Prince Yi's name behind you and make your gathering number one in the city!"
Silver, dream fulfilled, AND a royal patron—impossible to refuse! Pocketing the pouch gratefully, Dongfang Chenze hesitated before confessing honestly: "One tiny snag, Your Highness... right before you arrived, Brother Qiao came asking the exact same thing—swearing it is his final private meeting with Youqi Jushi. Should we push Your Highness's gathering back a day?"
Guessing Wenyuan wanted final closure straight from her lips so he could move on, Shen Jianchen stayed cool. "Funny how Wenyuan and I think alike. No need to delay—I trust Young Master Dongfang to choreograph it smoothly. Send location details to Bai Feng before dusk."
Left with zero escape, Dongfang Chenze bowed out, checked Cao Manor, tracked Cao Ban and An Mingxuan down outside a storytelling hall at Qiaotou Wa, and dragged both aboard a rented tea boat on the river to brainstorm.
"Both Brother Qiao AND Prince Yi hit me up today demanding a poetry salon tomorrow—just as cover to see Youqi Jushi!" Dongfang Chenze spilled while An Mingxuan poured tea.
"Wild!" Cao Ban gasped. "You guessed it, or they admitted it?"
"Admitted it straight to my face! Tell me how the hell I pull this off!"
An Mingxuan ticked off three hurdles cleanly: "Cannot turn down Prince Yi, so it happens tomorrow. One: pick a secluded spot nobody frequents. Two: bar Lady Miao and Princess Jiahe—and keep them clueless it exists. Three: craft a slick excuse to lure Youqi Jushi out on day two of her marriage without tipping off the Chens."
"Wait—after yesterday's raid, if Brother Qiao and Prince Yi both show up chasing Youqi Jushi, won't they brawl?!" Cao Ban worried.
"Already warned Prince Yi," Dongfang Chenze said. "He didn't blink—told me to stagger it."
"Then schedule Brother Qiao FIRST, Prince Yi SECOND!" Cao Ban proposed. "As for location—Yongtai Teahouse is out (Lady Miao haunts it), Goulan Wasi is too crowded. Must be outside city walls—what about Chen Haoyu's Zuiyun Tower?! Deserted and dead quiet!"
"What if Chen servants there rat out Youqi Jushi to Chen Haoyu?" Dongfang Chenze worried.
"Only two staffers there, busy with their own jobs—keep it discreet and nobody notices!"
Location locked, roles divided: "Some poetry gathering!" An Mingxuan sighed; "A poetry gathering in name only!" Cao Ban summed up as the three split off to put every cog in place.
#CH 204
#TITLE: Drinking in Town and Running into Her Ex-Husband
Dongfang Chenze handled notifying Prince Yi and Qiao Wenxian—carefully framing it as a men-only banquet rather than a poetry salon so wives and fiancées stayed home—plus inviting Xiangrong from Flower Cloud Pavilion, both to sing Youqi Jushi's lyrics so the salon looked authentic and to keep Youqi Jushi company between meetings.
Cao Ban and An Mingxuan took on inviting Youqi Jushi and borrowing Zuiyun Tower from Chen Haoyu, heading together to Chen Manor's closed south gate (skipping the busy west business gate). Knocking, An Mingxuan learned Young Master Chen had stepped out for anywhere from four days to half a month. Before the gatekeeper shut the door, Cao Ban jumped in: "Is Youqi Jushi home? We came for her too!"
"No Youqi Jushi here."
"Miss Zhu!" Cao Ban clarified.
"Oh, Young Madam! Still inside—let me announce you."
By sheer coincidence, with Matron Wei's crew turning her hall upside down with moving furniture, Qianli Xun had stepped out for a stroll through town and walked straight up to the gatekeeper—one of the bruised guards from her raid, who bowed eagerly to report two young gentlemen waiting outside.
Stepping out to greet Cao Ban and An Mingxuan, she heard Cao Ban's smooth pitch inviting her to Dongfang Chenze's poetry gathering tomorrow—and asked cautiously who else was invited.
"Brother Dongfang aims to make his name in Lin'an's literary world, so he invited ONLY top local poets," Cao Ban fibbed smoothly. "Brother Qiao is coming; Lady Miao falls short on renown so she was skipped; Prince Yi and Her Highness outrank us yet hold no literary fame, so neither was invited."
Hearing Prince Yi and Mengrong weren't coming eased Qianli Xun's nerves—she could face Wenyuan calmly now. "Why send you two in person instead of a formal invitation card?"
"We have cards—Brother Dongfang insisted luminaries like Youqi Jushi deserve a personal call!"
"Fair enough. Time and place?"
"Hour of the Snake tomorrow at Zuiyun Tower by the Qiantang River—southwest out the city gate, half an hour by carriage. Happens to be a Chen property too."
Wondering briefly why hold a big salon so far out yet shrugging it off, Qianli Xun promised to attend.
"Could Youqi Jushi tell us where Young Master Chen went?" An Mingxuan asked next. "Since Zuiyun Tower sits under his name, we need his nod to borrow it."
Amused they invited guests before locking down the venue, Qianli Xun waved it off. "Skip hunting Chen Haoyu—all his assets just transferred to me, so Zuiyun Tower is mine now. Everyone come freely tomorrow."
Thrilled to cross both errands off in one shot, the two scholars hurried off to report to Dongfang Chenze.
Skipping a carriage to walk into the bustling market, Qianli Xun let her thoughts drift first to Mengrong—hoping her wound healed clean—and straight from there to Shen Jianchen: running through the earthquake, sparring in the bamboo grove, hinting under the willow, playing chuiwan side by side, storming Zhu Manor, blocking her bridal sedan... Frame after frame played behind her eyes, pulling smiles and sharp stabs of grief by turns.
*Thought you swore off romance?* she scolded herself. Back as Qianli Xun she had kept her heart locked tight; inside Zhu Shuzhen's skin, her walls melted every time! Remembering Bai Feng hounding that poor piglet to death brought a bitter pang: *Why be that stubborn when fate wrote us off?* Surely time would dull Shen Jianchen just as it had dulled Wenyuan—yet why was SHE always the one forced to step back, never Miao Zhiyu or Mengrong?
Spotting a wine shop ahead, four words flashed in her mind—*drown your sorrows*—and she ducked straight inside to a window table, ordering a pot of Capital Spring. Normally untouched by strong liquor, one cup burned fiery trails down her throat and flushed her cheeks hot.
"Switch to sweet osmanthus wine, Miss?" Kuqiao suggested.
"Osmanthus wine is juice—today I want real liquor!" pouring a second cup.
"Who says osmanthus wine isn't liquor? Knocked you out cold last summer!"
*Last summer...* The exact night Shen Jianchen realized Zhu Shuzhen was Qianli Xun—watching her sit drunk in Wenyuan's lap! Why did they keep missing by inches?! All because of her loud mouth taunting *"When pigs climb trees!"* Swirling her cup, she muttered, "Wonder if taking those words back is too late..." Of course it was—she was wed, he held an imperial marriage decree.
Lifting the cup in gloom, a familiar voice broke in: "Drinking solo is dull—let me join you."
Looking up, she saw Lin Jue standing beside her table. "What do you want?"
"Relax—not stalking you! Just stepped out of Goulan Wasi listening to Master Qianli Xun's huaben, passed the window, spotted you drinking alone, and dropped in to say hi." Taking a seat uninvited, he waved for an extra cup, poured himself full, and knocked it straight down.
Holding zero grudge since their divorce, Qianli Xun let him sit. "Out solo without your bride?"
"Men have men's errands—who drags a wife everywhere?"
Letting out a tiny hiccup, Qianli Xun scoffed, "Pure chauvinism! Still sneaking Chun Jinqiao into Lin Manor?"
"Nope—my new wife is gentle and obedient, so no need. Still hit Chunxiang Brothel plenty, though," Lin Jue admitted freely.
Pouring herself another cup, Qianli Xun shook her head. "Leopard never changes his spots! Got a decent wife now—cherish her instead of living at the brothel! If you truly care for Chun Jinqiao, talk to your wife about buying her freedom as a concubine—keep this up and you are heading for divorce number two!"
Lin Jue laughed aloud. "Think every woman has your nerve to demand mutual divorce? My new wife lacks that kind of spine—save your worry."
"Like I care about your mess—just chatting."
A sudden glint crossed Lin Jue's eyes. "Hey—level with me: is that huaben writer Qianli Xun actually YOU?"
Sobering a quick notch, Qianli Xun blinked. "They call him a male master—how could it be me? Since when do I write huaben?"
"Really not?" Lin Jue squinted shrewdly. "Back in Lin Manor's hall during our divorce, you swore in front of my elders your name was Qianli Xun—forgot?! Back then I thought it was gibberish; once Master Qianli Xun blew up across Lin'an, I figured you picked a male pen name to hide writing fiction!"
Scrambling to cover that old slip, she waved it off. "Made a random name up on the spot to shake loose from you—pure fluke it matched the famous writer!"
"Shame—I really hoped it was you," Lin Jue sighed with genuine regret. "His huaben stories wipe the floor with your poetry! Never could stomach those fancy literary verses, yet I am hooked on Master Qianli Xun's tales!" Leaning closer, he dropped his voice. "You really are nothing like the old Zhu Shuzhen..."
"All thanks to you," Qianli Xun parried dryly.
Before Lin Jue could reply, a shadow fell across the table—looking up, both froze as Bai Meiruo stood right beside them with her maid! Out shopping for pastries and strolling the lively market, her maid had spotted Master drinking and laughing warmly across from his ex-wife—a bright, relaxed grin Bai Meiruo never saw at home—lighting instant jealousy inside her chest.
#CH 205
#TITLE: Parting in Anger with Meiruo and Returning to the Thatched Hall
The smile vanished off Lin Jue's face at once. "What are you doing here?"
Clamping tight control over her jealousy in front of her husband, Bai Meiruo dipped a smooth curtsy. "I came out for pastries, spotted Husband taking wine inside, and stepped in to greet you—never expected Youqi Jushi here too. Oh, excuse me—I should say Young Madam Chen now."
Catching the frosty prickle, Qianli Xun stood up. "Excuse me, I have errands. The wine is already paid—my treat, enjoy your drink together."
Unwilling to let her slip away unneedled, Bai Meiruo stepped in front of her. "Bolting the second I show up, like you are scared of me? Saying things to my husband you do not want my ears catching?"
"Quit talking nonsense!" Lin Jue snapped. "Got nothing to do, head home!"
Spooked by his sharp bark, Bai Meiruo flipped instantly into sugary warmth, catching Shuzhen's hand. "Only teasing, Youqi Jushi! Rare running into each other—do not dash off, sit and chat!"
Refusing to look guilty by fleeing, Qianli Xun raised an eyebrow. "Not calling me Young Madam Chen anymore?"
"Youqi Jushi sounds warmer! Sit, sit!"
Once both sat down, Lin Jue pushed back bored. "No taste for women's chatter—yamen business calls. Excuse me."
Crushed watching him bolt the instant she arrived, Bai Meiruo called softly at his back, "Come home early from the yamen, Husband—chicken soup is simmering in the kitchen to build up your strength."
Giving a clipped grunt without glancing back, Lin Jue strode clean out of the tavern.
Watching her swallow the cold shoulder melted Qianli Xun's irritation into quiet pity. "Lin Jue earned three lifetimes of luck marrying you."
Bai Meiruo let out a bitter sigh. "Hard to tell if marrying him was my luck or not..."
Qianli Xun stayed tactfully quiet.
"When Youqi Jushi lived at Lin Manor, did you know a courtesan named Chun Jinqiao?"
Unsure where she was steering—afraid of sparking household strife—Qianli Xun stayed mute behind a polite smile, lifting her cup.
Reading her caution, Bai Meiruo laid it bare. "No need to tiptoe around her name—Lin Manor's maids already told me how Husband used to drag Chun Jinqiao home to spite you before your divorce."
Relaxing, Qianli Xun set her cup down. "If you already know, why ask?"
A tiny gleam of smugness flickered across Bai Meiruo's face. "Since I married Husband, though, he has never once brought Chun Jinqiao through Lin Manor's door."
Her sudden pity feeling downright silly now, Qianli Xun observed dryly, "Sure he still visits Chunxiang Brothel plenty, though."
The smugness collapsed at once. Dropping her barb—unable to air dirty laundry to strangers, whereas this woman knew her husband inside out—Bai Meiruo leaned forward pleadingly. "Good Sister, give me advice—what do I do?"
Threw off stride by the sudden "Good Sister," Qianli Xun shook her head. "None of my business—better I stay out of your marriage."
Grabbing Lin Jue's cup, Bai Meiruo poured herself full—"To you, Sister!"—and knocked two strong cups straight down, choking into a violent red-faced coughing fit while her maid pounded her back.
Softened by the sight, Qianli Xun spoke plainly. "Everything comes down to what you want."
Catching her breath, Bai Meiruo asked, "What do you mean, Sister?"
"I could not stand a single speck of grit in my eye, so I chose mutual divorce. Maybe not this era's textbook choice, yet true to my own heart."
Understanding flickered in Bai Meiruo's eyes. "Sister has powerful family, beauty, and genius—fearing neither Heaven nor earth. I married UP into Lin Manor; my relatives envy my good match. Walk your road and my own family would disown me."
Her helpful streak kicking in once the wall dropped, Qianli Xun offered practical counsel. "Lin Jue's sole virtue is sticking to one courtesan—never switched off Chun Jinqiao the whole time I knew him, proving he genuinely cares for her. Buy her freedom yourself and bring her into Lin Manor as a concubine! One move earns Lin Jue's gratitude, puts Chun Jinqiao right under your thumb where you can keep her in check, AND stops him haunting brothels and drawing street gossip!"
Bai Meiruo's face lit right up. "Leave it to Sister's sharp mind! Why did I never think of that?! Going home tonight to put it in motion!"
Qianli Xun figured they would part on friendly terms—only for Bai Meiruo's face to snap ice-flat the very next breath!
"Thank you for the brilliant advice, Youqi Jushi—yet you and I can never be friends."
Whiplash! "Good Sister" one second, frosty wall the next! Making a mental note to vet people before offering free advice, Qianli Xun refused to eat the snub. "Flatter yourself—I was bored, figured you kept me company, and tossed out free tips. Never once planned on befriending you."
"Do not take offense—you are my husband's ex-wife. Becoming chummy would look bizarre to everyone."
Done wasting air, Qianli Xun stood up. "Business awaits—goodbye."
Unwilling to let her leave without landing her real sting, Bai Meiruo added sweetly: "Youqi Jushi certainly packs a punch—getting Princess Jiahe stabbed on your account! Tomorrow I am visiting Youlin Retreat to check on Her Highness—care to tag along?"
Longing to visit Mengrong yet knowing staying far away was the only kindness left, Qianli Xun felt salt grind straight into the raw wound and turned toward the door without replying.
"Afraid to face Her Highness with a guilty conscience?" Bai Meiruo purred behind her.
Snapping straight back with a tight fist thrust an inch from Bai Meiruo's nose, Qianli Xun hissed, "My fist normally skips women—keep flapping that venomous jaw and I make YOU my first exception!"
Frozen white in terror, Bai Meiruo shut her trap on the spot. Dropping her fist with a cold snort, Qianli Xun marched out—only after she cleared the door did Bai Meiruo dare mutter, "Total tigress! No wonder my husband dumped you!"
Walking home through the bustling streets to cool off, Kuqiao fumed beside her: "Division Commander Lin's wife is touched in the head—flips faces faster than turning a page!"
"Dead right!" Qianli Xun vented. "Seemed sweet and polished at the chuiwan grounds—never guessed she was two-faced! Waste of breath getting mad at someone that petty. Live and learn—next time I check folks close before lending a hand."
"Why treat you so nasty, Miss?"
"Looking back, her frosty dig at the start was her real face; calling me Sister once Lin Jue left was purely fishing for advice—the second she got it, the mask dropped! Probably bitter just because I was Lin Jue's ex."
"You already divorced him—why hold a grudge?!"
"People's hearts are murky—steer clear of her from now on."
As noisy markets gave way to quiet lanes under gathering dusk, Chen Manor's towering gate loomed ahead, bringing a quiet ache to Qianli Xun's chest. Since yesterday, this threshold marked her new waystation—maybe her final stop. Tired of fighting every wind, she hoped to live at ease with everyone inside—including figuring out how to charm Old Madam Xia.
Stepping inside and walking to her courtyard, moonlight illuminated fresh carved wood above the archway: **Moxiang Courtyard** (*Ink-Fragrance Courtyard*). Thrilled at Matron Wei's lightning efficiency—plaques hung in a single day!—she stepped past flowerbeds and pond to find **Suxin Thatched Hall** (*Pure-Heart Thatched Hall*) above the main door and **Yuening Room** above the side quarters ("My room has a real name now!" Kuqiao cheered).
Rounding into the back yard, **Jingyi Hall** graced Matron Wei's door while **Boya Study**—meaning "Erudite and Refined"—hung over Chen Haoyu's quarters.
"Fine name, Boya Study," Kuqiao giggled, "only it fits Young Master like silk on a pig!"
"Save cracks like that for my ears alone—slip up in front of Chen elders and we catch grief," Qianli Xun cautioned gently.
"Got it—zipping my lip from now on!"
"Does not match him, yet matches the courtyard—and he barely sleeps home anyway." Pausing, a brief twinge pricked Qianli Xun. "Honestly, Kuqiao—seizing his whole yard and shoving him out back—did I go too far?"
"Outsiders might say yes," Kuqiao answered carefully, "yet knowing the whole mess between you two, I say not one bit."
Rounding through the west moon-gate to admire **Zhongqin Quarters** for male servants and **Qin'an Lodge** for maids, Qianli Xun hurried back eagerly to **Suxin Thatched Hall**. Stepping inside took her breath away: gaudy bridal gold gone, replaced by serene simplicity, clean ink-scented stillness, and shelves stacked neat with her beloved scrolls. "Feels like home at last!"
"Making Miss a bowl of tea right now!" Kuqiao headed straight to the tea stand.
"Funny—feels like I never got married at all!"
"Of course it does—Miss turned a bridal suite into a scholar's thatched hall!"
Inspired by the calm ink fragrance, Qianli Xun sat straight down at her desk and picked up her brush to write her huaben.
#CH 206
#TITLE: The Second Night at Chen Manor
Absorbed in writing, Qianli Xun never heard Matron Wei step inside; unwilling to break her flow yet needing to report today's plans, Matron Wei signaled Kuqiao to keep quiet and watched her whisk tea.
"Your tea hand grows silkier by the day," Matron Wei whispered with a smile. "When you marry Bai Feng, who will make Miss's tea?"
"Already planned it, Matron! Even after marrying Bai Feng, I will come wait on Miss every day!"
"Silly girl—Bai Feng is Prince Yi's guard; sooner or later he heads back to Bianliang."
Adding hot water and whisking briskly, Kuqiao paused in sudden dismay. "Put like that, you make me hesitate..."
"Nothing to hesitate over. Hongdou is sharp—drilling servants all day is not enough; teach her tea-whisking in spare moments so Miss stays cared for once you wed."
"Smart thinking, Matron."
Setting the finished bowl gently beside her mistress's elbow, Kuqiao's fragrant brew drew Qianli Xun's brush to a halt. Sipping gladly, she spotted Matron Wei approaching and signaled Kuqiao to stash the huaben—Kuqiao hiding a grin since Matron Wei already knew Miss was Master Qianli Xun!
Rising warmly to seat Matron Wei, Qianli Xun praised the study's fresh layout.
"The rooms were easy; the yard takes three days," Matron Wei reported.
