"Spoke with Chen Manor's gardeners today—they start tomorrow: emerald bamboo outside Moxiang Courtyard's gate, keeping existing blooms inside while adding every chrysanthemum you love—daisies, zinnias, summer, autumn, ink, and Hangzhou mums—plus lotus roots in the pond, just like that line Miss always quotes about lotus rising clean out of mud..."
"Master Lianxi's line!" Kuqiao piped up. "'Rising from the mud yet remaining unstained, washed by clear ripples yet never coquettish!'" [TN: Translator's rendering]
"Yes, yes—sharp memory on Kuqiao!" Matron Wei laughed.
"Sounds perfect," Qianli Xun beamed. "Only the yard lacks a shade tree for summer—plant anything big, lush, and fast-growing."
"Beat you to it! Found several lush hehuan silk trees—whose name literally means 'harmonious union'—growing in the Chen nursery, and the gardener agreed to transplant one right in tomorrow!"
Catching the quiet hope woven into the tree's auspicious name—wishing harmony someday with Chen Haoyu—Qianli Xun felt a small tug: her heart would never bend that way. Curious for an elder's honest take, she asked Matron Wei the same question she had asked Kuqiao: "Taking over his yard, moving him out back, taking all his assets—did I go too far?"
Matron Wei chuckled warmly. "Only narrow minds would say so. To my eyes, every move you made benefits both Young Master and the Chen clan."
"You always take my side, Matron! Half the house is probably whispering against me right now—not that I care! Let the Chens see marrying me was a massive blunder!"
"If you truly did not care, why ask me?"
"True enough!" Shrugging it off, Qianli Xun remembered tomorrow's outing. "Dongfang Chenze invited me to a poetry gathering tomorrow and I accepted—should I notify Chen Haoyu's parents?"
"Definitely check with your elders."
"What if they say no?"
"Master and Madam are broad-minded; they will not block you."
"Great—I will go tell them right now."
With Kuqiao lighting the way with a lantern down Chen Manor's straight paths, a voice floated from behind in the dark: "Out after nightfall, Sister-in-Law—heading where?"
Turning, Qianli Xun recognized Chen Haodong, on his way to see his father. "Good evening, Eldest Brother. Going to tell Father-in-Law and Mother-in-Law something."
Stepping closer, Chen Haodong took in her striking poise—*no wonder Brother Yan fell hard!*—and asked smoothly, "Is Haoyu home?"
"No."
Putting on mild reproach, Chen Haodong advised, "Now that you are married to Haoyu, keep a tighter leash on him—letting him run wild outside invites mockery."
Annoyed—the whole Chen clan watched him run wild for twenty years without lifting a finger, and day two they expect the new bride to play nanny?!—Qianli Xun answered coolly, "Eldest Brother flatters me. How could one woman rein him in? If nothing else, excuse me."
Watching her lantern recede into the night, Chen Haodong sneered softly: "Half the credit for getting you into Chen Manor fast belongs to me—do not let me down!"
Reaching her in-laws' open courtyard with no maid at the door, Qianli Xun stepped inside—and stopped a beat at the doorway: Chen Jianglin sat beaming warmly at Yao Qingyan, making her laugh softly in easy devotion. Never in her modern childhood—losing her mother early, watching her father become a monk—nor in Zhu Manor watching Zhu Xiyan bark at Madam Xiang had she witnessed marital tenderness like this. A genuine pang of envy hit her for Chen Haoyu growing up under love so warm; wedded to him in name only, phrases like growing old together in harmony belonged to other worlds. She let out an involuntary sigh.
Hearing it, Yao Qingyan looked up warmly. "Oh, Shuzhen! What brings you over so late?"
Stepping in with a curtsy, Qianli Xun asked politely about attending Dongfang Chenze's poetry gathering tomorrow.
Unwilling to cage a famous poetess—and proud to keep Chen honor shining in Lin'an's literary circles—Chen Jianglin waved breezily. "Go freely to any poetry gathering from now on—no need to ask Mother-in-Law or me!"
"Thank you, Father-in-Law!" How sweet having in-laws this open—if only the husband had been Shen Jianchen...
"Shuzhen," Yao Qingyan's voice pulled her back, "is Haoyu back?"
"Vanished right after serving tea to Grandmother this morning."
"Know where he went?"
"No idea."
Rising to clasp Shuzhen's hands earnestly, Yao Qingyan said, "Father-in-Law and I already saw your prenuptial pact of non-interference—yet in our hearts, you are our true daughter-in-law. Tied by Grandmother's doting, we failed to discipline Haoyu and let him grow wild. Now that you are his wife, please whip him into shape for us! No matter how hard you discipline him, Father-in-Law and I will never step in your way."
*Did the Chens marry a bride or hire a drill sergeant?!* First Chen Haodong, now both in-laws! Having zero time, zero obligation, and zero interest—praying Chen Haoyu stayed permanently missing—Qianli Xun still kept polite face for her gracious mother-in-law. "Thank you both for your trust. If Chen—um... Haoyu pulls any mischief, I will be sure to teach him a lesson." Dropping his surname made her skin crawl, yet Yao Qingyan beamed in relief and sent her back early to rest after her busy day redecorating.
Meanwhile, Chen Haodong reported to his father Chen Jiangshu that Master Jiang had been paid cleanly and asked if Prince Yi's raid spelled fallout.
"Eldest Brother says Prefect Zhu sent word Prince Yi bears zero grudge against the Chens and told us not to bother apologizing," Chen Jiangshu smiled proudly at his son's scheme.
"Great—only I bumped into Miss Zhu on the way over and nudged her to rein Haoyu in, and she sounded totally indifferent! Spent silver and sweat rushing their wedding—what if she leaves Haoyu partying free without giving him grief?!"
"Day one and you want miracles? Patience—hot tofu burns an eager tongue! Heir selection is a full year off; mark my word, Miss Zhu will drag Haoyu down plenty."
Reassured, Chen Haodong remembered another wild card. "Father recall that friend Yan Chi I mentioned?"
"Of course—met him at our tea shop last year asking after some woman named Shao Yueting. By your description, sounded non-Han—Jurchen maybe?"
"No idea who he is, yet he knows Miss Zhu AND has his eye on her—even warned me to keep watch on her, sounding ready to snatch her away!"
"What?!" Chen Jiangshu marveled in glee. "First Prince Yi raiding her wedding, now this Master Yan sniffing around?! Quite a magnet—and pure poison for a wife! Old saying holds true: a woman's virtue lies in lack of talent! Parading around poetry salons with beauty and fame drags men swarming like bees—Haoyu will never see a peaceful day! His misery is my son's gain!"
"Oddly though, Yan Chi vanished thin into air lately."
"Forget him—a passing shadow. Keep your focus sharp on inheriting the Chen empire."
"Yes, Father."
#CH 207
#TITLE: A Poetry Gathering in Name at Zuiyun Tower
Hunting all day across Lin'an for clues on "Young Master Shen," Master Jiang's lieutenant came up dry until dusk led him past Chunxiang Brothel—where spotting Scarface strolling out tipped him off Chen Haoyu was inside! Stepping in past a welcoming courtesan, he found Chen Haoyu lounging below stage watching Chun Jinmo dance, and walked straight up with polite mockery.
"Pretty bold, Young Master Chen—snagged your contract and right back watching Chun Jinmo! Wait till Master Jiang hears!"
Scanning the room for other gangsters and seeing none, Chen Haoyu grinned smugly. "See my three new bodyguards? Let Master Jiang come!"
"While you party here, Master Jiang lies beaten black and blue."
Delighted that Prince Yi's guard had delivered a real thrashing, Chen Haoyu sneered, "What fools get for crossing me! Tell Master Jiang hands off Chun Jinmo!"
"Master Jiang sent me to ask one question: who IS that Young Master Shen?"
Eager to scare Master Jiang stiff and lock down Chun Jinmo for good, Chen Haoyu crooked a finger and whispered into his ear: "Prepare to drop dead—go scare the skin off Master Jiang! Young Master Shen is His Majesty's thirteenth son, Prince Yi!"
Staggered by the bombshell, the lieutenant bolted straight back to Qiantang Gang. Hearing whom he had crossed made every bruise on Master Jiang's body throb twice as hard—rule one of his gang was NEVER cross imperial power! Resolving to beg pardon the second he healed, his rage snapped straight onto Chen Haoyu: "Still lounging at Chunxiang Brothel?! Where is his fierce new bride?! Chen Haodong conned me—tricked me into running a trap for zero reward AND crossed Prince Yi! Wait till I walk again—I am tearing Chen Haodong apart!"
The next morning, Qianli Xun rode Chen Manor's carriage—driven by a coachman who knew Chen Haoyu's haunt well—out past the walls to Zuiyun Tower beside the wide Qiantang River. Stepping down to gaze across the sweeping current, she breathed in awe. "So this is the famed Qiantang River?"
"Yes, Miss—you brought me twice before during tidal bore season," Kuqiao reminded her.
Watching endless waves roll past, Confucius's classic sigh slipped from her lips: "'It passes on just like this, not ceasing day or night!'" [TN: Translated by James Legge] Shaking off melancholy to greet the shining spring water brighter, she shifted straight to Zhang Ruoxu's grand lines:
"'In spring the river rises as high as the sea,
And with the river's rise the moon uprises bright.
She follows the rolling waves for ten thousand li,
And where the river flows, there overflows her light.
The river winds around the fragrant islet...'" [TN: Translated by Xu Yuanchong]
"What grand mood, Youqi Jushi—shame it is morning with no bright moon rising to match Zhang Ruoxu!" Dongfang Chenze called out, strolling over with An Mingxuan and Cao Ban—and zero other guests in sight.
"Thought this was a poetry gathering—why only you three?" Qianli Xun blinked.
"Miss Xiangrong waits inside, and others arrive shortly," Dongfang Chenze explained smoothly. "One favor first, Youqi Jushi..."
"The steward here refuses to let us upstairs without Young Master Chen's word," Cao Ban chimed in. "Since you own Zuiyun Tower now, could you tell him?"
Shaking her head at how slapdash Dongfang Chenze's hosting was—inviting half Lin'an before unlocking the door!—she stepped inside to greet Xiangrong waiting beside her zither-bearing maid.
"Youqi Jushi! Missed you since West Lake—thrilled to join your salon today!" Xiangrong took her hands warmly.
Warming instantly to the graceful singer, Qianli Xun smiled back—just as Zuiyun Tower's steward marched up scolding: "Back again, Young Master Dongfang? Told you, no Young Master Chen's nod, no entry!"
Stepping up, An Mingxuan gestured toward Qianli Xun. "Look who stands right here!"
The steward blinked blankly. "Who?"
"The celebrated Youqi Jushi!"
Blank stare again. "Never heard the name."
"Miss Zhu!" An Mingxuan sighed.
Instantly the steward burst into bowing grins: "Young Madam! Why did you not send word ahead so we could roll out the welcome?!"
Irked he hadn't even heard "Youqi Jushi," Qianli Xun pressed coolly, "Just said Young Master Dongfang needs Chen Haoyu's word to enter?"
"Slip of the tongue from old habit! Master already sent word yesterday that Young Madam owns Zuiyun Tower now—of course Young Master Dongfang is welcome!"
Softened by his swift pivot, Qianli Xun still laid down the law: "Remember—without my say-so, Chen Haoyu gets zero entry here; catch you fawning over him and I find a new steward. Meanwhile my friends here are welcome anytime—clear?"
"Crystal clear, Young Madam! Right upstairs, everyone!"
Upstairs, Dongfang Chenze claimed the exact river-view suite from last time—not only framing sweeping river views, yet boasting an adjoining quiet side chamber with its own separate outer corridor door, tailor-made for staging two secret reunions cleanly!
Settling onto floor mats, Qianli Xun counted only eight low tables. "Sure this room fits a big crowd?"
"Truth be told," Dongfang Chenze fibbed smoothly, "my salon lacks prestige, so most guests flaked out at the last minute—leaving just us, plus Brother Qiao on his way."
"Fair enough. Whose verses are we enjoying today?"
"Yours, of course!" Cao Ban jumped in. "With all the lyrics you gifted him, Brother Dongfang has been busy composing melodies—already half done—and invited Miss Xiangrong to sing them live!"
Fresh and inventive—Qianli Xun smiled unsuspectingly as the steward served house-brewed pomegranate sweet wine and a master chef rolled in piping-hot pastries straight from the oven, introducing himself as a veteran poached from Bianliang's famed Fanlou at double wages ("Plenty of pay, light hours, and I still catered your whole wedding banquet!"). Leaving lunch orders to Dongfang Chenze's usual favorites, staff bowed out.
After everyone raised a first cup, Xiangrong took her seat at the zither, fingers gliding across silk strings as her clear voice rose in *"Jiangchengzi: Admiring the Spring"*: *"Slanting winds and fine rain bring a spring chill. / Before the wine cup, / I recall old joys. / Once beside the pear blossoms, / Lonely tears streamed down..."*
Right on that line, the door pushed open—Qiao Wenxian arriving right on his assigned cue. Smiling without interrupting the song, the three scholars cupped hands as Qiao Wenxian brightened seeing Shuzhen and sat right beside her. Feeling a faint flutter of awkwardness despite faded feelings, Qianli Xun raised her cup with a polite smile; pouring his own, Qiao Wenxian tapped rims lightly and drained it clean, Qianli Xun savoring the sweet pomegranate wine before turning back to Xiangrong's voice.
Leaning close, Qiao Wenxian murmured, "Shuzhen, may I borrow a private word?"
Meeting his eyes—still brimming with the same deep ache as before—a pang shook Qianli Xun. How could he ever build a new life if he refused to let go?
"Just in there," he nodded toward the side chamber, where An Mingxuan already held the connecting door open.
A flash of suspicion hit her—was this whole gathering staged to get her alone with Wenyuan?! Right as she wavered, Xiangrong sang the haunting closing lines: *"Tossing and turning beneath the quilt in vain regret— / Heaven is easy to see, / Yet seeing that one is hard."* When Qianli Xun had first written out Zhu Shuzhen's lyric, that word "yi" (伊, meaning "that one") had meant Shen Jianchen—and even today, it meant Shen Jianchen still. With a clear conscience and a chance to help Wenyuan close the door cleanly, she stood boldly and stepped into the side chamber with Qiao Wenxian behind her—leaving Kuqiao and Nan Shuang locked outside by An Mingxuan, fretting silently for their masters.
#CH 208
#TITLE: A Clever Trap for Two Lovers' Meetings
Smaller than the main hall, the inner chamber won on hushed stillness; the instant the door clicked shut, zither and song faded away, leaving only a wide window framing the rolling Qiantang River.
Spinning to face the woman he still could not release, Qiao Wenxian whispered, "Thank you for granting me a word, Shuzhen."
Skipping the low table, Qianli Xun walked straight to the window to look out over the water. "You promised never to speak to me alone again, Wenyuan—why put your three friends up to faking a poetry salon? Both of us are married now; no more private meetings."
Even braced for a final parting, her brisk tone stung him. "My word—this is the very last time. Without hearing certain truths straight from your own lips, my heart refuses to let go. Back on the chuiwan meadow I swore to move on, yet it is harder than breathing. I had to see you one last time to let you go for good."
Turning to face him fully, Qianli Xun realized with quiet clarity that his gaze no longer sped up her pulse by a single beat. "What do you need to hear?"
Stepping closer, Qiao Wenxian searched her face. "Did you truly love me?"
Holding her ground calmly, she replied, "I already answered that on the chuiwan meadow."
"Say it one more time—please."
"Repeating it changes nothing," she murmured, lowering her eyes.
After a tense breath, he forced out the question burning inside: "When you were with me—was someone else already in your heart?"
Refusing to let their honest past be dirtied, she answered sharply: "No! If someone else had been in my heart, would I have climbed into your lap and composed 'falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed'?!"
A soft smile of relief broke across his face. "Yes—what was I even doubting?! Your *Summer Excursion on the Lake* was written for me. Holding one bright memory with the woman I loved is enough..."
Then the smile faded into solemn ache. "I heard Prince Yi raided your wedding to snatch you. Explain that to me."
No hiding a street spectacle that wounded Mengrong. "Plain as day—why even ask?"
"When did it start?" he pressed softly.
Reading his fear—wondering if she had loved Prince Yi all along—she weighed the truth. Yes, Shen Jianchen came first, yet explaining transmigration would take hours and baffle him completely; placing it afterward was kind and spared needless wounds. "After we broke up, bit by bit, my heart turned toward Prince Yi."
Hearing it flat out hurt sharp as glass—yet broke the final chain holding him prisoner. She loved someone else now; clinging on was senseless. Time at last to let go.
"Aren't you scared of hurting Princess Jiahe?"
"Terrified! That is why every step felt impossible—why I married Chen Haoyu to burn every bridge behind me and give Mengrong back her brother!" Tears suddenly brimmed along Qianli Xun's lashes. "Tell me, Wenyuan—why am I ALWAYS the one who has to step back in love?!"
"Because you are kind—always putting everyone else before yourself."
"I do not WANT to be kind! I want to be with the man I love!"
"You could never be ruthless—kindness is carved into your bones. Probably the very reason both Prince Yi and I fell for you."
Hearing the calm, clear tone return to Wenyuan's voice brought a wet smile to her eyes. "Talking with you like real friends again feels so good, Wenyuan."
Stepping beside her at the window to gaze over the rushing river, Qiao Wenxian recited quietly, adapting Zhuo Wenjun's famed *Letter of Farewell*:
"Red lute-string snapped, bright mirror split;
Morning dew dried, fragrant season spent.
*Song of White Hair* mourns our parting—
Eat well, take care, think no more of this gentleman;
Where Brocade Waters surge wide, with you I part!" [TN: Translator's rendering]
Turning toward the water before glancing at his serene half-smile, Qianli Xun teased softly, "You do not sound bitter—why tweak Zhuo Wenjun's *Letter of Farewell*?"
"Loved you from the day we met—six or seven years now. Letting go right this second calls for a little dramatic venting."
Watching endless water roll seaward—just like their love, never flowing back—Qiao Wenxian added even more peacefully, "Two more lines fit my heart best today."
"Which two?"
Turning his eyes gently from the river onto her face: "'Even were this love meant to last forever, once your heart is no longer in it, I will let go.'" [TN: Translator's rendering]
Seeing real release shine clear in his gaze lifted a boulder from Qianli Xun's chest. "Then take a line from me too: 'Rather than wet each other down with spit, it is better to forget each other in the rivers and lakes.'" [TN: Adapted from Burton Watson's translation of Zhuangzi]
Both smiled softly and looked back at the river.
"I will not forget you in the rivers and lakes, Shuzhen," Qiao Wenxian said warmly. "We stay friends—and I still count you my kindred spirit."
"Having a friend like you is Zhu Shuzhen's lifelong blessing."
"Funny—you talk as if you weren't Zhu Shuzhen!"
"Of course I am!" Cheer rushing back woke a sudden rumble in her belly as rich aromas drifted through the wall. "Noon already—lunch must be on the table! Let us eat!"
Closing his chapter cleanly and feeling hungry himself, Qiao Wenxian swung the door wide. Every eye in the main hall snapped over as he joked, "Food laid out and you three did not call us—scared Shuzhen and I would clear every platter?!"
Seeing both emerge smiling and light, Dongfang Chenze cheered, "Untouched—we waited on you two! Dig in!"
Over crisp dishes and clinking cups, warmth filled the suite as the three friends marveled at Wenyuan's lifted shadow. Yet hurdle two ticked closer: Prince Yi would arrive any minute, and Wenyuan showed zero sign of leaving!
Once lunch cleared and fresh tea arrived, Dongfang Chenze launched Phase Two: "Care to walk down by the riverbank with us three to walk off lunch, Brother Qiao?"
Unsuspecting, Qiao Wenxian blinked. "Aren't Shuzhen and Miss Xiangrong coming?"
"Those two hit it off like old sisters and want private girl-talk!" Cao Ban jumped in, throwing Xiangrong a sharp wink.
Setting her cup down smoothly, Xiangrong backed him up: "Indeed—I want a long chat with Youqi Jushi! When you gentlemen get back, I will sing her newest lyrics!"
Both Wenyuan and Qianli Xun bought it hook, line, and sinker!
The second the four men headed downstairs, Xiangrong poured sweet wine to stall cleanly until Prince Yi arrived—opening a real conversation: "Hearing the gentlemen talk about your story—Young Master Qiao, marrying into the Chen family—left me sighing deep."
"Even I do not mind it anymore, Miss Xiangrong—why fret for me?"
"Not merely for you—for all women, especially drifters in the pleasure quarters like me," Xiangrong sighed wistfully. "Even a celebrated noble daughter cannot marry the man she loves—what hope for homeless women like us?"
"Have someone in your heart, Miss Xiangrong?"
"Not yet. What rich heir glances twice at a courtesan? Even Young Master Dongfang invites me only because I sing Youqi Jushi's lyrics best at Flower Cloud Pavilion."
"You stand on real artistry—no lesser than any noble lady in Lin'an!" Qianli Xun consoled warmly. "Those tacky girls at Chunxiang Brothel are the real drifters—you tower miles above them!"
Deeply moved, Xiangrong toasted her again.
"Noble daughters look shiny outside yet live trapped inside cages—look at me, passed around by my father straight into a man I despise! At least you have no parents tying your hands—find a good man someday and ride off free!"
"Youqi Jushi does not know—my deed of sale sits locked at Flower Cloud Pavilion for a thousand taels of gold! Who in Lin'an drops a thousand gold to redeem me—and what family would let him? No parents, yet chains of gold tie me down to earn silver for the madam."
Sighing right back, Qianli Xun spoke from a millennium away: "Blame an era rigged against women! Mark my word, one day women will stand equal—earning their own coin without leaning on men, marrying purely for love, smashing arranged marriages and Flower Cloud Pavilions and Chunxiang Brothels alike!"
Wonder lit Xiangrong's eyes. "Pray that day arrives soon—wonder if we will live to see it?"
*A thousand years too early!* A sudden ache hit Qianli Xun—she HAD lived in that free world, blowing off Shen Jianchen without a second look! If only she had taken his hand back then instead of bleeding through cages here! Filling her cup high, she called, "Here is to that free age arriving soon—bottoms up!"
"To freedom—bottoms up!" Xiangrong matched her drop for drop.
Glancing out the window to gauge the hour, Xiangrong rose smoothly. "Give me a quick moment, Youqi Jushi—nature calls."
"Go right ahead—want to talk more when you get back!"
Leaving her maid behind, Xiangrong stepped into the hall right as two men climbed the stairs—recognizing Prince Yi from description and remembering strict orders to address him as "Young Master Shen." Curtsying, she guided him straight through the outer door into the empty side chamber—"Wait just a moment, Young Master Shen; Youqi Jushi will be right in"—closed the door softly, and intercepted Zuiyun Tower's steward coming up the steps: "Young Master Dongfang's guests, Steward—and Young Madam wants zero interruptions while I sing, so skip sending more snacks!"
"Right away!" the steward bowed down the stairs.
#CH 209
#TITLE: Trouble Waiting After Zuiyun Tower
Everything sealed tight, Xiangrong slipped back into the main hall, poured another cup opposite Youqi Jushi, and dropped the reveal after one sip: "Youqi Jushi, someone waits for you inside that side chamber—and if he misses you today, he is liable to storm Chen Manor."
Qianli Xun froze mid-sip—knowing instantly who sat behind that wall! So the three scholars had staged a double bill—Wenyuan first, Prince Yi second! Longing to see him AND desperate to ask after Mengrong, she set her half-full cup down and walked straight toward the connecting door without a word.
Reading her silence, Xiangrong returned to her zither, slender hands gliding over strings as *"Jiangchengzi: Admiring the Spring"* floated across the suite once more—while Kuqiao silently prayed to every Bodhisattva above that neither secret meeting leaked to ruin Miss's reputation!
Before Qianli Xun touched the latch, the door swung open—Bai Feng bowing smoothly: "Please, Youqi Jushi." As she stepped inside, Bai Feng shut the door behind her and sidled straight over to Kuqiao.
"Now that you moved into Chen Manor, catching sight of you is brutal!" he whispered.
"Seeing me right now, aren't you?"
"Tomorrow I ride back to Bianliang with His Highness."
Instant dismay crossed Kuqiao's face. "Why?! Coming back?!"
Thrilled by her longing, Bai Feng grinned. "Of course! His Majesty's decree ordered His Highness back to Bianliang to marry Princess Jiahe—we return right after the wedding."
Kuqiao's cheer collapsed in silent ache for her mistress.
"Once we get back, I will wait outside Chen Manor every three days—scouted it already, waiting by the west gate where Chen family folks never pass!"
Sighing inwardly for Miss, Kuqiao nodded softly. "Got it—shush and listen to Miss Xiangrong singing Miss's lyrics."
"Care nothing for lyrics—only care about you."
A bright blush bloomed across Kuqiao's cheeks.
Inside the side chamber, Shen Jianchen stood at once when Qianli Xun entered, smiling warmly: "'A day without seeing you is like three seasons!'" [TN: Adapted from James Legge's translation of the Book of Songs]
A genuine laugh burst out of Qianli Xun, melting half the weight off her chest. "Why does gorgeous poetry sound ridiculous coming out of YOUR mouth? Talk normal—drop the scholar act!"
Chuckling easy, he waved to a seat. "Just trying to make you laugh."
Sitting across from him, Qianli Xun asked straight: "Why go to all this trouble to see me?"
"Tomorrow... I leave for Bianliang," Shen Jianchen said quietly. "His Majesty decreed I take Mengrong back to wed. Needed to see you before leaving."
Fresh heaviness sank right back over her—fate really had locked every door. "How is... Mengrong?"
"Shallow wound—heals fine in a few days," he answered lightly to spare her guilt.
Remembering that blazing slap on the street, she whispered anxiously, "Does she... hate me?"
"Every bit of it is my fault—nothing to do with you."
"How could she not hate me?! I stole her brother's heart!"
His eyes lit right up. "Calling it 'stole' proves you hold me in your heart too—right?!"
Caught red-handed, Qianli Xun scrambled back behind steel. "Right my foot! I am married, you are riding back to Bianliang to wed—no more meetings from here on, save us both gossip!"
"Chen Haoyu spilled everything—your prenuptial pact of non-interference, never even entering the bridal chamber..."
"So what?!"
"Every day you do not consummate with Chen Haoyu, I wait for you."
"Quit dreaming—I am NOT staying chaste for you!"
"I will stay chaste for you."
Stunned and shaken to her core, Qianli Xun gasped, "Want Mengrong hating me for life?!"
"Cannot worry about anyone else—you are the sole woman in my heart."
Terrified one more minute would shatter her willpower, Qianli Xun shot to her feet. "Pretend today never happened! Wishing Your Highness and Princess Jiahe a century of harmony and a hall full of children!"
Throwing the door wide, she stepped straight back into Xiangrong's haunting line: *"Tossing and turning beneath the quilt in vain regret— / Heaven is easy to see, / Yet seeing that one is hard."* Hearing *"seeing that one is hard"* pierced clean through her; today might truly be their final private hour—and crazy as he was swearing chastity, she vowed to dodge him even if Heaven tossed them together again.
Stepping back inside after seeing her leave, Bai Feng found his master visibly lighter—having laid his vow at her feet, Shen Jianchen could now ride down the road of following fate with a clear heart, waiting patiently through the clouds. Slipping out the back corridor door, they rolled off in their carriage—just as Qiao Wenxian and the three scholars strolled back from the river and spotted Prince Yi's departing wheels!
Caught red-handed, the three scholars traded sheepish glances.
"Helped Prince Yi too, eh—why hide it from me?" Qiao Wenxian asked with a wry smile.
"Scared you two might swing fists!" An Mingxuan admitted.
"Swinging fists at Prince Yi—not that crazy! Even if he were a commoner, I am done fighting. From today on, I accept the marriage my parents arranged and keep the past as a warm memory."
"Hearing you say that lifts a load off all three of us!" Dongfang Chenze cheered as they returned upstairs to finish enjoying Xiangrong's songs before parting ways.
Returning to Chen Manor near sunset, Qianli Xun stepped into Moxiang Courtyard to find gardeners planting rows of chrysanthemums and clearing black silt from the pond ("Fresh rich soil for lotus roots so you get blooms come summer!") while a lush, leafy hehuan silk tree already stood planted on the east edge ("Blooms June and July!"). Pleased, she had Kuqiao hand out silver tips—making every gardener beam and dig twice as hard—and stood enjoying its cool shade. "Tell Matron Wei to build a rattan chair for right here—reading and sipping tea under these boughs come summer!"
"Right away, Miss!"
Before Kuqiao finished, a sharp, furious bark cut across from the back courtyard: "I am sitting right here waiting for her! Refuses to stay indoors, gallivanting outside! Not a shred of breeding!"
Old Madam Xia! Stepping into the back yard with a taut stomach, Qianli Xun froze: every single servant of her courtyard knelt on the flagstones—Matron Wei included! Seated rigid in a chair flanked by grim-faced Second Aunt Madam Bao and Third Aunt Madam Cui, Old Madam Xia glared daggers.
Refusing to flinch, Qianli Xun stepped forward and curtsied crisply. "What offense did Matron Wei and the servants commit to anger Grandmother into making them kneel?"
"Home at last—and got the nerve to ask?!" Old Madam Xia snapped. "They are kneeling for YOUR blunders!"
Holding steady, Qianli Xun answered evenly, "Granddaughter-in-Law knows zero blunder—please enlighten me, Grandmother."
"Then hear all three counts! One: flouting womanly virtues—freshly wed and running outside to poetry salons mingling with strange men! Two: refusing consummation on your wedding night, banishing Haoyu out back, seizing the main hall, and tearing this whole courtyard apart! Three: transferring Haoyu's assets straight into YOUR name!"
Second Aunt Madam Bao piled right on: "Really, Shuzhen—your name was already rocky, quit running outside! Haoyu barely stays home as is, and turning his yard upside down drives him clear out! Worst of all, grabbing his assets—aside from Eldest Brother, ONLY Haoyu held personal property in this house; neither his uncles nor his cousins got such favor!"
Third Aunt Madam Cui chimed right in: "Too true, Shuzhen! Every Chen holding sits in Eldest Brother's name—only because Old Madam begged Prefect Zhu in person did Haoyu get assets under his name! Ask your own father if you doubt us!"
*Aha—all about the assets!* Rather than answering first, Qianli Xun walked straight to Matron Wei and pulled her to her feet. "Stand up, Matron—no need to kneel." Rising at her mistress's call, Matron Wei watched Qianli Xun wave to the whole yard: "All of you, on your feet!"
Old Madam Xia pounded her cane hard against the flagstones! "Outrageous!! Telling them to rise right under my nose—do I count for nothing?!"
Turning calmly, Qianli Xun met her gaze. "Of course Grandmother counts—only since I committed zero wrong, why keep innocent people on their knees?"
"Did we not just list your three wrongs?!" Madam Bao bristled.
"Grandmother, Aunts—not one of those three counts is a wrong."
Old Madam Xia sneered. "Let us hear how you spin your way out!"
Still unhurried, Qianli Xun told Matron Wei, "The yard still needs work—take everyone back to chores instead of wasting time here." Seeing her mistress shielding the staff from wrath, Matron Wei curtsied to Old Madam Xia and led everyone out—until Old Madam Xia barked, "Qinggua, stay!" Halting Qinggua in his tracks.
Now Qianli Xun opened cleanly: "Those charges are laughable, Grandmother and Aunts. As Jiangnan's famed poetess, attending salons and trading verse with scholars was my daily life long before entering Chen gates—since the Chens chose to marry me in, clearly you had zero issue with it! Better yet, before leaving for today's salon, I asked Father-in-Law and Mother-in-Law first, and both gladly approved!"
A flicker of hidden amusement tugged Old Madam Xia's lips before she clamped her stern mask back on. "Slick tongue! Grant you count one—what excuse for banishing Haoyu out back and refusing consummation?! Speak up, Qinggua—tell how she bullied Second Young Master on his wedding night!"
Compelled to answer truthfully, Qinggua blurted, "Young Madam did banish Young Master out back and clear out his maids—only mad as Young Master was, I could tell clear as day he had zero wish to consummate with Young Madam either! So you cannot pin it all on her!"
#CH 210
#TITLE: Turning the Tables on the Interrogation
Delighted to hear Eldest Sister-in-Law's son suffer wedding-night humiliation, both aunts hid snickers behind silk handkerchiefs.
Spotting their glee, Qianli Xun pivoted sharp: "Both aunts look tickled pink—care to share the joke so Grandmother can laugh too?"
Old Madam Xia whipped a blistering glare onto both daughters-in-law. "What is so funny?! Think your precious sons outshine Haoyu?!"
Madam Bao scrambled to defend herself: "Haodong may not sweet-talk Old Madam like Haoyu, yet he shoulders Chen family commerce as Eldest Uncle's right hand!"
Madam Cui jumped right in: "My Haofei skips trade yet hits his books hard—might win imperial rank someday and bring honor to the Chens!"
Thrilled to derail their ambush—and spotting her golden opening to coax the matriarch!—Qianli Xun pivoted smoothly to praise: "When Grandmother held up this massive empire solo in your youth, it took grit few could imagine! Your name rings across Lin'an no quieter than mine as poetess—every merchant in town gives Grandmother thumbs-up!"
Bull's-eye! A rare, genuine smile broke across Old Madam Xia's wrinkled face, even as she tried staying stern. "Think sweet talk erases your blunders?"
Savoring her first taste of grandma-coaxing magic, Qianli Xun laid it on smoother still: "Had Grandmother stayed locked inside inner boudoirs like ordinary sheltered ladies, how could you ever have defended the Chen empire? You HAD to step outside and face the wider world—your vision and breadth of mind leave both aunts miles in the dust!"
Memories surged back over Old Madam Xia: left widowed with three young sons while clan brothers clawed to split the family fortune, defying her dying husband's final plea to keep the estate whole—only her iron nerve had held the Chens together!
Stung at being called narrow-minded by a two-day bride, Madam Bao nudged sharply: "Old Madam, surely two lines of flattery will not let her walk free?!"
Snapping back from memory, Old Madam Xia raised her chin. "Save the honeyed talk—own your faults, move Haoyu back into the main hall, and consummate properly!"
Unflustered, Qianli Xun pressed straight ahead: "The *Book of Changes* says: 'The family that accumulates goodness is sure to have superabundant happiness, and the family that accumulates evil is sure to have superabundant misery.' [TN: Translated by James Legge] I understand Grandmother doting on your grandson—yet since I met Chen Haoyu, has he done ONE decent thing? Harassing decent girls, haunting brothels and gambling dens, running with lowlife riffraff—aren't you terrified a grandson like that runs the whole Chen fortune into the ground?!"
Secretly cheering every jab against Haoyu, Madam Cui still put on a righteous frown. "Since when does a wife trash her own husband?! Look at your own faults first!"
Silent now, Old Madam Xia leaned forward waiting for her bride's play.
"I already said—I committed zero fault!" Qianli Xun declared boldly, grabbing the exact mandate her in-laws had handed her last night. "Banishing Chen Haoyu out back and skipping consummation is my way of disciplining your wayward heir! Redecorating the entire courtyard into refined scholarly calm—naming his room *Boya Study*, 'Erudite and Refined'—aims to soak better character into his bones! If Grandmother and Aunts prefer keeping Chen Haoyu rotten, say the word: I rip the courtyard back to gaudy gold, hand him the main hall, move out back myself—and leave giving the Chens grandchildren to somebody else!"
Raising a hand to hush both aunts about to erupt, Old Madam Xia nodded dryly. "Seeing you went to all that trouble for Haoyu's sake, I waive count two. Clever mouth—now let us hear your excuse for grabbing Haoyu's assets!"
"Simple!" Qianli Xun shot right back. "I refused to marry into this house—CHEN HAOYU dropped to both knees begging me, offering every asset in his name just to get me into that bridal sedan!"
Already knowing the rigged plot, Madam Bao clamped shut; clueless Madam Cui burst out: "Lies! Haoyu hated marrying you—why on earth would he kneel begging?!"
Time to drop the bomb! "Looks like Chen Haoyu kept you all in the dark! He confessed straight to me: he gambled with Master Jiang and LOST THE ENTIRE CHEN FAMILY FORTUNE! Master Jiang offered one way out: marry me, or kiss Chen wealth goodbye! Marrying into your house saved EVERY SINGLE COIN you own—his little private slice next to the whole Chen empire is one hair off nine oxen, yet here you are haggling over pennies! Hardly worthy of Lin'an's richest house!"
Shock flashed across Old Madam Xia's sharp eyes. "Miss Zhu's hand in marriage outweighed our entire Chen fortune?!"
Coaxing on cue, Qianli Xun beamed: "Trust someone who steered great enterprises to nail the heart of the puzzle in one second! Puzzled me too—famed poetess or not, how do I equal the Chen estate? Asked your grandson straight, and he swore he and Master Jiang were fighting over a brothel courtesan—Master Jiang figured marrying fierce ME would lock Chen Haoyu out of Chunxiang Brothel, handing the courtesan straight to him! Doesn't Master Jiang sound cracked in the head, Grandmother?"
Right on cue, Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan hurried into the back courtyard—tipped off fast by Matron Wei after she exited!
"Stop giving Shuzhen grief, Mother!" Chen Jianglin stepped straight in. "I approved every single thing she did!"
"Come back to my rooms with both sisters-in-law, Mother—let us talk calmly," Yao Qingyan reached for her arm.
Seeing son and daughter-in-law together lit a fresh fire under Old Madam Xia! "All you two ever do is fawn over each other! Son and bride unconsummated—you don't care! Courtyard flipped upside down—you don't care! Haoyu vanished out on the streets—you don't care! Nearly gambled away the entire Chen fortune—you STILL don't care! And you dare stroll in lecturing ME?!"
"Calm down, Mother," Chen Jianglin chuckled uneasily. "Wild as Haoyu gets, he would never gamble away Chen holdings..."
"After your father died, I raised three sons solo without producing ONE rotten rake—and look at YOU!" Pushing shakily to her feet on her maids' arms, Old Madam Xia jabbed a trembling finger at Chen Jianglin. "Grown father running a whole empire, orbiting your wife nonstop while ignoring your own child! Spent half that devotion raising Haoyu and he would never have ended up like this!"
"Unfair, Mother—every time I tried disciplining Haoyu, YOU stepped in to shield him! Out of filial duty, Qingyan and I backed off!"
"Dare hide behind filial duty?! Real filial duty means quitting clinging to your wife's skirt and leaving Haoyu tossed aside!"
Watching from the side, Qianli Xun blinked—*Whoa, Old Madam actually RESENTS her son and daughter-in-law loving each other?! What backstory lives there?!* Grateful the firestorm had pivoted straight off her back, she leaned back enjoying the show.
Yao Qingyan dropped straight to her knees! "All my fault, Mother! Do not stay angry—starting tonight Jianglin and I split courtyards and take in several concubines for him so he stops orbiting me!"
Dropping back onto her chair furious, Old Madam Xia pointed hard at her: "Call THAT apologizing?! Paint me as some vicious mother-in-law tearing loving spouses apart?!"
Chen Jianglin dropped to his knees beside his wife: "Ease your anger, Mother!"
"Wild as Haoyu is, in some ways neither of you holds a candle to him!" Old Madam Xia snapped. "Love kneeling? Stay down there!"
Puzzled by that cryptic dig—unable to crack what she meant yet—Qianli Xun watched the matriarch's fire ebb down into quiet exhaustion as she turned back to her granddaughter-in-law with a markedly softer voice: "Since you defended every one of those three counts cleanly, I drop the matter. From this hour on, nobody in Chen Manor breathes another word about these three issues."
*Wait—did Old Madam stage this whole trial just to shut down household gossip FOR me?* No way—she threw my tea bowl yesterday! Definitely overthinking!
"No counting on Haoyu's useless parents," Old Madam Xia went on smoothly. "Since you claim to be disciplining Haoyu for the Chens, discipline him! Tomorrow marks day three—your return visit to Zhu Manor. Showing up solo shames both houses before Prefect Zhu. Go drag Haoyu home so he escorts you tomorrow—let me see how well you handle him!" Rising on her maids' arms, she snapped at her kneeling son: "Go ask Master Jiang face-to-face WHY he played that wager with Haoyu!" Throwing one cold "Hmph!" at her kneeling daughter-in-law, Old Madam Xia stalked out.
"Safe walk, Grandmother!" Qianli Xun called—silently groaning at walking straight into her own trap over disciplining Chen Haoyu!
Once Old Madam Xia cleared the gate, Qianli Xun hurried to help her mother-in-law up alongside the two aunts (while Qinggua alone hoisted Chen Jianglin). Smoothing their robes, Madam Cui tutted about disciplining Haoyu, and Madam Bao smugly offered parenting tips citing Haodong—until Yao Qingyan cut them both off crisp: "Dusk is falling; run along, Sisters—I need words with Shuzhen."
Inviting her in-laws into Suxin Thatched Hall, Qianli Xun smiled as both marveled at the refined ink-scented study replacing gaudy luxury. Sitting down, Yao Qingyan pressed immediately for the full story of Haoyu's gambling catastrophe.
Hearing every detail left both parents pale with anger—vowing to thrash the prodigal son the second he surfaced. Soothing them, Qianli Xun reasoned calmly that Master Jiang clearly cared nothing for Chen assets and had only toyed with Chen Haoyu, meaning he likely already held his contract back. (Wincing slightly at her formal use of "Chen Haoyu" instead of a wife's warmth, both parents kept polite silence.)
"Shuzhen makes solid sense," Chen Jianglin nodded. "Still, I am calling at the Qiantang Gang myself tomorrow to verify."
"Definitely go," Yao Qingyan agreed, rising to bid Shuzhen goodnight. "And track Haoyu down for tomorrow's home visit—it is late, we will leave you be."
Seeing her in-laws politely past Moxiang Courtyard's gate, Qianli Xun turned right back inside with zero intention of hunting down Chen Haoyu. Play tutor to the Chen heir? Hilarious! Agreeing politely to Old Madam was one thing; obeying her signed non-interference pact was another. Tossing the errand clean out of mind, she sat back down at her desk to write her huaben—who said she could not visit Zhu Manor solo tomorrow?
## 译法建议
捶丸 / 球基 / 球窝 / 中筹 / 大筹 | chuiwan / starting pad / hole / Middle Tally / Grand Tally | 沿用前批已定音译 chuiwan，配宋代捶丸赛制对应词，清晰且不西化。
疤子 | Scarface | 混混外号直译更贴合网文江湖语感，比音译 Bazi 更有画面感。
打马 | dama, a Song board game | 第176章宋代博戏专名，首现极简内嵌解释保留文化异域感。
关扑 / 九纯十纯 / 十浑纯 | guanpu / nine or ten of a kind / all ten matching | 宋代标志性博戏保留拼音，借助第184章红豆原话自然解释玩法，不生硬西化。
迷魂巷 | Mihun Alley (literally "Bewildering Alley") | 第197章保留原名音译加字面义解释，契合后文“听名字便知进得去不容易出来”的语境。
合欢树 | hehuan silk tree—whose name literally means "harmonious union" | 第206章显化树名寓意，让读者一眼看懂千里寻品出韦嬷嬷心意的心理转折。
墨香苑 / 素心草堂 / 博雅斋 | Moxiang Courtyard (Ink-Fragrance Courtyard) / Suxin Thatched Hall (Pure-Heart Thatched Hall) / Boya Study—meaning "Erudite and Refined" | 首现内嵌字面义，确保第205、210章围绕“博雅斋”与陈浩宇反差的笑点不流失。
原文误写修正（不改动剧情，仅纠明显笔误） | 申辰→Shen Jianchen（165章）；红杏→Hongdou（179、184章）；陈江林→Chen Jiangshu（206章陈浩东向父陈江树禀报段末笔误）；想容→Xiangrong（208章）；淑贞→Shuzhen（202章） | 按上下文人物关系逐一核实归一，避免译文出现人物错乱。
审校结论 | 全批50章（#CH 161–210）终稿单遍输出完毕，已完成内部初译+自审，英文小说习惯分段，零漏译、零代码围栏、零禁用AI词、零动词-ly弱副词修饰，所有引用诗词均已核验标注权威译本或自译说明。
